# Findings — what we actually know

<!-- owns:     every number and every decision this project relies on, one line each
     not mine: the design (transaction-decoupling.md), the schedule (build-plan.md),
               the narrative of how a measurement was taken (perf-results.md)
     rule:     living docs cite an ID. They do not restate a value. A number appearing
               in prose outside this file and the log is a bug. -->

The design says what we intend to build. The log says what happened on which day. This says
**what we know**, one line per item, with the regime it was measured in and a status.

**Why the regime column exists.** Nearly every contradiction the cross-check found was two
correct numbers from different conditions presented as one disputed number. A value without its
regime is not a finding.

| regime | what it means |
|---|---|
| `source` | read from the code, not measured |
| `loopback` | one host, Ryzen 9 3900X 12c/24t, peers on loopback |
| `wan-12` | twelve nodes across three continents. **Mostly 4-thread VPS, but the set includes a 24-thread and a 12-thread host**, and **no smartnode features were active** — no quorums, no islocks, no chainlocks — so every `wan-12` figure is an **upper bound** |
| `mario` | the Xeon 6517P, 16 physical cores / 32 threads |
| `bench-rt` | isolated regtest node on bowser, hand-driven (`bench_inputs`, `bench_sigops`, the probe) |
| `mainnet` | observed on a live mainnet node |
| `quorum-rt` | regtest with real smartnodes and a small quorum |
| `rig-8mb` | measured on this branch, where `MAX_DIP0001_BLOCK_SIZE` is 8 MB and **not** upstream's 2 MB |

Status is `stands`, `unreconciled` (two correct values, no single answer yet), or
`superseded by <ID>`.

---

## Constants (read from source)

| ID | constant | value | symbol / file | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| K-1 | block size cap, upstream | 2,000,000 | `consensus/consensus.h:MAX_DIP0001_BLOCK_SIZE` | stands |
| K-2 | block sigop cap | `MaxBlockSize()/50` → 40,000 at 2 MB | `consensus/consensus.h:MaxBlockSigOps` | stands |
| K-3 | per-transaction size cap | 100,000 B, **consensus** when DIP0001 active | `validation.cpp:MAX_STANDARD_TX_SIZE`, checked in `validation.cpp:ContextualCheckTransaction` | stands |
| K-4 | script cache | 524,288 entries (32 MB default, halved) | `validation.cpp:scriptExecutionCache.setup_bytes` | stands |
| K-5 | relay cap per trickle | flat **50,000** (F-5's own measured value), no longer indexed to block size (2.3, F-138, 2026-09-21 -- was `140 × MaxBlockSize()/1e6`, 1,120 under today's 8 MB default) | `net_processing.cpp:InvBroadcastMax` | **updated** |
| K-6 | trickle interval | 5 s inbound (Poisson mean), 2 s outbound, 1 s verified smartnode | `net_processing.cpp:INVENTORY_BROADCAST_INTERVAL` | stands |
| K-7 | max protocol message | 3 MB | `net.h:MAX_PROTOCOL_MESSAGE_LENGTH` | stands |
| K-8 | mempool default | 300 MB | `policy/policy.h:DEFAULT_MAX_MEMPOOL_SIZE` | stands |
| K-9 | ancestor / descendant limits | 25 count, 101 kB each | `validation.h:DEFAULT_ANCESTOR_LIMIT` | stands |
| K-10 | compact-block index ceiling | 65,535, an explicit deserialisation **guard**, not a wire limit (the wire form is CompactSize) | `blockencodings.h:BlockTxCount` | stands |
| K-11 | extra payload cap | 10,000 B | `consensus/consensus.h:MAX_TX_EXTRA_PAYLOAD` | stands |
| K-12 | IS wait gate | 600 s | `llmq/quorums_chainlocks.h:WAIT_FOR_ISLOCK_TIMEOUT` | stands |
| K-14 | **planning** bytes per transaction, for all storage arithmetic | **400 B** → 12.6 GB/yr per sustained tx/s | a choice, not a measurement — see D-12 and F-30 | stands |
| K-13 | mainnet quorum sizes above 600 smartnodes | IS 50; ChainLocks 200 (with `QUORUMS_200_8`) or 400 | `chainparams.cpp:UpdateLLMQParams` | stands |

## Measured facts

### Relay

| ID | fact | value | regime | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-1 | relay delivery against 1,500 offered, 21 connections | **~900-930 tx/s** (queueing-slope fit 936, cross-validated 864-922 by counting the miner's acceptances) | `wan-12` | stands. F-3's 824 is the **peer sweep's** run at the same peer count — 12% lower, run-to-run, not a contradiction |
| F-2 | relay is message-handling-bound, not schedule-bound | cutting the trickle interval 5× moved it **1.4%** | `wan-12` | stands |
| F-3 | per-peer share of msghand cost | ~40%: 21 conns → 824 tx/s at 1,084 µs/tx; 5 conns → 1,221 tx/s at 640 µs/tx | `wan-12` | stands |
| F-4 | per-peer delivery under the shipped cap | **56 tx/s** — 280 entries per trickle (K-5) on a Poisson timer averaging 5 s (K-6). Measured 44-65 across runs, which is Poisson trickle-count noise of ±18% at one sigma over 150 s, and every inv payload on the link was exactly 10,083 B = 3 + 280 × 36 | `source` + `loopback` | **stands.** The **74.7** figure quoted elsewhere is a single pre-#480 run on the busy-looping node and was never reproduced — do not use it |
| F-5 | re-indexed cap that converges | 50,000 converges 1,500 tx/s to 8 and 16 peers; 10,000 suffices at the v1 point; 7,500 is schedule-bound | `loopback` | stands |
| F-6 | peers hold nested, not complementary, subsets | union across peers contributed nothing over the best single peer | `loopback` | stands |

### Acceptance and validation

| ID | fact | value | regime | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-7 | acceptance ceiling | one ceiling, two statistics of it: **~4,400-4,500 tx/s** is the whole-run mean over a long saturated fill; **5,269 tx/s** is the per-second median (p90 5,550, peak 5,779). Both corpora are 2-in/2-out at depth 5 | `loopback` | **stands** — quote the mean for capacity planning and the median for a per-second ceiling, and say which |
| F-8 | msghand at 1,500 tx/s on a 4-thread VPS | **61-72%** of one core in the 15-s-block run, **89-90%** in the peer sweep, 89% when blocks come every 3 s | `wan-12` | stands — and this is why F-3's per-delivered cost is the comparable metric: a CPU percentage without the delivered rate beside it says nothing |
| F-9 | signature verification share of acceptance | **two thirds** (removing it: 5,269 → 15,842 tx/s) | `loopback` | stands |
| F-10 | naive pooling of ATMP script checks | **10% slower** than stock at 2× CPU — synchronisation exceeds ~59 µs/input of work | `loopback` | stands |
| F-11 | cost against input count, P2PKH | **linear** over the legal range; **156 µs/input**; worst legal transaction (650 inputs, 96 kB) **~100 ms** | `bench-rt` | stands |
| F-12 | cost against input count, multisig | P2SH 1-of-15 all keys tried: **1,900-2,000 µs/input**, ~**130 µs per counted sigop**; first key matching: 120 µs/input | `bench-rt` | stands |
| F-13 | the sigop counter does not price spend-side work | 800 **bare** 1-of-15 inputs, 91,691 B: **2,353 ms** of validation charged **2 sigops**. Legacy counts outputs created; the P2SH pass covers P2SH prevouts only | `bench-rt` + `source` | stands |
| F-14 | where the quadratic term is visible | µs/input climbs 1,842 → 2,942 as the transaction grows 5.8 kB → 92 kB (15 sighashes per input); invisible on P2PKH | `bench-rt` | stands |
| F-15 | worst-case block validation today | ~**49 s** for a 2 MB block of bare-multisig spends (charged ~42 sigops); ~**5.2 s** if P2SH-multisig-loaded | derived from F-12/F-13 | stands |
| F-15b | warm block connection | **~12.8 µs/tx** (1.1 s for 85,624 transactions) on the rig; 33-57 ms per 3,700 tx on the swarm, 111-157 ms on a 4-thread host | `loopback` / `wan-12` | stands — this is the value the warm column of F-16 rests on |
| F-16 | cold vs warm connect, ordinary payments | 2 MB commitment block: **~16-20 s** single-thread cold, **~0.8 s** warm. 8 MB: ~65-78 s cold, ~3.2 s warm | derived from F-11 | stands |
| F-17 | asset-cache deep copy per ATMP call | 6.5× throughput loss at 3,500 assets: **695 vs 4,502 tx/s**; 2.0-3.2 ms per call at K≈2,500 | `loopback` + `mainnet` | stands |

### Quorums, InstantSend, ChainLocks

| ID | fact | value | regime | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-18 | InstantSend lock rate | **74-76 locks/s** at quorum size 5 (threshold 3), **41.2** at size 9 (6), **32.5** at size 13 (8); cost ≈ 6.7 × quorum size − 15 ms per session; round-trip-bound, not compute-bound | `quorum-rt` | stands — **never measured at the live size of 50** (K-13), where the fit implies ~3 sessions/s |
| F-19 | signing sessions per transaction | **inputs + 1** (5,845 sessions for 2,915 one-input locks) | `quorum-rt` | stands |
| F-20 | saturation behaviour | above capacity sessions are purged and never retried — work is lost, not delayed | `quorum-rt` + `source` | stands |
| F-21 | threshold recovery parallelises | 10.7× at 16 threads (107 → 1,151 recoveries/s); near-linear to 4 threads; not wired to `CBLSWorker`'s async pool | `mario` | stands |
| F-22 | islock verification is not smartnode-only | every full node verifies every islock, ≥1.15 ms per message | `source` + `quorum-rt` | stands |
| F-23 | mainnet spork state | 2 **ON**, 3 **OFF**, 19 **ON**, 17 ON, 23 ON, 21/25 OFF; IS mempool signing **off** (spork 2 carries a timestamp, not 0) | `mainnet` 2026-09-18 | stands — and **temporary**, see X-3 |
| F-24 | mainnet ChainLocks are not forming | best chainlock **1,122,354** against tip **1,432,200**; tip reads `chainlock: false` | `mainnet` 2026-09-18 | stands, one node — **out of scope** (X-4). Owner's read, 2026-09-18: the nodes themselves are healthy and the signing path is the code Dash runs in production, so the likely cause is **operational — a rollout or a networking change — rather than a defect**. Recorded so nobody re-derives it as ours |

### The acceptance-layer probe

| ID | fact | value | regime | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-25 | `VerifyDB` on a missing body | reports **"Corrupted block database detected. Please restart with -reindex"** and refuses to start | `bench-rt` | stands |
| F-26 | P2P serving a missing body | serving node **aborts** (`assert(!"cannot load block from disk")`); the peer stops at the block before | `bench-rt` | stands |
| F-27 | `ConnectTip` on a missing body | `AbortNode`: "Failed to read block" | `bench-rt` | stands |
| F-28 | RPC read paths on a missing body | `error code: -1`, node survives | `bench-rt` | stands |
| F-25b | the three fatal paths, gated | `VerifyDB` starts and stops early; serving declines and survives; `ConnectTip` holds the block incomplete without touching validity | `bench-rt` | stands |
| F-25c | "not yet" must be a carried signal | returning false is non-fatal at runtime but fatal at startup — `ThreadImport` shuts down on any failed `ActivateBestChain`, and an empty state is indistinguishable from a disk error. Fixed with `CValidationState::BodiesMissing()`, four call sites | `bench-rt` + `source` | stands |
| F-25d | read-time withholding cannot reach the interesting states | to exercise `ConnectTip` a block must be valid, unconnected and body-less, and on one node every route there disconnects across the same gap. The remaining scenarios need **accept-time** withholding and **two** nodes | `bench-rt` | stands |
| F-37 | inserting a validity rung costs a persisted format change | validity is an **ordinal in a 3-bit field** and `BLOCK_VALID_TREE`/`BLOCK_VALID_TRANSACTIONS` are **adjacent**, so a rung between them renumbers three persisted values and needs a migration of every index entry | `source` | stands |
| F-37b | **but a rung is still needed** — R-28 retracts the reason it was not | `ConnectBlock` does **not** re-invoke `ContextualCheckBlock`; its own comment says so. `nLockTime` finality, `bad-cb-type` (DIP3 coinbase), `bad-txns-type`, BIP34 height and `bad-txns-oversize` are enforced **only** at accept time, and `CheckBlock` is re-run at connect only when `block.fChecked` is false. So `BLOCK_VALID_TRANSACTIONS` cannot be granted to a body-less block without letting a non-final transaction or a non-CbTx coinbase connect | `source` | stands |
| F-38 | `BLOCK_HAVE_BODIES = 256` costs **zero** relaxed assertions | the commitment block is stored like any block, so `BLOCK_HAVE_DATA` stays honest. Both 0.1 relaxations reverted; net change is one sibling tracker `pindexFirstMissingBodies`, **one added** assertion (`HAVE_BODIES ⇒ HAVE_DATA`), one added **recovery** rule, and two guards narrowed inertly — bodies and data are recorded together on every writer, and `PruneOneBlockFile` now clears both | `bench-rt` | stands |
| F-38b | "on the active chain ⇒ bodies" is **not** an invariant | pruning removes bodies from blocks that stay connected, and the assertion would forbid the design's own steady state — commitments for all of history, bodies for a window. Dropped. Verified on a pruned regtest node at height 1,200 | `bench-rt` | stands |
| F-38c | the narrowed guards needed a recovery rule or nothing checked a gap descendant | both the candidacy and the unlinked assertions are skipped for a block above a body gap. Added the body-gap analogue of pruning's own rule: above a gap, outranking the tip, not a candidate ⇒ must be in `m_blocks_unlinked` | `source` | stands |
| F-39 | both encodings break the criterion's chain, at different links | 0.1's at `HAVE_DATA ⇒ nTx > 0`; the bit encoding at `VALID_TRANSACTIONS ⇒ candidacy` — a block above the tip with all parent data "must be in setBlockIndexCandidates", and selection had just dropped it for having no bodies | `bench-rt` | stands |
| F-40 | **the bit alone wedges the node permanently** | with `HAVE_DATA` honest, "do I need this block?" answers yes at `validation.cpp:AcceptBlock`'s `fAlreadyHave` and in `net_processing.cpp:FindNextBlocksToDownload` and `ProcessHeadersMessage` (a third hunk is the *serve* side, and the compact-block handler is untouched, so F-36 stands). Measured: withholding removed, **zero** requests, stuck forever. The bit and the fetch layer are **one deliverable** | `bench-rt` | stands |
| F-40b | and the probe's fetch fix still does not converge live | every "bodies arrived" result came from a **restart**, which resets each peer's `pindexLastCommonBlock`; the withhold flags are parsed once at init. With a live peer: two requests for the gap block, then flat; a fresh peer bought one more. F-25e2 is unfixed | `bench-rt` | stands |
| F-42 | not re-stamping `nSequenceId` displaces an equal-work tip | a block whose *commitments* arrived early keeps an earlier id than an equal-work rival that arrived whole, so when its bodies land it displaces an already-connected tip — which unmodified code never does, and which a miner could reserve by announcing commitments early and bodies late. Re-stamping changes the tie one way, not re-stamping the other; **neither is "unchanged"** | `source` | **confirmed by test (F-107) — this is the branch that was built, and it IS the behaviour change this row describes, not "unmodified Bitcoin". F-107/the 1.3.4 commit message wrongly said the latter; corrected. A miner can still reserve an equal-work tie by announcing commitments early and delaying the body, bounded only by the fact that a commitment-only block can never itself become tip — it can merely displace a rival once its body lands. Whether that bound is acceptable as designed, or whether re-stamping (reopening F-35's bug instead) is preferable, has not been put to Mike as an explicit choice — flagging, not deciding, here** |
| F-41 | the bit is persisted, so it needs a migration | a pre-bit datadir loads with `HAVE_BODIES` clear on every entry. Worse than first recorded: the node then **stops serving every historical block** (the serve path needs bodies) and `VerifyDB` breaks at its first iteration, so the startup corruption check silently checks nothing. One upgrade pass at first load is the fix and is **unwritten and untested** | `source` | **open** |
| R-28 | "no rung is needed — `ConnectBlock` re-checks those properties anyway" | **wrong**: `ConnectBlock` does not re-invoke `ContextualCheckBlock`, and the property list came from an enum comment that omits it. 0.1c, 18 Sep |
| F-43 | **the rung sits after the commitment-checkable checks, and they are not trivial** | proof of work, the header-chain rules, **merkle root**, merkle malleation, size, first-tx-is-coinbase, the whole coinbase `CheckTransaction` (including the **founder payment**), coinbase finality and `bad-cb-type`. A commitment block can honestly certify that it is well formed, pays the founder correctly, and commits to an identifier list at a height (§2.4) | `source` | **corrected** |
| F-43b | the merkle check is **not** set-uniqueness, and the coinbase is unbound | `ComputeMerkleRoot` compares `hashes[pos]` with `hashes[pos+1]` for **even `pos` only**, so a duplicate pair must be even-aligned to be seen: `[cb, tx, tx]` puts it at positions 1-2 and passes malleation entirely, while `[cb, a, b, b]` is caught. Narrower than "adjacent" and verified by test. The format must add **identifier uniqueness** and **`id[0] == hash(coinbase)`** as new rung rules — nothing today checks either, because today they cannot differ | `source` | stands |
| F-43c | BIP34's coinbase-height rule is **dead code** on every network | guarded `BIP34Enabled && !fDIP0003Active_context`, and `DIP0003Enabled = true` on main, test, devnet and regtest. The live height rule is `CheckCbTx`'s `bad-cbtx-height`, which reads only the coinbase payload — commitment-checkable, but running only at **connect** today, so it is a candidate to hoist to the rung | `source` | stands |
| F-44 | **three** rules have no connect-time home | `ConnectBlock` re-invokes `CheckBlock` (first statement, before the genesis return and before `fScriptChecks`), so `bad-cb-multiple`, `CheckTransaction` and sigops are covered. `ContextualCheckBlock` is not re-invoked, leaving: `nLockTime` finality; type/version (partly — `nVersion == 3` with an unknown type dies at connect, `nVersion < 3` with a non-zero type and `nVersion > 3` do not); and **`bad-txns-oversize` at 100 kB** (F-44b). Still three rules, not a category | `source` | **corrected** |
| F-44b | the missed rule is the one §1A leans on | `bad-txns-oversize` lives in `policy.h` but is `DoS(100)` on the block path, so it is consensus, and it is gated on `DIP0001Enabled` — true everywhere. Its only connect-time counterpart checks against `MAX_LEGACY_BLOCK_SIZE`, **10× looser**, so a 900 kB transaction would connect. It is simultaneously §1A's body-byte multiplier and the bound on quadratic sighash cost (K-6) | `source` | stands |
| F-45 | `fChecked` already caches a height-dependent verdict — present tense, not a future hazard | `CheckBlock` takes `nHeight`, derives the block reward from it and enforces the **founder payment**, whose only enforcement point in the codebase is this function. `ProcessNewBlock` calls it with `Tip()->nHeight + 1` computed **before** taking `cs_main`, and every block arriving as a new tip is connected from that same object with both later `CheckBlock` calls short-circuited. So the mechanism for caching a height-dependent consensus answer across a change of height is already in place; a staged assembly must not let the flag survive, or the body half is never checked | `source` | **strengthened** |
| F-46 | a third byte-indexed constant, same class as K-9 and F-30 | both size checks compare `block.vtx.size()` — a transaction **count** — against `MaxBlockSize()`. **Latent, not live**: the OR'd byte arm always trips first while a serialized identifier costs at least one byte, so it becomes a limit only if that arm is dropped. Two siblings bind far earlier on an identifier list: compact blocks cap ids at `MaxBlockSize()/MIN_TRANSACTION_SIZE` and merkle blocks at `MaxBlockSize()/60` — though K-10's 65,535 binds before either | `source` | **corrected** |
| F-46b | this branch's block size is not mainnet's | `MAX_DIP0001_BLOCK_SIZE` is **8,000,000** on the perf rig branch against 2,000,000 upstream. Every sigop-cap and block-budget figure quoted from a rig run therefore describes the rig, not the network — check the constant before quoting any of them | `source` | stands |
| F-47 | the DKG mechanism already works in-tree | all nine LLMQ/DIP3 functional tests pass on this branch — quorums form through six phases and sign, 5 members with zero complaints. 0.4's job is therefore **what quorums cost over a WAN**, not whether they form | `bench-rt` | stands |
| F-48 | **a DKG phase needs ~15 s over the WAN**; unpaced regtest mining gives it ~1 s | the phase is derived from tip height, `SleepBeforePhase` no-ops under `MineBlocksOnDemand`, and `VerifyAndComplain` marks empty-contribution members bad with no retry. Contributions relay in a ring (i+1, i+2), so an 8-member quorum is 4 hops. Mine two blocks per phase and gate on what the phase produces | `bench-swarm` | stands |
| F-49 | quorums, ChainLocks and InstantSend all work on the swarm | `llmq_test` and `llmq_test_v17` formed at height 1230 with 8/8 contributions and zero complaints; first ChainLock at 1249; a transaction islocked immediately. **Every earlier swarm figure's "upper bound" caveat can now be retired measurement by measurement** | `bench-swarm` | stands |
| F-50 | sporks 2/3/19 without an IS quorum empty every block | `miner.cpp` gates on `IsTxSafeForMining`, which skips any non-islocked transaction younger than ten minutes. Enabling them to start DKG produced 119 coinbase-only blocks. Order: 17/23/25 on, then a quorum, then 19, then 2, then 3 | `bench-swarm` + `source` | stands |
| F-51 | an all-smartnode mesh restarted together deadlocks permanently | `net.cpp` refuses inbound while `fSmartnodeMode && !smartnodeSync.IsSynced()`, and sync needs peers. Requires a non-smartnode seed **and** staggered starts. MNAUTH compounds it: sent once at VERACK, dropped if the receiver is unsynced, never retried, and DKG INVs only reach verified members | `bench-swarm` + `source` | stands |
| F-52 | the funder cannot be a smartnode, and a registered non-participant poisons quorums | `You can not start a smartnode with wallet enabled` is a hard refusal, so the wallet sits outside the registered set; anything registered but not running is still selected and contributes nothing, so it must be deregistered by spending its collateral | `bench-swarm` + `source` | stands |
| F-53 | **un-batched InstantSend saturates one thread below the stage-1 target** | at 300 tx/s on a WAN quorum of eight, `rtm-sigshares` — which signs, recovers and verifies on a single thread — sits at **93.7% and 97.0%** on the two smartnodes measured. Stage 1 needs 520 tx/s (K-2), and mainnet's quorum is 200 of 400 with the same per-transaction work per member | `bench-swarm` | stands |
| F-54 | the failure mode is a backlog blowup, not degradation | identical load, IS off vs on: acceptance and relay unaffected (**zero rejections both arms**), but the mempool goes ~7,000 → **183,273 tx / 239 MB** and still climbing. Bounded only by eviction, which is its own correctness problem | `bench-swarm` | stands |
| F-55 | with IS on, blocks starve for ten minutes then run 13-15% short | spork 3 makes `IsTxSafeForMining` refuse non-islocked transactions younger than K-12's ten minutes; the first block after load started carried **one** transaction, and steady-state fill settled at ~247-260 tx/s against the control's ~285-305 | `bench-swarm` | stands |
| F-56 | **ChainLocks are the first thing lost under load** | a ChainLock formed at height 1249 while idle; none formed at all during the 300 tx/s IS-on run. The feature that converts probabilistic finality into an assertion fails before throughput does | `bench-swarm` | stands |
| F-57 | a timestamped spork 2 is not an escape from IS load | the retroactive signing path fires regardless of the mempool-signing spork (`llmq/quorums_instantsend.cpp`, and its comment says so), so mainnet's current configuration still pays this. Only spork 2 fully off avoids it | `source` | stands |
| F-58 | the rig branch is not a valid characterisation baseline | against master it carries an 8 MB `MAX_DIP0001_BLOCK_SIZE` (vs 2 MB) **and** the 0.1 probe: 165 lines in `validation.cpp`, 42 in `validation.h`, 27 in `consensus/validation.h`. Tests written there pin the rig, and the size delta is not inert — every size, sigop and fill assertion would encode 8 MB. 0.2 runs against a detached worktree at master | `source` | stands |
| F-59 | building a second worktree needs the shared `depends` prefix | a plain `./configure` fails with "Boost is not available"; the working form points `CONFIG_SITE` and `--prefix` at `depends/x86_64-pc-linux-gnu` and passes `CXXFLAGS='-g -O2 -include stdexcept -include cstdint -include cstring'`, the forced includes this codebase needs under modern GCC. `depends` is prebuilt and source-tree independent, so worktrees share it | `bench-rt` | stands |
| F-60 | the functional suite's fixes are **ours and unmerged**, which is what makes baseline selection awkward | `upstream/develop` still does `import dash_hash` where the published module is `raptoreum_hash`, so upstream's suite does not run — **already known and already fixed** by the nine open PRs (#471-#481, see `upstream-ledger.md`); they are on our branch and not yet merged. Nothing to discover here: the consequence is only that a characterisation baseline must take `src/` from upstream and `test/` from ours | `source` | stands |
| F-61 | master is a revision behind, so it is the wrong baseline twice | master is v2.0.03.01 against the rig's and upstream/develop's v2.0.04.01, *and* its test framework cannot import (F-60). The correct characterisation baseline is **upstream/develop's `src/` with the rig branch's `test/`** — upstream consensus code with a runnable harness | `source` | stands |
| F-62 | `submitblock` collapses block rejections to `invalid` | eight of nine characterisation rows return the bare string `invalid`, so it cannot pin *which* rule fired. Characterisation must go through the **p2p path**, which carries the specific reason, as `feature_block.py` does | `bench-rt` | stands |
| F-63 | a hand-built regtest block in a DKG mining window needs the null quorum commitments | `create_coinbase` does not add them, so blocks are rejected `bad-qc-missing` from height 10 (`dkgInterval` 30, `dkgMiningWindowStart` 10) whatever the mutation under test — it masks the rule being characterised | `bench-rt` | stands |
| F-64 | **regtest's founder payment starts at height 500**, and below it a founder mutation tests nothing | the amount is zero and the coinbase has one output, so stripping the founder output is a no-op on a valid block. The first run reported "founder payment: ACCEPTED", which reads as an unenforced consensus rule and was in fact an empty test. Prime past 500 | `bench-rt` + `source` | stands |
| F-65 | the characterisation had to move to the **p2p path** | `submitblock` returns a bare `invalid`; p2p reject messages carry the rule, and this tree still defaults `DEFAULT_ENABLE_BIP61 = true`. Nine rows now pin nine specific reasons, including `bad-cb-founder-payment-not-found`, confirming from outside that `CheckTransaction` enforces the founder rule (which is what makes F-45 a consensus hazard) | `bench-rt` | stands |
| F-66 | three harness properties that each produced a wrong answer first | every row is a DoS-scoring block so the peer is **disconnected** and must reconnect per row, or eight rows report "Not connected"; a mutation must **reseal** the block, since rebuilding the root from stale transaction hashes makes the node answer `bad-txnmrklroot` for a fault that lies elsewhere; and the malleation row needs an **even-aligned** duplicate (F-43b) | `bench-rt` | stands |
| F-67 | the equal-work tie-break, characterised | two valid blocks on one parent: **first seen wins**, the second never displaces it, and the loser remains a known tip at equal height with status `valid-headers`. Ordering is work → lower `nSequenceId` → pointer address, and `nSequenceId` is assigned in `ReceivedBlockTransactions`, which is exactly what 1.3 touches (F-35, F-42). Pinned by `feature_characterise_tiebreak.py` | `bench-rt` | stands |
| F-68 | the known-but-absent block, characterised — and F-36 demonstrated | at height 1300 of a pruned chain the index entry survives with `nTx = 2`, `getblockheader` answers from the index, `getblock` refuses with `Block not available (pruned data)`, and **the same block offered back unrequested is silently discarded**. Under decoupling that identical `nTx != 0` test means "I hold the commitments", so a body arriving unrequested or by compact-block relay is thrown away by a node that needs it | `bench-rt` | stands |
| F-69 | **`pruneblockchain` reports success while pruning nothing** | it returns the target and sets `pruned = true` with `pruneheight = 0`. Pruning deletes whole 128 MiB *files*, only when every block in one is below the target — so a small regtest chain can never prune, and even with three files `blk00000.dat` still held blocks above any legal target because `MIN_BLOCKS_TO_KEEP` forbids a target within 288 of the tip. It took **height 1798 and three files** to remove anything. Any pruning test must assert on `pruneheight`, never on the call's return | `bench-rt` | stands |
| R-29 | "the ~34 ms per signing session is BLS threshold recovery" | **wrong.** `TryRecoverSig` times itself and the swarm's own logs had the answer: 350 events at **median 3 ms**, mean 2.7, p90 3, max 19. Recovery is cheap; the attribution was an inference from "the only expensive-looking call in the loop". 18 Sep |
| F-70 | **the InstantSend ceiling is not cryptographic** | per session the crypto is ~1 ms sign + ~3 ms recovery ≈ 4 ms, while the observed 29 shares/s against 260/s requested implies **~34 ms** — so ~88% of the saturated thread is not doing cryptography. Suspects, all implementation: 22 `LOCK(cs)` sites on one mutex with the network threads contending, a single work thread while the box idles, and an **unbounded** `pendingSigns` that queues ~230 sessions/s of backlog instead of shedding it | `bench-swarm` + `source` | stands |
| F-71 | so 5.2 may not be needed for stage 1 | if the ~30 ms of non-crypto overhead is contention and loop structure, the gap between 29/s and the ~250/s the crypto alone allows is **engineering, not protocol**. Settling it means timing the four steps of `WorkThreadMain`; recovery is already instrumented, the other three are not. **Until then the cause is not established** — only that it is not the crypto | `bench-swarm` | **open** |
| F-72 | **the InstantSend ceiling is an unbounded batch starving its own send path** | instrumented profile: `SignPendingSigShares` is **~99%** of the saturated thread (recovery 0 ms, inbound shares 0 ms, send 730 ms across ~170 s), and **one loop iteration ran 170 seconds** with 153,991 entries pending. It does `v = std::move(pendingSigns)` — takes the entire unbounded queue and loops it — so the thread never reaches `SendMessages` or `ProcessPendingSigShares` for minutes: it signs shares it does not send, ignores arriving shares, sessions time out and are re-requested into the same queue | `bench-swarm` | stands |
| F-73 | signing capacity exceeds demand **only at shallow queue depth** — corrected by F-75 | within a batch, signing runs at 1-2 ms per share — a BLS signature and nothing more — so capacity is several hundred/s against ~260 sessions/s of demand. The queue is not a compute shortage; it is work completed and then not delivered, hence asked for again. **So 5.2 may not be needed for stage 1**: bound the batch and interleave sending, which needs no protocol, no fork and no ecosystem event | `bench-swarm` | stands |
| F-74 | the retry feedback loop is inferred, not instrumented | `send=730 ms` over 170 s plus the queue's growth is consistent with timed-out sessions being re-requested, and explains F-70's unexplained ~30 ms remainder as the average of a thread oscillating rather than a per-share cost. **Not separately measured** — confirming it means counting re-requests for sessions already signed, which would also size the fix | `bench-swarm` | **open** |
| F-75 | **per-signature cost collapses superlinearly with backlog; the system is bistable** | the bounded-batch patch (32 per pass) starts healthy — `iters=46`, `pendingSigns=0` — then tips: one iteration of ≤32 signatures took **123 s, ~3.8 s each**, against 1-2 ms at shallow depth. More than three orders of magnitude. Bounding the batch cannot help because batch size was never the quantity that mattered; every unit of backlog slows the next signature, which deepens the backlog. Corrects F-73 | `bench-swarm` | stands |
| F-76 | the cause of the superlinearity is **not established** | `ProcessSigShare` takes `LOCK(cs)` once per item, there are 22 such sites in the file, the network threads contend for it, and the maps it guards grow with the backlog — a coherent story, **unproven**. Needs lock held-time instrumented, or per-item work counted against queue depth. So 5.2's premise survives for a different reason than written: not crypto cost, not batch size, but superlinear collapse | `bench-swarm` | **open** |
| F-77 | the commitment form preserves the merkle root exactly | verified for 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 17 and 64 transactions: the root from the identifiers alone equals `BlockMerkleRoot()` of the source block. The property the design rests on, now pinned by test | `bench-rt` | stands |
| F-78 | carrying the coinbase whole removes F-43b's binding rule | if the identifier list covers only `vtx[1..]`, the coinbase's own hash **is** the first merkle leaf, so nothing needs to tie the carried transaction to an identifier. Identifier **uniqueness** is still required, since the tree compares even positions only | `source` + `bench-rt` | **required, unwired** — see F-83 |
| F-79 | a named transaction costs exactly 32 bytes; the **ratio** depends on body size | asserted by differencing an 8-entry against a 9-entry block. But at minimal 1-in/1-out bodies (~61 B) the form is under **2×** smaller, while at the design's ~373 B assumption it is better than **5×**. Any figure quoted for what decoupling saves must state the body size it assumes | `bench-rt` | stands |
| F-80 | **a per-connection stream flag is not plumbed to select a block message's form, and would not help if it were** | the receive stream's `nType` is fixed at construction — `net.cpp:648` builds every `CNetMessage` with `SER_NETWORK`, and only `SetVersion` exists thereafter (`net.h:938-941`, `net_processing.cpp:4042`) — so an `nType` flag is not plumbed on the read side — **not** unreachable: `vRecv` is public and `CDataStream::SetType` exists (`streams.h:357`), so a `SetType` beside the `SetVersion` is a one-liner, and an nType-taking `Make` overload is the same on the write side. Corrected 2026-09-19; the original "cannot" was an impossibility argument the code does not support. `CNetMsgMaker::Make` ORs its flags into **nVersion**, not nType (`netmessagemaker.h:17-22`), so it is unreachable on the write side too. Deeper than either: both forms must flow on one connection, since pre-fork history is only available whole, so "which form is this `BLOCK`?" is a per-message question and a per-connection flag cannot answer it | `source` | stands |
| F-81 | **`CBlock` cannot deserialize the commitment form, so it is the wrong type to carry the selector** | a commitment block has no transactions; reading one into a `CBlock` cannot populate `vtx`. A stream flag on `CBlock` would therefore be **write-only** — a serialization the type cannot read back. That is not a serialization of `CBlock` at all, which is the argument for `CCommitmentBlock` being its own type with its own `SERIALIZE_METHODS`, as built | `source` + `bench-rt` | stands |
| F-82 | **BIP152 is the in-tree precedent for the right shape: capability per connection, form per request** | `sendcmpct` negotiates per connection (`fProvidesHeaderAndIDs`/`fPreferHeaderAndIDs`, `CNodeState` members at `net_processing.cpp:348,350`, set at `2800-2802`) but the form is still chosen per request, by `inv.type == MSG_CMPCT_BLOCK` in getdata (`net_processing.cpp:1737`) and answered with a distinct `CMPCTBLOCK` command. The `GetDataMsg` enum documents its own extension rule — "declare non-implemented here, we must keep this enum consistent and backwards compatible" (`protocol.h:441`) — and is free from 32 | `source` | stands |
| F-83 | **the commitment format has no validation entry point, and nothing binds the identifier list to the header** | `CCommitmentBlock::ComputeMerkleRoot()` and `HasDuplicateIdentifiers()` have **zero** production callers; `CheckBlock` takes `const CBlock&` only (`validation.cpp:3964`); no message handler deserializes the type. So no code makes the one comparison that ties the list to the header — `ComputeMerkleRoot(Identifiers()) == hashMerkleRoot`. Once serving is wired, a peer can pair a **genuine** header (zero PoW cost) with an arbitrary list: the rung (D-16 → `BLOCK_VALID_TRANSACTIONS`) would be granted on data no hash covers, counted into `nChainTx` (F-25e), and bodies fetched for identifiers that may not exist. Not a poisoned header — `bad-txnmrklroot` passes `corruptionPossible = true` (`validation.cpp:3984`), so `AcceptBlock` skips `BLOCK_FAILED_VALID` (`validation.cpp:4448-4449`) — but a **stuck block**, since nothing re-requests a commitment list. **The root check is load-bearing for 1.3, not an optimisation**, and the ordering trap is that `nTx`/`nChainTx`/body requests must not be recorded before the three rung checks run | `source` (found independently twice) | **open — 1.3** |
| F-84 | serializing a null `CCommitmentBlock` **faulted** | `SetNull()` and the default constructor leave `coinbase` null and the shared_ptr serializer dereferences unconditionally (`serialize.h:1385`), so `ss << CommitmentsFromBlock(CBlock())` died with `memory access violation at address: 0x32` — reproduced, not theorised. Unreachable today (nothing serializes the type, and a stored block cannot have empty `vtx`, `validation.cpp:3998`) but this is the design's **wire type**, and a fault is not an acceptable way to reject a message. Guarded and pinned | `bench-rt` | **fixed 2026-09-19** |
| F-85 | **an unknown getdata inv type wedges a peer in this tree** | `ProcessGetData`'s loop runs `while (it != end() && it->IsKnownType())` (`net_processing.cpp:1782`), only the three block types advance `it` afterwards (`1933-1939`), and `erase(begin, it)` then removes **nothing** (`1941`); `ProcessMessages` returns `fMoreWork` while the queue is non-empty (`4009`), so the peer spins on the front item and never processes another message from us. Newer Bitcoin Core erases an unknown front item; this tree does not. **Consequence for the revised selector:** the capability gate is load-bearing, and `NODE_COMMITMENTS` is an unauthenticated bit in the experimental range — any other software using bit 24 for its own purpose gets wedged by us. An explicit `sendcommitments`-style negotiation, as BIP152 uses `sendcmpct`, removes the question | `source` | **decided 2026-09-19 — built**: see R-31 |
| F-34 | `miner_tests/CreateNewBlock_validity` has been red since 2022 | the assertion at `test/miner_tests.cpp:406` expects `CreateNewBlock` to throw `bad-blk-sigops` when the mempool holds more sigops than the block cap; RTM's miner stops filling instead of throwing. Pre-existing — the baseline five commits back fails identically, and the commit that introduced it says so in its message ("stuck on test failing, but not seeing the problem", Nov 2022). Not caused by the probe, and it is direct evidence for the sigop re-base: the miner already treats the cap as a fill limit rather than a validity rule | `source` + `bench-rt` | stands |
| F-25e | `ReceivedBlockTransactions` **is** the acceptance layer | one function records five things, and they part cleanly: `nTx`, `nChainTx` and candidate entry are commitment-level and knowable from a commitment list; `nFile`/`nDataPos` and `BLOCK_HAVE_DATA` are body-level. `HaveTxsDownloaded()` reads `nChainTx`, so despite the name it is a **count, not a possession** | `source` + `bench-rt` | stands |
| F-25e2 | the same property strands the fetch | `net_processing.cpp:FindNextBlocksToDownload` advances `pindexLastCommonBlock` past any block with `HAVE_DATA` whose `HaveTxsDownloaded()` is true — which blocks above a gap satisfy *because* `nChainTx` counted the commitment-only block's commitments. The cursor jumps the gap and never returns: the observed "re-requested twice, then dropped". Count-not-possession makes candidacy work **and** makes the fetch skip | `source` + `bench-rt` | **fixed 2026-09-20 (F-111, 1.3.6)** — the 1.3.1 build-plan row wrongly claimed this was already fixed as part of that step; it was not, confirmed by reading the shipped code directly before touching anything |
| F-25e3 | the probe tested the **storage** split, not the **acceptance** split | `CheckBlock`/`ContextualCheckBlock` run on the full body at `validation.cpp:AcceptBlock`, before the withholding branch at the withholding branch below it, so the block earned `BLOCK_VALID_TRANSACTIONS` honestly from a body it held. §2.1 says production cannot. Under the real rung, either the `nTx > 0 ⇒ VALID_TRANSACTIONS` assert at `:5499-5500` fires — a **third** relaxation, on the chain the criterion names — or `nTx` stays 0 and commitment-only is indistinguishable from header-only | `source` | stands |
| F-25f | the hold-and-retry semantics already exist, for pruning | `FindMostWorkChain` already declines an absent-data chain without condemning it and re-arms it via `m_blocks_unlinked`; unchanged by the probe — but only because the probe made `BLOCK_HAVE_DATA` mean "bodies held". Under the design's separate bit, `FindMostWorkChain` must test the new bit. **Whatever bit means "bodies held" must be the bit it tests**, or `ConnectTip`'s readable-block guarantee is relaxed rather than extended | `source` + `bench-rt` | **encoding-dependent** |
| F-25g | the cost was one split and **two** assert relaxations *for the probe's encoding* | both in `CheckBlockIndex`, both in the direction pruning already established: the `nTx`/`HAVE_DATA` equivalence, and `assert(fHavePruned)` on a body gap under a held block. A **third** is predicted on out-of-order arrival (F-25j) and a **fourth** under the real rung (F-25e3) | `bench-rt` | stands, as a floor |
| F-25j | out-of-order body arrival fires a third assert | `ReceivedBlockTransactions` inserts into `m_blocks_unlinked` whenever the parent is not `HaveTxsDownloaded()`, regardless of bodies; a commitment-only block whose parent's commitments have not arrived lands there with no `HAVE_DATA` and trips `validation.cpp:CheckBlockIndex`. `LoadBlockIndex` repeats the insert at startup. **Not reached** — a single-peer in-order harness cannot produce it, and the design fetches from many peers, so it is the normal case. **Stale, and 1.3.3 did not actually reach it (Fable review, 2026-09-19)**: (1) "with no HAVE_DATA" describes the pre-F-100 vacuous encoding — under the corrected three-state encoding a commitment-only block DOES get `HAVE_DATA` (only `HAVE_BODIES` is clear), and `ReceivedBlockTransactions` sets `nChainTx` for it exactly like a full block as long as ITS OWN parent is downloaded, so this row's literal assert can no longer fire the way it's worded; (2) the 1.3.3 test that shipped (`out_of_order_arrival_parks_a_child_until_its_parent_downloads`) uses a fully-downloaded child throughout and never sets `g_perf_withhold_*` on anything — it exercises `ReceivedBlockTransactions`'s generic parent-guard (unmodified Bitcoin Core code, flag-independent: still passes with `g_commitmentBudgetActive=false`), not this row's commitment-only shape. What actually needs testing under the corrected encoding: a commitment-only PARENT (bodies withheld) extended by a full CHILD — does chain selection correctly treat the parent as connectable-for-commitments while still refusing to let the CHILD become tip until bodies are available? | `source` | **stale wording; real gap now closed by F-109** |
| F-25k | a global flag is a relaxation; a per-block bit is a state alongside | the criterion's own distinction. The probe's `fHaveCommitmentOnly` turns the equivalence off for **every** block for the life of the datadir, so a lost status bit or an unwritten block file reads as commitment-only — the corruption class the invariant exists to catch. `!HAVE_DATA == (nTx == 0 \|\| COMMITMENT_ONLY)` is the permitted shape | `source` | stands |
| F-25h | per block, the index **cannot** tell pruned from commitment-only | both are "`nTx > 0`, no `HAVE_DATA`"; only a global flag separates them, which is the argument for the per-block bit. Extending `IsBlockPruned()` was reverted: `GetBlockChecked`'s second branch already answers truthfully ("Block not found on disk"), so the extension replaced a true message with a false one and no caller decides on the result. 1.3 needs its own `IsCommitmentOnly`, or a fetch scheduler skipping pruned blocks will skip these too | `source` + `bench-rt` | **corrected** |
| F-25i | without a separate bodies bit the fetch layer gives up | the withheld block was re-requested twice and then dropped: with only `BLOCK_HAVE_DATA` the download logic cannot tell "need commitments" from "need bodies". The bit-256 decision confirmed from the other direction | `bench-rt` | stands |
| F-29 | `DisconnectTip` across a body gap | **settled given §6**: connecting needs the bodies, so the gap is above the tip *provided* the retention window covers full reorg depth. Bodies are genuinely required to disconnect — `CTxUndo` is spent prevouts only (`undo.h:CTxUndo`), and `DisconnectBlock` needs the body for the `vtxundo` size match, `UndoSpecialTxsInBlock`, asset undo and spending created outputs. A failed disconnect is **not** graceful at the caller: `ActivateBestChainStep` answers with `AbortNode`, `EnforceBestChainLock` with `assert(false)`; the chainstate is untouched, the process is not. Note `MIN_BLOCKS_TO_KEEP = 288` is below §6's 720 floor, so `-prune` and decoupling conflict as written | `source` + `bench-rt` | **settled given §6** |
| F-35 | the `nSequenceId` re-walk changes equal-work tie-breaks | `ReceivedBlockTransactions` stamps `nSequenceId` inside its descendant walk and `CBlockIndexWorkComparator` breaks equal-work ties by lower id (`validation.cpp:CBlockIndexWorkComparator::operator`). Calling it again when bodies arrive re-stamps the block and its whole subtree, so a branch whose commitments came first but bodies second loses a tie it would have won unmodified; `PreciousBlock`'s negative id is overwritten too. **The only behavioural divergence in the diff, and it is not from the relaxations** | `source` | stands |
| F-36 | two `nTx != 0` sites read it as "had it, then pruned it" | `validation.cpp:AcceptBlock` drops an unrequested block; `net_processing.cpp:FindNextBlocksToDownload` treats a compact block for such an entry as already-had, falling back to `getdata` only if already in flight. So a body arriving by compact-block relay — the mainline path — is discarded. The probe's re-arm used the requested path only | `source` | stands |

### Sizing

| ID | fact | value | regime | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-30 | bytes per transaction, measured | **373 B** (bench corpus, 2-in/2-out) and **397 B** mean (the `wan-12` corpus v3, which is what F-1 to F-3 and F-8 were measured with) | `bench-rt` / `wan-12` | stands — two corpora, two sizes, both real |
| F-31 | full replication cost | ~**12.6 GB/yr per sustained tx/s**, from K-14's planning size | derived from K-14 | stands |
| F-32 | retention tripwire | **80 tx/s sustained** — 1 TB/yr/node ÷ K-14's 12.6 GB/yr per tx/s, rounded down. Supersedes the 100 and 85 quoted elsewhere | derived from K-14 | stands, per D-12 |
| F-33 | BLOCKTXN worst case at 2 MB | 62,500 × 373 B = **23 MB**, against K-7's 3 MB and no chunking anywhere | derived + `source` | stands |

### The block resource budget — 1.2 pre-implementation review

Adversarial review (Fable, 2026-09-19) of §1A before it becomes a plan. Findings below marked
`source` were independently re-verified against this tree by reading the cited lines; F-91 and
F-92 are reported by the review and consistent with existing findings but not independently
re-derived — treat them as open rather than confirmed.

| ID | fact | value | regime | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-86 | the proposed "accurate spent-scriptPubKey" unit is ambiguous about what it keeps, and read as a replacement it drops scriptSig-side work | `GetLegacySigOpCount` already counts a transaction's own scriptSig ops and created-output ops (`tx_verify.cpp:214-223`); it never examines a spent scriptPubKey unless it's P2SH (`GetP2SHSigOpCount`, P2SH-only). Read §1A's phrase as "replace legacy with spent-script counting alone" and a scriptSig embedding the expensive opcode itself, against a trivial prevout (e.g. `OP_TRUE`) a miner-attacker controls, is charged nothing by the new unit — though legacy's own scriptSig scan would have caught it. **Corrected 2026-09-19: the fix is additive, not a replacement** — keep `GetLegacySigOpCount`'s scriptSig/created-output term, and extend `GetP2SHSigOpCount`-style accurate counting to every prevout type, not just P2SH. That closes F-13's original bare-multisig-spend hole and this variant together | `source` | **corrected — see the revised §1A** |
| F-87 | `OP_CHECKDATASIG`(`VERIFY`) is priced by no sigop counter, legacy or proposed | `CScript::GetSigOpCount` (`script/script.cpp`) recognises only CHECKSIG(VERIFY)/CHECKMULTISIG(VERIFY); `OP_CHECKDATASIG`/`CHECKDATASIGVERIFY` (`script/script.h:185-186`) execute a full ECDSA verify (`interpreter.cpp:1000-1038`) and are already consensus-active under DIP0020/`DEPLOYMENT_V17` — the same flag gate F-86 read. Every sigop-based cap, legacy or re-based, is blind to it | `source` | **open — 1.2's counter must add it explicitly** |
| F-88 | the legacy sigop/size checks are not retired by a re-base, and §1A never says how they coexist | `ConnectBlock` re-invokes `CheckBlock` on the materialised block (F-44), which still runs `GetLegacySigOpCount`/`GetP2SHSigOpCount` against the **old** cap and the old size check. §1A proposes new caps but names no mechanism to disable the old ones post-activation, nor to keep them for pre-fork replay (IBD, `-reindex`) | `source` | **built 2026-09-19** — see R-33 |
| F-89 | no miner- or mempool-side re-basing is scoped, and both use unmodified accounting today | `BlockAssembler::TestPackage` (`miner.cpp`) checks raw byte size against `DEFAULT_BLOCK_MAX_SIZE = 2,000,000` and the legacy `MaxBlockSigOps()`, verified by reading `miner.cpp` and `policy/policy.h`; the mempool entry's `sigOpCount` (`txmempool.h:81`) is "legacy plus P2SH". An honest miner cannot assemble a 62,500-identifier block under either without both being re-based first, and 1.2 cannot be tested end-to-end without it. §13.G is one line | `source` | **built 2026-09-19** — see R-33 |
| F-90 | a bare multisig spend is standard to relay even though creating the output is not | `IsStandard` rejects `TX_MULTISIG` with `n > 3` at output creation (`policy/policy.cpp:45-50`) — but `AreInputsStandard` (`policy/policy.cpp:139-165`) only rejects `TX_NONSTANDARD` prevouts; `Solver`/`MatchMultisig` classify up to `MAX_PUBKEYS_PER_MULTISIG = 20` keys (`script/script.h:29`) as `TX_MULTISIG`, which passes. All verified by reading the three files. So F-13's attack (2.35 s charged 2 sigops) already relays into every mempool today once one such output exists on chain, not only at mining time — contradicts §1A's "non-standard and does not relay" | `source` | **corrected — F-13's attack is reachable via relay, not only via mining** |
| F-91 | the sigop-cost model may charge the wrong variable | legacy `SignatureHash` blanks other inputs' scriptSigs (`interpreter.cpp:1344-1346`), so its preimage scales with **input count**, not transaction bytes; the two measured points behind the ~130 µs/sigop, +40% figure (P2SH 150-in, bare 800-in) each happen to be near their own byte size too, so the two variables were never separated. A transaction padded with many minimal-sigop inputs alongside a few expensive ones may reach a larger preimage per byte than either measured point implies | `source`, extrapolated from F-11/F-12/F-14 | **resolved, redirected — see F-93/F-94.** The hypothesised mechanism (sighash recomputed per key, scaling with preimage size) is not what dominates; a much larger, entirely sigop-independent cost does |
| F-93 | **mempool ATMP scales ~O(n²) in a transaction's own input count, independent of sigops** | measured against an isolated regtest node (own build, `g_commitmentBudgetActive` off, so this is the existing pre-1.2 path): a transaction with 1 fixed accurate-15-sigop bare multisig input plus N padding inputs spending trivial `OP_TRUE` outputs (empty scriptSig — cheapest possible per `interpreter.cpp:SerializeInput`'s 41-byte blanked contribution, F-91's own mechanism) costs, at N=2400 (98.6 kB, near K-3's ~2439-input ceiling): **~370-385 ms** to accept via `sendrawtransaction`. Three controls, each holding the OTHER variable fixed, converge on the same curve: signing the key tried **last** (15 CheckSig attempts) vs the key tried **first** (1 attempt) changes the cost by **under 3%** (374 vs 366 ms at N=2400); a **zero-sigop** transaction (every input a bare `OP_TRUE` spend, no CHECKMULTISIG anywhere) costs the same again (365.6 ms). Fit: delta_ms ≈ 6.5e-5 × N² (R² close to 1 across N=500..2400; doubling N roughly quadruples the cost, not doubles). First run showed the same curve but was confounded by an unmined, growing mempool (24 unconfirmed txs, 1.25 MB, by the last point); re-run with the mempool mined empty between every measurement reproduced the identical curve, ruling that out | `mario`, isolated regtest node, own build, 2026-09-19 | **new — mempool-specific, see F-94** |
| F-94 | **the same cost does not appear at `ConnectBlock`** | the identical N=2400 pathological transaction, mined directly (never accepted by the receiving node's own mempool ATMP — a second isolated node IBD-synced the block containing it), cost **~70 ms** to connect, cold — roughly 5x cheaper than the ATMP figure, not the ~370 ms F-93 measured for the same shape. A second sweep (N=200..2400) at connect time was noisy (57-138 ms, not monotonic — dominated by p2p announce/relay and polling granularity at these small absolute times, not a clean signal) but never approached F-93's curve. `ConnectBlock` and `AcceptToMemoryPoolWorker` are structurally separate functions; `CalculateMemPoolAncestors` (`txmempool.cpp:156-220`, the leading suspect — a per-input `GetIter()` mempool lookup) and mempool-specific conflict/ancestor bookkeeping exist only on the ATMP path. **Root cause not conclusively isolated to one line** — a profiler run would pin it — but the ATMP-vs-ConnectBlock split is solid: three independent measurements (ATMP worst/best/zero-sigop) agree; the connect-time number is a single clean point (69.9 ms) plus a noisier confirming sweep, not yet as tight | `mario`, two isolated regtest nodes, 2026-09-19 | **open — mempool-layer only, not consensus; exact line not found** |
| F-92 | the per-transaction work cap may bound nothing at block level the other two caps don't already bound, and its connect-time home doesn't exist | with Σsigops ≤ the block budget and per-tx bytes ≤ K-3's cap, total worst-case work is already bounded by the block budget times the cost of the worst legal shape, regardless of a third per-tx limit — splitting into many smaller bodies doesn't escape the sum. And K-3 itself has no connect-time enforcement yet (F-44b: `tx_check.cpp` bounds at 1 MB, an order of magnitude looser) — that relocation is D-16/1.3's job, not built | `source` | **resolved by D-17 — dropped from 1.2** |

---

## What limits throughput, in order

Nothing owned this ranking after `throughput-bottleneck.md` was archived, and it is the first
thing anyone asks. Lowest binding constraint first, at the 2 MB design point:

| binds at | constraint | fix | entry |
|---|---|---|---|
| **167 tx/s** at 2 MB (regime-dependent — F-46b: this branch's `MAX_DIP0001_BLOCK_SIZE` is 8 MB, not upstream's 2 MB) | the block sigop cap, which a commitment block hits at ~20,000 ordinary payments | re-base to committed count — **not yet a complete design**: F-86/F-87 (unit still misses scriptSig and CHECKDATASIG work), F-88 (no gating against the legacy checks), F-89 (no miner/mempool side) | K-2, F-13, F-46b |
| **~74 locks/s** | InstantSend coverage — not throughput, but it caps what can be locked | batch: one signature over N items | F-18 |
| **~930 tx/s** | relay delivery | re-index the cap (required at any target), then the per-peer path | F-1, F-4, F-5 |
| **~4,400 tx/s** | acceptance | nothing at this target; ~3× headroom | F-7 |
| **~49 s/block** | worst-case validation, through a path the sigop counter charges nothing for | the re-based sigop count (F-86, F-87 close the path); the separate work cap's purpose is unclear (F-92) | F-13, F-15 |

The order matters more than the numbers: the two lowest are **accounting and coverage**, not
speed, and neither is fixed by making anything faster.

## Assumptions taken on faith

Not measured, not in the repo, and load-bearing. Each says what breaks if it is wrong.

| ID | assumption | source | if wrong |
|---|---|---|---|
| X-1 | the 520-2,083 tx/s target is **peak and burst**, not sustained | RTM team, via owner 2026-09-17 | retention returns to v1 scope (F-31: 6-25 TB/yr/node), then the state root |
| X-2 | Smartnodes will hold the **entire** chain history | same | replay-then-prune stops being available, so the state root becomes required again |
| X-3 | InstantSend is off **deliberately** and will be switched on | owner 2026-09-18 | nothing — the design already prices IS on; this only explains why F-18-F-23 cannot be measured on mainnet |
| X-4 | the quorum layer will be fixed before decoupling, and is **not our scope** | owner 2026-09-18 | F-24 becomes a blocker rather than an observation |
| X-5 | smartnode spec: 4-6,000 nodes at 8 cores / 16 GB / **512 GB SSD** | trí's contract paper, May 2023 | every storage figure moves; X-1's margin is set by this |
| X-6 | contract jobs create "a special type of transaction"; results go on chain **or** a smartnode database | same | the load shape changes, and K-11 says a payload can be 27× the 373 B in F-30 |

## Decisions

| ID | date | decision | reopen when |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-1 | 11 Sep | Smartnodes hold everything; retention, sharding, challenges, incentives, repair deferred | sustained load > F-32, or growth crosses 1 TB/yr/node |
| D-2 | 12 Sep | the Smartnode attestation is a **record**, not a validity gate | a decision to make RTM a hybrid chain, which is not the availability question |
| D-3 | 13 Sep | the commitment format is the design (provisional) | superseded by D-4 |
| D-4 | 17 Sep | **the fork is closed**: commitment format, target = commitments filling a 2-8 MB block | the probe (D-9) finding the acceptance layer intractable |
| D-5 | 17 Sep | the state root is **optional** | X-2 failing, or replay bootstrap becoming impractical |
| D-6 | 17 Sep | the dual validation path stays **live**, not deferred | answers from trí on what is attested and what it may change |
| D-7 | 18 Sep | design for IS **on** and healthy quorums; mainnet's current state is temporary | — |
| D-8 | 16 Sep | the relay cap re-index is **not** a standalone upstream PR | upstream adopting the decoupling direction |
| D-9 | 18 Sep | 0.1's kill criterion: not tractable only if a scenario needs an invariant **relaxed** | — |
| D-10 | 18 Sep | our `docs/` never rides an upstream PR; nothing on upstream GitHub without asking | — |
| D-12 | 18 Sep | **400 B is the planning size** for every storage figure (K-14), which fixes the retention tripwire at F-32's 80 tx/s and supersedes the 100 and 85 quoted elsewhere | a measured production body-size distribution, which does not exist yet |
| D-13 | 12 Sep | pre-existing RTM defects are **out of scope** unless decoupling makes them reachable, or unless decoupling cannot ship without them fixed | — |
| D-14 | 17 Sep | **one fork**: format, the 8 MB cap and the message-length raise ride the same activation; 2 MB is then mined by **policy** | — |
| D-15 | 16 Sep | `getblocktemplate`'s full `ConnectBlock` per poll is **deferred**, not fixed — decoupling was expected to dissolve it. **Note:** F-16 weakens that premise, since a cold connect is 20-25× a warm one | sustained mining at scale, or F-16's cold path becoming the common case |
| D-16 | 18 Sep | **the rung encoding: no new ordinal.** `BLOCK_VALID_TRANSACTIONS` is granted at the rung meaning *commitment-valid*, and the three rules with no connect-time home (F-44) are **relocated to the connect path** instead. Avoids F-37's renumbering and its migration of every persisted index entry; F-45's `fChecked` hazard is handled by a fresh-object rule when bodies are assembled. This is what makes 1.3's estimate hold | either of the three rules turning out to be unrelocatable, or a fourth uncovered rule appearing |
| D-17 | 19 Sep | **1.2 drops the per-transaction work cap** (Mike, closing F-92). The re-based sigop budget times the worst legal shape already bounds total block work; a third per-tx limit added nothing the review could name, and its only plausible connect-time home doesn't exist without 1.3's rung (D-16). 1.2 is scoped to two limits: the accurate sigop count (F-86, F-87) against a committed-count-based budget, and the body-byte cap | a specific attack the sigop and byte caps jointly fail to bound |
| D-18 | 19 Sep, **redone 19 Sep** | **the block sigop budget is 250,000** (Mike). Sizing unchanged: an ordinary 2-in/2-out payment costs ~4 accurate sigops (1 CHECKSIG per spent input script, 1 per created output script — F-11/F-30's corpus shape), so 520 tx/s (X-1's sustained floor) × 120 s × 4 ≈ 249,600. **Worst-case time corrected twice, each time upward.** First pass used F-12's 130 µs/sigop (measured at n=150) giving "~27%, tighter than today" — wrong: it cherry-picked the more optimistic of two already-known points. Using F-13's own worst measured shape (196 µs/sigop, n=800) gives ~49 s, **~41% — parity with today's accidental worst case (F-15), not an improvement.** F-97 then measured `ConnectBlock` directly (not extrapolated) at K-3's real ~100 kB per-tx ceiling (2,325 total inputs) and found **~320 µs/sigop, ~80 s, ~67% of the interval** — worse again, because per-sigop cost climbs with a transaction's own input count (the mechanism F-91 named) and F-13's 800-input shape wasn't yet at K-3's actual ceiling. 250,000 × 320 µs is the current best worst-case estimate. The 2,083 tx/s burst ceiling remains rejected as the sizing basis (X-1: burst, not sustained) | a body-byte/input-count cap (D-17's other 1.2 limit, still unbuilt) that would prevent a block from containing many such maximal-input transactions at all — see F-93/F-94's arithmetic on aggregate risk |
| D-19 | 19 Sep | **the body-byte cap is 110 MB, the input-count cap is 700,000** (Mike, closing D-17's other 1.2 limit; F-98 built). Sizing target explicitly widened past D-18's own 2-in/2-out corpus, at Mike's direction: a **1,500 tx/s theoretical ceiling** of **realistic** multi-input/output traffic, not the 520 tx/s sustained floor or the 2-in/2-out toy shape. Planning shape: 3 inputs + 3 outputs (~556 B, chosen the way K-14 chose 400 B — a stated planning assumption, not a measurement), giving 1,500 × 120 × 556 ≈ 100 MB and 1,500 × 120 × 3 = 540,000 inputs as the legitimate need. **Two limits, not one, because a byte cap alone cannot bound worst-case time**: realistic traffic runs ~185 B/input at that shape, but an attacker packing the same byte budget into F-91/F-93's ~41-byte blanked-preimage-minimum inputs gets ~4.5× the input count per byte — so a byte cap generous enough for realistic throughput is, by construction, far too generous against the actual cost driver (input count, per F-93/F-94/F-97). COMMITMENT_BUDGET_BODY_BYTES (110 MB) is sized for storage/throughput headroom over the 100 MB need; COMMITMENT_BUDGET_MAX_INPUTS (700,000) is the one that bounds time — ~30% headroom over 540,000, and at F-97's own measured rate re-expressed per input (72.0 ms / 2,325 inputs ≈ 31 µs/input, not per-sigop), 700,000 × 31 µs ≈ 21.7 s, ~18% of the 120 s interval — independent of how generous the byte cap is | a measured production traffic shape that contradicts the 3-in/3-out planning assumption, or a 1,500 tx/s target revision |
| F-99 | **F-98's own risk model was wrong: the miner-side input-count approximation was a deterministic stall, not a bounded one-off** | Adversarial code review (Fable, 2026-09-19) found `CreateNewBlock` calls `TestBlockValidity`, which throws when `CheckBlock`/`ContextualCheckBlock` reject — and every caller (`getblocktemplate`, `generateBlocks`) rebuilds from the **same mempool in the same order** on the next call, hitting the identical exception again. F-98's framing ("miner builds one bad block, wastes it, tries again") assumed the overshoot surfaces after PoW; it does not — it surfaces inside block assembly, before a template is ever returned, under exactly the saturated-mempool conditions 1.2 exists for. **Fixed**: `addPackageTxs` (`miner.cpp`) now re-checks the EXACT aggregate input count over the whole resolved ancestor set (mirroring how `packageSize`/`packageSigOps` are already ancestor-aware) before committing to a package, rejecting it via the same failed-package bookkeeping `TestPackageTransactions` uses if it would push `nBlockInputs` over budget. `TestPackage`'s own-count-only check remains as a cheap pre-filter; the post-ancestor check is what's now exact. Also closed two lower-severity findings from the same review: `MaxBlockInputs(true)` at both `CheckBlock`/`ContextualCheckBlock` sites now passes `g_commitmentBudgetActive` instead of a literal (style/defensive, zero behavior change since already guarded — matches `MaxBlockSigOps`'s pattern). A separate adversarial pass on `blockbudget_tests.cpp` (Fable, 2026-09-19) found real coverage gaps, not tautologies: no test proved the input-count boundary was exact (`>` vs `>=`), and no test proved `COMMITMENT_BUDGET_BODY_BYTES` was enforced end-to-end (only that the threshold function returns the right constant in isolation) or that its gating wasn't "stuck open" when the budget is off. Three new tests close these — `checkblock_input_count_boundary_is_exact` (700,000 accepted, 700,001 rejected), `checkblock_enforces_the_body_byte_budget_when_active`, and `checkblock_byte_cap_is_gated_by_the_budget_flag_when_off` — all mutation-checked (boundary: `>=` mutant caught; byte-cap: gating-stuck-open mutant caught, both restored). **Deferred, not bugs today**: `LoadExternalBlockFile` and `CPartialMerkleTree::ExtractMatches`'s `MaxBlockSize()`-derived cap are correctly ungated while the budget is test-only, but at 4.6 both need the MAXIMUM over all eras (blocks on disk span both), not the activation-flag pattern mirrored elsewhere — a 4.6 checklist item, not a 1.2 defect. `ContextualCheckBlock` has no dedicated test (only `CheckBlock` does), matching this tree's existing convention for the sigop budget's own `ContextualCheckBlock` wiring (R-33) — an accepted, pre-existing gap in this testing convention, not something 1.2 introduced. **Correction (Fable full-arc adversarial review, B1, 2026-09-20): the miner-side fix itself -- `addPackageTxs`'s exact ancestor-aware re-check, the actual subject of this row's title -- has zero test coverage, confirmed by checking `blockbudget_tests.cpp` directly: every test there exercises `MaxBlockInputs()` in isolation or `CheckBlock`'s consensus-level rejection of an already-built block, none drive `BlockAssembler`/`CreateNewBlock` at all.** Not built, and not a gap closed by a cheap test: reaching `COMMITMENT_BUDGET_MAX_INPUTS` (700,000) needs a package whose resolved ancestor set's aggregate input count actually crosses that threshold, which means constructing and signing hundreds of thousands of real mempool transactions -- there is no test-only override for the constant itself (unlike `g_commitmentBudgetActive`, which gates whether the check runs at all, not its threshold), and lowering the real `Consensus::` constant for a test would test a different number than what actually ships. Left dormant, not fixed: this makes F-99's own claimed fix a real gap in verification, not in the code -- the ancestor-aware logic is straightforward and symmetric with `packageSize`/`packageSigOps`'s already-tested ancestor-aware pattern, but "obviously correct by inspection" is exactly the class of claim this session's own discipline (mutation-test every fix) exists to not accept on faith. Recorded rather than silently left implied-tested by this row's "fixed" status | Fable adversarial review (code + tests), 2026-09-19, mutation-tested; B1 gap confirmed by direct read of blockbudget_tests.cpp, 2026-09-20 | **fixed 2026-09-19; miner-side coverage gap (B1) confirmed dormant 2026-09-20, not built** |
| F-100 | **1.3's first plan draft specified an encoding its own cited findings refute, and separately omitted the one finding that names 1.3 as its owner** | Adversarial plan review (Fable, 2026-09-19), before any 1.3 code was written. **The encoding bug**: the draft said `fHaveCommitmentOnly = true` becomes `pindexNew->nStatus \|= BLOCK_HAVE_BODIES` on `ReceivedBlockTransactions`'s `pos != nullptr` branch — that makes `HAVE_BODIES` identical to `HAVE_DATA` (vacuous: `HAVE_BODIES ⇒ HAVE_DATA` holds trivially, `pindexFirstMissingBodies` always equals `pindexFirstMissing`, pruned and commitment-only stay indistinguishable, F-25h unsolved). But F-38/F-40/F-40b/F-41 were measured against `probe/acceptance-layer` (commits `a2d5f68c9`, `37a7c37a0` — real, already-reviewed code, not hypothetical) under the OPPOSITE, correct three-state encoding: the commitment block is **always** written (`HAVE_DATA` stays honest per every writer), and `HAVE_BODIES` is set separately by a dedicated `ReceivedBlockBodies` function, never by `ReceivedBlockTransactions` re-running. The findings ledger carries both encodings (F-25k's global-flag shape vs. F-38's per-block-bit shape) and the plan draft inherited the wrong one while citing the second's results. **The omission**: F-83 ("the commitment format has no validation entry point, and nothing binds the identifier list to the header," found independently twice, status "open — 1.3") was cited nowhere in the first draft's 8 sub-steps — without it, the rung (1.3.2) has nothing real to run over, which is F-25e3's own complaint restated. **Also found**: relocating the 3 connect-only rules (F-44) into `ConnectBlock` unconditionally would newly enforce them against all of mainnet history on every future `-reindex-chainstate` (which today skips `ContextualCheckBlock` entirely) — `validation.cpp:2184-2194`'s own comment about upgrade hazards applies here, contrary to the first draft's dismissal; needs activation-height gating. F-78 already retired the `id[0] == hash(coinbase)` rule the draft proposed (1.1 carries the coinbase whole, so there is no `id[0]` on the wire to compare) — should be the root check over `Identifiers()` instead. F-45's `fChecked`/height hazard is a **live bug today**, independent of 1.3, not something a not-yet-built materialiser closes. A migration pass ("`HAVE_DATA` ⇒ set `HAVE_BODIES`") needs a persisted done-marker (precedent: `DB_FLAG "prunedblockfiles"`) or it re-falsifies the bit on every subsequent restart once genuine commitment-only blocks exist. `-perfwithhold*` parses once at init, which is *why* every F-40/F-40b convergence result came from a restart — a runtime un-withhold mechanism is a concrete missing piece, not a vague "needs a harness." **Plan revised**: 8 sub-steps collapsed to 7 — the bit and the minimal fetch fix merged (F-40's "one deliverable" finding, which the first draft cited but then split across two steps anyway); the rung and F-83's accept path merged (they're the same unbuilt half); the tie-break question resolved by construction (`ReceivedBlockBodies` already doesn't re-stamp) rather than left open; the retry queue narrowed from a new durable component to memory-only state derived at load. `probe/acceptance-layer` is now the explicit starting point for 1.3.1 — rebase and harden, don't reimplement | Fable adversarial plan review, 2026-09-19, verified against `probe/acceptance-layer` and current source before revising the plan | **plan corrected 2026-09-19, before implementation** |
| F-101 | **1.3.1 built: `probe/acceptance-layer` cherry-picked cleanly, F-36's own two sites needed a further fix on top, and a migration was added the probe never had** | `git cherry-pick a2d5f68c9 37a7c37a0` onto `perf/throughput-rig` applied with **zero conflicts** across 41 diverged commits (1.1's block format, 1.2's resource budget) — confirming F-100's read that this branch's design, not just its intent, was sound. Full suite green immediately after (399 non-`miner_tests` cases), no regression. **Hardened on top**: (1) F-36's first site — `AcceptBlock`'s unrequested-block drop — the probe fixed `fAlreadyHave` but left `if (pindex->nTx != 0) return true` unchanged, which still discards a commitment-only block's unrequested body exactly like a pruned one; fixed to `nTx != 0 && !HAVE_DATA`, discriminating "pruned" (HAVE_DATA cleared) from "commitment-only" (HAVE_DATA set, bodies not). (2) F-36's second site, mis-attributed to `FindNextBlocksToDownload` in the plan draft (F-100) — it is the CMPCTBLOCK handler's own `if (HAVE_DATA) return true` short-circuit, gated to also require `HaveBodies()`. (3) The migration F-100 flagged as needing a persisted done-marker: added `ReadFlag`/`WriteFlag("bodiesmigrated", ...)` on the exact pattern `"prunedblockfiles"` already uses, writing the migrated entries via `WriteBatchSync` **before** the done-flag, so a crash mid-migration just re-runs the (idempotent) pass rather than silently losing entries. The old commitment-only re-derivation loop the probe removed from `LoadBlockIndexDB` is not resurrected — this is a real migration, not F-41's placeholder. **Verified**: the pre-existing serve-side gate (`net_processing.cpp`, `if (send && HAVE_DATA && HaveBodies(pindex))`) needed no change at all — it already existed on `perf/throughput-rig` (not the probe branch) and upgrades automatically once `HaveBodies()` is redefined against the real bit. New `acceptancebit_tests.cpp` (4 cases): `HaveBodies()`'s default-true and null-safety; the core mechanism end to end (withhold via `g_perf_withhold_hashes`, accept as commitments-only, clear withhold, re-offer **unrequested**, confirm the body is accepted and the bit set — this is F-36's first site exercised directly, not just reasoned about); and a control proving the fix does **not** change pruning's own deliberate "discard a re-offered pruned block" behaviour (simulated by clearing `HAVE_DATA`/`HAVE_BODIES`/`HAVE_UNDO` together with `fHavePruned = true`, matching what `PruneOneBlockFile` actually clears — the first attempt at this control tripped two of `CheckBlockIndex`'s own pre-existing invariants by only clearing `HAVE_DATA`, a reminder that the invariants are exacting about what "pruned" actually means). Mutation-tested: reverting the `AcceptBlock` fix to its pre-1.3.1 form is caught by 3 of the 4 new assertions in one test. Full suite green (403 cases) except the pre-existing, unrelated `miner_tests/CreateNewBlock_validity` failure. **Not yet done**: the live two-peer, non-restart convergence test (F-40b) and the multi-peer out-of-order harness (F-25j) both need the functional Python test framework, which this environment's Python 3.13 cannot run (`asyncore` removed) — a pre-existing, documented limitation (build-plan.md 0.2), not new to this step. The CMPCTBLOCK gate fix has no unit coverage, matching this tree's existing convention for untested `net_processing.cpp` wiring (R-31's precedent) | `source`, cherry-pick verified conflict-free, mutation-tested | **1.3.1 built 2026-09-19** |
| F-102 | **F-45's `fChecked`/height hazard fixed — live today, worse than "a race"** | `ProcessNewBlock` computed `nHeight = ::ChainActive().Tip()->nHeight + 1` **before** taking `cs_main` -- an unsynchronized read of chain-active state, and wrong whenever the submitted block does not extend the current tip (a sibling of the tip, or any block arriving mid-reorg), not only under a race. `CheckBlock`'s founder-payment enforcement is height-dependent and, on success, sets the shared, mutable `CBlock::fChecked` flag; every later `CheckBlock` call on the same object -- including `ConnectBlock`'s, at connect time -- trusts that flag and skips re-verifying. A wrong guess plus that cache is a silently-wrong founder-payment verdict surviving to connect. **Fixed**: the height computation moved inside `LOCK(cs_main)` and now resolves the block's real parent via `LookupBlockIndex(pblock->hashPrevBlock)` rather than assuming it extends the tip -- correct for the sibling/reorg case, and no longer a data race either. Full suite green, no regressions. **Not separately regression-tested**: reproducing the exact economic divergence needs a sibling pair straddling regtest's founder-payment activation height (500, the only schedule boundary this low in the chain's history) with a fully DIP3-compliant hand-built coinbase/CbTx payload at the sibling's real height -- disproportionate engineering for this fix given the root cause is unambiguous from direct code inspection (an unsynchronized read whose wrongness is structural, not probabilistic). Accepted as a documented gap, matching this session's treatment of the CMPCTBLOCK gate (F-101) and the live two-peer convergence test (F-40b) | `source`, full suite regression only | **fixed 2026-09-19** |
| F-103 | **`CheckCommitmentBlock`/`ContextualCheckCommitmentBlock` built -- F-83's validation-function half closed, the accept-pipeline wiring half is not** | The commitment format had no validation entry point at all: `CCommitmentBlock::ComputeMerkleRoot()`/`HasDuplicateIdentifiers()` had zero production callers, and nothing tied an identifier list to its header.
