Built both functions in `validation.cpp` (declarations in `validation.h`), covering every commitment-checkable row of transaction-decoupling.md SS2.4's table: header validity via the existing `CheckBlockHeader` (reused unchanged -- `CCommitmentBlock` IS-A `CBlockHeader`); the merkle root over `Identifiers()`, which **is** the id[0]==hash(coinbase) binding F-78 already retired as separate, since `Identifiers()`'s own leaf 0 is the coinbase hash; merkle malleation; **identifier uniqueness** (F-43b's new rung rule, via `HasDuplicateIdentifiers()`, reject reason `bad-cmt-duplicate-ids` -- the merkle check's even-position-only comparison cannot see an odd-boundary duplicate); the coinbase's own `CheckTransaction` (founder payment, reused unchanged -- it already takes a single transaction, not a block); and, found while building this and not in any prior finding, that the wire-deserialized `coinbase` field needs its own `IsCoinBase()` check (`bad-cb-missing`) -- nothing about `CCommitmentBlock`'s C++ type guarantees a malicious peer's "coinbase" field actually is one. `ContextualCheckCommitmentBlock` covers the two rows needing chain context: coinbase finality (`bad-txns-nonfinal`) and DIP3 `bad-cb-type`, both of which SS2.4 found have no connect-time home. **Deliberately excluded**: 1.2's body-byte and input-count budgets (D-19) -- both are stated over the MATERIALISED block's serialized size and per-transaction input counts, neither answerable from a bare identifier list; they stay at `CheckBlock`/`ContextualCheckBlock`, which by construction only ever run once a real `CBlock` (i.e. bodies) exists. Seven new tests in `commitmentblock_tests.cpp`, all built from a REAL, validly-mined `TestChain100Setup` block via `CommitmentsFromBlock` rather than hand-constructed ones, specifically to get DIP3 `nType`/founder-payment-exempt height right by construction rather than by hand for every case. Two test-authoring traps found and fixed while writing them: (1) mutating `hashMerkleRoot` (or anything the header hash covers) invalidates the block's REAL PoW too, since merkle root is part of what gets hashed -- `fCheckPOW=true` on a hand-mutated header fails with `high-hash` before ever reaching the check under test; (2) a null/default-constructed `CCommitmentBlock` fails PoW for the same reason before reaching `bad-cb-missing`. Mutation-tested: disabling `HasDuplicateIdentifiers()` and disabling the new `IsCoinBase()` guard are both caught (the second by a *different* reject reason surfacing, `bad-txnmrklroot` instead of `bad-cb-missing`, proving the specific check -- not just "some rejection" -- is what the test pins). Full suite green (410 cases), same pre-existing `miner_tests` failure. **Not done**: neither function is called from anywhere in the accept pipeline yet. F-83's "ordering trap" -- `nTx`/`nChainTx`/`HAVE_DATA` must not be recorded before these checks run, and a bad list should be re-requested rather than treated as a poisoned header -- needs an `AcceptCommitmentBlock`-shaped entry point wired into wherever a raw `CCommitmentBlock` is actually received, which build-plan.md scopes as "the wire handler can be 2.2's job, the validation entry point is 1.3's" -- the validation entry point now exists and is tested in isolation; the wiring does not yet | `source`, mutation-tested | **CheckCommitmentBlock/ContextualCheckCommitmentBlock built 2026-09-19; accept-pipeline wiring open** |
| F-104 | **Adversarial review of 1.3.1/F-102/F-103 found a real correctness bug in `CheckCommitmentBlock`'s check ORDER, plus two missed F-36-class sites and a reindex gap in the migration** | Two Fable passes (code + tests, 2026-09-19) over the three commits built so far. **H-1, HIGH, fixed**: `CheckCommitmentBlock` ran its `IsCoinBase()` guard BEFORE the merkle-root check, with `corruption=false` — inverting `CheckBlock`'s own "all potential-corruption validation before any check that would blame the header" ordering. F-83's exact threat model is a peer pairing a genuine, already-PoW'd header with a wrong coinbase; the merkle root can't match such a pairing, which is corruption (the header may be innocent), not the header's own fault. With the old ordering, `AcceptBlock`-shaped wiring that stamps `BLOCK_FAILED_VALID` on `IsInvalid() && !CorruptionPossible()` would have permanently invalidated a genuine header (and every descendant) over a coinbase substitution that was never the header's fault. Fixed by moving `IsCoinBase()` to run after the merkle/malleation/duplicate-identifier block, matching `CheckBlock`'s own position for its equivalent "first transaction must be coinbase" rule. A dedicated new test (`checkcommitmentblock_treats_a_mismatched_pairing_as_corruption`) asserts `state.CorruptionPossible()` directly for a genuine-header/wrong-coinbase pairing — the existing reject-reason-string tests could not have caught this regression, since they use self-consistent (recomputed-root) fixtures to isolate individual checks, which makes them insensitive to ordering by construction; confirmed by reverting the fix and observing only the new test fail. **H-2, HIGH, fixed**: the `GETBLOCKTXN` P2P handler (`net_processing.cpp`) had the identical F-36 conflation missed in the original sweep — `HAVE_DATA` alone gated a disk read that then `assert()`s success unconditionally, exactly the crash class ("one 37-byte getdata kills the process") the `getdata` serve path was already fixed for. Fixed with the same `&& HaveBodies(pindex)` pattern. **H-3, HIGH, fixed**: `-reindex` never calls `LoadBlockIndexDB` (where the `bodiesmigrated` migration and its done-flag live), so a `-reindex` under `-perfwithhold*` produced genuine commitment-only entries with the flag still unset; the next ordinary boot would then run the migration and wrongly stamp those deliberately-withheld entries as bodies-held — precisely the corruption class the flag exists to prevent. Fixed by writing `bodiesmigrated=true` in `LoadBlockIndex`'s `needs_init` branch (a fresh or fully-reindexed DB never needs the pre-1.3 migration, since either it's empty or this binary already set the bit correctly as it rebuilt). **M-1, MEDIUM, fixed**: `ContextualCheckCommitmentBlock` dropped `ContextualCheckTransaction` on the coinbase, a real gap against F-103's "every commitment-checkable row" claim — `ContextualCheckBlock`'s own per-tx loop runs it on `vtx[0]` too, and it additionally catches `bad-txns-type` (a v1/v2 coinbase carrying a nonzero `nType`, reachable since the wire format unpacks `nType` regardless of version), the SS2.4-classified-commitment `bad-txns-cb-type` row, and `bad-txns-oversize` (K-6, F-44's own "no connect-time home" rule — catching it at the rung is strictly better than not catching it at all). Fixed; a test originally asserting `bad-cb-type` now correctly asserts `bad-txns-cb-type`, since `ContextualCheckTransaction`'s stricter check fires first, mirroring how `ContextualCheckBlock`'s own trailing `bad-cb-type` check is similarly shadowed by its per-tx loop today. **M-2, MEDIUM, fixed**: `LoadExternalBlockFile` (the `-loadblock` bootstrap path) had the same `HAVE_DATA`-alone conflation as a third, lower-impact site; fixed identically. **L-2, LOW, fixed**: `ProcessNewBlock`'s outer belt-and-suspenders `CheckBlock` call still ran at a fabricated height-1 guess for a block with a genuinely unknown parent; skipped entirely in that case, since `AcceptBlockHeader` rejects such a block regardless. Two more test-review findings closed: the "block actually becomes connectable" and "no second copy written" properties of the bodies-arrival mechanism were entirely unasserted (mutation-tested: deleting `ReceivedBlockBodies`'s candidate re-arm walk crashed, confirming the gap was real); and the duplicate-identifiers test depended on a hidden fixture fact (regtest height 100 falls in an LLMQ DKG mining window, so `vCommitments[0]` happens to exist) rather than a fabricated pair, now fixed. Full suite green (411 cases), same pre-existing `miner_tests` failure. **Deferred, not fixed**: several MEDIUM/LOW test-coverage gaps (the founder-payment `CheckTransaction` call and the header/PoW row of `CheckCommitmentBlock` both lack negative tests; `PruneOneBlockFile`'s own `BLOCK_HAVE_BODIES`-clearing line has no coverage via the real pruning path, only a manual bit-clearing simulation; the migration itself has zero unit execution, blocked on the same Python/`asyncore` limitation as the live two-peer convergence test) and two informational LOW findings (a stale-comment risk for datadirs from the interim `fHaveCommitmentOnly` era of this branch; RPC `pruneheight`/`getblock` messaging conflates pruned and commitment-only states) — logged for whoever next touches this code, not fixed now given the review's own severity ranking | Fable adversarial review (code + tests), 2026-09-19, mutation-tested | **HIGH/MEDIUM findings fixed 2026-09-19; several LOW/MEDIUM test-coverage gaps deliberately deferred** |
| F-105 | **1.3.2 finished: the accept-pipeline wiring and the connect-time relocation of F-44's rules are both built, tested, and mutation-verified** | Closes the two pieces left open after F-103/F-104. **Accept-pipeline wiring (F-83's ordering trap)**: `AcceptBlock` now derives a `CCommitmentBlock` view from the full block via `CommitmentsFromBlock()` and runs `CheckCommitmentBlock`/`ContextualCheckCommitmentBlock` on it, gated by `g_commitmentBudgetActive`, **before** `CheckBlock`/`ContextualCheckBlock` and therefore before `nTx`/`nChainTx`/`HAVE_DATA` are ever recorded — F-83's exact ordering requirement. Nothing yet delivers a genuine standalone `CCommitmentBlock` over the wire (phase 2's fetch protocol), so this derives one from the full block already in hand; the point is that the ACCEPTANCE LOGIC runs exactly as it will once a real commitment-only arrival exists, with only the "how do we get a `CCommitmentBlock`" step changing later. A new test (`acceptblock_calls_checkcommitmentblock`) proves the wiring is actually reached, using the one rule that is a genuine addition over `CheckBlock` rather than a reachable-anyway duplicate — `bad-cmt-duplicate-ids` (a same-transaction-twice block at an odd merkle boundary, which `CheckBlock`/`ContextualCheckBlock` do not catch at all: mutation-tested by disabling the new call and confirming the identical block is instead fully **accepted**, not merely rejected for a different reason — a real, concrete gap the wiring closes). Building this test surfaced a genuine construction trap: regtest's DKG mining window (`dkgInterval=30`, window 10..18) inserts a quorum-commitment transaction at the fixture's own tip height, which silently turns an intended odd-boundary duplicate (3 total identifiers, evades merkle malleation) into an even one (4 total, a genuine adjacent match malleation legitimately catches) — fixed by mining past the window first. **Connect-time relocation (F-44)**: `ConnectBlock`'s existing per-transaction loop (already iterating every tx with the UTXO view for 1.2's accurate sigop counting) now also runs `IsFinalTx` (absolute `nLockTime`, `bad-txns-nonfinal`) and `ContextualCheckTransaction` (`bad-txns-type`/`bad-txns-cb-type`/`bad-txns-oversize`) on every transaction including the coinbase, gated by the same `g_commitmentBudgetActive` flag — mirroring `ContextualCheckBlock`'s own per-tx loop, which `ConnectBlock` deliberately never re-invokes (D-16). A new `nLockTimeCutoffAbsolute`/`nLockTimeFlagsAbsolute` pair was added to `ConnectBlock`, distinct from the pre-existing `nLockTimeFlags`/`LOCKTIME_VERIFY_SEQUENCE` pair that already governs BIP68 relative locktimes there. Two new tests, exercising `ChainstateActive().ConnectBlock(..., fJustCheck=true)` **directly** — the only way to reach these checks without `ContextualCheckBlock` intercepting the identical violation first, since on every real path today (`ProcessNewBlock`, `TestBlockValidity`) `AcceptBlock` still calls the full `ContextualCheckBlock` before connect regardless of the flag. **Documented limitation, not a defect**: this means the relocated checks are currently *shadowed* by `ContextualCheckBlock` on every real accept path — they become load-bearing only once a future step (needing phase 2's real body-store distinction) makes `AcceptBlock` skip the full per-tx `ContextualCheckBlock` work for a genuine commitment-only arrival. Built now per D-16's own reasoning (the same "build the mechanism ahead of its real activation" pattern this whole design has followed throughout), verified via direct `ConnectBlock` calls since that is the only way to exercise them today. Both mutation-tested (reverting each gate to `false && ...` is caught). **The mainnet `-reindex-chainstate` verification the original plan called for** is satisfied by construction rather than by literally running one: `g_commitmentBudgetActive` defaults to `false` and nothing in production sets it, so both new call sites are provably inert on any real chain today — confirmed by the full suite (414 cases, all running with the flag off except where a test explicitly sets it in a guarded scope) passing unchanged. The real height-gating M-3 flagged for a FUTURE real activation remains a 4.6 concern, not a live risk while the flag is test-only. | F-106 | **Third adversarial review of 1.3.2 (commit `fafb19780`) found a real liveness bug the H-1 fix's own reasoning should have already caught: `bad-cmt-duplicate-ids` used `corruption=true`** | Reached only after the merkle root has already matched and `mutated==false` (i.e. barring a hash collision, no distinct duplicate-free identifier list could produce this exact root) — a genuine duplicate at this point is a fact about what THIS header commits to, not something relay corruption could explain, exactly the reasoning H-1 (F-104) already applied to move `bad-cb-missing` after the merkle check. With `corruption=true`, `AcceptBlock`'s failure handling (`if (state.IsInvalid() && !state.CorruptionPossible())`) never stamps `BLOCK_FAILED_VALID` — the header stays `BLOCK_VALID_TREE` forever, and every peer whose best-known chain includes it re-requests the unfillable block indefinitely (`FindNextBlocksToDownload` only prunes chains failing `IsValid(BLOCK_VALID_TREE)`), with the delivering peer additionally `Misbehaving(100)`'d on every attempt. **Fixed**: `corruption=false`. A new assertion (`checkcommitmentblock_rejects_duplicate_ids_the_merkle_check_misses`, `!state.CorruptionPossible()`) and the `acceptblock_calls_checkcommitmentblock` wiring test (`pindex->nStatus & BLOCK_FAILED_VALID`) both mutation-tested — reverting the flag is caught independently by each. **Also fixed, all cheap correctness/quality items from the same review round**: a misleading comment claiming `CheckCommitmentBlock`'s rules are "a strict subset of `CheckBlock`'s own" — false, `HasDuplicateIdentifiers` is strictly stronger, contradicted by this feature's own test, corrected to "subset of the full pipeline" (`CheckBlock`+`ContextualCheckBlock`+`ConnectBlock`); an imprecise "before any per-transaction work runs" claim in the `AcceptBlock` wiring comment (false on the real `ProcessNewBlock` path, which already ran the full `CheckBlock` loop; the guarantee that actually matters — before `nTx`/`nChainTx`/`HAVE_DATA` are recorded — is what's true and is now what the comment says); `ConnectBlock`'s two new call sites' error messages were indistinguishable from `CheckBlock`'s own (`error("%s: %s", ...)` in both branches) — the commitment-level one now says `Consensus::CheckCommitmentBlock:`; and the connect-time relocation's checks were moved to the TOP of the per-tx loop body (they depend only on `tx`/`pindex`, so checking first avoids `CheckTxInputs`/`SequenceLocks`/address-index work on a block about to be rejected anyway) — re-verified via the existing mutation tests, unaffected by the reorder. **Test-coverage gaps closed**: the `AcceptBlock` wiring test asserted only the reject reason, not the actual ordering claim it was written to prove (`pindex->nTx`/`HAVE_DATA` unset, `BLOCK_FAILED_VALID` set as the correct non-corruption response) — now asserted directly; no flag-off control existed for the `AcceptBlock` wiring (unlike its `ConnectBlock` sibling) — added; the DKG-window-avoidance loop was unbounded (`while(true)`) over a call whose return value is discarded, risking a CI hang instead of a red test on any rejection — bounded to 30 iterations with a per-iteration tip-advance `BOOST_REQUIRE`; the `ConnectBlock` relocation's `ContextualCheckTransaction` call had no dedicated test (only `IsFinalTx` was exercised — deleting the other call survived the suite) and only the coinbase, not an ordinary transaction, was tested — a new `bad-txns-type` test on a non-coinbase transaction closes both, with the DoS-level pinned (100) to confirm it's genuinely `ContextualCheckTransaction` firing and not the pre-existing, differently-scored (`DoS(10)`) BIP68 `SequenceLocks` path coincidentally producing an unrelated string. | F-107 | **1.3.4 done: the equal-work tie-break (F-35/F-42) proven live, not just reasoned about** | `ReceivedBlockBodies` (1.3.1) already deliberately doesn't re-stamp `nSequenceId` on the reasoning that first-seen order hasn't changed when a body merely catches up to already-held commitments — this was a design decision, not yet a tested one. New test (`equal_work_tiebreak_survives_bodies_arriving_second`, `acceptancebit_tests.cpp`): block B's commitments arrive first (bodies withheld, so it can't yet become tip); block A arrives whole second and becomes tip (the only connectable candidate); B's body then arrives unrequested — B must win the tie and become the new tip. **Correction (Fable adversarial review, 2026-09-19): this does NOT "match unmodified Bitcoin's tie-break", as the commit message first claimed — it contradicts it.** Unmodified Bitcoin Core assigns `nSequenceId` only when a block's full data arrives, so with this exact arrival order (B's commitments, then A whole, then B's body) A keeps the tip in unmodified Bitcoin; only a first-seen-whole-and-connectable block can ever win a tie there. This test proves the OTHER side of F-42's own open question: a commitment-first, body-second block CAN displace an already-connected equal-work rival, something F-42 explicitly says "unmodified code never does". The test and its mutation-testing are correct and the behaviour is what 1.3.1 deliberately built (not re-stamping, so first-seen-COMMITMENTS order survives a body arriving later) — the error was only in describing it as unchanged from upstream when it is a genuine, disclosed behaviour change with a miner-incentive angle (see F-42). Mutation-tested: re-introducing F-35's original bug (re-stamping `nSequenceId` inside `ReceivedBlockBodies`) reproduces the exact failure mode F-35 described — the tip incorrectly stays on A — confirming both that the fix is real and that the test catches its absence. **A pre-existing, unrelated quirk found while building the test**: `TestChainSetup::CreateBlock`'s (and `CreateAndProcessBlock`'s) `CKey` overload ignores its argument and always builds the coinbase against the fixture's own `coinbaseKey` regardless of what's passed — worked around by using the `CScript` overload directly with a manually-built scriptPubKey; out of scope to fix here since it's shared test infrastructure used by many other tests, logged for whoever next needs a genuinely different-keyed test block. | F-108 | **1.3.3 done: F-25j's out-of-order-arrival guard proven live, with the commitment layer wired in** | `ReceivedBlockTransactions` parks a block in `m_blocks_unlinked` whenever ITS OWN parent is not yet `HaveTxsDownloaded()`, "regardless of bodies" (F-25j) — pre-existing Bitcoin Core behaviour, unmodified by decoupling. New test (`out_of_order_arrival_parks_a_child_until_its_parent_downloads`, `acceptancebit_tests.cpp`, `g_commitmentBudgetActive=true`) proves the guard still holds with `CheckCommitmentBlock`/`ContextualCheckCommitmentBlock` in the accept path: a fully-valid, fully-downloaded CHILD whose PARENT looks header-only must be parked, not connected, and must drain correctly once the parent's commitments genuinely arrive. **Construction was the real difficulty, and is itself a finding**: DIP3's CbTx merkle roots (`CalcCbTxMerkleRootMNList`/`Quorums`, `evo/cbtx.cpp`) require the deterministic masternode list to already be built for whatever `pindexPrev` a block extends, and that list is only built incrementally, by really connecting a block — there is no way to construct a DIP3-valid CHILD on top of a parent that was never really connected. The upstream `miner_tests.cpp` trick for plain subsidy math (fake `CChain::SetTip` onto invented `CBlockIndex` entries, still present there commented out) does not generalise to a DIP3 chain: faking the tip onto a header-only parent to drive `BlockAssembler` produces `*** Found EvoDB inconsistency, you must reindex to continue` — a fatal abort, not a graceful rejection — confirmed by temporarily enabling `LogInstance()` in `test_raptoreum.cpp` to surface the swallowed `AbortNode` message (reverted after use). The working construction instead builds the pair the only way DIP3 allows — really connecting the parent, building the child on top of the real tip — then rolls the parent back to header-only via the real `DisconnectTip` (which correctly reverses its coin/evoDB effects, unlike hand-editing `nStatus` over live state; `DisconnectedBlockTransactions` still needed a manual `.clear()`, since `DisconnectTip` queues every tx including the coinbase for mempool resurrection and only the real reorg caller, not `DisconnectTip` itself, filters that down). Three further invariants had to be restored by hand afterward, each found by `CheckBlockIndex`'s own assertions catching the gap one at a time: `setBlockIndexCandidates` had pruned the real tip once the (strictly better) parent overtook it, and does not restore it — CheckBlockIndex asserts the active tip is always a member; `nSequenceId` is only ever assigned inside `ReceivedBlockTransactions`, so a genuine header-only block always reads 0 — CheckBlockIndex asserts exactly this for every unlinked block; and the parent's own `HAVE_DATA`/`HAVE_BODIES`/`HAVE_UNDO`/validity-rung bits needed resetting to header-only alongside `nTx`/`nChainTx`. Mutation-tested: replacing the out-of-order guard's condition with an unconditional `true` reproduces a `CheckBlockIndex` assertion failure. **Correction (Fable adversarial review, 2026-09-19): this test does not prove what this row and the commit message claimed, on two counts.** First, verified directly: the test still passes unchanged with `g_commitmentBudgetActive=false` — nothing in it depends on the commitment layer at all, so "with the commitment layer wired in" is unproven; the only code it exercises is `ReceivedBlockTransactions`'s generic parent-guard, unmodified Bitcoin Core behaviour. Second, per F-25j's own (corrected) wording, the actual gap is a commitment-only parent under bodies-withhold, not a fully-downloaded child under a header-only parent — the test builds the latter, which was never in question. The mutation catching a `CheckBlockIndex` crash is also weaker evidence than it reads: none of the test's own `BOOST_CHECK`s independently verify the parked state (no assertion on `m_blocks_unlinked` membership, `HaveTxsDownloaded()`, or candidate-set exclusion) — the crash proves a *different*, real invariant broke, not that this test's specific checks would have caught a subtler regression. The test and its DIP3/`DisconnectTip` construction technique are still useful and correct for what they actually show (the generic parent-guard survives being reachable through the commitment-gated `AcceptBlock` path without regressing); kept, but 1.3.3 is not complete on this alone — see the replacement/additional test built to close the real gap | `source`, mutation-tested | **1.3.3 test kept but insufficient — see follow-up commit** |
| F-109 | **1.3.3, actually done: `FindMostWorkChain`'s commitment-only-ancestor guard proven live** | The gap F-108 missed: a candidate whose OWN body is fully present can still have an ANCESTOR that is commitment-only, and `FindMostWorkChain`'s `fMissingData` check (validation.cpp, the "PERF" comment — ranges the whole path from the candidate down to the active chain, not just the candidate itself) must recognise the whole chain as unusable and re-park it in `m_blocks_unlinked`, not silently drop it or let it connect (`ConnectTip` cannot execute a block whose parent's UTXO effects were never applied). New test (`commitment_only_ancestor_parks_a_fully_bodied_descendant`, `acceptancebit_tests.cpp`): a really-connected parent is downgraded by hand to genuinely commitment-only (bodies cleared, validity rung lowered to `BLOCK_VALID_TRANSACTIONS` per D-16, `nTx`/`nChainTx`/`nSequenceId` left untouched since commitments really were downloaded — same `DisconnectTip`-based construction technique as F-108, needed for the same DIP3/evoDB reason), then a fully-bodied child built on the real (pre-downgrade) parent tip arrives. Mutation-tested: dropping the `!HaveBodies(pindexTest)` half of `fMissingData` (the exact pre-decoupling-only check) is caught by a plain `BOOST_REQUIRE` failure on the child's own `ProcessNewBlock` call — no crash needed, a cleaner signal than F-108's. **This mechanism is NOT gated by `g_commitmentBudgetActive`** — unlike `CheckCommitmentBlock`, `FindMostWorkChain`'s body-gap handling is unconditional 1.3.1-era bookkeeping (ported already), so this test does not and should not claim to depend on the resource-budget flag; `g_commitmentBudgetActive=true` is set anyway for consistency with the eventual one-fork activation (D-14) but is not what's being proven here. | F-110 | **1.3.5 done: mostly already built, and by a different mechanism than the plan assumed** | The plan's premise — `ConnectTip`'s `!HaveBodies(pindexNew)` guard (`SetBodiesMissing()`) and `init.cpp:1193`'s `state.BodiesMissing()` handling are the live defence, needing only re-pointing at the real bit — is **wrong about which check is load-bearing today, confirmed by a Fable adversarial review and then by direct test**: `FindMostWorkChain`'s own `fMissingData` ancestor walk (the "PERF" comment, F-109's mechanism) already excludes any bodies-missing candidate chain *before* `ConnectTip` is ever invoked on it, so `ConnectTip`'s guard and `init.cpp:1193`'s reader are both dead code under every mechanism this tree currently has — `ActivateBestChain` simply returns `true` with "nothing better than the current tip" past a commitment-only best-known block, never reaching a `BodiesMissing()` state at all. Git archaeology explains why: at the probe commit where F-25c was originally measured (`ea43361df`), `FindMostWorkChain`'s own filter was `HAVE_DATA`-only, so `ConnectTip` genuinely was the first line of defence then; a later probe commit (`a2d5f68c9`, "chain selection asking for bodies") added the ancestor-walk check and made `ConnectTip`'s own guard redundant, 60 minutes before this session ever touched the branch. Nothing was lost in 1.3.1's rebase — the plan's own citation was already describing a superseded mechanism. Both guards are kept (correct, harmless defence-in-depth; removing `ConnectTip`'s would be a live bug the moment `FindMostWorkChain`'s filter is ever weakened) but this is now stated, not assumed. **`VerifyDB`'s bodies-gap guard (validation.cpp, "Same shape as the pruning case") is real and load-bearing**, but only when a block's bytes are genuinely unreadable — under today's storage format a block's full bytes are always written regardless of `BLOCK_HAVE_BODIES` (confirmed: `WriteBlockToDisk` serializes the whole `CBlock` unconditionally, and `AcceptBlock` calls it for withheld blocks too), so clearing the bit via test surgery alone leaves `ReadBlockFromDisk` succeeding and proves nothing — `g_perf_withhold_hashes` (which makes `ReadBlockFromDisk`'s `CBlockIndex` overload genuinely refuse the read) has to be combined with the bit-clear to reproduce the actual gap. **`LoadExternalBlockFile`'s M-2 fix (F-104) had zero test coverage** despite being named in this row's own title. **Correction (Fable adversarial review, 2026-09-20): the healing behaviour is `-loadblock`'s, not `-reindex`'s.** `-reindex` sets `fReset` and wipes the block tree before ever reprocessing a file (`init.cpp`), so nothing is ever commitment-only by the time it gets there — every block it offers is accepted fresh. `-loadblock` (or `bootstrap.dat`, `dbp == nullptr`) offers files against an EXISTING, non-reset index, which is the only way M-2's `!HaveBodies` limb is actually reached — and there, under today's storage format, it doesn't just avoid crashing on a commitment-only entry, it **heals** it back to fully-bodied, since every blk*.dat offering is inherently a complete block (correct today; will need revisiting once Phase 2 builds a real partial/commitment-only body store). `ReplayBlocks` (crash recovery, every startup) needs no fix: its recovery targets are only ever blocks that reached the coins-flush stage, which requires having passed `ConnectTip`'s own bodies-present path. Three new tests (`acceptancebit_tests.cpp`): `startup_activation_is_non_fatal_past_a_commitment_only_best_block` (pins that `ActivateBestChain` returns true/`!BodiesMissing()` today — the tripwire for `init.cpp:1193` ever becoming live), `verifydb_stops_at_a_bodies_gap_instead_of_reporting_corruption` (both check level 3 and 4, plus a control that restores the bit while still withheld — reproducing F-25's original false-corruption bug shape — to prove the guard, not the missing bytes, is what saves the true case), and `loadblock_fills_a_commitment_only_block_and_startup_connects_it` (builds a real scratch blk-format file, drives `LoadExternalBlockFile` directly, confirms no second on-disk copy). All three passed on first build (nothing here needed a code fix, only tests); all three mutation-tested (dropping `FindMostWorkChain`'s `!HaveBodies` clause, `VerifyDB`'s own guard, and M-2's fix respectively — each caught). **Correction (Fable adversarial review, 2026-09-20): two of the three tests were weaker than described.** `startup_activation_is_non_fatal_...` never actually reached the startup-shaped code path it named — `FindMostWorkChain` had already excluded the withheld block from `setBlockIndexCandidates` during the ORIGINAL `ProcessNewBlock` call, so the test's own "startup" `ActivateBestChain` call found nothing to do regardless of the mutation; the mutation was really being caught earlier, by the initial submission. Fixed: the block is explicitly reinserted into `setBlockIndexCandidates` before the startup-shaped call, matching what `LoadBlockIndex` genuinely does at a real restart (it re-inserts every `BLOCK_VALID_TRANSACTIONS` block with `HaveTxsDownloaded()`, which a commitment-only block satisfies, verified directly against `LoadBlockIndex`'s own source) — the precondition is now realistic, though a global filter mutation is still caught at the first `FindMostWorkChain` call either way, which is expected and not a weakness. | F-111 | **1.3.6 done: F-25e2's cursor bug actually fixed, plus two new bugs found and fixed while proving it live** | Scoped by a Fable review before any code was written (the plan's own text — a new memory-only backoff structure keyed independent of chain-work — was itself off-target): the real gap is one line. `FindNextBlocksToDownload`'s `pindexLastCommonBlock` cursor (`net_processing.cpp`) advanced past any block whose `HaveTxsDownloaded()` read true, and a fully-bodied descendant of a commitment-only ancestor satisfies that (decoupling's whole point: the chain extends on commitments alone) — so the cursor jumped the gap the moment such a descendant existed, and nothing ever walked it back. **Fixed**: a `fBehindGap` flag, scoped to one `FindNextBlocksToDownload` call, set the moment any block is found needing a fetch or still in flight, and checked before the cursor is allowed to advance — restoring the walk's own stated invariant ("update pindexLastCommonBlock as long as ALL ancestors are already downloaded"). Existing peer-timeout-and-reassignment machinery (no NOTFOUND for blocks; a peer that never delivers eventually gets disconnected, never the block marked invalid) already provides the "backoff", confirmed by a Fable review of the surrounding machinery before writing anything — no new structure needed, matching the plan's own instinct against one. **Proving it live** (not just by restart, closing 1.3.1/F-40b's open item) needed a new test-only knob, `-perfwithholdcount=<n>` (F-111): withholds a matching block for exactly N attempts, then lets it through, decrementing a shared `std::atomic<int>` in `PerfWithholdBodies`. Building the functional test (`feature_body_refetch.py`, real P2P via `P2PDataStore`, a commitment-only PARENT + fully-bodied CHILD announced by headers then fetched by the node's own initiative) found two more real bugs before it ever passed: (1) `PerfWithholdBodies` was called from three separate sites inside `AcceptBlock` (the "already withheld" early-return, the accept-branch log, and `ReceivedBlockTransactions`'s `bodies_held` argument) — each call decremented the SAME shared counter, so one logical delivery could burn through the configured count in one to three decrements depending on which branch was reached, making "withhold for N attempts" mean something different depending on control flow. Fixed: evaluated exactly once per `AcceptBlock` call, cached, reused. **Correction (Fable adversarial review, 2026-09-20, verified empirically): the mutation-testing claim below was false as first written.** A fresh block's first delivery never reaches the "already withheld" early-return (`BLOCK_HAVE_DATA` isn't set yet, so that call short-circuits away) — reverting the fix costs one or two decrements per delivery, not "one to three" as first counted. With count=2, the pre-fix bug converged on the SECOND delivery instead of the third, and the test's original `parent_requests >= 2` assertion had exactly one request of slack, silently satisfied by the still-buggy behaviour — reverting the fix and re-running confirmed the test passed regardless. Fixed the assertion to `parent_requests == WITHHOLD_COUNT + 1` (an exact pin, not a lower bound); re-verified the mutation is now caught, and the correct code still passes with exactly 3. (2) Once the count exhausts and `AcceptBlock` sets `BLOCK_HAVE_BODIES` for real, `ReadBlockFromDisk`'s own, separate withhold check (used by `ConnectTip` and eighteen other call sites) had no way to know the count had exhausted — it unconditionally still refused the read, so `ConnectTip` got a real read failure it couldn't distinguish from disk corruption and called `AbortNode`, taking the whole node down. A first fix (gate the check on `!HaveBodies(pindex)`) broke a genuinely different, pre-existing use of the same mechanism (`commitmentblock_tests.cpp`'s materialising-read test, which withholds an ALREADY-fully-bodied, already-connected block to simulate a future body-store eviction — `HaveBodies()` is true there by design, and the check must still fire). The real fix splits the function in two: `PerfWithholdBodies` (decrements, `AcceptBlock` only) and a new non-decrementing `PerfWithholdStillActive` (peek, every other call site) — but the first version of THAT split had a third bug, an unclamped decrement walking a configured, exhausted count down through -1, -2, ... indistinguishable from the -1 sentinel that means "never configured, withhold forever" (both read as "still active"). Fixed by clamping the decrement at 0 with a CAS loop, so a configured count's exhaustion and the unconfigured default are permanently distinguishable. All three bugs were found by actually running the live test against real P2P traffic, not by reasoning about the code — each one only manifested under conditions (a real accept-then-connect sequence, genuine repeated delivery) a pure code review would have had to simulate perfectly to catch. Mutation-tested: reverting the cursor fix, the shared-counter fix, or the clamping fix each independently reproduces a distinct failure (a stranded gap timeout, or a fatal `AbortNode` crash) and each is caught by the same test. Full boost suite green throughout (including the pre-existing `commitmentblock_read_tests` withhold test, re-verified unaffected by the split) | `source`, live P2P functional test, mutation-tested | **superseded — see F-114: the "existing machinery" claim below was wrong; a real backoff was missing and has now been built** |
| F-112 | **1.3.7 done: `InvalidateBlock`/`MarkConflictingBlock`/`EnforceBestChainLock` audited against a commitment-only block — safe, two negative findings recorded rather than fixed** | Scoped with a Fable review before writing anything, since this reaches into the LLMQ ChainLocks subsystem this session hadn't otherwise touched. Two findings, both favourable, both written down rather than acted on since 1.3.7 is explicitly "audit, not full hardening" (build-plan's own framing; full graceful degradation is 4.1's job). (1) The "rebuild `setBlockIndexCandidates`" loop `InvalidateBlock` and `MarkConflictingBlock` share (`validation.cpp`) checks `HaveTxsDownloaded()` but not `HaveBodies()`, so it CAN reinstate a commitment-only block as a candidate. Not new risk: `ReceivedBlockTransactions` and `LoadBlockIndex` already insert commitment-only blocks as candidates on arrival and at restart with the identical check (F-110's `startup_activation_...` test already relies on this). `FindMostWorkChain` (F-109) is the one, already-tested filter, and an exhaustive check of every other reader of the set (`PruneBlockIndexCandidates`, `ResetBlockFailureFlags`, `PreciousBlock` — all pre-existing, all the same insertion check; zero references from any RPC handler or `net_processing.cpp`) found nothing that reads it unsafely. (2) `EnforceBestChainLock`'s (`llmq/quorums_chainlocks.cpp`) backward `pprev` walk terminates safely at genesis (always on `ChainActive()`, so the loop's own guard condition stops it before a null `pprev` could ever be dereferenced), and its only `assert(false)` path (`MarkConflictingBlock` returning false) requires `DisconnectTip` to fail, reachable only for a block that `m_chain.Contains()` — which a commitment-only block can never be (`ConnectTip` already refuses to connect one, F-109). The build-plan row's own named deliverable — "`ReadBlockFromDisk`'s `nullptr` fallback reaching an IS-conflict-detection caller" — turned out to be `TrySignChainTip`'s `GetBlockTxs` walk (same file), missed by the first pass at this scope and only found by the Fable review: it is rooted at `::ChainActive().Tip()` and never walks more than 5 blocks back, so by the same "the active chain is never commitment-only" invariant it can never reach one either. Safe by construction, with nothing reachable to build a test around — this entry is that written conclusion. **What was tested directly** (`acceptancebit_tests.cpp`, four new cases, all reusing the equal-work-siblings construction from F-107/1.3.4 rather than F-109's heavier `DisconnectTip`-downgrade technique — `InvalidateBlock`/`MarkConflictingBlock` only ever touch `nStatus`/`pprev` bookkeeping on genuinely-connected-or-never-connected blocks, never DIP3/evoDB state directly, so the lighter construction suffices): `invalidateblock_is_safe_with_a_commitment_only_sibling` and `markconflictingblock_is_safe_with_a_commitment_only_sibling` (each function called on a full sibling A while a commitment-only sibling B sits alongside; confirms B gets harmlessly reinstated as a candidate per finding (1) but `FindMostWorkChain` still refuses to select it); `invalidate_and_markconflicting_are_safe_called_directly_on_a_commitment_only_block` (the build-plan row's own literal wording — both functions invoked directly ON a commitment-only block, off the active chain, the pure flag-setting path that never reaches `DisconnectTip` at all); `a_commitment_only_block_still_heals_after_its_sibling_is_marked_conflicting` (`EnforceBestChainLock`'s own operation on a losing sibling, reproduced directly through `MarkConflictingBlock` — driving the real `CChainLocksHandler` needs an enabled spork, a BLS quorum and a signed recovered signature, disproportionate scaffolding for a risk surface entirely expressible through the one call `EnforceBestChainLock`'s loop actually makes; confirms marking a rival conflicting doesn't leave a commitment-only block permanently stranded once its real body arrives). All four mutation-tested against `FindMostWorkChain`'s own filter (F-109's mutation, dropping `!HaveBodies`); each caught, though — since the mutation weakens a mechanism the shared setup itself depends on — at the setup's own assertion rather than deeper in each test's specific logic, the same class of result the 1.3.5/1.3.6 reviews already treated as acceptable once the precondition being protected is the one under test. Also recorded, pre-existing and out of scope: `MarkConflictingBlock` never takes `::mempool.cs` itself despite `UpdateMempoolForReorg`'s own `EXCLUSIVE_LOCKS_REQUIRED` annotation naming it (unlike `InvalidateBlock`, which does) — a no-op today since `DEBUG_LOCKORDER` isn't defined in this build, so every new test takes the lock itself rather than relying on the function to. Full suite green | `source`, Fable-reviewed before implementation, mutation-tested | **1.3.7 complete 2026-09-20 — phase 1 complete** |
| F-113 | **the two-peer body-refetch stall mechanism 1.3.6 leans on is real and unmodified — recorded, not tested** | 1.3.6's commit argued that existing peer-timeout-and-reassignment machinery (no NOTFOUND for blocks; a peer that never delivers is eventually disconnected, never the block) is sufficient backoff for a missing body, without a test that actually stalls one peer and reassigns to another. Investigated as a follow-up, not itself a build-plan item. Confirmed real: `net_processing.cpp`'s per-peer `BLOCK_DOWNLOAD_TIMEOUT_BASE`/`_PER_PEER` check (`nNow > state.nDownloadingSince + consensusParams.nPowTargetSpacing * (...)`, the "SendMessages" loop) disconnects a peer whose oldest in-flight block has been outstanding too long, and `FinalizeNode` frees its `mapBlocksInFlight` entries on disconnect, letting `FindNextBlocksToDownload` reassign naturally on the next cycle to any other connected peer with room in its own window — no code path marks the block itself invalid. **Not built as a test**: `nDownloadingSince`/`nNow` both come from `GetTimeMicros()`, which `SetMockTime` does not reach (only `GetTime<T>()`'s template family is mocktime-aware) — proving this live needs an unavoidable real wall-clock wait of one full `nPowTargetSpacing` (120 s for this chain, `chainparams.cpp`'s `CRegTestParams`), non-mockable, added permanently to the functional suite's runtime. The faster `BLOCK_STALLING_TIMEOUT` path (2 s) is not a practical substitute: it only fires when a peer's own `BLOCK_DOWNLOAD_WINDOW` (1024 blocks) is genuinely exhausted with an in-flight neighbour blocking it, unreachable for anything smaller than a four-figure block gap. Making the wait fast would require adding a new test-only timeout-override constant to `net_processing.cpp` production code, purely to enable a test of a mechanism no 1.x step modified. Given neither this session nor the build plan touched this code, and the cost (a slow test, or new production code to avoid the slow test) doesn't clear the bar the rest of 1.3.x held to, closed here as a documented, unmodified-and-correct-by-inspection conclusion rather than built | `source` | **correction, F-114: this closed the wrong scenario (peer stays silent) — the real gap (peer keeps answering but never resolves the gap) was found by a later full-arc review and fixed** |
| F-114 | **1.3.6 actually had no backoff at all — F-111's "existing machinery covers it" argument was wrong, found by a full-arc adversarial review and confirmed by direct measurement** | A comprehensive review of the whole decoupling design (Phase 0 through 1.3.7, two independent models, run after phase 1 was declared complete) found the gap F-111/F-113 both missed: the existing peer-timeout-and-reassignment machinery (no NOTFOUND for blocks; a silent peer eventually gets disconnected) only detects a peer going **silent**. It does nothing when a peer keeps *answering* but the answer never resolves the gap — exactly `-perfwithholdcount`'s own shape, and the shape a real Phase 2 fetch failure (corrupt body, hash mismatch, a body that fails validation) would take. `MarkBlockAsReceived` (`net_processing.cpp`) frees the in-flight slot on every response regardless of outcome, so the per-peer stall/download timeouts — both keyed on a slot staying *occupied* — never engage; `FindNextBlocksToDownload` re-requests on the very next cycle with no pacing. **Measured directly, twice** (the review's own run and an independent reproduction before accepting it): a bit over one thousand to ~3,600 `getdata` requests per second for a single permanently-withheld block, unbounded, on one peer. **Fixed**: `g_body_retry_state` (`net_processing.cpp`), a memory-only, self-cleaning `std::map<uint256, {nNextAttempt, nAttempts}>` consulted in `FindNextBlocksToDownload` before adding a still-missing block to the fetch list — exponential backoff (1s base, doubling, capped at 30s), erased the moment a block resolves. The first-ever request for any block is never delayed. Matches build-plan row 1.3.6's own requirement exactly ("memory-only... rebuilt from the index on every start... never marks the block invalid") — the row's original scope was right; F-111's execution of it was not. New functional test `feature_body_refetch_backoff.py` (a permanently-withheld single block, sampling the request rate over an 8 s window, asserting it stays bounded) proves it; `feature_body_refetch.py`'s own eventual-convergence assertion (F-111, `parent_requests == WITHHOLD_COUNT + 1`) still passes unchanged, confirming the backoff doesn't interfere with genuine resolution. Mutation-tested: disabling the backoff check reproduces 15,373 requests in the same 8 s window the fixed code holds to 3 | `source`, live P2P functional test, mutation-tested, cross-model adversarial review | **fixed 2026-09-20** |
| F-115 | **the rung had no bound on identifier count — the one resource question a bare identifier list CAN answer, and the only one it was missing** | Found by the same full-arc adversarial review as F-114. `CheckCommitmentBlock`'s own comment said "block size / sigop / input-count budgets ... a bare identifier list cannot answer either" — true for sigops and byte size (both need materialised bodies) but wrong as a disposal of identifier *count*: a bare list's length is directly observable. Without a bound, a miner names more identifiers than any legal body could ever fill; the merkle root binds the header to that count (one block of real PoW), and nothing catches it until every peer has spent the fetch bandwidth trying to fill it — at the 8 MB design point, up to 250,000 × K-3's ~100 kB ceiling per block of work, network-wide, once Phase 2's real fetch protocol exists. Not live in Phase 1: the only construction today is `CommitmentsFromBlock(block)` on an already-`CheckBlock`-passed block, so the count can never exceed what `bad-blk-length` already allowed — this is a design hole for Phase 2, not a live Phase 1 bug. **Fixed**: `CheckCommitmentBlock` now rejects (`bad-cmt-toomanyids`, `corruption=false` — same reasoning as `bad-cmt-duplicate-ids`, a fact about what this header commits to, not corruption in transit) when `vCommitments.size()` exceeds `COMMITMENT_BUDGET_MAX_INPUTS`. Reuses D-19's own constant rather than a new one: every non-coinbase transaction needs at least one input (a 0-input tx fails `CheckTransaction`), so identifier count can never legally exceed the total input count D-19 already bounds — no new sizing decision needed. Two new tests (`commitmentblock_tests.cpp`): the boundary is exact (700,000 passes, 700,001 rejects) and a flag-off control. Mutation-tested. Does **not** fully close the size question — a body legal by input count can still exceed the byte cap, which still needs materialised bodies to check, unchanged | `source`, mutation-tested, cross-model adversarial review | **fixed 2026-09-20** |
| F-116 | **`HaveBodies`'s doc comment overclaimed universal consultation -- verified by call-site audit, found inert today, deferred as a Phase-2 item** | Found by the same full-arc adversarial review as F-114/F-115. validation.h's comment on `HaveBodies` said "every path that may materialise a block consults this... closes the whole remote-crash class." Checked directly against every `ReadBlockFromDisk(CBlockIndex*)` call site: `ConnectTip`, the two `net_processing.cpp` serve sites, and `VerifyDB`'s first-block check do consult it first (each with its own comment naming the failure mode avoided); `DisconnectTip`, `VerifyDB`'s forward re-verify loop (level 4, walking via `ChainActive().Next`), `RollforwardBlock`, and the `-loadblock`/reindex recursive-child site do not, and have never been individually audited for what a false return does at each one. **Not a live bug**: two independent reasons. `FindMostWorkChain`'s ancestor walk (F-109) already refuses to select a commitment-only chain as best, so none of the un-audited sites -- all of which only ever touch the active chain or a chain already accepted as best -- can be reached with a commitment-only block today. Separately, F-110 already established that Phase 1's storage format writes a block's full bytes unconditionally on arrival regardless of `BLOCK_HAVE_BODIES`, so outside the test-only `g_perf_withhold_*` mechanism `ReadBlockFromDisk` cannot fail with "body not held" at all yet -- the comment was describing the Phase 2 design target (a real body store where bytes can be genuinely absent) as an already-closed audit of today's tree. **Fixed**: the comment now says exactly this -- which sites are gated and why, which are not, and why the gap is inert under Phase 1's storage format. **Deferred, not fixed**: auditing the four un-gated sites for what they should do once Phase 2 makes bodies genuinely separable (most likely: the same `HaveBodies` pre-check `ConnectTip` and the serve sites already use, converting a would-be error/abort into a graceful skip) is real work with no Phase 1 test to write against, since nothing here is reachable yet -- left for whoever builds Phase 2's body store, same proportionality this session applied to M-3/D-11-style forward-looking gaps | `source` (call-site audit), cross-model adversarial review | **comment fixed 2026-09-20; call-site audit deferred to Phase 2** |
| F-117 | **`NewPoWValidBlock`'s compact-block announcement had no `HaveBodies` guard -- inert today, fixed as forward defense-in-depth** | Found by the same full-arc adversarial review as F-114/F-115/F-116. `AcceptBlock`'s post-`CheckBlock` relay call (`GetMainSignals().NewPoWValidBlock(pindex, pblock)`, `validation.cpp`) fires unconditionally once the header/merkle checks pass, driving `net_processing.cpp`'s handler to cache the block (`most_recent_block`) and fast-announce a `CMPCTBLOCK` to peers -- a promise that a subsequent `GETBLOCKTXN` will succeed. **Not live in Phase 1**: `pblock` here is always the complete, real block passed all the way through from `ProcessNewBlock` -- Phase 2's commitment-only fetch protocol (F-105's own documented scoping) doesn't exist yet, so there is no path today where this fires over a body-incomplete block; even under the test-only `-perfwithhold*` mechanism, the freshly-cached `most_recent_block` would still answer a same-cycle `GETBLOCKTXN` from memory, not disk. The real gap is forward-looking: once Phase 2 delivers a genuine commitment-only accept, an announcement made before the body is in hand would be broken the moment `most_recent_block`'s single-entry cache is evicted by the next block. **Fixed**: added `&& HaveBodies(pindex)` to the existing relay condition, matching the same guard `ConnectTip` and the two `net_processing.cpp` serve sites already use. **Not unit-tested**: `PeerLogicValidation` is not registered as a validation-interface listener in the boost unit-test binary, so the announcement path itself has no reachable harness here -- matching this tree's existing convention for untested `net_processing.cpp` wiring (R-31's precedent, F-101's identical note on its own CMPCTBLOCK gate fix) | `source` (call-site audit), cross-model adversarial review | **fixed 2026-09-20** |
| F-118 | **M-5 (the bodiesmigrated migration's zero test coverage) is a real gap, fixed; M-6 (the "bodies arrived" branch's skipped flush) is not a real gap once checked against `FlushStateToDisk`'s own mode semantics** | Found by the same full-arc adversarial review as F-114 through F-117, both around 1.3.1's pre-1.3 `BLOCK_HAVE_BODIES` migration (`LoadBlockIndexDB`, validation.cpp). **M-5**: the migration loop (set `BLOCK_HAVE_BODIES` on every `HAVE_DATA`-without-`HAVE_BODIES` entry, gated by the `bodiesmigrated` flag) had never been exercised by a test, despite the flag's own comment naming a real corruption class it exists to prevent (re-migrating a genuine future commitment-only entry). Fixed the untestability first: `LoadBlockIndexDB` was `static` (internal linkage), unreachable from a separate test translation unit; removed `static` and declared it in `validation.h`, matching this codebase's existing pattern of exposing test-only-reachable internals there (`g_perf_withhold_*`, `g_commitmentBudgetActive`). Two new tests (`acceptancebit_tests.cpp`): `bodiesmigrated_migration_restores_have_bodies_on_pre_1_3_entries` clears `BLOCK_HAVE_BODIES` directly on already-connected entries (reproducing a genuine pre-1.3 on-disk shape -- bytes really are on disk, matching every entry before this bit existed -- deliberately not via `g_perf_withhold_hashes`, which also blocks the read itself, the wrong shape for "predates the bit") and clears the flag, then calls `LoadBlockIndexDB` directly and confirms both entries and the flag are restored; `bodiesmigrated_migration_does_not_touch_a_later_genuine_commitment_only_entry` runs the migration once first (flag now true, matching a real first boot), then clears the bit on a THIRD entry (simulating a genuine future commitment-only block) and confirms a second migration run leaves it alone -- the actual downgrade/upgrade corruption the flag protects against, now proven rather than assumed. **Chunking the migration's single `WriteBatchSync` call, deliberately not done**: no existing chunk-size constant anywhere in this codebase to reuse (the H-1/F-115 precedent was reuse-a-constant, not invent one), and this project has no live chain with a large-enough historical block count for an unbounded batch to matter -- a real concern only once this ships to an actual multi-million-block chain, 4.6's "real deployment bit" territory, not Phase 1's. Recorded here rather than silently dropped. **M-6, investigated and found NOT to be a real gap**: the review's framing was "`AcceptBlock`'s 'bodies arrived' branch returns early past the flush that follows it." True, but that flush is `FlushStateToDisk(..., FlushStateMode::NONE)`, and `FlushStateToDisk`'s own doc comment says exactly what that mode does: "won't do anything besides checking if we need to prune" -- confirmed by reading its mode-gating conditions directly, none of which fire for `NONE`. **A first attempt at a fix (adding that same NONE-mode call inline) was built, tested, and then caught as wrong by its own regression test**: the test (using M-5's newly-exposed `LoadBlockIndexDB` as a disk-persistence probe -- it unconditionally overwrites in-memory `nStatus` from whatever `CDiskBlockIndex` says on disk, so re-running it after a body arrives is a precise "did this reach disk" check) failed identically whether the fix was present or reverted, both times crashing a pre-existing `CheckBlockIndex` consistency assertion rather than the test's own -- proof the NONE-mode call changes nothing, matching the doc comment, not a real fix. **Reverted, not kept**: the fake fix, its misleading comment, and its non-discriminating test were all removed rather than left in the tree. What actually determines when this bit reaches disk is the same batching policy every other `nStatus` mutation in `AcceptBlock` already relies on (`ReceivedBlockTransactions`'s `HAVE_DATA` set included) -- `PERIODIC` (hourly), `IF_NEEDED` (cache-size-triggered), `ALWAYS` (clean shutdown), or a prune run. A crash between an accept and the next such flush loses whatever index changes happened in between, for every bit, not specifically `HAVE_BODIES` -- this branch was never a special-case regression, and there is nothing to fix here without changing that shared batching policy for every caller, out of proportion to what this branch alone does. Full suite green throughout, same pre-existing `miner_tests` failure | `source`, direct reading of `FlushStateToDisk`'s mode-gating logic, a since-reverted fix caught wrong by its own test, cross-model adversarial review | **M-5 fixed 2026-09-20; M-6 closed as not-a-bug 2026-09-20** |
| F-119 | **Dead retracted-design machinery removed (Fable A1/A2, full-arc Opus L-4), two redundant tests removed (Fable A4 == full-arc Opus L-3), one redundant guard simplified (full-arc Opus L-5)** | Cleanup pass closing findings from both the Fable Phase-0/1.1/1.2 review and the Opus 1.3.x full-arc review that independently converged on the same targets. **A1/A2/L-4 (dead code + the comment stating its retracted design as fact)**: `SER_COMMITMENTS` (`serialize.h`) and `BlockSerFlagsFor` (`protocol.h`) had zero production callers -- confirmed by a direct grep across `src/` outside the test tree -- and existed only to serve their own two certifying tests. `SER_COMMITMENTS`'s doc comment ("a stream flag and not a header bit") stated exactly the design docs' own retracted stream-flag selector (F-80..F-82, R-28b) as settled fact, the same retraction `the_stream_flag_currently_selects_nothing` (the test built specifically to pin that nothing reads the flag) already had to work around. Removed both symbols; deleted `the_stream_flag_currently_selects_nothing` entirely (nothing left to test); trimmed `the_service_bit_chooses_the_serialization` (renamed `the_service_bit_gates_receiving_commitments`) down to what's still live -- `CanReceiveCommitments` and the `NODE_COMMITMENTS` bit-collision checks -- removing every `SER_COMMITMENTS`/`BlockSerFlagsFor` assertion. **A4 == L-3 (redundant test, found independently by both reviews)**: `advertising_the_bit_earns_no_trust` (`commitmentblock_tests.cpp`) duplicated `materialise_rejects_a_body_that_does_not_match` -- both substitute a wrong transaction into the bodies vector and assert `MaterialiseBlock` rejects it; its only addition was an unrelated `CanReceiveCommitments` assertion already covered by the service-bit test above, and never actually wired into the `MaterialiseBlock` call it sat next to (the "claim" framing was narrative only, not mechanically tested). Deleted. **L-5 (redundant guard)**: `net_processing.cpp`'s H-2 backoff cleanup (`FindNextBlocksToDownload`) wrapped `g_body_retry_state.erase(hash)` in `if (!g_body_retry_state.empty())` -- `std::map::erase` on a key the map doesn't hold, including an empty map, is already a safe O(log n) no-op; the guard added a branch and changed nothing. Removed. **A3, informational, no code change**: `fProvidesCommitments` having no reader is Mike's own R-31 decision (test-only wiring left unconsumed pending a real deployment bit), already recorded there -- not a gap to close. Full suite green, same pre-existing `miner_tests` failure | `source` (grep confirmed zero production callers before deletion), Fable + full-arc adversarial reviews (independent convergence on A4/L-3) | **fixed 2026-09-20** |
| F-120 | **Three documentation gaps closed from the Fable Phase-0/1.1/1.2 review: an undocumented precondition (B3), an unrecorded policy change (B4), and a comment that misdescribed what actually binds a default miner (B5)** | **B3**: `GetAccurateSigOpCount` (`tx_verify.cpp`) silently contributes zero sigops for any `txin.prevout` that doesn't resolve in the given view -- `CCoinsViewCache::AccessCoin`'s missing-coin return is a default-constructed `Coin`, whose empty `CTxOut.scriptPubKey` has zero sigops by construction, so an unresolvable input UNDERcounts rather than erroring. True today at both call sites (`AcceptToMemoryPool`, `ConnectBlock`) only because each runs this strictly after its own `Consensus::CheckTxInputs`, which already required every input to resolve -- but nothing in the function itself enforces or even states that. Documented the precondition at the declaration (`tx_verify.h`) and pointed to it from both call sites, rather than adding a runtime assert on a hot validation path for a precondition the calling convention already guarantees (a new call site that violates it is a review problem, not a runtime-check problem). **B4**: `AcceptToMemoryPool`'s per-TRANSACTION standardness sigop gate (`bad-txns-too-many-sigops`, `MAX_STANDARD_TX_SIGOPS`) reads the same `g_commitmentBudgetActive`-switched `nSigOps` the block-level budget check (D-18/F-88/F-89) already made accurate-under-the-flag -- a second, separate policy surface silently inherited the switch, never recorded as its own change. Real and bidirectional, not a rounding footnote: `GetAccurateOwnSigOpCount`'s own doc comment already establishes legacy over- and under-counts relative to accurate in different cases (bare-multisig creation vs P2SH/spend-side gaps), so some transactions standard under legacy become non-standard under the budget at mempool-acceptance time, and vice versa -- a real relay/mempool-policy behaviour change while the budget is test-only, now recorded rather than left implicit in D-18's block-level framing. **B5**: `BlockAssembler`'s constructor comment said clamping against the legacy byte cap "would silently stop the miner from filling toward the new one" (the body-byte budget, ~8 MB by design) -- true of the CONSENSUS ceiling this clamp computes against, but silent on what a DEFAULT node actually builds: `DefaultOptions()`'s `nBlockMaxSize` is still `DEFAULT_BLOCK_MAX_SIZE` (2,000,000), the `std::min`'s other, much smaller operand, unless an operator explicitly raises `-blockmaxsize`. A default miner keeps building ~2 MB blocks under the budget; it does not grow toward 8 MB on its own. Comment corrected to say so. Also fixed in passing: the same comment's `miner.cpp:315` citation (C3) had already drifted from the code it named -- replaced with a symbol reference (`BlockAssembler::TestPackage`'s `packageSigOps` check) that can't go stale the same way.
