| F-126 | **2.1.1 built: the body-store record format and file series, mutation-tested — plus a process gap found and fixed in the previous commit** | Phase 2's first component. `bodystore.h`/`.cpp`: a fourth `FlatFileSeq` (`bdy*.dat`, `flatfile.h`), grounded directly in `BlockFileSeq()`/`FindBlockPos()`'s existing allocate/rollover pattern rather than designed from scratch. **Independent file numbering from blk/rev, decided rather than left open**: a commitment block's bytes are small and roughly constant-size, its bodies range from empty to the full resource-budget ceiling, so coupling the two series' rotation would create a many-to-one relationship with no benefit. Per-block record is self-delimiting -- `[CompactSize count][count x CompactSize cumulative end-offset][tx bytes...]` -- so `ReadBodyAt` can seek directly to any index's bytes via `CAutoFile::ignore()` without deserializing anything before it, transaction-decoupling.md §5.2's `(height, index)` addressing requirement. `FindBodyPos` mirrors `FindBlockPos` exactly, including reusing `MAX_BLOCKFILE_SIZE`'s value (128 MiB) as `MAX_BODYFILE_SIZE` rather than inventing a new number; in-memory only for now, does not survive a restart (2.1.2's job). Seven new tests (`bodystore_tests.cpp`): empty/multi-body round-trips, out-of-order random-access reads (deliberately including first and last index, so a mutation that only fixes index 0 can't pass by accident), an out-of-range rejection, and two `FindBodyPos` bookkeeping tests (contiguous allocation within one file; rollover to a fresh file once the current one would exceed `MAX_BODYFILE_SIZE`, verified with a real ~128 MiB `posix_fallocate` call that cost under 60ms -- measured, not assumed, before accepting it as a cheap test). Both structurally load-bearing pieces mutation-tested: disabling `ReadBodyAt`'s offset-table skip (always reading from the record's start) was caught by 5 of 6 index assertions; disabling `FindBodyPos`'s rollover condition was caught by both of the rollover test's assertions. **Test-fixture gap found and fixed along the way**: `BasicTestingSetup` alone (used by every other test in this file's neighbourhood, e.g. `commitmentblock_tests.cpp`) never points `-datadir` at its own temp root or clears `GetBlocksDir()`'s path cache -- harmless for tests that never touch a real file, but these are the first `BasicTestingSetup`-based tests in this tree to do so, and without it `GetBlocksDir()` can return a path a DIFFERENT, earlier test already deleted on teardown. Fixed with a small `BodyStoreTestingSetup` wrapper calling `SetDataDir`+`ClearDatadirCache`, the same two calls `TestingSetup`'s own constructor already makes for exactly this reason. **Also found while building this: commit b0a8ee824 (the previous commit) does not compile.** A1's cleanup (`commitmentblock_tests.cpp`) missed a third `SER_COMMITMENTS` reference at `round_trips_over_a_stream` (only two of three were found and removed at the time), and this session's own "full suite green" verification for that commit was run against a stale `libraptoreum_server.a` that hadn't been rebuilt to notice -- an incremental-build gap, not a re-check that was skipped, but the effect is the same: a claimed verification that wasn't actually run against current code. Fixed (the flag combination wasn't load-bearing to what that test checks; replaced with plain `SER_NETWORK`). **Process lesson, recorded so it doesn't repeat**: after touching a file whose object file might not obviously get relinked (new source files added to `Makefile.am`, or a change automake needs to notice), confirm with `ar t <archive> | grep <symbol>` that the archive actually contains what changed, or delete the stale `.a` first -- a green exit code alone does not prove a full rebuild happened. Full suite green (433 cases via `!miner_tests`), same one pre-existing `miner_tests/CreateNewBlock_validity` failure | `source`, mutation-tested (2 mutations, both caught), a measured (not assumed) disk-allocation cost | **2.1.1 built 2026-09-20; b0a8ee824's compile error found and fixed same day** |
| F-127 | **Fable review of 2.1.1 (commit 09936c085) found one real HIGH bug and six MEDIUM gaps -- all fixed, all mutation-tested. F-126's "mirrors FindBlockPos exactly" and "O(1) seek" claims were both wrong.** | Reviewed shortly after building, per this project's standing practice. **HIGH**: offsets were `CompactSize`, which `serialize.h`'s `ReadCompactSize` unconditionally refuses above `MAX_SIZE` (32 MiB) -- well under `COMMITMENT_BUDGET_BODY_BYTES` (110 MB), so every record whose bodies exceeded 32 MiB was unreadable on every index, not just the ones past that point. Verified directly against the tree's own `serialize.h` before accepting. **Fixed**: offsets are now fixed-width 4-byte (`uint32_t`), which a `static_assert` in `bodystore.h` ties to the real budget (`COMMITMENT_BUDGET_BODY_BYTES` + the input-count budget's own offset-table overhead, comfortably under `MAX_BODYFILE_SIZE`) so a future budget raise fails to compile rather than silently reintroducing the ceiling. New test writes a ~36 MB record (40 medium transactions, not one huge one -- `CScript`'s own length prefix is *also* `CompactSize`, an orthogonal pre-existing limit a single huge script would have hit regardless of this fix) and round-trips both the first and last index. **MEDIUM, real bugs**: (1) `FindBodyPos`'s rollover loop had no bound -- an `nAddSize >= MAX_BODYFILE_SIZE` would loop forever resizing an ever-growing vector, since a fresh file's `nSize` starts at 0 and the exit condition can never become false; fixed with an early `error()` return, now tested. (2) `FindBodyPos` never called `FlatFileSeq::Flush(finalize=true)` on rollover, unlike `FindBlockPos`'s `FlushBlockFile` -- finished body files stayed at their full chunk-sized preallocation forever; this is exactly what F-126's own leaked files were evidence of (see below). Fixed and now asserted directly (a finished file's on-disk size after rollover must equal its actual used bytes, not the preallocated chunk). (3) `ReadBodyAt` used `CAutoFile::ignore()`, which still `fread`s every skipped byte rather than seeking -- reading the last body of a 100+ MB record read the whole record from disk, not O(1) as both the header comment and F-126 claimed. Fixed with a real `fseek` on the underlying `FILE*`; a new test proves the difference functionally (corrupts an earlier body's on-disk bytes after writing, confirms a later index still reads correctly -- a sequential-decode implementation would fail this). (4) No exact-size helper existed, so nothing proved `WriteBodyRecord` writes what a caller computed for `FindBodyPos`'s `nAddSize` -- a one-byte disagreement would silently overlap the next record, and nothing tested two records back to back at all. Added `GetBodyRecordSerializedSize` plus an exact-size test and an adjacency test. (5) Every test transaction serialized to the same length, so a wrong write-side offset table (e.g. computed from the wrong body's size) could pass every test -- fixed by padding each test transaction's script by a distinct amount. (6) `ReadBodyRecordHeader` resized its offsets vector to whatever `count` claimed before reading a single real offset -- a corrupt count could drive a large transient allocation; bounded by `COMMITMENT_BUDGET_MAX_INPUTS` (F-115's own reasoning: no legal record ever names more transactions than the input-count budget allows), now tested. **Also found, not a code bug**: the pre-fixture version of these tests (before the `BodyStoreTestingSetup` fix already in commit 09936c085) had written 167 MB of real `bdy*.dat` files into a live, currently-running personal `raptoreumd` node's actual data directory (`~/.raptoreumcore/blocks/`, not a throwaway path) -- `BasicTestingSetup` with no `-datadir` set falls back to the real default datadir, not merely a stale cached one as the original fixture comment assumed. Files identified and removed (owner confirmed before deletion, since they sat in a live personal resource outside the repo). All 5 review findings verified directly against source before fixing (the `MAX_SIZE` ceiling, the infinite-loop condition, the missing `Flush` call, `CAutoFile::ignore()`'s real behaviour, and the live datadir pollution) -- none accepted on the reviewer's word alone. Every fix mutation-tested individually (4 mutations run: reverting the fixed-width offsets, reverting the `fseek` to a no-op skip, disabling the rollover `Flush` call, and disabling the count bound -- all four caught cleanly by the tests written for them). Full suite green (439 cases via `!miner_tests`), same one pre-existing `miner_tests` failure | Fable adversarial review, 2026-09-20, all findings independently verified against source, 4 mutations tested | **fixed 2026-09-20** |
| F-128 | **2.1.2 built: the body-file bookkeeping now persists via `pblocktree`, proven by a real restart simulation, mutation-tested** | `CBodyFileInfo` (`bodystore.h`) is `CBlockFileInfo`'s persisted analogue for the body-file series -- deliberately simpler (just `nSize`) since nothing here is a pruning or reindex decision yet (2.1's own "can delete later, but won't at first" scope, §8.4). New `CBlockTreeDB` methods (`txdb.h`/`.cpp`): `WriteBodyFileInfoBatch`/`ReadBodyFileInfo`/`ReadLastBodyFile`, using new DB key prefixes `y`/`Y` (confirmed unused against the existing prefix list before picking them), mirroring `ReadBlockFileInfo`/`ReadLastBlockFile`/`WriteBatchSync`'s own shape -- but a separate batch write, not folded into `WriteBatchSync` itself, since body-file bookkeeping has no reason to share the block-index flush's atomicity requirements and `WriteBatchSync`'s signature is already tightly coupled to `CBlockFileInfo`/`CBlockIndex`. `LoadBodyFileInfo`/`FlushBodyFileInfo` (`bodystore.cpp`) load from and write dirty entries to `pblocktree`, tracked via a new `setDirtyBodyFileInfo` set marked inside `FindBodyPos` exactly where `vinfoBlockFile`'s own dirty-marking happens for blk*.dat. **Proven with a real restart simulation, not just reasoned about**: a test-only `TestOnlyResetBodyFileState()` wipes the in-memory bookkeeping the same way a process restart would; a test writes a record, flushes, resets, reloads, and confirms `FindBodyPos` continues from the persisted position rather than reusing file 0 -- which would silently overwrite the record just written. Mutation-tested twice: disabling the `pblocktree` reload in `LoadBodyFileInfo`, and separately disabling the dirty-marking in `FindBodyPos`, both reproduced -- concretely, not just structurally -- the exact real-world failure this component exists to prevent (the reloaded position landed back at file 0/position 0, and the previously-written record was gone, read back as 0 transactions instead of 4). Testing this needed a real `pblocktree`, which `BasicTestingSetup` alone doesn't construct (only the much heavier `TestingSetup` does, for its own full chain init) -- the test fixture now also constructs a private in-memory `CBlockTreeDB`, taking only that one piece from `TestingSetup` rather than pulling in the rest. **Callable and correct, deliberately not yet wired into real node startup/shutdown**: there is still no `LoadBodyFileInfo()` call anywhere near `init.cpp`/`LoadBlockIndexDB`, and no `FlushBodyFileInfo()` call near `FlushStateToDisk` -- nothing in production calls into `bodystore.cpp` at all yet (2.1.4's job), so wiring the load/flush calls in now would be dead code with nothing to test end-to-end; deferred to land alongside 2.1.4 instead. Full suite green (442 cases via `!miner_tests`), same one pre-existing `miner_tests` failure | `source`, mutation-tested (2 mutations, both caught, each reproducing a concrete data-loss scenario) | **fixed 2026-09-20** |
| F-129 | **2.1.3 built: `CBlockIndex`/`CDiskBlockIndex` can now record a body-store position, needing no migration -- proven, not just argued, and mutation-tested** | New `BLOCK_HAVE_BODY_RECORD` status bit (`chain.h`) plus `nBodyFile`/`nBodyPos` fields and a `GetBodyPos()` accessor, on exactly `nFile`/`nDataPos`/`GetBlockPos()`'s own pattern. **Deliberately a separate bit from `BLOCK_HAVE_BODIES`, not a reuse**: `BLOCK_HAVE_BODIES` has meant "we consider this block's bodies held" since 1.3.1 and is true for essentially all of history under Phase 1's storage format (F-110); gating the new fields' serialization on it would mean every existing on-disk entry -- which already has that bit set -- needs the new fields present too, and none of them do, since nothing writes a real body-store record until 2.1.4. A bit that stays false for every entry until 2.1.4 genuinely sets it needs no active migration at all, unlike `BLOCK_HAVE_BODIES`'s own 1.3.1 migration (F-101) -- an unset bit decodes correctly from old bytes by construction, the same way any never-before-used bit in an existing `VARINT`-encoded field already does. **This claim was verified directly, not assumed**: `CDiskBlockIndex`'s serialization already conditionally includes `nFile`/`nDataPos`/`nUndoPos` based on `nStatus` bits (`chain.h`, confirmed by reading it before designing anything), so the new fields follow an established, already-safe pattern rather than inventing one. Three new tests (`bodystore_tests.cpp`): `GetBodyPos()` is null until the bit is set (matching `GetBlockPos()`/`GetUndoPos()`'s own convention exactly); a full round-trip through `CDiskBlockIndex` serialization with the bit set, checking every other field survives unaffected (proving the new bytes don't shift anything after them); and the one that matters most -- two otherwise-identical entries, one with garbage sitting in `nBodyFile`/`nBodyPos` but the bit unset, serialize to byte-identical output, proving the in-memory garbage never reaches disk and a historical entry decodes exactly as if the new fields didn't exist. Mutation-tested: forcing the new fields to serialize unconditionally (ignoring the status bit) was caught immediately by the byte-identical-output assertion. **A downgrade hazard is real but out of scope here, and recorded rather than solved**: a binary older than 2.1.3 reading an entry where a *future* 2.1.4+ binary genuinely set `BLOCK_HAVE_BODY_RECORD` would misparse the extra bytes as the start of the next field -- the general, already-accepted "don't downgrade past a node that wrote a newer on-disk format" property every prior block-index extension shares, not a new problem 2.1.3 introduces, and inert today since nothing sets the bit yet. Belongs to component H's "dual-format period" (transaction-decoupling.md §13), not to 2.1.3. Full suite green (445 cases via `!miner_tests`), same one pre-existing `miner_tests` failure. **Process note, not a code finding**: a background rebuild during this step was checked with a truncated/incomplete error grep and falsely reported clean while a real compile error (`HexStr`'s actual signature takes a `Span`, not two iterators) sat undetected -- caught only by directly re-running the specific failing compile and reading its full output, then confirmed by checking the object file actually existed with a fresh timestamp before trusting the next build. Matches F-126/F-127's own "verify a build actually happened" lesson -- worth repeating because it recurred even after being learned once already | `source` (direct read of `CDiskBlockIndex`'s existing conditional-serialization pattern before designing), mutation-tested | **fixed 2026-09-20** |
| F-130 | **Fable review of 2.1.2+2.1.3 (323af46d7, a78c12efd) found a real design hazard 2.1.3 created for 2.1.2's own rationale, a test gap that let 3 named mutants of the persistence path survive, and several stale claims -- all fixed or recorded, all verified directly before acting** | **HIGH, design, latent (nothing writes yet)**: 2.1.2's `WriteBodyFileInfoBatch` was justified as "body-file bookkeeping has nothing to do with a block-index flush's own atomicity requirements" -- true when written, false the moment 2.1.3 landed. Verified directly against `validation.cpp`: `FlushStateToDisk` deliberately bundles `FlushBlockFile()`, block-file-info, last-file, and every dirty `CBlockIndex` into ONE atomic `WriteBatchSync` call, precisely so an index entry can never be durable while the file-size bookkeeping protecting its bytes from reuse is not. Since 2.1.3, `CDiskBlockIndex` persists `nBodyFile`/`nBodyPos` -- exactly that coupling, reopened by a separate, independently-timed batch. **Failure scenario once 2.1.4 wires real writes**: index batch durable with `BLOCK_HAVE_BODY_RECORD -> (f, p)`; crash before `FlushBodyFileInfo`; restart reloads a stale (smaller) `vinfoBodyFile[f].nSize`; the next `FindBodyPos` hands out position `p` again and silently overwrites the record the surviving index entry points at. **Fixed**: corrected the misleading rationale in `txdb.h` with the real hazard and the two options 2.1.4 must choose between (fold into `WriteBatchSync`, or enforce `FlushBodyFile -> FlushBodyFileInfo -> WriteBatchSync` ordering strictly) -- not decided here, since 2.1.4's actual write-path shape isn't built yet, but no longer left to be discovered by accident. **MEDIUM, real test gap**: `find_body_pos_continues_after_a_simulated_restart` never left file 0, so it could not distinguish a correctly-persisted `nLastBodyFile` from one hardcoded to 0 -- three real mutants (dropping the `ReadLastBodyFile` call; persisting a constant 0 instead of the real last file; reloading only the LAST file's own size instead of every file 0..last) all passed it. **Fixed**: rewritten to force a genuine rollover to file 1 first -- the actual steady state of a real node once the first ~128 MiB fills -- plus a new test-only `TestOnlyGetBodyFileSize` accessor to make the third mutant (partial reload) directly observable, since `FindBodyPos` alone only ever consults the LAST file and would never surface that gap on its own. All three mutants verified to fail against the new test, then reverted; the third was independently confirmed to have survived the OLD test before being caught by the new one, proving the rewrite actually closed a real gap rather than just adding assertions. **MEDIUM, real design gap recorded, not solved**: `-reindex` wipes `pblocktree` -- every persisted body-file entry and every `CDiskBlockIndex`'s `nBodyFile`/`nBodyPos` -- while leaving `bdy*.dat` on disk untouched, and the record format is self-delimiting but not self-identifying (no block hash in a record), so rebuilding the index-to-record mapping from the files alone needs a design decision 2.1.4 must make explicitly (wipe-and-rewrite vs. add an identifier to the record header), not default into. Replaced every citation with a plain `F-127` reference. **LOW, fixed**: the test fixture never released its private in-memory `pblocktree`, unlike `TestingSetup`'s own destructor -- added `~BodyStoreTestingSetup()`. **LOW, noted, not actioned** (disproportionate for what's inert today): `LoadBodyFileInfo` tolerates a missing per-file DB entry silently rather than treating it as corruption (parity with `LoadBlockIndexDB`'s own behaviour, not a regression); `FindBodyPos` persists a phantom size gap on `out_of_space` before the allocation actually fails (harmless today, `FindBlockPos`'s sibling has the same ordering but `AbortNode`s instead); whether `BLOCK_HAVE_BODY_RECORD` should imply `BLOCK_HAVE_BODIES` for `PruneOneBlockFile`'s sake is a 2.1.4-or-later decision, not one to make speculatively now. Full suite green (445 cases via `!miner_tests`), same one pre-existing `miner_tests` failure | Fable adversarial review, 2026-09-20, all findings independently verified against source (the `FlushStateToDisk` atomicity claim read directly, all three mutants run and caught), 3 mutations tested | **fixed/recorded 2026-09-20** |
| F-131 | **`-reindex` decided for the body store: match `blk*.dat`/`rev*.dat`'s existing behaviour exactly, no new mechanism** | F-130 left this as an open decision for 2.1.4 (wipe-and-rewrite `bdy*.dat` vs. add a block identifier to the record so the mapping can be rebuilt from the files alone). Resolved by first establishing what "now" actually does, verified directly against `init.cpp` rather than assumed: `-reindex` wipes `pblocktree` at the start (`fReset = fReindex`, passed as `CBlockTreeDB`'s own `fWipe`) and never deletes `blk*.dat`/`rev*.dat` themselves outside prune mode (`CleanupBlockRevFiles()` is prune-only); `ThreadImport`'s `-reindex` branch then re-scans every `blkNNNNN.dat` in file order and re-processes every block it finds through the ordinary `LoadExternalBlockFile` -> `ProcessNewBlock` -> `AcceptBlock`/`ConnectBlock` path, exactly as if freshly received over the network. The whole index and file bookkeeping (`nFile`/`nDataPos`/`nUndoPos`, `vinfoBlockFile`, `nLastBlockFile`) is rebuilt from file 0 forward as a side effect of that reprocessing -- old bytes get overwritten in place with equivalent (often byte-identical) new ones. This is a wipe-the-index-and-rederive-by-reprocessing model, not a scan-the-files-and-reconcile one -- `blk*.dat` records were never self-identifying either, and nothing correlates old bytes to old positions across a reindex. **Decision: the body store does the same, with no new code specific to `-reindex` at all.** `pblocktree`'s existing wipe already covers `CBodyFileInfo`/`nLastBodyFile` and `CDiskBlockIndex`'s `nBodyFile`/`nBodyPos` (same database, same wipe); `bdy*.dat` files are never deleted, matching `blk*.dat`/`rev*.dat`; and as long as 2.1.4's `AcceptBlock` wiring writes a body record unconditionally on every accept -- mirroring `SaveBlockToDisk`'s own unconditional write regardless of whether the accept is reindex-triggered -- reindex naturally rebuilds `bdy*.dat` from file 0 forward for free, the same way it already rebuilds `blk*.dat`'s own bookkeeping. The self-identifying-record alternative F-130 raised would have been a *more robust but different* model than what `blk*.dat` already does; matching existing behaviour means the simpler one. The one real constraint this places on 2.1.4: the write path must not special-case "is this a reindex" -- it has to fire the same way on every accept, or the free rebuild-by-reprocessing stops being free | `source` (direct read of `init.cpp`'s `-reindex` handling before deciding, not assumed) | **decided 2026-09-20 — no code change, guides 2.1.4's write path** |
| F-132 | **F-130's flush-ordering hazard resolved and implemented: body-file info now flows through `CBlockTreeDB::WriteBatchSync`'s own atomic batch, not a separate call** | F-130 recorded the hazard but left the choice open (fold in vs. enforce ordering). Decided (owner, 2026-09-20): fold in, one flush with no ordering to get wrong. `WriteBatchSync` (`txdb.h`/`.cpp`) gained two trailing, defaulted parameters -- `bodyFileInfo` and `nLastBodyFile` (sentinel default `-1`) -- so it now writes `DB_BODY_FILES`/`DB_LAST_BODY_FILE` into the SAME `CDBBatch` as block-file-info/last-file/block-index, guarded by `if (nLastBodyFile >= 0)`. The sentinel exists so the one other call site (`LoadBlockIndexDB`'s pre-1.3 `bodiesmigrated` migration, `validation.cpp`) needs no change at all: it still calls the 3-argument form, the defaults kick in, and the body-file keys are never touched -- confirmed by a new regression test, not just argued, since passing a fabricated `0` instead of a real sentinel would have silently stomped a real, already-persisted last-body-file value on that call site's next real flush. `WriteBodyFileInfoBatch` (the old separate-batch function) is deleted -- it existed only to do what `WriteBatchSync` now does atomically, and keeping both would leave two ways to persist the same state with different atomicity guarantees. In its place, `bodystore.cpp` gained `GetDirtyBodyFileInfo` -- a getter, not a flush: it gathers every dirty file plus the current last-body-file (reported unconditionally, dirty or not, exactly mirroring how `nLastBlockFile` is always passed to `WriteBatchSync`) and clears the dirty set as it goes, on the same "cleared on this attempt regardless of the write's outcome" pattern `validation.cpp` already uses inline for `setDirtyFileInfo`. `FlushStateToDisk`'s real call site (`validation.cpp`, the one place `WriteBatchSync` is actually invoked for a live flush) now calls `GetDirtyBodyFileInfo` right before it and threads the result through -- this is a genuine, live wiring change: every real flush now touches `bodystore.cpp`'s state, not dead code waiting on 2.1.4 the way 2.1.2's load/flush calls still are. Inert today only because nothing populates `vinfoBodyFile`/the dirty set yet (`FindBodyPos` still isn't called from `AcceptBlock`, still 2.1.4's job) -- `LoadBodyFileInfo` itself is unaffected, load-side persistence was never part of this decision. Three tests replaced the old `FlushBodyFileInfo`-based ones (`bodystore_tests.cpp`): `write_batch_sync_persists_body_file_info` (round-trip through the new parameters), `write_batch_sync_without_body_args_touches_no_body_file_keys` (the migration call site's regression guard), `get_dirty_body_file_info_gathers_and_clears`/`get_dirty_body_file_info_is_empty_when_nothing_is_dirty` (gather-and-clear semantics); `find_body_pos_continues_after_a_simulated_restart` updated to exercise the real new path instead of the deleted function. Mutation-tested (3 mutations, all caught): removing the `nLastBodyFile >= 0` guard was caught by the migration-safety regression test (a fabricated 0 became readable where none should exist); skipping the dirty-set clear in `GetDirtyBodyFileInfo` was caught by the gather-and-clear test's second call; dropping the `nLastFileOut` assignment was caught hardest -- 6 failures across two tests, including the restart-persistence test, confirming this specific mutant would have broken real restart recovery, not just an assertion. Full suite green (448 cases via `!miner_tests`), same one pre-existing `miner_tests` failure | `source`, mutation-tested (3 mutations, all caught) | **fixed 2026-09-20** |
| F-133 | **Fable review of F-132 (commit 4d9b048b8) found a real, mutation-proven test blind spot, a latent pointer-lifetime hazard, and two genuine "2.1.4 must also do this" gaps -- all fixed or precisely recorded, none were live bugs** | Reviewed shortly after building, per this project's standing practice. **MEDIUM, real test gap, proven by mutation**: every existing test's first real flush already left file 0 behind (`write_batch_sync_persists_body_file_info` used `nLastBodyFile=1`; the restart test forces a rollover to file 1 first) -- nothing ever drove `WriteBatchSync` with `nLastBodyFile == 0`, so a `nLastBodyFile > 0` mutant (instead of the correct `>= 0`) passed every test silently. In production that mutant would mean a real node persists NO body-file info for its entire first ~128 MiB of bodies -- every flush before the first rollover would silently skip both the `DB_BODY_FILES` and `DB_LAST_BODY_FILE` writes. **Fixed**: new test `write_batch_sync_persists_body_file_info_while_file_zero_is_current` drives the real `GetDirtyBodyFileInfo -> WriteBatchSync` pipeline while file 0 is still current -- independently verified by applying the reviewer's exact `>` mutant, confirming it now fails loudly (`ReadLastBodyFile` critical-check failure), then reverting. **MEDIUM, latent lifetime hazard, not a live bug**: `GetDirtyBodyFileInfo` returned raw pointers into `bodystore.cpp`'s internal `vinfoBodyFile` after releasing its own lock (`cs_LastBodyFile`) -- safe today only because nothing calls `FindBodyPos` concurrently, but structurally different from the block-file-info family it was modeled on: `FlushStateToDisk` holds `cs_LastBlockFile` across its ENTIRE gather-then-`WriteBatchSync` span (confirmed directly, `validation.cpp:2899-3002`), so `vinfoBlockFile` pointers can't be invalidated mid-flush; `GetDirtyBodyFileInfo`'s own lock scope ends when it returns, so an equivalent guarantee for body-file info would depend on every future writer holding `cs_main` -- true of `AcceptBlock` but unenforced (`bodystore.h` carries no lock annotations). **Fixed**: `GetDirtyBodyFileInfo`/`WriteBatchSync`'s `bodyFileInfo` parameter both changed to return/accept `CBodyFileInfo` BY VALUE instead of by pointer -- cheap (one `unsigned int`) and removes the hazard class entirely rather than documenting an invariant every future caller must honour. **LOW, fixed**: added `assert(nLastBodyFile >= 0 || bodyFileInfo.empty())` to `WriteBatchSync` -- a negative sentinel paired with real entries to write would mean the caller has a bug, now caught rather than silently dropped. **LOW, real test-quality gap, fixed**: `write_batch_sync_without_body_args_touches_no_body_file_keys` asserted `ReadBodyFileInfo`/`ReadLastBodyFile` fail after the bare 3-argument call -- true on a fresh database regardless of whether the call did anything right, so it proved nothing. Fixed by seeding a real persisted value first, then asserting the 3-argument call leaves it untouched. **MEDIUM, real gap, RECORDED not fixed (2.1.4's job, not this commit's)**: `FlushStateToDisk` has no body-file equivalent of `FlushBlockFile()` -- nothing flushes the CURRENT body file's data to disk outside of `FindBodyPos`'s own rollover-time finalize (confirmed directly: `FlushBlockFile`, `validation.cpp:2105-2117`, flushes the block/undo file's CURRENT position unconditionally on every full flush; `FindBodyPos`'s only flush call is on rollover, for the file being LEFT). A crash right after F-132's batch goes durable can leave an index entry naming a body position whose bytes never left the page cache -- not consensus-affecting, but the node believes it holds bodies it cannot actually read. Recorded in `bodystore.h`'s file-level comment and `WriteBatchSync`'s own doc (`txdb.h`): 2.1.4 must add a `FlushBodyFile()`-equivalent call (guarded against `vinfoBodyFile` being empty before anything has loaded or written -- a naive unconditional port of the blk pattern would index out of bounds on a fresh, unloaded node) at the same point `FlushBlockFile()` runs. **MEDIUM, real sequencing hazard, RECORDED not fixed**: the write side (`GetDirtyBodyFileInfo`, called from `FlushStateToDisk`) is now genuinely live on every real flush; the load side (`LoadBodyFileInfo`) is still not called anywhere near real startup. This asymmetry is new to this commit and is now load-bearing, not just incomplete: if 2.1.4 wires a real `FindBodyPos` caller into `AcceptBlock` WITHOUT wiring `LoadBodyFileInfo()` into `LoadBlockIndexDB` in the SAME change, the first flush after that change persists a fabricated `nLastBodyFile=0` (the unloaded in-memory default) over whatever was really last written -- reproducing F-130's own overwrite hazard by a different route. Recorded in `bodystore.h` as a MUST, not a should. **LOW, noted, not actioned**: the eager-clear-before-write-succeeds pattern `GetDirtyBodyFileInfo` uses (matching `validation.cpp`'s own existing pattern for `setDirtyFileInfo`/`setDirtyBlockIndex`) is equivalent in severity to the blk case under `AbortNode` (which shuts the node down), with one body-specific asymmetry worth knowing about: a re-dirtied `CBlockIndex` after a failed flush gets a second chance to be caught by the undo-write path re-dirtying its block file, but body files have no such second touch -- requires a failed leveldb write followed by a successful one to matter, the same assumption upstream Bitcoin already makes for blk files, so left as-is. Confirmed the untouched migration call site (`validation.cpp:5379`, the `bodiesmigrated` block) is textually still the 3-argument form -- this diff's only `validation.cpp` hunk is the flush call site. | F-134 | **2.1.4 built: the body store's write path is wired into `AcceptBlock`, `LoadBodyFileInfo` into real startup, and `FlushBodyFile` into the real flush -- Phase 2.1 complete, both F-133 constraints satisfied together as required** | New `static FlatFilePos SaveBodyToDisk(const CBlock &block)` (`validation.cpp`), `SaveBlockToDisk`'s own shape -- extracts `block.vtx[1..]` (bodystore.h's own indexing convention, coinbase never stored), computes the exact size via `GetBodyRecordSerializedSize`, calls `FindBodyPos`/`WriteBodyRecord`. Called UNCONDITIONALLY alongside `SaveBlockToDisk` at both real call sites (`AcceptBlock` and `AddGenesisBlock` -- the compiler caught the second one for free once `ReceivedBlockTransactions`'s signature gained a required `bodyPos` parameter) -- per F-131's decision, the write path must not special-case a withheld (`PerfWithholdBodies`) or reindexed accept, exactly mirroring how `SaveBlockToDisk` itself already writes the full block's real bytes regardless of either. `ReceivedBlockTransactions` (`validation.cpp`/`.h`) now takes `bodyPos` and sets `pindexNew->nBodyFile`/`nBodyPos` unconditionally (mirroring `nFile`/`nDataPos` from `pos`) while `BLOCK_HAVE_BODY_RECORD` is gated on `bodies_held`, exactly the existing `BLOCK_HAVE_BODIES` split -- the real bytes are always on disk before the index entry is even created, only the STATUS CLAIM to hold them is deferred when withheld. `ReceivedBlockBodies` (the "bodies arrived later" path) now also sets `BLOCK_HAVE_BODY_RECORD` alongside `BLOCK_HAVE_BODIES`, using the `nBodyFile`/`nBodyPos` already populated on the original (withheld) accept -- no second write, matching how `nFile`/`nDataPos` are never rewritten there either. **The two F-133 constraints, closed together as required, not sequentially**: `LoadBodyFileInfo()` added to `LoadBlockIndexDB` immediately after the existing block-file-info load (safe on an empty database, bodystore.h's own doc); new `bool FlushBodyFile(bool fFinalize)` (`bodystore.cpp`/`.h`, FindBodyPos's own analogue of `FlushBlockFile`) called from `FlushStateToDisk` right after `FlushBlockFile()` and before the `WriteBatchSync` batch -- guarded against `vinfoBodyFile` being empty (a no-op, not UB) for the window before any load or write has happened. Six tests added: two in `bodystore_tests.cpp` (`FlushBodyFile`'s empty-guard no-op, and a real flush-then-read-back round trip); four in `acceptancebit_tests.cpp` -- `accepted_block_writes_its_bodies_to_the_body_store` (a real spend of a mature coinbase, mined through the full `ProcessNewBlock` path, its body read back byte-correct from the position the index now records); `withheld_bodies_are_written_but_the_record_bit_lags_until_arrival` (mirrors the existing `unrequested_arrival_fills_a_commitment_only_block` test for the new bit -- proves the real bytes land on disk even while withheld, `GetBodyPos()` stays null until arrival exactly as `bodystore_tests.cpp` already proved for the accessor in isolation, and no second write happens on arrival); `body_store_bookkeeping_survives_a_real_loadblockindexdb_reload` (drives the SAME real `LoadBlockIndexDB` call the existing `bodiesmigrated_migration_restores_have_bodies_on_pre_1_3_entries` test uses, proving the write and load sides actually agree after a restart for AcceptBlock-driven data, not just bodystore_tests.cpp's own hand-fed restart proof). **Two real test-design traps found and fixed while building this, both would have let a real bug through**: (1) the reload test's first draft compared `posAfter.nPos` against `TestOnlyGetBodyFileSize()` computed AFTER the same (possibly-broken) reload -- a mutant that dropped the whole `LoadBodyFileInfo()` call made BOTH sides of that comparison collapse to the same wrong zero together, so the assertion passed despite the bug; independently confirmed by running the mutant (3 failures with the fix in place, 0 without it) and fixed by computing the expected size from the block's own content instead, never from post-reload state. (2) directly calling the full `CChainState::FlushStateToDisk` from this test fixture segfaulted during fixture teardown (a `CCheckQueue` worker-thread assertion, unrelated to the body store) -- no existing test in this codebase drives `FlushStateToDisk` directly, so this diff doesn't either; the reload test instead calls `GetDirtyBodyFileInfo`/`WriteBatchSync` directly, the same pattern `bodystore_tests.cpp` already established, which still proves the write/load coupling using the real production functions. **A third, independent bug found by mutation testing FlushBodyFile's own empty-guard**: the first mutation run (removing the `vinfoBodyFile.empty()` check) silently SURVIVED -- `TestOnlyResetBodyFileState()` used `vector::clear()`, which drops elements but keeps capacity, so in the shared-process test binary the "reset" vector still held a real (if logically removed) `CBodyFileInfo` from an earlier test case's buffer, and indexing past `size()` read that stale-but-plausible value instead of faulting. Fixed by changing `TestOnlyResetBodyFileState` to assign a true zero-capacity vector (`vinfoBodyFile = std::vector<CBodyFileInfo>();`) -- re-running the same mutant then reliably faulted (`memory access violation... no mapping at fault address`, a genuine null dereference), proving the guard is load-bearing and the test now actually exercises what it claims to. All mutations independently applied and reverted with clean diffs confirmed against backups (6 total across this step: `LoadBodyFileInfo` call removed, `WriteBodyRecord` call skipped, `BLOCK_HAVE_BODY_RECORD` made unconditional, `ReceivedBlockBodies`'s new bit dropped, `FlushBodyFile`'s guard removed twice -- once before and once after the `TestOnlyResetBodyFileState` fix). **Explicitly NOT in scope, per build-plan.md's own framing of 2.1.4**: nothing reads FROM the body store in production yet (`GetTransaction`, block assembly, the fetch protocol) -- this is a parallel, redundant write path alongside the existing full-block storage in `blk*.dat`, proving the store is populated correctly so a later phase (2.2's fetch protocol or beyond) can cut a real read path over to it; `FlushStateToDisk`'s own wiring (both the new `FlushBodyFile()` call and F-132's `GetDirtyBodyFileInfo` call) has no dedicated unit test driving the full function directly, matching this codebase's existing convention -- no test anywhere drives `FlushStateToDisk` itself, verified by direct code reading instead. Full suite green (454 cases via `!miner_tests`, 5 more than F-133's 449), same one pre-existing `miner_tests` failure | `source`, mutation-tested (6 mutations across the write wiring and both F-133 constraints, all caught after two test-design fixes) | **built 2026-09-21 -- Phase 2.1 (the body store) complete** |
| F-135 | **Fable review of F-134 (commit f02492e62) found two real HIGH bugs that made the just-claimed "write and load sides agree after a restart" false for the per-block index -- both fixed and mutation-tested; plus a MEDIUM error-ordering gap and two LOW gaps, also fixed** | Reviewed shortly after building, per this project's standing practice. **HIGH, real bug**: `CBlockTreeDB::LoadBlockIndexGuts` (`txdb.cpp`) copies `nFile`/`nDataPos`/`nUndoPos`/`nStatus`/`nTx` from `CDiskBlockIndex` back onto the live `CBlockIndex` on every reload, but never copied `nBodyFile`/`nBodyPos` -- a real, correctly SERIALIZED position (`CDiskBlockIndex`'s own conditional serialization, `chain.h`, writes it whenever `BLOCK_HAVE_BODY_RECORD` is set) was silently dropped on every restart regardless, reverting to `CBlockIndex::SetNull()`'s 0/0 default. The reviewer proved this with a real probe (`CreateAndProcessBlock` -> `WriteBatchSync({pindex})` -> `LoadBlockIndexDB`): on-disk `nBodyPos=9453`, reloaded `GetBodyPos()=(0,0)`. **Fixed**: added the two-line copy, unconditional, matching the existing fields' own unconditional-copy style (`CDiskBlockIndex`'s own conditional serialization already leaves the fields at 0/0 when the bit was unset, so nothing here needs its own guard). New test `body_position_on_the_index_survives_a_real_loadblockindexdb_reload` (`acceptancebit_tests.cpp`) persists the real `CBlockIndex` through `WriteBatchSync`, clobbers the in-memory fields to exactly what a fresh restart's newly constructed entry starts as (0,0), reloads via the real `LoadBlockIndexDB`, and confirms the real position comes back -- independently re-verified by reverting the fix and confirming the mutant fails (`0 != 9453`). The EXISTING F-134 reload test (renamed `body_file_bookkeeping_survives_a_real_loadblockindexdb_reload` to stop overclaiming) could not see this bug at all: it never persists the `CBlockIndex` itself and captures the expected position before any reload, so it only ever proved the body-FILE bookkeeping side. **HIGH, real bug, design-level**: `BLOCK_HAVE_BODY_RECORD` was modeled as `BLOCK_HAVE_BODIES`'s sibling (both gated on `bodies_held`) -- but `CDiskBlockIndex` serializes `nBodyFile`/`nBodyPos` ONLY when `BLOCK_HAVE_BODY_RECORD` is set (2.1.3's own design, `chain.h`), so a withheld block's real, already-written position was never durable at all, only held in memory for the life of the process -- independent of the bug above (fixing the loader alone changes nothing here, since there was nothing on disk to load). The reviewer proved this with a second probe: withheld accept, real bytes at `(0,19403)`, on-disk `CDiskBlockIndex` reads `nBodyFile=0 nBodyPos=0`. **Consequence**: withhold -> flush -> restart -> bodies "arrive" -> `ReceivedBlockBodies` durably stamps `BLOCK_HAVE_BODY_RECORD` onto the fresh entry's (0,0) -- genesis's own empty record -- while the real record sits unreferenced. No error is raised, since (0,0) is a legitimate position. **Root cause identified**: `BLOCK_HAVE_BODY_RECORD`'s actual job is `BLOCK_HAVE_DATA`'s job for the body store ("we know where these bytes are", true the instant `SaveBodyToDisk` succeeds) -- NOT `BLOCK_HAVE_BODIES`'s job (the separate, deliberately-withholdable "we claim to hold/serve real content" meaning the perf-harness needs). Modeling it as the latter was the mistake. **Fixed**: `BLOCK_HAVE_BODY_RECORD` is now set unconditionally in `ReceivedBlockTransactions`, right alongside `nBodyFile`/`nBodyPos`, exactly as unconditional as `BLOCK_HAVE_DATA`; `ReceivedBlockBodies`'s now-redundant (and wrong) copy of the same line is removed. The withhold test (`withheld_bodies_are_written_but_the_record_bit_lags_until_arrival`, renamed `body_record_bit_is_unconditional_even_when_bodies_are_withheld`) is rewritten to assert the CORRECTED behaviour -- the position is valid and readable on the withheld accept itself, only `BLOCK_HAVE_BODIES` lags until arrival -- independently re-verified by reverting the fix (the original test's own assertions) and confirming the mutant fails. **This means one of F-134's own 6 "caught" mutations (making `BLOCK_HAVE_BODY_RECORD` unconditional) was mutation-testing a test that enforced a real bug** -- the mutant was closer to correct than the code it was tested against; recorded so the same "the test caught it, so the code must be right" inference isn't repeated uncritically. **MEDIUM, real gap, fixed**: `SaveBodyToDisk` ran AFTER `SaveBlockToDisk` in both `AcceptBlock` and `AddGenesisBlock` -- a body-side failure (e.g. disk exhaustion) then left a real block's bytes permanently in `blk*.dat`, with `vinfoBlockFile`'s own bookkeeping already dirtied and persisted, orphaned forever since the index never got `BLOCK_HAVE_DATA`. **Fixed**: reordered to write the body FIRST at both call sites -- a body-side failure now orphans nothing, since `SaveBlockToDisk` never runs for that attempt. (A related, narrower asymmetry -- `FindBodyPos`'s own `out_of_space` case stays a soft `error()` where `FindBlockPos`'s escalates to `AbortNode` -- was evaluated and left as a recorded, not-yet-decided item: properly distinguishing that one failure cause from `FindBodyPos`'s other, non-fatal ones needs a real signature change, and the reordering above already removes the worse consequence, permanently wasted disk space, on its own.) **LOW, real gap, fixed**: `UnloadBlockIndex` (`validation.cpp`) reset `vinfoBlockFile`/`nLastBlockFile`/`setDirtyFileInfo` but had no body-store counterpart -- a reindex retried within one process (`init.cpp`'s retry loop) kept stale in-memory body-file bookkeeping after `pblocktree` itself was wiped. Fixed by promoting `TestOnlyResetBodyFileState`'s body to a real, non-test-only `ResetBodyFileState()` (bodystore.h/.cpp), called from both `UnloadBlockIndex` and the now-thin test wrapper. **LOW, test-quality, fixed**: `accepted_block_writes_its_bodies_to_the_body_store` never asserted the block actually connected (the body store is written and the bit set during `AcceptBlock`, before `ConnectBlock` runs, so an invalid spend would have passed every other assertion) -- added a tip-hash check. `flush_body_file_flushes_the_current_file_after_a_real_write` never left file 0, so a hardcoded wrong-file mutant inside `FlushBodyFile` could have survived it -- rewritten to roll to file 1 first, matching `find_body_pos_continues_after_a_simulated_restart`'s own reasoning. **Evaluated and declined**: the reviewer's suggestion to add a `CheckBlockIndex` invariant (`BLOCK_HAVE_BODY_RECORD => BLOCK_HAVE_DATA`) turned out NOT to be "free" as characterized -- two existing tests (`genuinely_pruned_arrival_is_still_discarded` and the sibling in the header-race test) simulate pruning by manually clearing `BLOCK_HAVE_DATA`/`BLOCK_HAVE_BODIES`/`BLOCK_HAVE_UNDO` on a live `CBlockIndex` without also clearing `BLOCK_HAVE_BODY_RECORD` (which this review's own fix now makes durably set on every real block) -- adding the assert would trip on the very next `CheckBlockIndex` call inside those tests' own `ProcessNewBlock` calls, asserting an invariant that pruning genuinely does not maintain today, by design (F-130's still-open, not-yet-decided pruning question, unrelated to this review). Locking discipline, exhaustive call-site enumeration, genesis correctness, and flush ordering were all independently checked and confirmed correct as claimed -- no changes needed. Full suite green (455 cases via `!miner_tests`, 1 more than F-134's 454 from the new index-position reload test), same one pre-existing `miner_tests` failure | Fable adversarial review, 2026-09-21, all findings independently verified against source and both HIGH fixes mutation-tested (reverted, confirmed the mutant fails, re-fixed) | **fixed 2026-09-21** |
| F-136 | **3.1 built: DIP8's signing-attempts process -- ChainLocks can now retry a stuck height instead of failing permanently after one attempt, closing the quorum-split attack transaction-decoupling.md §3A.7 makes attacker-schedulable** | `TrySignChainTip`'s original `pindex->nHeight == lastSignedHeight` guard (`llmq/quorums_chainlocks.cpp`) permanently blocked EVERY future signing attempt at a height once ANY attempt was made there, whether or not it ever produced a recovered signature -- the exact defect the file's own comment already named ("DIP8 defines a process called Signing attempts... we skip this... this will fail when multiple blocks compete") but never fixed. **Researched, not assumed, before designing**: DIP-0008's real spec text (`dashpay/dips`) calls for an incrementing `attemptNum` folded into the request id (`SHA256("clsig-attempt", height, attemptNum)`), converging via a `GetMostSignedSession` query neither this codebase nor Dash Core's OWN current `develop` branch has ever implemented -- confirmed directly by fetching Dash's real `TrySignChainTip`-equivalent source, which carries the identical unfixed skip-comment today. **Decided (owner, 2026-09-21)**: build a minimal fix -- retry with a fresh, TIME-SLOTTED request id (not a per-node attempt counter, which would desync nodes onto different ids for the same real moment and never combine into a threshold signature) rather than the full DIP's cross-candidate convergence machinery. **A hard constraint found during design, not optional**: `ProcessNewChainLock`'s verification recomputes a request id from height ALONE (`CLSIG_REQUESTID_PREFIX`, no attempt number), and `CChainLockSig`'s wire format has no room for one -- so an attempt's own recovered signature could never be verified by a peer if broadcast directly as the CLSIG. Confirmed this is exactly why DIP-8 specifies a real TWO-round structure (attempts converge internally under `CLSIG_ATTEMPT_REQUESTID_PREFIX`; once one succeeds, a second, fixed-id round under the existing `CLSIG_REQUESTID_PREFIX` produces the actual broadcastable CLSIG) -- decided (owner) to build both rounds, not skip the second. Implementation: `GetChainLockAttemptNumber(nAdjustedTime)` divides `GetAdjustedTime()` (already used elsewhere in this file for cross-node timing) into 30-second slots, so every quorum member computes the same attempt number at roughly the same real time regardless of when their own process started retrying; `GetChainLockAttemptRequestId` hashes `("clsig-attempt", height, attemptNum)`. New `CChainLocksHandler` state (`lastSignedAttempt`, `lastSignedIsFinalization`) tracks which round is outstanding. The actual retry/finalization DECISION logic is extracted as four pure, `static inline` free functions directly in `quorums_chainlocks.h` (`DecideChainLockSignAction`, `DecideRecoveredSigOutcome`, plus the two above) -- `ShouldNegotiateCommitments`'s own pattern (`protocol.h`) -- specifically so the actual risk (the retry/one-shot-finalization state machine) is unit-testable without a live smartnode, BLS quorum or signed recovered signature, which this tree's own precedent (`acceptancebit_tests.cpp`'s `a_commitment_only_block_still_heals_after_its_sibling_is_marked_conflicting` comment) already declined as disproportionate scaffolding. A narrow race found and closed while designing: if the 30-second slot rolls over WHILE a finalization round is still outstanding, a naive re-check of just (height, attemptNum) would restart a fresh attempt and abandon the in-flight finalization, silently losing a CLSIG that might have converged moments later (`HandleNewRecoveredSig` only recognises a recovered signature matching the CURRENT `lastSignedRequestId`) -- `DecideChainLockSignAction` explicitly checks `lastSignedIsFinalization` first and refuses to restart regardless of attempt-slot rollover. New `test/chainlocks_tests.cpp` (17 tests, registered in `Makefile.test.include`): time-slot arithmetic pinned at the interval boundary; the two request-id prefixes proven to never collide (the entire reason two prefixes exist); and exhaustive coverage of both decision functions' branches -- including `starts_a_new_attempt_when_the_slot_advances_at_the_same_height`, which is the literal fix (the original code would have returned "do nothing" here forever) and `never_restarts_an_attempt_while_finalization_is_outstanding`, which is the race guard above. Mutation-tested (4 mutations: the finalization guard removed, the attempt-number check removed entirely -- reproducing the ORIGINAL bug exactly and caught by the fix's own core test, `attemptNum` dropped from the request-id hash, and the two `RecoveredSigOutcome` branches swapped), all caught, each reverted with a clean diff confirmed against a backup. Full suite green (472 cases via `!miner_tests`, 17 more than F-135's 455), same one pre-existing `miner_tests` failure. **Not a consensus change**: `CChainLockSig`'s wire format, `ProcessNewChainLock`'s verification, and every other consumer of `HasChainLock`/`GetBestChainLock` are untouched -- this is node-local signing/retry behaviour, the same category as the existing `Cleanup()`/scheduler mechanism it extends, not a hard-fork rule, so it needs no `g_commitmentBudgetActive`-style activation gate. **Still open, deliberately out of scope**: no explicit timeout on a stuck finalization round -- if one never converges, the height simply falls back to PoW-only finality once the tip advances past it, identical in severity to today's baseline (bounded, self-resolving, matching §3A.7's own "severity is bounded" framing); a full DIP-8 implementation (attemptNum + `GetMostSignedSession` + `IsMajorityPossible`) remains unbuilt anywhere, including Dash mainnet, and was explicitly declined in favour of this minimal fix | `source` (DIP-0008 spec text + Dash Core's real current implementation, both fetched and read directly, not assumed), mutation-tested (4 mutations, all caught) | **built 2026-09-21** |
| F-137 | **Fable review of F-136 (commit 9a8d36e54) found a real MEDIUM regression in the honest, no-attacker case -- the fix could get WORSE than the original bug it replaced under ordinary timing -- plus a real MEDIUM test-coverage gap and several LOW hardenings; all fixed except one recorded, one deferred** | Reviewed with unusual rigor: the reviewer built `raptoreumd` at the reviewed commit, ran the actual `test/functional/feature_llmq_chainlocks.py` end-to-end (79s, passes -- not cited in F-136's own commit message, should have been), and classified every request id in the resulting node logs by independently recomputing the finalization id formula. **MEDIUM, real regression, fixed**: BLS threshold aggregation and network gossip take real, non-negligible time (not microseconds) -- if an attempt's recovered signature takes long enough to propagate back to a node that its own 5-second scheduler tick already rolled local state into the NEXT 30-second attempt slot, `DecideRecoveredSigOutcome`'s exact `recoveredId != lastSignedRequestId` check discards that node's OWN successful attempt as "stale". If enough nodes race ahead the same way (the reviewer's timing model: roughly when the recovered signature lands 2-3 seconds after a slot boundary), too few nodes are left still watching the old attempt's id to start finalization, AND the new attempt slot they raced ahead to never collects votes from the nodes that stayed behind -- height H gets stuck until H+1 makes everyone move on. This is a regression introduced BY the fix, in the ordinary no-attacker case, that the original one-shot code never had (it had no slot to fall out of) -- confirmed by the reviewer's own node-log analysis (0 of 103 tried heights needed a genuine retry in their run, but 7 attempt sessions timed out unconverged, consistent with the mechanism). **Fixed**: `DecideRecoveredSigOutcome` gained a `nLastSignedAttempt` parameter and now also accepts a recovered signature matching any of the last `CLSIG_ATTEMPT_LOOKBACK` (2) attempt slots at the same height -- not just the exact current one -- as long as the msgHash still matches (so nothing wrong can ever be finalized; only WHICH attempt's signature is recognised widens, never WHAT it's a signature over). The widening applies only while still attempting, never while already finalizing (finalization's id is fixed and unambiguous, no slot concept applies to it). Two slots (60s) is sized comfortably above normal aggregation+gossip latency while staying well short of where the msgHash itself would plausibly change underneath it. Verified by mutation: reverting the lookback loop entirely made the new regression test fail exactly as expected, confirming the fix is load-bearing. **MEDIUM, real test-coverage gap, fixed**: the ORIGINAL 17 tests could not see a hoisted-finalization-guard mutant (moving `if (fLastSignedIsFinalization) return kNone;` above the height-equality check, so a node that ever finalizes never signs any LATER height either) -- the reviewer ran this exact mutant and it survived all 17. The production code was already correct on this point (the guard is correctly nested inside the height-equality check), but nothing proved it. **Fixed**: new test `starts_a_new_attempt_at_a_higher_tip_while_finalization_is_outstanding` -- independently re-verified by reproducing the reviewer's exact mutant, confirming it now fails, then reverting. **LOW, fixed**: no re-check of `InternalHasConflictingChainLock` before starting the finalization round in `HandleNewRecoveredSig` -- `TrySignChainTip` checks this before an attempt, but a conflicting CLSIG could arrive at a LOWER height in the seconds between an attempt starting and converging, and `DecideRecoveredSigOutcome`'s own `nBestChainLockHeight` check only catches a CLSIG at or above `lastSignedHeight`, not a conflicting one below it. Fixed with a direct re-check under the same lock, immediately before committing to finalization. **LOW, fixed**: `AsyncSignIfMember`'s return value was ignored on the finalization call -- if the signing manager refuses (e.g. this node isn't actually a member for this id, or it already voted conflictingly), the node had already committed `lastSignedIsFinalization=true` and would sit frozen contributing nothing, silently, until the tip advances past this height. Fixed with a log line at the existing `BCLog::CHAINLOCKS` category; the underlying exposure itself is left as-is (same bounded/self-resolving severity class as a finalization that simply never converges for any other reason). **LOW, doc fix**: the original comment overclaimed `GetAdjustedTime()` as "network-synchronized" -- it is each node's own local clock offset by the median of peer-claimed time (`timedata.cpp`, capped ±70 minutes), not an NTP-grade synchronized clock, so ordinary per-node skew of a few seconds is ordinary, expected behaviour the 30-second interval and the new lookback are BOTH sized to tolerate, not something the design assumes away. Corrected in place, with a note that every attempt's own recovered signature is now also relayed and stored for `DEFAULT_MAX_RECOVERED_SIGS_AGE` (7 days) network-wide, roughly doubling ChainLock-related recovered-sig volume compared to the original one-shot design -- accepted as the direct, necessary cost of retrying at all. **LOW, test-quality fix**: `attempt_request_id_never_collides_with_the_finalization_request_id` only proved SHA256 of two specific distinct byte strings differs, not that no collision could ever exist in principle -- comment corrected to state what it actually shows (a concrete regression check on today's constants) versus what actually guarantees no collision (the two prefixes' own distinct, length-prefixed serialization). **LOW, recorded, not fixed**: the finalization round's `AsyncSignIfMember` call (like the pre-existing, unmodified attempt-round call) doesn't pass an explicit `quorumHash`, so the signing manager selects a quorum based on the CURRENT chain tip at call time -- for the original code this gap was microseconds; for the finalization round, which now starts asynchronously, seconds after the attempt that triggered it, the window in which the tip could have moved (and so a different quorum could be selected than what `ProcessNewChainLock`'s verification will later pick, since it selects based on `clsig.getHeight()`) is now meaningfully wider. Rare (reviewer's estimate: roughly 1-in-360 heights times the probability the tip moved during that window) and fails safe (a lost CLSIG at that height, never a wrongly-accepted one) -- the correct fix (`SelectQuorumForSigning` explicitly at `lastSignedHeight`, passing its hash into `AsyncSignIfMember`) is understood but deferred, since it touches quorum-selection code this review didn't otherwise need to modify and the risk is bounded. **MEDIUM, recommended, deferred**: a Python functional-test scenario forcing the LITERAL second-attempt-at-the-same-height path against a real 3-of-3 `-llmqtestparams` quorum (isolate one node, mine a competing block, `bump_mocktime` past a slot boundary, reconnect, confirm the height still ChainLocks) -- the reviewer's own node-log analysis found the literal retry path has never executed outside the pure-function unit tests, in either their test run or (necessarily) in production. This is real, valuable, additional coverage the existing `feature_llmq_chainlocks.py` run (confirmed passing, no regression) does not provide on its own. Deferred rather than built in this pass: it requires the Python functional-test framework and multi-node orchestration (`isolate_node`/`reconnect_isolated_node`/mocktime helpers), a genuinely different toolchain from every other test added in this whole project so far, and the reviewer's own estimate (1-2h) reflects nontrivial new scaffolding, not a quick addition -- recorded here precisely so it isn't lost. Full suite green (476 cases via `!miner_tests`, 4 more than F-136's 472), same one pre-existing `miner_tests` failure | Fable adversarial review, 2026-09-21, unusually thorough (built and ran the real daemon against the real functional test suite, analyzed real node logs, ran the reviewer's own mutants against the reviewed commit before reporting) -- every MEDIUM finding independently re-verified by reproducing the reviewer's own mutants after fixing, both now caught | **fixed 2026-09-21, one item recorded not fixed, one functional-test recommendation deferred** |
| F-138 | **2.3 built: the relay trickle cap is re-indexed off block size entirely, to F-5's own measured 50,000** | `InvBroadcastMax()` (`net_processing.cpp`) computed `INVENTORY_BROADCAST_MAX_PER_1MB_BLOCK * MaxBlockSize() / 1e6` -- a formula that assumed block bytes track how much has to propagate, true before decoupling and false after: a commitment block's own bytes are tiny and roughly constant, unrelated to the actual body volume that needs to relay. Worse than merely stale: the coupling to `MaxBlockSize()` meant this cap would silently jump ~14x the day `g_commitmentBudgetActive` flips true (`COMMITMENT_BUDGET_BODY_BYTES` = 110 MB vs `MAX_DIP0001_BLOCK_SIZE` = 8 MB, `consensus/consensus.h`), a side effect of an unrelated activation flag, not a throughput decision anyone made. Confirmed directly: with `g_commitmentBudgetActive` off (its state everywhere outside test scenarios today), the formula evaluates to `140 * 8,000,000 / 1,000,000 = 1,120` -- consistent with F-4's own measured 50-65 tx/s per peer delivered under the shipped cap. **Fixed**: replaced with a flat `INVENTORY_BROADCAST_MAX = 50,000`, F-5's own directly-measured value -- the smallest cap that converged the full 1,500 tx/s design-point offered rate to both 8 and 16 peers in the `loopback` swarm (10,000 sufficed at the smaller v1 point; 7,500 was schedule-, not throughput-, bound). The now-dead `INVENTORY_BROADCAST_MAX_PER_1MB_BLOCK` constant is removed entirely (no other call site referenced it) rather than left alongside the new one. The `-perfinvmax`/`-perfinvinterval`/`-perfinvnosort` test-only override machinery is untouched -- orthogonal to this fix, already separates burst size from interval for measurement purposes. **Necessary, not sufficient, and documented as such in the constant's own comment**: perf-results.md's 2026-09-17 entry already found message-handling cost itself ceilings relay at ~900 tx/s regardless of this constant once it's no longer the binding limit -- reaching the full 1,500 tx/s design point needs the relay path itself to get cheaper or concurrent, real work tracked as its own, separate, not-yet-scheduled item, not something this constant change claims to solve. **No dedicated new test**: `InvBroadcastMax()`/`INVENTORY_BROADCAST_MAX` are `static`, file-local to `net_processing.cpp`, not exposed via any header, and the change itself is a single constant substitution with no branching logic to mutate -- verified by full-suite regression (476 cases unchanged, confirming nothing else depended on the old formula's specific behaviour) and direct inspection rather than a dedicated unit test, proportionate to a change this codebase's own build-plan.md already rated "straightforward, 1 day" | `source`, cross-checked against F-4/F-5's own measured values | **built 2026-09-21** |
| F-139 | **2.2's implementation spec (drafted, not yet built) reviewed BEFORE becoming a plan, per this project's own standing rule -- found one CRITICAL false "confirmed" claim that would have reproduced B4 by copying it, plus a large missing deliverable and several mis-scoped items; spec revised, none of it built yet** | Following the discipline used for every prior significant change (Fable review of a spec before it becomes a plan, of fixes before they ship): before writing any 2.2 code, a phased spec was drafted and adversarially reviewed against the actual current source and `transaction-decoupling.md`. **CRITICAL, false claim caught before it became code**: the draft's serving-path phase (2.2.3) said the new body-serving handler could copy `ProcessGetBlockData`'s "lock-then-release-before-disk-read" architecture, citing it as confirmed against current source -- and the design doc's own §13.F said the same thing ("Serving a block does not hold `cs_main` across a disk read"). Both were wrong. Verified directly (by the reviewer, then independently re-verified by reading `net_processing.cpp:1688-1848` myself): `ProcessGetBlockData` re-acquires `cs_main` at `:1720` with no closing scope before the function ends at `:1848`, holding it straight through `ReadBlockFromDisk` (`:1780`) and every response `PushMessage`. There is no off-`cs_main` disk-serving precedent anywhere in this codebase. Had this shipped as written, the new body-serving code -- built specifically to close B4 (seek-amplified `cs_main`-pinning DoS, §14.3) -- would have reproduced B4 by copying a pattern that doesn't actually avoid it, under a spec and a design doc that both called it verified. **Fixed**: the false claim removed from both the spec and `transaction-decoupling.md`'s own §13.F (corrected in place, with the real current behaviour and line numbers); 2.2.3 now specifies the real requirement directly (resolve through the new body-store index under a dedicated lock, release before any I/O, no `cs_main` anywhere in the serving path) as original design work, not a port. **HIGH, real missing deliverable, added**: the spec's first draft never mentioned that a body-ARRIVAL write path doesn't exist today -- `ReceivedBlockBodies` only flips a status bit, and `SaveBodyToDisk` has only ever been exercised against a block whose full bytes already existed locally (the `-perfwithholdbodies` harness simulates withholding over an already-complete block, never a genuine fetch-from-nothing). Nothing today takes bytes fetched over the wire and turns them into a stored, indexed, connectable block. Added as its own explicit 2.2.4 deliverable: `MaterialiseBlock`, run the body-dependent `CheckBlock`/`ContextualCheckBlock` rows a commitment-only accept couldn't, `WriteBodyRecord`, set the position and status bits, update both new indexes -- under the SAME crash-ordering discipline F-133/F-135 already established for the existing accept path, applied to a second call site rather than invented fresh. **HIGH, real gap, fixed**: the draft made NOTFOUND uniformly non-punitive; `§14.1` rule 2, `§8.4` decision 2 and `§14.8a` all require a regime split (free for an honest history-range miss; misbehaviour if the block's own ANNOUNCER answers NOTFOUND to a tip-path request, since it already took on today's delivery obligation) -- without the split, "one peer announces every block, delivers none, forever" (§8.4's own named failure) comes back, and B1's own fix explicitly needs this rule too (§14.1: "B1 needs rule 2 as well"). Fixed by specifying the split directly in the wire-format phase (2.2.2), plus a response-must-echo-the-served-block-hash requirement so an honest reorg race (server answers correctly for a branch the requester has since abandoned) is distinguishable from real misbehaviour (hash matches, body doesn't). **HIGH, corrected framing, several items**: much of what the draft scoped as new work already shipped in earlier phases -- A1's network-side fix (1.3.1, F-101/F-104/F-117, confirmed directly: `ProcessGetBlockData` already gates on `HaveBodies(pindex)` before any read, with a code comment documenting the exact crash this closed) and B3's per-block exponential backoff (`g_body_retry_state`, already live) both need only their real remaining gaps closed (a NOTFOUND specifically for block requests and `NODE_NETWORK` non-advertisement for A1, both already build-plan 4.1's text; an aggregate concurrent-chase cap and re-keying the existing retry loop to body-ranges for B3), not new builds from scratch. The draft's own 2.2.5 phase (relay-side Rule 1, `EnforceBestChainLock`'s stall/crash, the fail-open safety walk) was found to substantially duplicate build-plan.md's own existing item 4.1, which already scopes all of it with its own estimate and gate on 4.6 -- **dropped as a separate phase** (owner confirmed) rather than forked across two rows; Rule 1 itself is largely already satisfied by construction (`NewPoWValidBlock` already gated on `HaveBodies`, F-117) so 4.1 should start as an audit, not new relay code. Lock-ordering claim corrected: the real established order is `cs_main -> cs_LastBlockFile -> cs_LastBodyFile` (F-134/F-135), not the chain the draft assumed -- the new index needs its own dedicated, leaf-most lock, never shared with `cs_LastBodyFile` (which holds across an `fsync` on rollover) and never held across the serving handler's own I/O. Height-index design corrected: the hash-keyed half must be populated at record-write time (accept or arrival), never at connect/disconnect, since side-chain blocks are never connected at all; only the height-keyed half is connect/disconnect-maintained, for the active chain only. **MEDIUM, completeness gaps closed**: several named findings (A3, B6, B7, B8, B9, C2, C4-ii, C4-iii, C9) were either missing from both the "in scope" and "deferred" lists, or mis-categorized (C2 is already closed by 3.1/F-136-137, not a 2.2 concern at all; C4-iii is scheduled work in 2.2.4, not deferred; C4-ii/C9 are snapshot/state-root items, not fetch-protocol scheduling) -- all now correctly placed. **Three narrower decisions confirmed by owner** rather than left for implementation to guess: drop 2.2.5 into 4.1 (above); reuse `NODE_COMMITMENTS`/`fProvidesCommitments` as the body-fetch capability bit rather than adding a second one; rate-limit budgets/chunk size/concurrent-chase cap ship config-driven with conservative defaults, measured and tuned later via the same `loopback`/swarm methodology already used for F-4/F-5, rather than guessed now. **Nothing has been built yet** -- this is a spec-review cycle only; the phased plan (2.2.1 body-store index, 2.2.2 wire format, 2.2.3 serving, 2.2.4 fetching+arrival) is now ready to implement | Fable adversarial review of a pre-implementation spec (not code), 2026-09-21 -- the critical finding independently re-verified against `net_processing.cpp` directly before accepting it | **spec revised and accepted 2026-09-21, implementation not started** |
| F-140 | **2.2.1 built: the body-store-owned height+hash index, entirely decoupled from `cs_main`/`CBlockIndex`, per F-139's accepted design** | New `bodystore.h`/`.cpp` API -- `RecordBodyPositionByHash`/`LookupBodyPositionByHash`, `RecordBodyPositionAtHeight`/`EraseBodyPositionAtHeight`/`LookupBodyPositionAtHeight`, `ResetBodyIndex` -- backed by two maps (`mapBodyPosByHash` keyed on `uint256` via `StaticSaltedHasher`, matching `quorums_chainlocks.h`'s own `BlockTxs` pattern; `mapBodyPosByHeight` for the active chain only) guarded by a single new `cs_bodyIndex`, deliberately independent of `cs_LastBodyFile`/`cs_LastBlockFile` and never held across I/O by any caller. Wired into four `validation.cpp` call sites exactly as F-139 specified: the hash half populates inside `ReceivedBlockTransactions` (record-write time, covers `AcceptBlock` and `AddGenesisBlock`, works whether or not the block is ever connected -- a side-chain block reaches this line but may never connect at all); the height half populates in `ConnectTip` (right after `m_chain.SetTip`, overwriting any stale entry a prior branch left at that height) and is erased outright (never redirected) in `DisconnectTip`; and `CChainState::LoadChainTip` rebuilds BOTH halves from scratch on every boot -- iterating the full `BlockIndex()` map for the hash half and `m_chain[0..Height()]` for the height half, reading each entry's own already-persisted `GetBodyPos()` (null until `BLOCK_HAVE_BODY_RECORD` is set, the same convention `GetBlockPos()`/`GetUndoPos()` already use) -- no new on-disk format, matching how `vinfoBlockFile`/`setBlockIndexCandidates` are already rebuilt the same way. `bodystore.h`/`.cpp` gained no new dependency on `CBlockIndex`/`CChain`/`BlockMap` -- the rebuild loop lives entirely in `validation.cpp`, calling only the chain-agnostic six functions above, keeping the body store's existing "knows nothing about `CBlockIndex`" design intact. TDD throughout: 9 new `bodystore_tests.cpp` cases against the bare index API written and watched fail (`undefined reference`) before any implementation existed; 3 new `acceptancebit_tests.cpp` integration cases proving the real wiring (accept+connect populates both halves with the correct position; `DisconnectTip` erases only the height half, the hash half survives since the block's bytes are still on disk regardless of chain membership; a genuine `LoadChainTip` reload -- forcing `m_chain.SetTip(nullptr)` first, not merely calling it again with an already-matching tip, which would hit the early return and prove nothing, exactly the "early-return skip logic" gap F-135 already warned about for a different reload path -- rebuilds both halves correctly across the whole chain including genesis). Mutation-tested: 5 mutants against the bare index logic (inverted `LookupBodyPositionByHash` condition, no-op `RecordBodyPositionByHash`, non-overwriting `RecordBodyPositionAtHeight`, no-op `EraseBodyPositionAtHeight`, `ResetBodyIndex` clearing only one map) and 6 mutants against the four `validation.cpp` call sites and both `LoadChainTip` rebuild loops independently -- all 11 died. Full suite: 488 cases (`!miner_tests`) clean; `miner_tests` shows only the one known pre-existing `CreateNewBlock_validity` failure, unchanged | mutation-tested (11/11 mutants killed) and integration-tested against real `AcceptBlock`/`ConnectTip`/`DisconnectTip`/`LoadChainTip` calls, not hand-fed state | **built 2026-09-21, reviewed and fixed 2026-09-21 (F-141)** |
| F-141 | **Fable review of F-140 (commit 2524d9121) found the index itself correct but its boot/rebuild wiring genuinely broken on two real paths -- 1 HIGH, 3 MEDIUM, 2 LOW, all fixed** | The reviewer confirmed the core design sound (lock discipline verified directly: `cs_bodyIndex` never nested with `cs_LastBodyFile`/`cs_LastBlockFile`, never held across I/O; the normal reorg path keeps the height half correct at every step) and focused on the boot/rebuild story, which every claim in F-140 rested on. **HIGH, independently re-verified against `init.cpp` before accepting**: F-140's "rebuilt every boot" claim was only true on the ordinary boot path. `init.cpp`'s own `is_coinsview_empty` lambda (`fReset \|\| fReindexChainState \|\| ...`) gates the `LoadChainTip` call -- `-reindex-chainstate` sets `fReindexChainState`, a genuinely distinct flag from `-reindex`'s `fReindex` (confirmed: three separate call sites reference it), so `LoadChainTip`'s rebuild (the ONLY place the hash half was rebuilt) never runs on that path. `ThreadImport`'s own `if (fReindex)` gate confirms `-reindex-chainstate` alone never re-streams `blk*.dat` through `AcceptBlock` either, so nothing else repopulates the hash half -- every existing block reconnects via `ConnectTip` (height half only), leaving the hash half permanently empty for the process's life. A second, related gap: the free `UnloadBlockIndex(CTxMemPool*)` -- called by both `init.cpp`'s retry loop AND every `TestingSetup`'s own teardown (confirmed: `test_raptoreum.cpp:176`) -- reset `vinfoBlockFile`/`ResetBodyFileState()` but never this index, so an in-process reindex retry inherited stale entries pointing at bytes about to be overwritten from file 0, and every test case in the binary silently inherited the previous case's entries. **Fixed**: `ResetBodyIndex()` added to `UnloadBlockIndex`; the hash-half rebuild moved from `LoadChainTip` into `LoadBlockIndexDB` (which runs on every load path except a full `-reindex`, where the index starts empty and repopulates naturally) -- `LoadChainTip` keeps only the height-half rebuild, the one that genuinely needs `m_chain`. **MEDIUM, independently re-verified against `chain.h`'s own migration note**: `ConnectTip` recorded `pindexNew->GetBodyPos()` unconditionally, but `chain.h` states outright that "no pre-2.1.4 entry has ever had a real body-store record written" -- a `-reindex-chainstate` replay of a pre-2.1.4-synced datadir reaches `ConnectTip`'s `!pblock` branch (which only requires the OLDER, already-migrated `BLOCK_HAVE_BODIES` bit, not `BLOCK_HAVE_BODY_RECORD`) for such an entry, writing a poisoned `(nFile=-1)` position that `LookupBodyPositionAtHeight` then reported as a false "found". **Fixed**: guarded exactly like `LoadChainTip`'s own rebuild loop, `IsNull()` skips the write. **MEDIUM, test-coverage gap**: every existing integration test used a strictly linear chain, so `BlockIndex()` (every known block) and `m_chain[0..Height()]` (active chain only) always named the same set -- a loop-source-swap mutant would have passed all of them. **Fixed** with a new test building a genuine equal-work sibling pair (one connects, one is genuinely accepted -- body written -- but never connects), proving the hash half resolves the loser and the height half never does, through a real `LoadBlockIndexDB`+`LoadChainTip` reload. (F-142 below found this construction itself imperfect and corrected it.) **MEDIUM, scoped as an open decision for 2.2.3, not fixed here**: the index answers "where are the bytes", not "may I serve them" -- a `-perfwithholdbodies` block has `BLOCK_HAVE_BODY_RECORD` set (hash-resolvable) but `BLOCK_HAVE_BODIES` clear (the existing serving path's own `HaveBodies()` gate refuses it); a 2.2.3 handler resolving purely through this index would need either its own serveability flag or to tolerate "found in index, refused at serve" in 2.2.2's NOTFOUND semantics -- 2.2.2 must decide this explicitly, not inherit it silently. **LOW, fixed**: `DisconnectTip` erased the height entry AFTER `m_chain.SetTip`, leaving a `cs_main`-free reader a brief window to see the disconnected block's stale-but-real position still resolve at that height -- swapped to erase-before-`SetTip`, matching `ConnectTip`'s own order and making the height map a strict subset of `m_chain` at every observable point, matching the header comment's actual promise. Not caught by any test (a sub-instruction race window under a single-threaded harness) -- fixed by inspection/design reasoning alone, the mutant that reverts it survives the full suite, consistent with this project's own precedent for LOW-severity ordering fixes. **LOW, fixed as a byproduct of the HIGH fix**: the test-fixture leak (`UnloadBlockIndex`'s missing reset) is the same root cause; the memory-growth LOW (the hash map is never trimmed, ~100 MB order-of-magnitude at mainnet block counts, estimated not measured) is accepted per F-139's own design decision, recorded here rather than fixed. 5 new regression tests added (`acceptancebit_tests.cpp`), all confirmed RED against the pre-fix code before the fixes landed; the existing `body_index_survives_a_real_loadchaintip_reload` test updated to call `LoadBlockIndexDB` too, since the hash-half rebuild moved out of `LoadChainTip` alone.
