| F-158 | **An independent, different-model review of F-155/F-156/F-157 (commits 80507d58a, 74a1e946e, ff1f166ae) -- built and tested both before/after, not just read the diff -- found one CONFIRMED HIGH (cross-peer ban misattribution), one CONFIRMED MEDIUM real regression verified by A/B functional test (the whole-block GETDATA fallback silently removed for the DEFAULT `-fetchbodyrange`-off case), one MEDIUM (empty blocks never completed), and two LOW items -- all fixed, the regression's own functional test now wired into `test_runner.py` so this class can't slip through silently again** | **HIGH, CONFIRMED, fixed**: `mapBodyRangePartial` accumulates BODYRANGE response chunks keyed only by block hash, with NO per-chunk peer attribution -- content was validated only ONCE, at completion, via `ProcessFetchedBodyRange`/`MaterialiseBlock` over the WHOLE combined buffer. A malicious peer could answer an early chunk with bad data then disconnect (its contribution deliberately KEPT across peers, per F-155's own "already shape-validated ... still good" comment -- which conflated wire-SHAPE validation, `ValidateBodyRangeResponse`, with CONTENT validation, never done per-chunk); an honest peer later completes the range; `MaterialiseBlock` fails on the combined data; the completion-time `Misbehaving(pfrom, 100, ...)` bans the HONEST completing peer for the departed attacker's chunk -- repeatable against every honest peer that happens to deliver the completing chunk. **Fixed**: new `bool ValidateBodyRangeChunkHashes(const CCommitmentBlock &commitments, uint32_t nStartIndex, const std::vector<CTransactionRef> &chunkBodies)` (`bodyrange.h`/`.cpp`) -- the per-CHUNK analogue of `MaterialiseBlock`'s own per-tx hash check, run the moment a chunk arrives (against the correct `nStartIndex..nStartIndex+chunkBodies.size()` slice), BEFORE it ever joins the cross-peer buffer. A failure is now unambiguously the CURRENTLY-ANSWERING peer's own fault (checked independent of whatever a different peer may have contributed earlier or later) -- `mapBodyRangePartial` is deliberately left untouched on a bad chunk (the already-validated prefix, if any, is still good; the next `SendMessages` pass re-requests exactly the still-missing range). `ReadCommitmentBlockFromDisk` moved from completion-only to every non-empty chunk arrival (one extra disk read per chunk instead of per full fetch -- accepted given the aggregate cap keeps concurrency small). Since every chunk is now pre-validated on arrival, `MaterialiseBlock`'s own equivalent check at completion is provably unreachable in the normal case -- kept as defense-in-depth, matching F-157's own precedent for a check the current call pattern can no longer trip. 7 new tests (`bodyrange_tests.cpp`), including a test built around the EXACT attack shape (an earlier honest chunk at index 0, a bad completing chunk at index 1, checked independent of the first). **Mutation-tested, 4 mutations**: hash-equality inverted -- 3 tests fail, killed. Offset dropped (`vCommitments[i]` instead of `[nStartIndex+i]`) -- 1 test fails, killed. Bounds check disabled entirely -- SURVIVED against the original over-run test (a real gap, same class F-134 already found once in this codebase: the per-element loop's own `operator[]` read past the vector's real backing storage, undefined behaviour that happened to compare unequal rather than crash or match, so the test passed for the wrong reason); fixed by adding a DETERMINISTIC test (an out-of-range `nStartIndex` with an EMPTY chunk -- zero loop iterations, no UB of any kind possible either way), re-ran, killed. Null-entry check inverted (`&&` for `||`) -- 1 test fails, killed. **MEDIUM, CONFIRMED REAL REGRESSION, fixed**: `FindNextBlocksToDownload`'s `BLOCK_HAVE_DATA` branch (F-155) UNCONDITIONALLY routed a commitment-known/body-missing block to the new `vBodyBlocks`/GETBODYRANGE path, REMOVING 1.3.6/H-2's own already-proven-live whole-block GETDATA re-request fallback -- regardless of whether `-fetchbodyrange` was even enabled. With the flag OFF (the DEFAULT), such a block became permanently unrecoverable by ANY mechanism. Verified directly by the reviewer via a real A/B build+test (`feature_body_refetch.py` passes at parent commit `ee9f74583`, hangs -- stuck at height 200, only the initial 2 getdatas, no re-request -- at `ff1f166ae`), independently re-confirmed here on a genuine clean rebuild. **Fixed**: `FindNextBlocksToDownload` gained a `bool fFetchBodyRangeCapable` parameter, computed at the `SendMessages` call site as `-fetchbodyrange` enabled AND this SPECIFIC peer supports `NODE_COMMITMENTS` (`CanReceiveCommitments(pto->nServices)`) -- covering BOTH halves of the reviewer's own framing ("off, or the peer doesn't support it"). The `BLOCK_HAVE_DATA` branch now only routes to `vBodyBlocks` when `fFetchBodyRangeCapable`; otherwise it falls through to the SAME whole-block-GETDATA branch a totally-unknown block already uses (the branch's own condition needed no change beyond removing an accidental, INCORRECT `(pindex->nStatus & BLOCK_HAVE_DATA) ||` disjunct added and caught in the SAME edit -- would have skipped the "already in flight" tracking for a body-missing block with an outstanding GETDATA, duplicating requests). **1.3.6/H-2's own retry-backoff, RESTORED for this fallback sub-case**: F-155 had REMOVED `g_body_retry_state`'s consultation from this branch entirely, on the premise (stated in its own comment) that the whole-block re-delivery failure mode "moved entirely to the GETBODYRANGE path" -- false whenever `fFetchBodyRangeCapable` is false, since this branch is reachable again. Without restoring it, a commitment-known block that keeps re-arriving without resolving (`-perfwithholdcount`'s own exact shape, which `feature_body_refetch.py` exercises) would reproduce 1.3.6's own original bug: an unbounded, unpaced re-request loop. Restored, gated on `pindex->nStatus & BLOCK_HAVE_DATA` (a totally-unknown block has no such failure mode of its own -- the standard `mapBlocksInFlight`-driven stall/timeout machinery already paces that case, a MORE precise scoping than the original code's own unconditional application, which pre-2.2.4 had no way to distinguish the two cases at this point in the walk). Verified: both `feature_body_refetch.py` (eventual convergence, 3 requests before resolving, matching `WITHHOLD_COUNT+1`) and `feature_body_refetch_backoff.py` (bounded retry rate -- 3 requests in an 8s sampling window, genuinely exercising backoff, NOT the reviewer's reported vacuous "0 requests in 8s") now pass, run directly via `test/functional/test_runner.py feature_body_refetch.py feature_body_refetch_backoff.py` against a real built `raptoreumd`/`raptoreum-cli` from this exact worktree, on a genuine clean rebuild. **Both tests now wired into `test_runner.py`'s `BASE_SCRIPTS`** (previously neither was -- confirmed by direct grep before this fix -- which is exactly how F-155's own regression shipped past a fully-green C++ unit suite; matches this project's own `feature_characterise_*.py` precedent of rig-specific functional tests, which remain unwired -- out of scope for this finding, noted as an open observation below). **MEDIUM, real, fixed**: an empty (coinbase-only) commitment-only block (`BodyRangeWantedCount(pindex) == 0`) was `continue`'d past with a comment calling this "never reachable in practice" (reasoning: an honest SERVER would BAN a `GETBODYRANGE` request for it, `ValidateGetBodyRange`'s own coinbase-only tier) -- true of the server side, irrelevant to the CLIENT side, which reaches this code BEFORE ever sending a request: this project's own harness routinely produces coinbase-only blocks, and Raptoreum mines genuinely empty ones on mainnet, so such a block's body was never completed by ANY mechanism once `-fetchbodyrange` routed it away from the whole-block fallback. **Fixed**: calls `ProcessFetchedBodyRange(pindex, commitments, {}, ...)` directly -- `MaterialiseBlock` already accepts `bodies.size()==0==vCommitments.size()`, no wire round trip needed. Deliberately does NOT call `ActivateBestChain` from this new call site: `SendMessages` holds `cs_main` for the rest of its own body via a bare, unscoped `TRY_LOCK` (confirmed directly, line ~5029) -- calling it here would reproduce the EXACT F-157 bug class this same finding's own review round already closed elsewhere in this file, and restructuring `SendMessages`'s own locking is out of scope for this fix. `ProcessFetchedBodyRange` alone still makes the block genuinely connectable (status bits set, body durably written) -- the next block this node accepts for ANY reason (routine network traffic, at most one block interval away) calls `ActivateBestChain` itself and picks it up. **LOW, real, fixed**: `mapBodyRangePartial` had no disconnect/abandonment cleanup at all, unlike its sibling maps (`mapBodyRangeInFlight`, `g_body_retry_state`), which got exactly this treatment in F-155/F-157 -- a fetch abandoned because its only announcer left, the block reorged away, or it resolved through a different path entirely kept its accumulated bodies in memory for the process lifetime. Rather than per-peer tracking (deliberately absent by design -- a completing chunk can come from a different peer than an earlier one), added `mapBodyRangePartialLastTouched` (a per-entry timestamp, matching `mapBodyRangeInFlight`'s own `nRequestTime`/`IsBodyRangeRequestStale` pattern) and a new `EraseBodyRangePartial` helper (pairing the two maps, matching `EraseBodyRangeInFlight`'s own F-157 precedent against invariant drift) -- reaped in the SAME throttled maintenance pass as the stale in-flight reaper: immediately if the block already has its bodies via a different path (`HaveBodies`, cheap to detect), or after `BODY_RANGE_PARTIAL_STALE_MICROS` (5 minutes, generous relative to the single-retry `BODY_RETRY_MAX_MICROS` cap since a multi-chunk fetch can legitimately need several such gaps in a row) for the general case with no cheap predicate of its own. `FinalizeNode`'s own comment (the one this review quoted as conflating shape and content validation) corrected in place. **LOW, documentation only, recorded**: with the whole-block fallback now conditional on `fFetchBodyRangeCapable`, a peer/config combination where it IS true still depends ENTIRELY on the announcer ring for recovery once a block routes to `vBodyBlocks` -- and F-154's own finding already documents the ring provides close to no accountability during a long catch-up (IBD or a long reconnect). Stated explicitly here and in build-plan.md's 2.2 row: the fetch client is tip-path-only for this reason; nobody building on top of it should assume it covers IBD/long-catchup recovery too. Full suite: 583 cases (`!miner_tests`), up from F-157's 575; `miner_tests` alone unchanged. Both functional tests (`feature_body_refetch.py`, `feature_body_refetch_backoff.py`) pass against a real built node. Verified via a genuine `make clean` + full `-j32` rebuild of `test_raptoreum`, `raptoreumd`, and `raptoreum-cli` together, with 3 repeated clean runs of the full C++ suite (one incremental-cycle run hit an unrelated `acceptancebit_tests` fixture-teardown memory-access crash matching F-144/F-145's own documented stale-build-artifact signature exactly -- retracted the same way, 3/3 clean on the genuine rebuild) | `source`, mutation-tested (4 mutations on `ValidateBodyRangeChunkHashes`: 3 killed on first try, 1 initially-surviving mutant closed by replacing an unreliable UB-dependent test with a deterministic one), live functional-test A/B verification (both before/after states independently reasoned about against source; the fixed state's own pass independently re-run on a genuine clean rebuild, not re-trusted from a single run) | **fixed 2026-09-25** |
| F-159 | **A second, fresh-model independent review of F-158 (commit a3db7cf2f, no shared context, built and ran everything itself) found F-158's OWN fix introduced a real bug of the SAME CLASS it was closing (a timing race that could still ban an entirely honest peer), plus a real lock-discipline regression (per-chunk disk I/O under `cs_main`) -- both fixed, along with three lower-severity items folded in per the reviewer's own invitation. `mapBodyRangePartial` and its sibling `mapBodyRangePartialLastTouched` MERGED into one struct-valued map, closing the two-map-drift bug class at its root rather than patching another instance of it** | **CONFIRMED MEDIUM, real, fixed**: F-158's own new TTL reaper for `mapBodyRangePartial` erased purely by elapsed time, with no check against `mapBodyRangeInFlight` for whether a request was still legitimately outstanding -- and the response-arrival handler's own append never verified the buffer's actual current size against the response's claimed `nStartIndex` (only that it echoed what THIS SPECIFIC REQUEST asked for, via `ValidateBodyRangeResponse`). Failure scenario, no malicious peer required: a validated prefix of size N sits in the buffer; a legitimate request for `[N, N+k)` is outstanding; the reaper's own (shorter) TTL fires on the PARTIAL and erases it while the in-flight request is still live; the honest response for `[N, N+k)` later arrives, passes `ValidateBodyRangeResponse` (echoes what was asked) and `ValidateBodyRangeChunkHashes` (correctly validates against its own claimed offset N -- the per-chunk check alone doesn't see the buffer's own state), and gets appended at buffer position 0 instead of its true logical position, silently misaligning the assembly; `MaterialiseBlock` fails on the corrupted result at completion, `CorruptionPossible()` is true, and the LAST, entirely honest peer gets banned -- purely a timing race between two independent TTLs, the exact ban-misattribution class F-158 itself had just closed for a malicious-peer cause. **Fixed, both layers the reviewer named as guarding different failure modes**: (1) new `bool IsBodyRangeChunkAligned(size_t nAccumulatedSoFar, uint32_t nResponseStartIndex)` (`bodyrange.h`/`.cpp`, pure, matching this project's established split) checked at the append site BEFORE ever inserting -- a misaligned-but-hash-valid chunk is silently discarded (not misbehaved, since this genuinely isn't that peer's fault) and the buffer's already-validated prefix, if any, is left untouched; the reviewer's own "more durable" preference, since it catches misalignment from ANY future cause, not just this specific reaper race. (2) the reaper itself now also skips any hash with a live `mapBodyRangeInFlight` entry regardless of its own TTL, closing the SPECIFIC race directly as defense-in-depth. The `CorruptionPossible()` branch's own comment (which the reviewer noted was directly contradicted by this path) updated to credit BOTH guards -- per-chunk hash validation AND the alignment check -- for why `MaterialiseBlock`'s equivalent is now provably unreachable in the normal case. **CONFIRMED MEDIUM, real, fixed**: `ReadCommitmentBlockFromDisk` (a full block deserialize plus a rehash of every transaction) ran on EVERY chunk arrival under `cs_main`, not once per completed fetch -- confirmed directly, the `LOCK(cs_main)` scope opened at the top of the `BODYRANGE` handler still enclosed that call, unconditionally, per message. Exactly the "disk I/O held under `cs_main`" class this project's own serving side (F-149) explicitly refused; at this project's own ~110MB/700k-tx target scale with 1MiB chunks (~110 chunks/block), this was ~110x the necessary reads+rehashes per fetch, with `-maxbodyrangeinflight` carrying no clamp tied to how expensive each slot actually is. **Fixed by a structural redesign, not a point patch**: `mapBodyRangePartial` (the accumulated bodies) and F-158's own separate `mapBodyRangePartialLastTouched` (a staleness timestamp, paired only by call-site DISCIPLINE via `EraseBodyRangePartial`) merged into one `struct BodyRangeAccumulation { vBodies; commitments; nLastTouched; }` in one map -- `commitments` read from disk exactly once, on the chunk that first creates an entry, cached and reused for every later chunk of the SAME accumulation; the two-map split can no longer drift apart by construction, which is also the direct fix for LOW item 3 below (found by the SAME redesign, not patched separately). **LOW, real, fixed**: the request-tracking site in `SendMessages` read the (then-separate) `mapBodyRangePartial` via `operator[]`, silently creating an empty entry with no corresponding `LastTouched` entry whenever a request got a miss or no answer at all -- the LastTouched-keyed reaper could never see or reap it, an orphan for the process lifetime. Switched to `find()`, matching this project's own "don't create state you don't yet have data for" convention; the merge above means an orphan like this is now at least reachable by the reaper regardless, but not creating one is the more direct fix. **LOW, real, fixed**: the empty-(coinbase-only)-block completion branch F-158 added ran AFTER the `fWasAnnounced`/`ShouldRequestBodyRange` eligibility gate, so a coinbase-only block evicted from the announcer ring (F-154's own documented long-catch-up accountability gap) or blocked by the aggregate cap/backoff never got completed -- even though it needs no peer, announcement, or wire round trip at all. Moved before that gate. **LOW, coverage gap, recorded not closed**: no functional test exercises the fetch-client path with `-fetchbodyrange`/`-servebodyrange` enabled end to end -- `feature_body_refetch.py`/`feature_body_refetch_backoff.py` (F-158) only exercise the flag-OFF whole-block fallback. Evaluated and NOT built this round: the Python `test_framework` has zero `GETBODYRANGE`/`BODYRANGE` message support (confirmed by grep across `test_framework/*.py`), and a genuine two-real-node commitment-only-relay test isn't constructible yet either -- build-plan.md's own 4.1 row (real commitment-only relay/accept) is still unbuilt, which is exactly why both existing functional tests use `-perfwithhold*` on a SINGLE node rather than two real nodes exchanging a real commitment-only accept. Closing this gap for real needs either new Python-side wire-format code (a `P2PDataStore`-driven mock BODYRANGE responder) or waiting on 4.1 -- explicitly flagged here rather than implied covered, per the reviewer's own instruction. All five items verified against the current source directly before fixing, not taken on the reviewer's word alone (the two CONFIRMED items independently re-derived from the exact code shape before any fix was written). Full suite: 588 cases (`!miner_tests`), up from F-158's 583; `miner_tests` alone unchanged. Both functional tests re-verified passing against a real built `raptoreumd`/`raptoreum-cli`, genuine clean rebuild | `source`, mutation-tested (`IsBodyRangeChunkAligned`'s equality inverted -- 6 tests fail, killed), full clean rebuild independently re-verified, live functional-test re-verification (not re-trusted from a prior run) | **fixed 2026-09-25** |

Fixed: reset the attempt counter on genuine progress** | Traced `g_body_retry_state`'s own `nAttempts` field (`net_processing.cpp`) end to end: incremented every time a `GETBODYRANGE` request is actually sent (`:5858`, unconditional), but NEVER reset when a chunk successfully arrives and advances the accumulation (`NetMsgType::BODYRANGE`'s handler, `:3676-3818` current lines) -- only erased entirely once the WHOLE block resolves, via the unrelated whole-block-download path (`:1125`). This project's own comment elsewhere in the same file states its target scale directly: "~110MB/700k-tx... with 1MiB chunks... ~110 reads... per fetch" (`:3748`, `DEFAULT_MAX_BODYRANGE_BYTES = 1MiB`, `:118`) -- a single max-size block legitimately needs on the order of 110 round trips to complete, six times `BODY_RANGE_DISCONNECT_ATTEMPTS` (18). Confirmed by direct trace, not assumed: nothing resets `nAttempts` between chunks, so a peer answering EVERY chunk correctly and promptly would still hit the disconnect check (`:5815`) on its 18th request and be dropped mid-transfer, which directly contradicts F-185's/build-plan.md's own stated design intent ("a peer that's merely slow under normal backoff must not be punished the same as one that's genuinely never going to answer") -- the counter as built could not distinguish "18 failed attempts" from "18 successful, incremental chunks of one large block." **Fixed**: the `BODYRANGE` response handler now resets `retry.nAttempts = 0` (via `find()`, not `operator[]`, so it never creates a fresh entry of its own) the moment a chunk is validated and appended to the accumulation (`acc.vBodies.insert(...)`) -- `nAttempts` now counts CONSECUTIVE non-productive attempts only, matching the threshold's own stall-detection intent; an honest miss, a hash-mismatched chunk, or a misaligned-offset chunk (the three other branches in the same handler) do NOT reset it, since none of those represent progress. No dedicated regression test: `ProcessMessage`'s `BODYRANGE` branch is not directly callable from the boost-test harness (confirmed -- no existing test in this tree calls `ProcessMessage` directly; the project's own established convention, F-149/F-157/F-162/1.3.7-F-112, is to verify this class of `net_processing.cpp` dispatch glue by direct reading rather than build new scaffolding for it) -- this fix is verified by that same direct trace, and by the fact that `ShouldDisconnectForBodyRangeAttempts`'s own boundary tests (F-185, unaffected by this fix) still hold since the predicate itself was never wrong, only when `nAttempts` gets incremented vs. reset around it. | F-190 | **4.4 ("Mining and template") audited and scoped into 3 ordered sub-steps, matching F-160's own precedent for a phase this size -- audit only, no production code. Read `getblocktemplate`/`submitblock` (`rpc/mining.cpp`), `CreateNewBlock` (`miner.cpp`), `CCommitmentBlock`/`CommitmentsFromBlock`/`MaterialiseBlock` (`primitives/block.h`/`.cpp`, already built), and re-derived the plan's own "80-byte header is byte-identical" and "seconds of `cs_main`" claims against current source rather than trusting either** | **Q1, what `getblocktemplate` returns and where commitment-mode fits -- NOT a new RPC, a capability negotiated on the existing one.** `getblocktemplate` (`rpc/mining.cpp:429`) already has a live client-rule-negotiation mechanism, `setClientRules` (`:570,615`), checked twice today (`:783,794`) to gate whether a pending softfork's own template fields appear -- the exact BIP9-style "client declares support via `rules`, server changes its response" shape upstream's own `!segwit` uses. Commitment-mode should be one more named rule (e.g. `"commitments"`) checked the same way, not a new RPC method and not a new top-level `mode` value (the existing `mode` key already means something else -- `template`/`proposal`, `:583`). Recommend gating the ONE thing that actually needs to change: the `transactions` array's per-entry shape (`:722-748`), which today always calls `entry.pushKV("data", EncodeHexTx(tx))` (`:735`, confirmed the literal call site) -- full transaction bytes, hex-doubled, for every non-coinbase transaction, unconditionally. Nothing else in the function needs a branch: `CreateNewBlock` (`miner.cpp:148`) keeps building an ordinary, full, in-memory `CBlock` exactly as today, from the SAME mempool selection logic, and the RPC-boundary serialization is the only thing that changes shape. **Q2, `submitblock` and whether it can reuse `CommitmentsFromBlock`/`MaterialiseBlock` directly -- yes, both already exist, tested, and reachable from `rpc/mining.cpp` with no new glue beyond the RPC handler itself.** `CommitmentsFromBlock(const CBlock&)` (`primitives/block.cpp:107-119`) is a cheap, already-tested pure function -- `CTransaction::GetHash()` is a cached member (`primitives/transaction.h:236`, `const uint256 hash`, computed once at construction), so converting a 62,500-tx block to its commitment form costs one cache-hit read per transaction, not a rehash; used in production today at three call sites (`validation.cpp:1230,4957`). `MaterialiseBlock(commitments, bodies, blockOut)` (`:121+`) is likewise already wired into the real accept path (`validation.cpp:5120`, `ProcessFetchedBodyRange`) and independently tested since 2.2.4 (F-155). `submitblock` (`rpc/mining.cpp:903`) decodes a hex string via `DecodeHexBlk` (`:923`) and hands the result straight to `ProcessNewBlock` (`:948`) -- that back half needs ZERO changes for either serialization, since `MaterialiseBlock`'s whole job is producing an ordinary, fresh `CBlock` (`fChecked=false` by construction, `primitives/block.h:195-197`) that flows into the exact same `ProcessNewBlock` call already there. What's missing is only the front half: an explicit way to say "this hexdata is a `CCommitmentBlock`, not a `CBlock`" and a bodies-resolution step. **Flagged, not glossed over**: auto-detecting the serialization by trying one parse then falling back is NOT safe and should not be built -- both types begin with the identical 80-byte `CBlockHeader` prefix (`READWRITEAS(CBlockHeader, obj)`, both `primitives/block.h:90` and `:146`, confirmed byte-for-byte identical field lists, see Q3) and BOTH continue with a value of type `CTransactionRef` immediately after it (`CBlock::vtx[0]` vs `CCommitmentBlock::coinbase`), so the first transaction decodes the same either way; only what follows THAT differs (more full transactions vs. a `vector<uint256>`), which a parser could misinterpret as a garbled attempt at more transactions rather than failing cleanly. Recommend an explicit new named parameter on `submitblock` (e.g. `"format":"commitment"`, default `"full"`) -- this is a local RPC boundary, not a P2P wire format, so there is no BIP9-style negotiation need, just an explicit caller declaration, matching this project's own established preference for explicit signaling over inferred-from-bytes (F-143's own hash-only-addressing decision, reached for an analogous reason). Bodies resolution for the commitment-mode path: for each `vCommitments[i]`, look up the transaction in the LOCAL mempool (`mempool.get(hash)`, the same accessor `GetTransaction`'s mempool branch already uses, `validation.cpp:1114`) -- correct specifically because a commitment submitted BY this node's own miner names only transactions that were in ITS OWN mempool moments earlier when `CreateNewBlock` selected them, so this node is always the trivial, immediate, no-network-fetch source of every body it needs to materialise its own submission. A missing or hash-mismatched body must produce a clean `JSONRPCError`, matching this codebase's established convention (F-178's own precedent) -- never propagate `MaterialiseBlock`'s `false` return as a crash or an unclear generic error. **Q3, the "80-byte header is byte-identical" claim -- CONFIRMED by direct structural read, not trusted from the design doc's prose.** `CBlockHeader` (`primitives/block.h:23-66`) declares exactly `nVersion, hashPrevBlock, hashMerkleRoot, nTime, nBits, nNonce` (4+32+32+4+4+4 = 80 bytes) and its own `SERIALIZE_METHODS` writes exactly those six fields in that order (`:39`). `CBlock` (`:69-113`) and `CCommitmentBlock` (`:136-183`) both publicly inherit `CBlockHeader` and both begin their own `SERIALIZE_METHODS` with the identical `READWRITEAS(CBlockHeader, obj)` call (`:90` and `:146` respectively) before anything else -- there is exactly one header-serialization code path in this codebase, shared by construction, not two implementations that merely happen to agree today. The claim holds structurally, not by convention. **Q4, the concrete cost of dropping the full `ConnectBlock` per poll -- reasoned from this project's own existing measurements (F-15b/F-16), not guessed, and the real number is smaller than the row's own alarming framing suggested.** `CreateNewBlock` (`miner.cpp:276`) already calls `TestBlockValidity` (`validation.cpp:5245`), which runs a real `ConnectBlock` in check-only mode (`:5271`) -- over EVERY transaction selected into the block, today, unconditionally, for ordinary (non-commitment) blocks already. The transactions it processes were ALL individually verified moments earlier by mempool `AcceptToMemoryPool` (script-cache-hot), which is exactly this project's own established "warm" scenario (F-15b: warm connection measured at ~12.8 µs/tx, 1.1 s for 85,624 transactions; F-16: derived to ~0.8 s warm for a full 2 MB / 62,500-tx commitment block, ~3.2 s warm at 8 MB / 250,000 tx) -- NOT the ~16-78 s COLD path F-16 also measured, which is a structurally different scenario (a block whose bodies were never seen at acceptance, reconstructed from disk with a cold script cache; a miner's own mempool-sourced block is never that scenario). So the real `ConnectBlock` cost at design-point throughput is **0.8-3.2 s of `cs_main`, not 16-78 s** -- real, but roughly 20-25x smaller than a naive reading of F-16's own headline cold-path number would suggest if misapplied here. **Separately, and found by this audit, not named in the row's own text**: `getblocktemplate` (`rpc/mining.cpp:690-711`) ALREADY caches `pblocktemplate`/`pindexPrev` as `static` locals and only re-runs `CreateNewBlock` (and therefore `ConnectBlock`) when the tip changes or when 5+ seconds have passed since the last rebuild AND the mempool has changed (`:693-694`) -- so the `ConnectBlock` cost is already bounded to at most once per 5 seconds regardless of poll frequency, a real, existing mitigation the row's own framing ("per poll") does not credit. **What is NOT cached, and IS the real, unconditional per-poll cost**: the `transactions` JSON array (`:722-748`) is rebuilt from `pblock->vtx` on EVERY call regardless of whether `pblocktemplate` itself was just rebuilt or reused -- at 62,500 full-transaction hex entries (each ~373 B x2 for hex, F-30, plus UniValue object/key overhead per entry), this is the "tens of MB of JSON" the row names, and unlike `ConnectBlock` it pays this cost on literally every poll, uncached, which is exactly what switching the array to bare 32-byte identifiers (Q1) fixes as a direct side effect -- cutting a ~46 MB payload to roughly 2 MB. **Conclusion for the effort estimate**: the `ConnectBlock` half of the row's stated risk is real but smaller and already partially mitigated by existing caching; recommend 4.4.1's own build step include one real, measured benchmark (not a fresh guess) confirming the ~0.8-3.2 s figure against an actual 62,500-tx mempool before treating it as settled, matching this project's "measure, don't guess" discipline (F-150/F-151's own precedent) -- but this does NOT block scoping 4.4 now, since the existing 5-second-or-tip-change cache already bounds the worst case to a level no sub-step needs to build new machinery to fix. **Q5, the miner's own active serving obligation (§9.1a/§14.10) -- recommend a hard, explicit gate, not a silent operator responsibility.** Read `transaction-decoupling.md` §9.1a (`:1619-1652`) and §14.10 (`:2335-2349`) in full, not just the quoted fragment: "today, publishing a block discharges the miner's obligation completely. Under this design it does not" -- a commitment-mode block's assembly-time-only transactions (a pool's own fee payout, anything the miner built and never relayed) are held by NO OTHER NODE until served, and orphaning happens specifically "if the miner will not or cannot serve." The existing mechanism for this is already built and already gated: `-servebodyrange` (`init.cpp:809-814`, `net_processing.cpp:3643`) defaults `false` and is `DEBUG_ONLY`, specifically because its own per-connection server-side rate limit is a CONFIRMED, still-open gap -- re-checked directly against `docs/findings.md`, not assumed: F-150's MEDIUM finding ("no per-message-type rate limit anywhere" for `GETBODYRANGE` serving) was only ever upgraded from a doc-comment gate to an executable one (F-151, the `-servebodyrange` flag itself), and F-155's own explicit 2.2.4 scope boundary lists "per-connection server-side rate limiting" as still deferred -- nothing between F-155 and F-189 closes it. **Recommendation**: 4.4's own commitment-mode mining path should REQUIRE `-servebodyrange` be on whenever commitment-mode template/submission is used -- either auto-enabling it with a logged warning the moment a commitment-mode `getblocktemplate` capability is requested or a commitment-mode `submitblock` succeeds, or refusing to serve a commitment-mode template at all until it's set -- rather than leaving "a miner must serve" as documentation only that pool operators must independently discover and configure. Either way, the log/help text must say explicitly that this reopens the still-unbuilt rate-limit gap outside a controlled devnet (matching F-150's own "must not run on a live/exposed network" convention verbatim) -- this project's own scope note for 4.4 ("external pool coordination is the schedule risk, out of scope") does not excuse silence about a gate that bites the FIRST party using this feature, on a devnet the owner explicitly said this phase targets. **Q6, sub-step breakdown**: **4.4.1 -- commitment-mode `getblocktemplate`** (capability-negotiated `transactions` array shape, reusing `CommitmentsFromBlock`; `CreateNewBlock`/caching untouched; new tests confirming bare identifiers + a real measured `ConnectBlock` benchmark at 62,500 tx) -- HIGH confidence, LOW-MEDIUM effort, no consensus/validation-logic changes, every reused primitive already built and tested. **4.4.2 -- `submitblock` dual-serialization support** (`"format"` param, `CCommitmentBlock` decode, local-mempool body resolution via `mempool.get`, `MaterialiseBlock`, clean `JSONRPCError` on any resolution/mismatch failure, unchanged `ProcessNewBlock` tail) -- HIGH confidence, MEDIUM effort (the orchestration and its error-path tests are the real work; no new validation logic). Independent of 4.4.1, buildable in either order or in parallel. **4.4.3 -- enforce the active serving obligation** (auto-enable-or-refuse gate on `-servebodyrange` for commitment-mode mining, explicit devnet-only warning text) -- HIGH confidence, LOW effort in code, but functions as this sub-step's own safety gate the same way 4.1 gated 4.6 -- recommend sequencing it to land alongside or immediately after 4.4.1/4.4.2 land, not deferred, since it is what keeps 4.4 honest about the obligation §14.10 states plainly. **Not a separate sub-step, folded into 4.4.1's own build/verify step**: the `ConnectBlock`-cost benchmark (Q4) -- reasoning already grounds the estimate; a fresh measurement is verification, not new design work | `source`, direct read of `rpc/mining.cpp`/`miner.cpp`/`primitives/block.h`/`.cpp`/`validation.cpp`/`init.cpp`/`net_processing.cpp` against current line numbers; the header-identity and `ConnectBlock`-cost claims independently re-derived rather than trusted from `transaction-decoupling.md`'s or `build-plan.md`'s own prose; the `-servebodyrange` rate-limit gap re-confirmed still-open by re-reading F-150/F-151/F-155 directly rather than assuming it was closed by later work; no code change, audit and scoping only, matching F-160's own established discipline for this size of phase | **audited and scoped 2026-09-26 -- 3 sub-steps proposed (4.4.1, 4.4.2, 4.4.3), build not started, reported back to the coordinator before Step 2** |

Rather than chase DIP0001 activation to force a literal 62,500-tx run (assessed as disproportionate scaffolding for a grounding measurement, matching this project's own established cost-discipline), measured at the real, achievable N and extrapolated linearly, matching F-15b's own already-established linear (µs/tx) methodology. **Measured**: `CreateNewBlock` (selection + `TestBlockValidity`/`ConnectBlock` together, the exact call `getblocktemplate` makes) took 34,556 µs for 5,225 real, individually-ATMP-verified transactions = **6.61 µs/tx** -- extrapolated, **~0.41 s at 62,500 tx (2 MB design point)**, **~1.65 s at 250,000 tx (8 MB design point)**, both smaller than F-190's own already-conservative 0.8-3.2 s estimate. **Caveat stated plainly, not glossed over**: these are minimal 1-in/1-out P2PKH transactions (~191 B, 1 sigop each), not F-30's own ~373 B mixed-traffic average, so 6.61 µs/tx is a lower bound for a realistic transaction mix, not a prediction of the exact design-point figure -- it corroborates F-15b/F-16's own more representative real-mempool-traffic derivation (~12.8 µs/tx) landing in the SAME regime (single-digit seconds at design-point scale, not the tens of seconds a cold reconnect would cost), which is the entire point of running it. The scratch benchmark itself was NOT committed (30,000 real ECDSA sign+verify operations cost ~4.85 s of setup alone, disproportionate to run on every future test invocation for a one-time grounding measurement, matching this project's own established precedent of some measurements being recorded facts rather than shipped regression tests, e.g. F-15/F-15b/F-16 themselves) -- reverted cleanly after the number was captured, confirmed by a clean `git status`/`git diff` showing no residue. **Kept 4.4.3's future need in mind, per the coordinator's own instruction**: nothing in this build forecloses gating commitment-mode on `-servebodyrange` later -- the `fCommitmentMode` flag and `BuildGBTTransactionEntry`'s own call site are both single, clear insertion points a future guard can extend without restructuring anything built here. | F-193 | **A real commitment-mode block can legitimately name a non-coinbase transaction that was never in, and will never enter, the local mempool -- an LLMQ quorum-commitment special transaction -- so F-190's own stated justification for mempool-only body resolution does not universally hold. Found by F-192's own round-trip test failing on its first genuine run, not anticipated by the audit** | `CreateNewBlock` (`miner.cpp:GetMineableCommitmentTx`) inserts a mineable quorum commitment (`TRANSACTION_QUORUM_COMMITMENT`, `primitives/transaction.h:TRANSACTION_QUORUM_COMMITMENT`, nType 6) directly from `llmq::quorumBlockProcessor`'s own internal store whenever `fDIP0003Active_context` and a commitment is due -- entirely bypassing `addPackageTxs`'s mempool selection (the same function's own very next lines). This is not a hypothetical: `TestChain100Setup`'s own regtest params run with `consensus.DIP0003Enabled = true` unconditionally (`chainparams.cpp`, `CRegTestParams`), and building a 101st candidate block during F-192's own round-trip test produced exactly this -- a `TRANSACTION_QUORUM_COMMITMENT` transaction never staged into the mempool, causing `MaterialiseSubmittedCommitmentBlock`'s honest "not found in local mempool" error against a completely legitimate, self-produced block. **`CommitmentsFromBlock` (`primitives/block.cpp:CommitmentsFromBlock`) does not distinguish this transaction class from an ordinary one** -- it becomes one more entry in `vCommitments`, indistinguishable from a mempool-sourced payment once serialized -- and `getblocktemplate`'s own commitment-mode `transactions` array (`rpc/mining.cpp`, `BuildGBTTransactionEntry`, F-191) has the exact same blind spot: a pool reading that array has no signal that one entry's hash is a quorum commitment rather than a mempool transaction, so it could not resolve one from ITS OWN mempool either even if it wanted to. **Not fixed in F-192's build**: widening `MaterialiseSubmittedCommitmentBlock`'s resolution source (e.g. also consulting `quorumBlockProcessor` or accepting a caller-supplied bodies list) is a genuine scope decision -- it would mean the "local mempool is always sufficient" claim this whole sub-step's design rested on is false for at least one real transaction class, which the coordinator should decide on explicitly rather than have silently widened underneath the "orchestration only" instruction. **Practical severity, stated plainly**: harmless on a chain with no active masternode quorums doing real DKG sessions (the ordinary case today), but a REAL, live-fire blocker for commitment-mode mining the moment this project's own quorums are live and a commitment happens to fall due at the same height as a submission -- not a corner case at scale, a periodic one. F-192's own tests route around this by staging every non-coinbase transaction `CreateBlock` actually produced into the mempool before submitting (`test/submitblock_tests.cpp:commitment_format_round_trips_through_the_local_mempool`), which correctly isolates and confirms the mempool-resolution logic itself, but does not paper over or fix the gap this row names | `source` + a genuine test failure, not a hypothetical -- `test/submitblock_tests.cpp`'s own round-trip case failed against real `TestChain100Setup` state on its first run, the specific missing hash traced directly to `nVersion=3, nType=6` (`TRANSACTION_QUORUM_COMMITMENT`) by direct inspection before any fix; `GetMineableCommitmentTx`'s call site confirmed by direct read of `miner.cpp`, not guessed from the symptom | **FIXED 2026-09-27, same day.** Coordinator checked whether 2.2.x's own P2P fetch-protocol path (`ProcessFetchedBodyRange`, `validation.cpp:5094`) shared this gap first, before deciding how to fix it here -- it does not: that function's `bodies` parameter is sourced entirely from real `GETBODYRANGE` wire responses (peer-served bytes), never from a local mempool assumption, so a quorum-commitment transaction resolves there the same as any other. The gap is genuinely narrow: specific to `submitblock`'s own mempool-lookup shortcut, taken because its caller is presumed to be the same node that just mined the block. Fixed narrowly, matching that scope: new `FindMineableCommitmentTxByHash` (`rpc/mining.h`/`.cpp`) reconstructs each currently-mineable quorum-commitment transaction for the next block exactly as `CreateNewBlock` does (same loop over `GetEnabledQuorumParams`, same `GetMineableCommitmentTx` call, same height), and returns the one whose hash matches -- the same source `GetMineableCommitmentTx` already uses, not a new one. `MaterialiseSubmittedCommitmentBlock` falls back to it only when `mempool.get` misses. New regression test (`test/submitblock_tests.cpp:commitment_format_resolves_a_quorum_commitment_tx_the_mempool_never_held`) deliberately withholds the quorum-commitment transaction from the mempool (confirmed present via `BOOST_REQUIRE(sawQuorumCommitment)`, so the test fails loudly if the fixture ever stops producing one) and confirms the block is still accepted with the chain tip advancing. Mutation-checked: forcing `FindMineableCommitmentTxByHash` to always return `nullptr` made the new test fail with an uncaught `UniValue` exception; restored, clean. The existing `commitment_format_throws_a_clean_error_on_a_missing_body` test is unaffected -- confirmed by direct read of `vCommitments`' ordering and rerunning: the quorum-commitment hash now resolves via the fallback, and the still-missing ordinary spend transaction (which has no fallback) still throws the same "not found in local mempool" message. Full suite green (submitblock_tests: 5 cases, up from 4). getblocktemplate's own `BuildGBTTransactionEntry` blind spot (an external pool reading the commitment-mode `transactions` array has no signal that one entry is a quorum commitment) is unaffected by this fix and remains a separate, lower-severity, cosmetic gap -- not a submission blocker, since resolution happens entirely on the `submitblock` side against this same node's own state |
| F-194 | **4.4.3 built: the miner's own active serving obligation (§9.1a/§14.10) is now enforced, not just documented -- commitment-mode `getblocktemplate` REFUSES to serve until `-servebodyrange` is explicitly enabled** | **Built exactly to F-190's own recommendation, one of the two named options chosen deliberately**: `EnforceCommitmentModeServingObligation(fCommitmentMode)` (`rpc/mining.h`/`.cpp`) is a no-op for full-mode requests and, in commitment mode, either returns silently (if `-servebodyrange` is already on) or throws a `JSONRPCError(RPC_INVALID_PARAMETER, ...)` -- called from `getblocktemplate` (`rpc/mining.cpp`) immediately after `fCommitmentMode` is computed and before the `transactions` array is built, so a refused request never computes or leaks any commitment-mode-shaped response. **Chosen enforcement style: REFUSE, not auto-enable -- justified, not just picked.** Auto-enabling would let the CONTENT of an RPC request (a client-declared `"commitments"` rule, from whatever process holds RPC access -- not necessarily the operator who read and typed the flag) reach past the RPC boundary and silently flip a network-facing, known-rate-limit-gap flag on, defeating `-servebodyrange`'s own explicit "opt-in" design (F-150/F-151's "must not run on a live/exposed network unopted-in"). Refusing also matches this exact function's own sibling precedent a few lines up in `getblocktemplate` (the forced-update-rule-not-supported `JSONRPCError` throw) and F-178's established "clean `JSONRPCError` over a silent/crashing fallback" convention -- there is no precedent anywhere in this codebase for a runtime RPC handler mutating a global `DEBUG_ONLY` security-relevant flag on the caller's behalf; the `SoftSetBoolArg`-based "parameter interaction" pattern (`init.cpp`) that resembles auto-enable is exclusively a startup-time, static-configuration mechanism, never invoked from inside request dispatch, and reusing it here would have been a novel, undiscussed extension of that pattern to a live security boundary. **Message wording matches F-150's own convention, confirmed by direct string search, not by memory**: `init.cpp`'s existing `-servebodyrange` help text is grepped directly for its own phrasing ("must not run on a live/exposed network unopted-in") and that exact phrase is reused verbatim in the new error message, which also names the section numbers (9.1a/14.10), F-150/F-155 by ID, and explicitly states that enabling the flag "reopens that gap outside a controlled devnet" -- all four content requirements the coordinator's brief set, not just the tone. **TDD, genuine RED first, confirmed by an actual compile, not assumed**: wrote the test (`miner_tests.cpp`, `enforce_commitment_mode_serving_obligation_refuses_without_servebodyrange`) against the header-only declaration, then `git stash`'d just `rpc/mining.h`/`.cpp` (keeping the test) and rebuilt -- three genuine compile errors, `'EnforceCommitmentModeServingObligation' was not declared in this scope` (not a link-time RED like F-191's, since this call now fails at the caller's compile step) -- then `git stash pop` restored the implementation and the same rebuild went GREEN. The test covers all three branches: full-mode is always a no-op (`BOOST_CHECK_NO_THROW` with `fCommitmentMode=false` regardless of the flag), commitment-mode with the flag off throws a `UniValue` whose `"message"` field is checked (via `BOOST_CHECK_EXCEPTION`'s predicate form, matching this codebase's own `rpc_tests.cpp`/`AmountFromValue` precedent for testing a directly-thrown `JSONRPCError` without a live RPC dispatch) for three substrings -- the flag name, the verbatim F-150 phrase, and "rate limit" -- and commitment-mode with the flag on returns cleanly. Saves and restores the global `gArgs` flag around the test, matching `denialofservice_tests`' own `-banscore` save/restore precedent, so no other test in the same binary process observes it flipped. **Mutation-checked the gate itself, per the coordinator's own instruction**: temporarily replaced the `-servebodyrange` check with an unconditional early `return` (disabling the gate while leaving the rest of the function, including the throw, intact and unreachable), rebuilt, reran just the new test case -- it failed exactly as expected (`exception UniValue expected but not raised`), confirmed the mutation was real by reading the diff before reverting, then restored the original logic and rebuilt clean. **Full suite**: 612 test cases, exactly 1 failure -- `CreateNewBlock_validity`, `bad-blk-sigops`, same line (`test/miner_tests.cpp:408-409`) and same assertion F-191 already named, confirmed unchanged rather than assumed. `miner_tests` itself: 4 cases (up from 3), the 1 new case green, the 1 known pre-existing failure unchanged. **Scope held, nothing silently decided beyond the brief's own two named options**: `submitblock`'s own commitment-mode path (4.4.2) is not built in this worktree, so there is no second call site to wire today -- confirmed by grepping the whole tree for `fCommitmentMode`/`GBT_RULE_COMMITMENTS`/`WantsCommitmentModeTemplate` and finding exactly the one call site this row adds a gate immediately after, matching F-191's own note that it left this exact insertion point open for 4.4.3 to extend. No consensus-adjacent gap or interaction found that didn't fit the "auto-enable or refuse" framing -- flagged as the honest answer, not manufactured to look thorough | `source`, direct read of `init.cpp`/`net_processing.cpp`'s existing `-servebodyrange` gate and its exact help text before writing the new message; TDD (genuine compile-failure RED, then GREEN); a real mutation check on the critical gate, reverted and reconfirmed; full suite run clean except the one pre-existing, independently reconfirmed failure | **built 2026-09-27 -- 4.4.3 CLOSED, all three 4.4 sub-steps (4.4.1/4.4.2/4.4.3) now have their own findings row; 4.4.2 remains not started in this worktree** |
| F-195 | **Independent adversarial review of F-192/F-193 found `FindMineableCommitmentTxByHash` keyed its reconstruction to the LIVE CHAIN TIP at call time, not to the submitted block's own recorded parent -- a stale-but-valid submission (a miner losing a race is routine, or a new/better quorum commitment arriving mid-mining) misses and throws a misleading "not found in local mempool". Fixed before merge, same day** | The reviewer's mechanism, confirmed by direct read rather than trusted: `FindMineableCommitmentTxByHash` (`rpc/mining.cpp`) computed `pindexPrev = ::ChainActive().Tip()` and `nHeight = pindexPrev->nHeight + 1` -- if the tip moves between template creation and submission, this reconstructs for the WRONG height (a different quorum session/mining-window state), producing a transaction whose hash cannot match what was actually mined into the stale-but-otherwise-valid block; `submitblock` in `"full"` format would have accepted the identical block fine, since it never depends on the live tip to resolve anything. **Fixed**: the function now takes `hashPrevBlock` (the submitted block's own recorded parent, via `commitments.hashPrevBlock` -- every `CCommitmentBlock` carries this as part of its `CBlockHeader` base) and resolves `pindexPrev` via `LookupBlockIndex(hashPrevBlock)` instead of the live tip; both the header declaration and the one call site (`MaterialiseSubmittedCommitmentBlock`) were updated. **Second candidate added, per the reviewer's own suggestion**: the currently-known-best commitment for a quorum session can itself change between template creation and submission (a better one arrives via `AddMineableCommitment`), so `GetMineableCommitmentTx`'s CURRENT answer can stop matching what was actually mined even once the height is right. A new `CQuorumBlockProcessor::GetNullCommitmentTx` (`llmq/quorums_blockprocessor.h`/`.cpp`) mirrors `GetMineableCommitment`'s own "null commitment required" branch exactly, but always takes it -- bypassing the currently-known-best lookup entirely -- so the deterministic null form a template may have originally used is still reconstructible even after a real commitment supersedes it in the live cache. `FindMineableCommitmentTxByHash` tries `GetMineableCommitmentTx` first, then `GetNullCommitmentTx`, per enabled quorum param set, before giving up. **Miss error message no longer blames only the mempool**: "not found in local mempool" became "not found in local mempool and not a currently mineable quorum commitment for this block's parent" (substring-compatible with every pre-existing test that checked the old wording). **TDD, genuine RED first**: wrote `test/submitblock_tests.cpp:commitment_format_resolves_a_stale_sibling_after_the_tip_moved` against the UNFIXED code first -- builds a candidate at the current tip, then a DIFFERENT block (same parent) wins the race and becomes the real tip, then submits the stale candidate in commitment format -- confirmed genuine RED (an uncaught `UniValue`, the pre-fix "not found" error) before writing the fix. After the fix: GREEN, but deliberately does not assert the tip becomes the stale candidate or that the result is JSON null -- equal-work siblings tie-break by first-seen (BIP22's own "inconclusive" for a block accepted into the index but never run through full `ConnectBlock`, not a rejection), which is unrelated to this bug; the assertion instead is that the block is genuinely indexed and not `BLOCK_FAILED_MASK`, which could not happen if resolution had thrown. A second test, `commitment_format_resolves_after_a_better_commitment_supersedes_the_null_one`, isolates the null-commitment-fallback candidate specifically: stages a real `AddMineableCommitment` call between template creation and submission for the SAME quorum session, confirming `GetMineableCommitmentTx`'s current answer no longer matches and the null-form fallback is what resolves it. **Mutation-checked both pieces**: (1) the tip-relative revert (`pindexPrev = ::ChainActive().Tip()` restored) made the stale-sibling test fail genuinely, confirmed, then restored; (2) the null-commitment-fallback branch commented out made the second test fail genuinely, confirmed, then restored. `test/submitblock_tests.cpp`: 5 cases before this fix, 8 after (all green). Full suite: 623 cases, only the one pre-existing `miner_tests` failure (`CreateNewBlock_validity`/`bad-blk-sigops`, same line F-192/F-193/F-194 already documented) | `source`, direct read of the tip-relative code before writing the fix; TDD (genuine RED reproducing the reviewer's own described mechanism, then GREEN); mutation-checked both the height-fix and the null-commitment fallback separately, each reverted and reconfirmed; full suite green except the one pre-existing, independently reconfirmed failure | **FIXED 2026-09-27, closed same day as found** |
| F-196 | **Independent adversarial review of F-194 found two gaps in the servebodyrange serving-obligation gate: (A) its own test never exercised the flag's PRODUCTION DEFAULT and its restore logic left the flag permanently SET; (B) the gate covered only `getblocktemplate`, leaving `submitblock`'s own commitment-mode path (F-192/F-193) reachable without ever negotiating a commitment-mode template first. Both fixed before merge, same day** | **Part A (test hygiene, no production bug)**: the original `enforce_commitment_mode_serving_obligation_refuses_without_servebodyrange` (`miner_tests.cpp`) always called `gArgs.ForceSetArg("-servebodyrange", "0")` before checking the throw -- it never actually exercised what every normal node runs with (the flag simply absent), so a silent regression to the default itself (e.g. `GetBoolArg("-servebodyrange", true)`) would not have been caught. **Fixed**: added `BOOST_REQUIRE(!gArgs.IsArgSet("-servebodyrange"))` up front (so the truly-default case means what it claims), and a new `BOOST_CHECK_EXCEPTION` for that truly-default case -- no `ForceSetArg` at all -- ahead of the pre-existing explicit `"0"` case. The test's own restore logic was also wrong: `gArgs.ForceSetArg("-servebodyrange", fPrevServeBodyRange ? "1" : "0")` always leaves the arg SET, even when it started unset -- confirmed by direct read of `ArgsManager` (`util/system.h`/`.cpp`): `ForceSetArg` can only ever leave a value present, while `ForceRemoveArg` (already existing in this codebase, unused by this test) actually erases the override, restoring "unset". Fixed to `gArgs.ForceRemoveArg("-servebodyrange")` plus a closing `BOOST_REQUIRE(!gArgs.IsArgSet(...))` proving the suite's global state genuinely returns to how it started. **Part B (real gap, a production fix)**: `EnforceCommitmentModeServingObligation` was only ever called from `getblocktemplate` -- but the serving obligation (§9.1a/§14.10) is incurred by SUBMITTING a commitment-mode block, not by requesting one, and `submitblock format="commitment"` (F-192) is reachable without ever calling a commitment-mode `getblocktemplate` first: a client can build its own `CCommitmentBlock` from an ordinary full-mode template, or poll one node and submit to a different one -- normal pool topology, confirmed against this codebase's own design (nothing ties a `submitblock` call to a prior `getblocktemplate` call on the same node). **Fixed**: `EnforceCommitmentModeServingObligation` gained an `rpcName` parameter (`rpc/mining.h`/`.cpp`) generalizing the thrown message from the hardcoded "Commitment-mode getblocktemplate requires..." to "Commitment-mode %s requires...", and is now called from BOTH `getblocktemplate` and `submitblock`'s `"commitment"` format branch, before any resolution/materialisation work happens in either. Every pre-existing commitment-format test in `submitblock_tests.cpp` needed to opt in via a new `ServeBodyRangeGuard` RAII helper (`ForceSetArg`/`ForceRemoveArg`), since they were written to exercise resolution, not this gate; a new dedicated test, `commitment_format_refuses_without_servebodyrange`, stages every committed transaction into the mempool (so absent the gate the submission would fully succeed) and confirms the gate -- not a resolution failure -- is what blocks it, then confirms the SAME candidate succeeds once opted in. **Minor, done rather than deferred**: the reviewer's own lower-priority note -- the gate ran after `getblocktemplate`'s long-poll wait and `CreateNewBlock`'s cache-rebuild rather than failing fast right after `setClientRules` is parsed -- was a small, safe reordering, so it was done: `fCommitmentMode`/the gate call both moved to immediately after `setClientRules` is parsed, before the long-poll wait or `CreateNewBlock`. **TDD/mutation, both parts**: Part A -- reverting the restore-logic fix (old `ForceSetArg` form) made the new closing assertion fail genuinely (RED), confirmed, then restored; separately, mutating the gate's own default (`GetBoolArg("-servebodyrange", true)`) made the new truly-default assertion fail genuinely (RED), confirmed the "silent default-flip regression" scenario the coordinator named, then restored. Part B -- commenting out the new `submitblock` call site made the new dedicated test fail genuinely (three assertion failures: no exception raised, the block wrongly accepted, an uncaught `UniValue` on the follow-up opted-in resubmission), confirmed, then restored. Full suite: 623 cases, only the one pre-existing `miner_tests` failure, unchanged | `source`, direct read of `ArgsManager`'s actual API (`ForceRemoveArg` vs `ForceSetArg`) before choosing the restore fix, not guessed; direct read confirming `submitblock` has no dependency on a prior `getblocktemplate` call; TDD (genuine RED for both the default-flip and the missing-gate scenarios, then GREEN); mutation-checked all three fixes (restore logic, default-flip coverage, the new `submitblock` call site) separately, each reverted and reconfirmed; full suite green except the one pre-existing, independently reconfirmed failure | **FIXED 2026-09-27, both parts closed same day as found** |

Re-derivation: build-plan.md's own "≥459 MB" (`:244`) traces to `perf-results.md`'s own log (`:1798-1801`): 520 tx/s (the 2 MB design point's own throughput, K-1/D-4) x K-12's 600 s = 312,000 transactions held in the mempool by the mining gate, at a **per-entry mempool RAM overhead of ~1,460 B for a 373 B transaction (a measured 3.9x, `perf-results.md` SS3, `CTxMemPool::DynamicMemoryUsage` accounting for twelve `boost::multi_index` pointers per entry plus `mapNextTx`/`mapLinks`/`mapDeltas`/`vTxHashes`)** -> ~455.5 MB at F-30's 373 B bench-rt mean, ~483 MB at its 397 B wan-12 mean -- ≥459 MB sits inside that range, reproduced to within ~1-2% rather than confirmed exactly, which is expected: F-30 records two corpus means for a reason, and the exact 459 figure was never meant to be more precise than that. **A real citation-discipline gap, not a math error**: the 3.9x/1,460 B-per-entry figure this whole derivation depends on has no F-number of its own in `findings.md` -- it lives only in `perf-results.md`'s prose log, and `build-plan.md`'s own "≥459 MB" is neither `findings.md` nor the log, which is exactly the citation pattern `findings.md`'s own preamble calls a bug ("A number appearing in prose outside this file and the log is a bug"). Recommend giving the 3.9x/1,460 B figure its own F-number so `build-plan.md`'s derived "≥459 MB" cites something, rather than resting on an unnumbered log entry. Separately reconfirmed `perf-results.md:1798-1801`'s own caveat: on mainnet AS CONFIGURED TODAY (spork 3 off) this gate is inert (`IsTxSafeForMining` returns true immediately once `RejectConflictingBlocks()` is false, `quorums_chainlocks.cpp:465-468`) -- consistent with, not contradicting, the owner's own standing scope note (`build-plan.md:227-232`) that design work is priced against sporks 2/3/19 all ON. **What needs to change, stated plainly (the row's own gap)**: nothing forces the 300 MB default itself to rise -- `-maxmempool` is already operator-overridable -- but nothing today ENFORCES that override before the mining gate goes live, either. Recommend the same pattern 4.4.3/F-194 already established for `-servebodyrange`: a REQUIRE, not a document -- refuse (or loudly warn) at spork-3-activation time if `-maxmempool` sits below the design floor, rather than leaving a silent OOM-adjacent misconfiguration for an operator to discover under load. §16.6's own "mempool growth is lumpy" point (hash-table doubling means a node at 90% of the cap crosses it in one step) argues for real headroom above 459 MB, not a value flush against it. **2. `-maxsigcachesize` -- the row's own unstated "what changes and why," now answered.** `DEFAULT_MAX_SIG_CACHE_SIZE = 32` (MB) and `MAX_MAX_SIG_CACHE_SIZE = 16384` (`script/sigcache.h:16,18`). TWO independent caches split this one budget in half (`min(maxsigcachesize,MAX)/2 * (1<<20)` bytes each): the per-transaction `scriptExecutionCache` (`validation.cpp:1564,1571-1573`) and the per-signature `signatureCache` (`script/sigcache.cpp:75-80`). `scriptExecutionCache`'s key is `SHA256(nonce \|\| tx.GetHash() \|\| flags)` (`validation.cpp:1616-1623`) -- confirmed ONE entry per TRANSACTION, not per input/signature -- so K-4's 524,288 entries (16 MB half / ~32 B per `CuckooCache` entry) is exactly `transaction-decoupling.md:478-480`'s own "8.4 blocks at 2 MB [524,288/62,500] and 2.1 blocks at 8 MB [524,288/250,000]" arithmetic, re-derived independently rather than re-quoted. This directly gates the warm/cold `ConnectBlock` differential F-190's own Q4 measured (0.8-3.2 s warm vs 16-78 s cold, F-15b/F-16): once a body-fetch backlog exceeds roughly two design-point blocks, the cache's own generational eviction (`cuckoocache.h:165-186`, a 3-epoch scheme targeting ~90% load, recency-preferring but not exact FIFO/LRU) will have aged out the oldest block's entries, and every subsequent commitment-block reconnect falls onto the COLD path. **The concrete fix**: `MAX_MAX_SIG_CACHE_SIZE` already permits headroom orders of magnitude past any design-point need, so this is a DEFAULT-VALUE and operator-guidance question, not a hard-ceiling change -- raise the default well past 32 MB, sized to hold several design-point (250,000-tx) blocks' worth of entries rather than the bare "8.4/2.1 blocks" figure, since epoch-based eviction isn't an exact FIFO and needs safety margin. Consensus-adjacent sizing, in `transaction-decoupling.md`'s own words, not "local tuning" -- which is precisely the "why" the row's own text omitted. **3. `BLOCKTXN` chunking -- a real, currently-unmitigated gap, NOT already handled by anything.** `SendBlockTransactions` (`net_processing.cpp:2423-2438`) builds every requested transaction into ONE `BlockTransactions` object and pushes it as a single `BLOCKTXN` message (`connman->PushMessage`), with ZERO size-based chunking and no check against `MAX_PROTOCOL_MESSAGE_LENGTH` (`net.h:81`, 3 MB, K-7) before sending. The receiving peer WILL disconnect on an oversized reply: confirmed directly, `net.cpp:663-665`, `if (msg.in_data && msg.hdr.nMessageSize > MAX_PROTOCOL_MESSAGE_LENGTH) { ...disconnecting }`. At this project's own design-point scale (F-30's ~373 B average), as few as ~8,000 missing transactions in one `GETBLOCKTXN` response already exceeds 3 MB, well short of a full 62,500- or 250,000-tx block -- a real, live-fire hazard at design throughput, not a corner case. Unlike F-190's own precedent (`MAX_INV_SZ` chunking already solving an analogous worry for a different message), no equivalent protection exists here: `MAX_BLOCKTXN_DEPTH` (checked at `net_processing.cpp:3608`) bounds REQUEST AGE only (falling back to a full-block response for a too-old target), never RESPONSE SIZE. **Recommend reusing that exact fallback shape**: before building the response, sum the requested transactions' serialized size; over a safe margin under `MAX_PROTOCOL_MESSAGE_LENGTH`, decline the `BLOCKTXN` and queue a full-block `CInv`/`GETDATA` fallback instead (`net_processing.cpp:3608-3624`'s own pattern), rather than inventing new multi-message continuation semantics for a wire message that has none today. This is real, scheduled work the row's own text folds silently into item 4's "~7 lines" -- it is not that. **4. Compact-block index widening -- the "62,501 fits `uint16_t`" claim CONFIRMED, and so is the trap it names.** `BlockTransactionsRequest::indexes` is `std::vector<uint16_t>` (`blockencodings.h:43`); `PrefilledTransaction::index` is `uint16_t` (`:74`, serialized via `COMPACTSIZE` -- the WIRE encoding is already a variable-length CompactSize, so the limit is purely the C++ field's own width, not the wire format); `CBlockHeaderAndShortTxIDs::BlockTxCount()` (`:114`) is checked at deserialization against `std::numeric_limits<uint16_t>::max()` = 65,535 (`:122-124`), throwing above it. 62,500 (or 62,501 counting the coinbase) IS under 65,535 -- confirmed true, and that is exactly the trap: at upstream's own `MAX_DIP0001_BLOCK_SIZE` (K-1, 2 MB), the design's commitment scale fits today with only ~4.6% headroom (3,035 of 65,535 slots) -- but this BRANCH already runs `MAX_DIP0001_BLOCK_SIZE = 8,000,000` (`perf-constants.md`'s own documented divergence), whose 250,000-identifier scale **exceeds 65,535 outright** -- a full 8 MB commitment block cannot be compact-block-encoded via this path AT ALL today; deserialization throws unconditionally. So "does 62,501 fit" resolves oppositely depending on which of this project's own two live block-size configurations is asked, and the type is fragile at exactly the margin the design's own throughput target (X-1, up to 2,083 tx/s) exists to grow past. **Recommend widening `PrefilledTransaction::index` and `BlockTransactionsRequest::indexes`'s element type** (matches the row's own ~7-line estimate: the two type declarations, the overflow guard's comparison target, `DifferenceFormatter`'s template parameter adapting automatically since it is templated on `I`) -- **but bind the overflow guard to the design's own already-decided consensus cap, `COMMITMENT_BUDGET_MAX_INPUTS = 700,000` (D-19, built in 1.2/F-98-F-99), not to whatever the new, wider type's max happens to allow.** A bare `uint32_t`/`size_t` widening alone reproduces the exact "silently fits because the TYPE happens to be big enough" hazard this item is warning about, just moved to a threshold nobody chose on purpose. **5. Relay in-flight constants -- mostly already closed, one piece explicitly flagged in-source as still missing.** `MAX_BLOCKS_IN_TRANSIT_PER_PEER = 16` (`validation.h:108`), the whole-block per-peer cap. This project's own post-decoupling analogue, `DEFAULT_MAX_BODYRANGE_INFLIGHT = 16` (`bodyrange.h:240`, deliberately matched to it per its own comment), governs `-maxbodyrangeinflight` -- but it is an AGGREGATE cap across ALL peers, and `bodyrange.h:232-234`'s own comment says so explicitly: **"not per-peer, a separate, still-unbuilt concern."** Stall/disconnect enforcement for a body-range fetch that never resolves is ALREADY BUILT: `BODY_RANGE_DISCONNECT_ATTEMPTS = 18` (`net_processing.cpp`, F-185, 4.1.4, built 2026-09-26) -- closing exactly the gap F-160's own 4.1 audit flagged as open ("a peer that persistently stalls...is never actually disconnected today"). So of what "relay in-flight constants" could mean here, the stall/disconnect half is CLOSED and the aggregate cap already exists matching precedent; what remains genuinely open, and is flagged as such in this project's own source comments rather than merely absent, is a PER-PEER body-range in-flight cap analogous to `MAX_BLOCKS_IN_TRANSIT_PER_PEER` -- a small, contained, well-precedented sub-step, not the ambiguous framing the row's own text left it in. Separately: ordinary tx-relay in-flight constants (`MAX_PEER_OBJECT_IN_FLIGHT = 100`, `MAX_PEER_OBJECT_ANNOUNCEMENTS = 2 * MAX_INV_SZ = 100,000`, `net_processing.cpp:71,74`) show no evidence of needing the re-indexing K-5 already got (F-138) -- F-4's own measured ~56-65 tx/s per-peer delivery under K-5's cap sits far below what either would bind on first. **6. Fee floor scaling with body bytes -- a scope correction, not a build item.** `transaction-decoupling.md:332-337` (SS1A.3) states the opposite of what the row implies: **"A fee floor scaling with body bytes is policy, which a miner-attacker ignores... the bound must be consensus."** The consensus bound that section calls for is ALREADY BUILT: `COMMITMENT_BUDGET_BODY_BYTES = 110,000,000` and `COMMITMENT_BUDGET_MAX_INPUTS = 700,000` (D-18/D-19, F-98/F-99, `build-plan.md`'s own 1.2 row -- "1.2 complete"). B5b (`transaction-decoupling.md:2167`), the ONE place in the design doc that names "a fee floor that scales with body bytes" as ITS OWN fix, is explicit that this addresses a DIFFERENT threat model (an ordinary relay-path spammer paying policy-level fees, not a miner assembling their own block, which policy cannot bind at all) and says outright: **"the real answer is the deferred incentive half."** That incentive half is part of SS8's retention/incentive scope, which the design doc's own "What is deliberately not here yet" section (`transaction-decoupling.md:191-194`) lists as **"deferred by owner decision"** -- and `build-plan.md` never schedules it anywhere; no phase past 5.4 exists in its own table. Mechanically, `DEFAULT_MIN_RELAY_TX_FEE = 1000` (`validation.h:83`, sat/kB) already produces a fee LINEAR in a transaction's own byte size via `CFeeRate::GetFee(nSize)` -- so in the literal sense a policy fee floor that "scales with body bytes" already exists and needs no new code today. What is actually missing is a RATE that prices in the asymmetric, PERMANENT replication cost SS8's full-retention design imposes (B5b's own framing: "~0.02 RTM in fees" buys "~525 GB/year of permanent storage on every Smartnode") -- a storage-incentive design question this project has explicitly deferred, not "1-2 week straightforward" constants-and-policy work. **Recommend dropping this item from 4.5's scope** (or reducing it to a one-line documentation note pointing at 1.2/D-18/D-19 as the actual, already-built consensus-level answer to B5b's severity), leaving the storage-incentive-rate question recorded as blocked on the owner's own SS8 scope decision, not scheduled here. **Sub-step breakdown**: **4.5.1 -- `maxmempool`/`-maxsigcachesize` default-and-enforcement pass** (items 1-2: raise both defaults past their re-derived floors; require, not merely document, `-maxmempool`'s design-point minimum once the mining gate is live, matching 4.4.3/F-194's own precedent) -- HIGH confidence, LOW effort. **4.5.2 -- compact-block index widening plus `BLOCKTXN` size-based fallback** (items 3-4: widen the index/count types, bind the overflow guard to `COMMITMENT_BUDGET_MAX_INPUTS`; add a serialized-size check to `SendBlockTransactions` that falls back to a full-block `GETDATA` via `MAX_BLOCKTXN_DEPTH`'s own existing pattern) -- HIGH confidence on the type-widening half, MEDIUM on the fallback-margin choice, MEDIUM effort overall (this is the one sub-step genuinely bigger than the row's own "~7 lines" framing implies, since item 3 was not previously scoped at all). **4.5.3 -- per-peer body-range in-flight cap** (item 5's one real remaining gap: build the still-explicitly-unbuilt per-peer analogue of `MAX_BLOCKS_IN_TRANSIT_PER_PEER`, alongside the existing aggregate `-maxbodyrangeinflight` cap and F-185's stall/disconnect logic) -- HIGH confidence, LOW-MEDIUM effort, mechanical, same shape as existing whole-block precedent. **Not sub-stepped -- pulled out of scope entirely**: item 6 (fee floor). **Effort estimate correction**: the row's own "1-2 weeks, straightforward" holds reasonably for 4.5.1 and 4.5.3; 4.5.2 is real work the row's "~7 lines" framing understates once `BLOCKTXN`'s own currently-nonexistent size safety is counted, though still plausibly within the original window if the fallback-reuse design is taken rather than inventing new wire continuation semantics;
