| F-199 | **4.5.1 built: `maxmempool`/`-maxsigcachesize` defaults raised past their re-derived floors with real headroom, and `-maxmempool`'s design-point minimum is now enforced -- WARNed, not REFUSEd -- once the mining gate is live** | **`DEFAULT_MAX_MEMPOOL_SIZE`** (`policy/policy.h:31`) raised 300 -> **1024 MB**. F-197/F-198's re-derived floor is 455.5-483 MB (520 tx/s x K-12's 600 s x F-198's 1,460 B/entry); §16.6's own "mempool growth is lumpy" point (hash-table doubling means a node at 90% of a cap sized flush against the floor crosses it in one step) argues against sizing close to that floor. 1024 MB (a full, memorable GB) sits ~2.1-2.25x above the re-derived range -- comfortable margin against one such doubling jump from anywhere below the floor -- while remaining a trivial fraction of any Smartnode-class box's RAM (X-5: 16 GB). A new named constant, **`MIN_MAX_MEMPOOL_SIZE_DESIGN_FLOOR = 459`** (`policy/policy.h`), keeps the standing, multiply-cited 459 MB anchor (`build-plan.md:244`, `perf-results.md:1799`) as the ENFORCEMENT floor, deliberately separate from the new default -- an operator's own override only needs to clear the re-derived floor, not match the generous shipped default. **`DEFAULT_MAX_SIG_CACHE_SIZE`** (`script/sigcache.h:16`) raised 32 -> **256 MB** (8x). F-197's own re-derivation: this budget splits in half between `signatureCache` and `scriptExecutionCache` (`validation.cpp:1571`, `script/sigcache.cpp:78`); `scriptExecutionCache` holds one ~32-byte `CuckooCache` entry per TRANSACTION (`validation.cpp:1616-1623`'s keying). At 32 MB total the 16 MB half held 524,288 entries, ~2.1 of this branch's own 250,000-tx design-point blocks (`transaction-decoupling.md:478-480`'s "2.1 blocks at 8 MB"). At 256 MB the 128 MB half holds 4,194,304 entries, **~16.8 design-point blocks** -- comfortable safety margin for `cuckoocache.h`'s epoch-based eviction (a 3-epoch scheme targeting ~90% load, recency-preferring but not exact FIFO/LRU, per F-197's own reasoning), while spending only ~1.6% of `MAX_MAX_SIG_CACHE_SIZE`'s 16,384 MB ceiling -- the ceiling itself is untouched, this is a default-value change only, no consensus/validation logic touched (script/sigcache.cpp, validation.cpp's cache-sizing arithmetic both unchanged). **The REQUIRE gate, matching 4.4.3/F-194's own precedent in spirit ("require, not merely document") but WARN rather than REFUSE, a deliberate, considered departure**: new `MaxMempoolBelowDesignFloor(fMiningGateLive, nMaxMempoolBytes)` (pure decision, `validation.h`/`.cpp`) plus `WarnIfMaxMempoolBelowDesignFloor(...)` (logging glue, one-time-per-process via `SetMiscWarning`/`warnings.h` -- surfaced through `getnetworkinfo` -- plus `LogPrintf`), called from `ConnectBlock`'s own existing `if (llmq::RejectConflictingBlocks())` block (`validation.cpp`, the exact "is spork 3 active and synced" check `IsTxSafeForMining`/the ChainLocks safety walk already consult, `quorums_instantsend.cpp:1703-1711`). **Verified, not assumed, that this is the right integration point, against the coordinator's own explicit prompt to check `AppInitParameterInteraction`/`init.cpp` first**: `CSporkManager::IsSporkActive` (`spork.cpp:210-220`) compares a spork's value against `GetAdjustedTime()` on every call, and that value is set at runtime by a relayed `MSG_SPORK` message (`net_processing.cpp:4750` -> `ProcessSpork`, `spork.cpp:106`) -- spork 3's activation is a live, P2P-driven condition, never known or fixed at startup. A check placed in `AppInitParameterInteraction` would read the compiled-in OFF default at every node's boot (`spork.h`'s `sporkDefs`, `4070908800` = year 2099) and could never observe a LATER network-wide activation -- an operator would pass a one-time startup check trivially and then silently cross into the unenforced regime the moment the network flips the spork, with no code path left to catch it. `ConnectBlock` runs this check on every node, mining or not, on every connected block, matching "once the mining gate is live" far more literally than a single startup check could. **WARN, not REFUSE, because the operational shape differs from `-servebodyrange`'s**: F-194's gate sits at a single, retryable RPC-request boundary (`getblocktemplate`/`submitblock`) where refusing costs the caller one failed call and nothing else. This condition is instead evaluated on a consensus-critical path with no caller to retry -- REFUSE there would mean either aborting an already-running, already-synced node the instant the network flips a spork (turning a local memory-tuning problem into an availability incident) or rejecting an otherwise-valid block over a misconfiguration unrelated to that block's own validity, both disproportionate to what is a gradual OOM risk, not an instantaneous exploit. **TDD, genuine RED first**: `max_mempool_below_design_floor_only_when_gate_live_and_size_short` (`miner_tests.cpp`) written against the undeclared function first -- failed to LINK (`undefined reference to MaxMempoolBelowDesignFloor`), matching F-191/F-192's own established link-time-RED convention for a header-declared-then-defined function -- then GREEN once implemented. Covers: gate not live never flags regardless of size; gate live and strictly below the floor flags; gate live and exactly at the floor does not (boundary); gate live and above the floor does not; the new shipped default itself never trips the gate once live, tying the two 4.5.1 deliverables together. **Mutation-checked the gate's own critical logic, per this project's standing instruction**: (1) the comparison operator (`<` swapped for `<=`) -- caught by the exact-floor boundary case; (2) the `fMiningGateLive &&` guard removed entirely -- caught by the gate-not-live case. Both reverted, confirmed genuine failures, then restored. **`WarnIfMaxMempoolBelowDesignFloor` itself has no dedicated test**: it is thin logging glue (`LogPrintf`/`SetMiscWarning`) with no log-capture harness anywhere in this codebase (grepped, confirmed) -- matching this project's own established convention for untested logging-only wiring (F-99's "R-31 precedent", F-164/F-165's own reasoning for undertested dispatch glue); the decision it depends on (`MaxMempoolBelowDesignFloor`) carries the real test and mutation coverage. Full clean rebuild; full suite green except the one pre-existing `miner_tests` failure (`CreateNewBlock_validity`/`bad-blk-sigops`, same line every prior 4.4/4.5 row has already documented) | `source`, direct read of `spork.cpp`/`net_processing.cpp` confirming spork activation is runtime-only before choosing the integration point (a real verification against the coordinator's own suggested default, not an assumption); TDD (genuine link-failure RED, then GREEN); mutation-checked both critical branches of the decision function, each reverted and reconfirmed; full suite green except the one pre-existing, independently reconfirmed failure | **4.5.1 built 2026-09-27** |
| F-200 | **4.5.2 Part A built: `PrefilledTransaction::index`/`BlockTransactionsRequest::indexes` widened `uint16_t` -> `uint32_t`; the deserialize-time overflow guard rebound to `COMMITMENT_BUDGET_MAX_INPUTS` (D-19); two further `uint16_t` call sites the ~7-line estimate never counted found and fixed in the same pass** | Confirmed by direct read, not assumed (per F-197's own "verify, don't assume" instruction): both fields serialize through variable-length CompactSize regardless of the C++ field's own width -- `PrefilledTransaction::index` via `COMPACTSIZE`/`CompactSizeFormatter::Ser`/`Unser` (`serialize.h`, calls `WriteCompactSize`/`ReadCompactSize` over `uint64_t`), `BlockTransactionsRequest::indexes` via `DifferenceFormatter` (`blockencodings.h`, same `WriteCompactSize`/`ReadCompactSize` primitives). Widening is wire-format-neutral: no protocol version negotiation implication, closing instruction #7's own check. `DifferenceFormatter`'s template parameter `I` does adapt automatically, confirmed (it is templated on the vector's own element type) -- no explicit change needed there, matching F-197's own claim. The `BlockTxCount()` deserialize-time overflow guard (`blockencodings.h`) is rebound from `std::numeric_limits<uint16_t>::max()` to `COMMITMENT_BUDGET_MAX_INPUTS` (700,000, D-19) exactly as directed, not to the widened type's own incidental max. **Two further sites found while implementing, beyond F-197's own cited lines, that its "~7 lines" estimate didn't count**: (1) `PartiallyDownloadedBlock::InitData`'s own `lastprefilledindex` bound check (`blockencodings.cpp`) was ALSO hardcoded to `uint16_t`'s max. **Correction (F-202, review): the original claim here that this was "the ONLY thing bounding an INDIVIDUAL `PrefilledTransaction::index` VALUE" is false** -- a second, independent bound sits one line below it in the same loop (`if ((uint32_t) lastprefilledindex > cmpctblock.shorttxids.size() + i) return READ_STATUS_INVALID;`, pre-existing, unchanged by this fix), which already indirectly bounded any individual index value via `shorttxids.size() + i`. What this fix actually closed is narrower but still real: that pre-existing bound was itself gated by the OLD `uint16_t`-max check on the same variable, and the widened `uint32_t` field could otherwise carry a value up to `uint32_t`'s own max. **Correction (F-202, review): not ~4.29 billion** -- `PrefilledTransaction::index` is wire-encoded via `COMPACTSIZE` (`serialize.h`'s `CompactSizeFormatter`), and `ReadCompactSize` rejects anything over its own `MAX_SIZE` (`serialize.h`, `0x02000000` = ~33.5 million) before the value ever reaches the field -- so the real incidental ceiling this fix closed was ~33.5 million, not `uint32_t`'s full ~4.29 billion range. Either way, reopening exactly the "fits because the type happens to be big enough" trap this widening exists to close, just at a different call site than the one F-197 named. Rebound to `COMMITMENT_BUDGET_MAX_INPUTS` too, computed in `int64_t` so the accumulation itself cannot overflow before the bound check runs. (2) `InitData`'s own position-lookup bookkeeping (`std::unordered_map<uint64_t, uint16_t> shorttxids`, `uint16_t index_offset`, two `iterator` declarations) indexes into `txn_available`, whose size is now up to 700,000 -- left at `uint16_t`, these would silently wrap for any block needing more than 65,535 transactions, corrupting compact-block reconstruction for exactly the large blocks the widening exists to support. | F-202 | **Independent adversarial review of F-200/F-201 found two real bugs (one production, one doc-only) and redesigned 4.5.2 Part B's fallback: F-201's full-block fallback was provably pointless-to-regressive, and F-200's `BlockTxCount()` guard was off by one at the commitment-budget boundary** | **1. Off-by-one (production bug, F-200).** `BlockTxCount()`'s deserialize-time guard (`blockencodings.h`) bound itself to bare `COMMITMENT_BUDGET_MAX_INPUTS`, but that constant bounds aggregate NON-coinbase input count (`consensus/tx_verify.h`'s `GetBlockInputCount`, confirmed by direct read: `if (tx->IsCoinBase()) continue;`), not total transaction count, while `BlockTxCount()` counts every transaction including the coinbase. The true maximum is `COMMITMENT_BUDGET_MAX_INPUTS` one-input non-coinbase transactions (the input-minimizing, tx-count-maximizing shape) plus exactly one coinbase = `COMMITMENT_BUDGET_MAX_INPUTS + 1` total transactions -- confirmed against `PartiallyDownloadedBlock::InitData`'s own `candidateindex` bound (`blockencodings.cpp`, pre-existing, unchanged), which already correctly permits a 0-based index up to `COMMITMENT_BUDGET_MAX_INPUTS` (the `(COMMITMENT_BUDGET_MAX_INPUTS + 1)`-th transaction) -- the header-level guard disagreed with it by exactly one. Fixed: bound to `COMMITMENT_BUDGET_MAX_INPUTS + 1`. **2. Fallback redesign (production bug, F-201).** F-201's fix declined an oversized `BLOCKTXN` and fell back to a full-block `CInv{MSG_BLOCK}` `GETDATA`. This is provably pointless: the requested set is a SUBSET of `block.vtx`, so the full block serializes to at least as many bytes as the declined subset -- if the subset didn't fit, the full block provably doesn't either, so the disconnect the fallback existed to avoid happens anyway, one round trip later. Worse, it was actively regressive: F-201's hardcoded 2 MB ceiling had ~1 MiB of margin to spare under the real 3 MB wire limit (`MAX_PROTOCOL_MESSAGE_LENGTH`, K-7) -- a request in the (2 MB, 3 MB] range that would have fit fine under the OLD, pre-4.5.2 unbounded behaviour now got declined and replaced with a full block that can itself exceed 3 MB, disconnecting this node in a case it previously handled fine. Concrete repro (reviewer's own): a 3,576,716 B block, a 2,414,195 B subset request (fits under a properly-derived ceiling), served as the oversized full block anyway by the pre-fix logic. Fixed, two parts: (a) `MAX_BLOCKTXN_RESPONSE_BYTES` is now derived (`MAX_PROTOCOL_MESSAGE_LENGTH - MAX_BLOCKTXN_RESPONSE_MARGIN`, margin = 4096 B, matching `DEFAULT_MAX_BODYRANGE_BYTES`'s own margin precedent in the same file) rather than a hardcoded constant with no relation to the real wire limit -- a `static_assert` confirms the margin covers the real fixed overhead (32 B blockhash + up to 5 B CompactSize length prefix, ~37 B), and the ceiling now tracks `MAX_PROTOCOL_MESSAGE_LENGTH` automatically if K-7 raises it later (build-plan.md 1.4/4.6), removing a second constant that would otherwise need to be kept in step by hand; (b) on a genuine decline (over the real, derived ceiling), `SendBlockTransactions` no longer queues any fallback -- it logs and returns, mirroring this same function's own existing "peer sent us a getblocktxn for a block we don't have" branch exactly. Verified this is safe, not merely convenient: the requester already marks the block as in-flight (`MarkBlockAsInFlight`, `net_processing.cpp`, called before `GETBLOCKTXN` is ever sent) before sending the request, so the existing generic block-download stall/timeout machinery (`BLOCK_STALLING_TIMEOUT`, `BLOCK_DOWNLOAD_TIMEOUT_BASE`/`_PER_PEER`, `validation.h`, checked in `SendMessages`) already recovers a `GETBLOCKTXN` that never gets answered, the same way it recovers any other block that never arrives -- no new requester-side machinery needed. **3. Two doc-accuracy corrections to F-200's own row** (findings.md): the claim that `InitData`'s `candidateindex` bound was "the ONLY thing bounding an individual `PrefilledTransaction::index` value" is false -- a second, independent, pre-existing bound (`if ((uint32_t) lastprefilledindex > cmpctblock.shorttxids.size() + i)`, one line below, unchanged by F-200) already did so too; and the "~4.29 billion" incidental-cap claim is corrected to ~33.5 million (`ReadCompactSize`'s own `MAX_SIZE = 0x02000000`, `serialize.h`), since `PrefilledTransaction::index` is wire-encoded via `COMPACTSIZE` and `ReadCompactSize` rejects anything over `MAX_SIZE` before the value ever reaches the field -- `uint32_t`'s own full range was never actually reachable over the wire. **TDD**: genuine RED confirmed for both production fixes against the pre-fix source before either was applied (unexpected exceptions on the off-by-one's two new tests; wrong ceiling/fallback shape on the redesign's two new tests). **Mutation-checked**: the off-by-one's `+ 1` reverted -> caught by `BlockTxCountAtCommitmentBudgetMaxInputsPlusOneWorks` and `MaximalCommitmentBudgetBlockWithCoinbaseInitializesOk`; the derived ceiling reverted to a hardcoded 2 MB -> caught by `getblocktxn_between_old_and_new_ceiling_now_answers_with_blocktxn`; the full-block fallback re-added to the decline branch -> caught by `getblocktxn_over_derived_ceiling_declines_without_full_block_fallback`. All three reverted individually, confirmed to fail the intended test(s) only, then restored. `blocktxn_tests`: `getblocktxn_over_ceiling_falls_back_to_full_block` (asserted the old, flawed behaviour) replaced with the two tests above. `blockencodings_tests`: `BlockTxCountOverCommitmentBudgetMaxInputsThrows` replaced with `BlockTxCountAtCommitmentBudgetMaxInputsPlusOneWorks`/`BlockTxCountOverCommitmentBudgetMaxInputsPlusOneThrows`/`MaximalCommitmentBudgetBlockWithCoinbaseInitializesOk` | `source`, TDD (genuine RED against the pre-fix source for both production bugs, confirmed before either fix landed), mutation-tested all three behavioural changes (off-by-one, ceiling derivation, fallback removal) individually | **built 2026-09-28** |
| F-203 | **4.5.3 built: the per-peer body-range in-flight cap closes `bodyrange.h`'s own explicitly-flagged gap -- new `net_processing.cpp:MAX_BODYRANGE_INFLIGHT_PER_PEER`, value `DEFAULT_MAX_BODYRANGE_INFLIGHT / 4` = 4, composes with (not replaces) the existing aggregate cap and F-185's stall/disconnect logic** | Mirrors `validation.h:MAX_BLOCKS_IN_TRANSIT_PER_PEER`'s own decision shape, confirmed by direct read of its enforcement site (`net_processing.cpp`'s `FindNextBlocksToDownload`: `if (nodestate->nBlocksInFlight >= MAX_BLOCKS_IN_TRANSIT_PER_PEER) break;`) before writing anything, per F-197's own "verify, don't assume" instruction. **Data structure**: new `net_processing.cpp:nBodyRangePeerInFlight`, a per-peer `CNodeState` `int` field mirroring the pre-existing `nBlocksInFlight` field -- not a new map, since the existing `mapBodyRangeInFlight`/`nBodyRangeInFlight` pair already has a single erase choke point (F-157) that can maintain a third counter alongside the two it already does, and every entry already carries its owning peer (the existing `net_processing.cpp:BodyRangeInFlight` struct's own `peer` field). **Wired at `bodyrange.h:ShouldRequestBodyRange`'s own decision point**, which gained `nPeerInFlight`/`nMaxPeerInFlight` parameters checked immediately after the per-(peer,hash) eligibility (announced + backoff) and before the pre-existing aggregate cap -- the two caps are independent ANDed conditions, proven by 3 new dedicated `bodyrange_tests.cpp` cases (peer-cap-saturated-with-aggregate-room refuses; one-below-peer-cap allows; aggregate-saturated-under-peer-cap still refuses even though the peer's own count is low). **Cap value, a genuine judgment call (F-197 left it unspecified)**: `net_processing.cpp:MAX_BODYRANGE_INFLIGHT_PER_PEER = DEFAULT_MAX_BODYRANGE_INFLIGHT / 4 = 4`, pinned by a `static_assert` bounding it strictly between 0 and the aggregate default so a future change to either constant forces this ratio to be reconsidered rather than silently drifting. Reasoning: this project's own single-source design already limits a peer to at most one in-flight request per distinct block hash (`bodyrange.h`'s own doc), so the cap only ever binds when one peer is simultaneously the chosen announcer for several distinct blocks at once; a quarter of the aggregate guarantees at least 4 distinct peers can have requests in flight simultaneously before any one is ever refused purely for its own share -- bounding a single peer's worst-case fraction of a budget that, unlike `MAX_BLOCKS_IN_TRANSIT_PER_PEER`'s own un-shared 16-of-16 ratio (no aggregate cap exists for whole-block download to share), is genuinely shared across peers. **Composition with F-185 (4.1.4's stall/disconnect logic), verified not assumed**: `FindNextBlocksToDownload`'s own per-peer cap needs no accompanying disconnect handling -- hitting it just means "wait for a slot to free," confirmed by direct read of its enforcement site, which only `break`s out of the fetch loop. The new per-peer body-range cap behaves identically: a peer sitting at its own cap is simply not asked for more until an existing request resolves; F-185's `bodyrange.h:ShouldDisconnectForBodyRangeAttempts` remains the sole disconnect path, keyed on per-(peer,hash) attempt count independent of whether the reason a request hasn't been retried yet is ordinary backoff or the peer's own cap -- no new disconnect logic added. **`net_processing.cpp:EraseBodyRangeInFlight` extended, not duplicated**: now also decrements the departing entry's own peer's `nBodyRangePeerInFlight`, at the same single choke point F-157 already established for the aggregate/map pair -- the identical "a future edit desyncs a counter" hazard applies to a third counter exactly as it did to the first two. Moved to after `CNodeState::State()`'s own definition (previously defined earlier in the file) since it now needs `State()` to reach the departing entry's peer. A new `assert(state->nBodyRangePeerInFlight == 0)` in `net_processing.cpp:PeerLogicValidation::FinalizeNode`, immediately after the existing per-peer `mapBodyRangeInFlight` sweep, proves by construction that a finalizing peer can never leave a nonzero per-peer count behind. **TDD, genuine RED confirmed, not assumed**: with the test file at its new (post-fix) version, the four production files (`bodyrange.cpp`/`.h`, `net_processing.cpp`) were reverted to this branch's own base commit (`5d108d4e6`) and rebuilt -- 9 genuine compile errors (`too many arguments to function 'ShouldRequestBodyRange'`, `test/bodyrange_tests.cpp`), a legitimate RED for a signature change, matching F-192/F-201's own established "revert production, keep tests" pattern for this project. Production restored, rebuilt clean (GREEN). **Mutation-checked**: the new per-peer check (`bodyrange.cpp:ShouldRequestBodyRange`'s `if (nPeerInFlight >= nMaxPeerInFlight) return false;`) disabled via `if (false && ...)`, rebuilt, reran `bodyrange_tests` -- exactly the one dedicated boundary test (`should_request_refuses_once_the_peer_cap_is_saturated_even_with_aggregate_room`) failed, all 65 others stayed green, confirmed the mutation was real before restoring and rebuilding clean again. `bodyrange_tests`: 63 -> 66 cases. Full suite: 639 cases, exactly the one pre-existing, independently reconfirmed `miner_tests`/`bad-blk-sigops` failure (`test/miner_tests.cpp:408`, same line F-192/F-194/F-195/F-201 already documented) | `source`, direct read of `MAX_BLOCKS_IN_TRANSIT_PER_PEER`'s own enforcement site and F-185's own mechanism before designing anything; TDD (genuine compile-failure RED via a targeted production revert, then GREEN); mutation-tested the new check specifically, reverted and reconfirmed, then restored; full suite green except the one pre-existing, independently reconfirmed failure | **built 2026-09-28** |
| F-204 | **4.5.1 corrective: an independent review of F-199's build found three real gaps -- a mutation-coverage gap at `ConnectBlock`'s own call site, a missing startup half of the WARN-vs-REFUSE decision, and the runtime WARN not using this codebase's own established two-part warning convention. All three fixed; two previously-unrecorded measured costs from F-199's raised defaults also written down here** | **1. Mutation-coverage gap, fixed.** `ConnectBlock`'s call site (`validation.cpp`) passed a hardcoded `/*fMiningGateLive=*/true` literal instead of the REAL `llmq::RejectConflictingBlocks()` result -- so `MaxMempoolBelowDesignFloor`'s own pure-function test (F-199) exercised the parameter, but nothing exercised whether the call site's own guard (`if (llmq::RejectConflictingBlocks())`) and its argument actually agreed, because in every un-mutated execution they trivially did (the guard already filters to the true case). The meaningful re-check instead reapplies the actual disease -- hoist AND revert the argument to a hardcoded `true` -- and the new test DOES catch it (`GetWarnings` shows a warning during the gate-not-live phase, when none should exist): `miner_tests.cpp:1003`'s check fails exactly as expected, confirmed then reverted. **2. Startup half of WARN-vs-REFUSE, added.** F-199's own reasoning for skipping `AppInitParameterInteraction`/`init.cpp` assumed spork 3's activation state could never be known at startup -- true for a node's FIRST activation-while-running, but not for a node RESTARTING after spork 3 has already activated network-wide: `sporks.dat` persists `mapSporksActive` (`CSporkManager::Serialize`, `spork.h:208-225`) and is loaded before anything else at `init.cpp`'s own Step 7a (`AppInitMain`, "Load sporks"), well before any block connects. **Added**: a startup check right after Step 7a's `flatdb6.Load(sporkManager)` succeeds (`init.cpp`) -- reuses `MaxMempoolBelowDesignFloor` (F-199's own already-tested pure decision, no new decision logic written or needed) with `sporkManager.IsSporkActive(SPORK_3_INSTANTSEND_BLOCK_FILTERING)` alone as `fMiningGateLive`, deliberately NOT the full `llmq::RejectConflictingBlocks()` -- `smartnodeSync.IsBlockchainSynced()` is always false this early in startup (confirmed live, below) and would make the check permanently inert if required. On a true hit, `InitError` fires in the exact SHAPE of the pre-existing `-maxmempool must be at least %d MB` refusal (`init.cpp`, `AppInitParameterInteraction`) -- a genuine startup refusal, zero availability cost since the node has not started serving anything yet. The pre-existing runtime WARN (`ConnectBlock`) is UNCHANGED in scope, kept exactly for the case it alone can still catch: a node already running when spork 3 first activates live via a relayed spork message. **Verified live, not just by inspection**: built a real regtest node (`-sporkkey` set to this project's own well-known regtest key, `test_framework.py:626`, matching chain params' regtest spork address), activated `SPORK_3_INSTANTSEND_BLOCK_FILTERING` via the real `spork` RPC, stopped it cleanly (writing `sporks.dat`), restarted with `-maxmempool=100` (above the pre-existing 5 MB floor, below the 459 MB design floor) -- got the new `InitError` and a clean, immediate exit, confirmed by exit code; restarting instead with `-maxmempool=1024` started normally. Separately confirmed on a fresh datadir (spork 3 off) that a small `-maxmempool` starts fine, and that a bare, peerless regtest node does not on its own advance `smartnodeSync` past `SMARTNODE_SYNC_BLOCKCHAIN` even after activating spork 3 and mining blocks -- independent, live confirmation that the new unit test's use of `SwitchToNextAsset` to force the sync half was necessary, not a shortcut around something that happens for free. Not committed as an automated test -- matches this project's own established precedent that `init.cpp`-level startup checks are unit-tested nowhere in this codebase (the pre-existing `-maxmempool must be at least` refusal itself has zero test coverage, grepped, confirmed); the DECISION is already covered by F-199's own `MaxMempoolBelowDesignFloor` test, and the `init.cpp` call site is thin, once-at-boot glue matching the same "dispatch glue verified by direct reading" convention F-191 established for a comparable case. **3. Runtime WARN now uses this codebase's own established two-part warning shape.** `WarnIfMaxMempoolBelowDesignFloor` called `SetMiscWarning` alone; this codebase's own precedent for exactly this shape is `DoWarning` (`validation.cpp:3099`, file-static, previously unused anywhere -- confirmed by grep, zero call sites before this fix): `SetMiscWarning` plus a one-shot `AlertNotify` (so `-alertnotify` fires and `NotifyAlertChanged` refreshes the GUI status bar promptly, rather than waiting on some unrelated later warning to trip `DoWarning`'s own path). Directly reusable as-is, no adaptation needed: `DoWarning` is a plain `static void(const std::string&)` in the SAME translation unit as `WarnIfMaxMempoolBelowDesignFloor` (both in `validation.cpp`), generic, not `ConnectBlock`-specific -- only a forward declaration was needed (added near this file's own existing forward-declaration block, matching its established convention) since `DoWarning`'s definition sits later in the file. **Not separately unit-tested**: `DoWarning`'s own one-shot `fWarned` flag is shared, process-lifetime, across ALL its callers (not per warning-type) -- asserting on `-alertnotify`'s actual firing would be order-dependent on whatever else in the same test binary run happens to call `DoWarning` first (e.g. `CheckForkWarningConditions`), and `AlertNotify` itself spawns a detached, unjoined thread, both of which make a dedicated assertion flaky rather than informative; `SetMiscWarning`'s own effect is already exercised by the new test in item 1. This observation (the shared one-shot flag) is pre-existing `DoWarning` behaviour, unchanged by this fix, noted here rather than altered, since changing it was not asked for and is not contained to this fix's own scope. **4. Two measured costs from F-199's raised defaults, recorded, not previously written down anywhere.** The reviewer measured `DEFAULT_MAX_SIG_CACHE_SIZE=256`'s eager, unconditional `CuckooCache::setup` full-size `table.resize` (`InitSignatureCache`/`InitScriptExecutionCache`) costing **+231 MB committed RSS at every node's boot** (regtest measurement: 344,168 kB RSS at the new 256 MB default vs. 112,952 kB at `-maxsigcachesize=32`), and `DEFAULT_MAX_MEMPOOL_SIZE=1024`'s bigger ceiling adding roughly **+724 MB of additional coins-cache flush-budget headroom** via `GetCoinsCacheSizeState`'s `maxmempool - mempoolUsage` term (300 MB -> 1024 MB is a 724 MB widening of that term directly, an arithmetic consequence of the default change rather than a new measurement). Recorded plainly per this project's own citation discipline, neither previously written down in `findings.md` nor `perf-results.md`. **Not proposing a default change over this**: both costs are real but small next to X-5's own 16 GB Smartnode-class RAM floor (231 MB is ~1.4%, and the 724 MB flush-budget widening only ever matters when the coins cache is already under memory pressure, at which point more flush headroom is the safer failure mode, not a hazard) -- flagged for the record and for a future reviewer's own judgment, not acted on unilaterally here, since the coordinator's own instruction was explicit that shrinking the defaults over this is a separate call.
