The `UpdateManager` bit -- D-14 already decided to bundle it, and the codebase already has a live, tested precedent for exactly that shape; only one of the three bundled items has zero scaffolding.** `update/update.h:337-370`'s `UpdateManager` carries no payload beyond a name and its own voting parameters (`bit, roundSize, startHeight, votingPeriod, votingMaxRounds, graceRounds, forcedUpdate, minerThreshold, nodeThreshold`, `update.h:79-169`, bit-range checked at `update.h:100`, `bit < 0 || bit > 28`) -- semantics live entirely in call-site code gated on `IsActive()`, never in the `Update` object. Not novel territory: `EUpdate::ROUND_VOTING` (bit 1, `chainparams.cpp:212-213`) already gates two independently-named behaviours under one bit today -- DKG round-voting format (`llmq/quorums_dkgsessionmgr.cpp:372`, `quorums_dkgsession.cpp:152,210,1011,1266`) and quorum-commitment version (`llmq/quorums_commitment.cpp:92`) -- confirming "one bit gates several unrelated call sites" is precedented, shipped machinery, not a novel risk 4.6 would be first to try. D-14 (`findings.md`) already settled the bundling QUESTION as an owner decision ("one fork: format, the 8 MB cap and the message-length raise ride the same activation; 2 MB is then mined by policy"), so this audit does not re-litigate whether to bundle -- it audits whether the bundle is mechanically sound and what it concretely gates. **Two of the three items already share one TEST-ONLY flag doing exactly this job**: `g_commitmentBudgetActive` (`validation.h:212`, `validation.cpp:128`, `std::atomic<bool>{false}`) already gates block size (`MaxBlockSize`, `consensus/consensus.h:52-55`, `miner.cpp:111`, `validation.cpp:4589`), input count (`MaxBlockInputs`, `consensus/consensus.h:64-66`, `miner.cpp:322,536`, `validation.cpp:4367,4616`), sigops (`MaxBlockSigOps`, `miner.cpp:306`, `validation.cpp:2618,4612`), the commitment-count budget itself (`validation.cpp:4482`, reusing D-19's `COMMITMENT_BUDGET_MAX_INPUTS`), AND commitment-block validation (`CheckCommitmentBlock`/`ContextualCheckCommitmentBlock`, `validation.cpp:5020`) -- five behaviours under one flag today, already wider than the row's own three-item list -- and `validation.cpp:5004-5006`'s own comment says so explicitly: "Gated by g_commitmentBudgetActive, matching D-14's \"one fork\" activation for every other 1.x rule -- test-only until 4.6 has a real bit." F-122 independently reached the same characterisation ("a single global boolean gating every 1.x rule uniformly"). **A stale-number correction found by re-deriving, not re-quoting**: D-14 (17 Sep) named "the 8 MB cap"; D-18/D-19 (19 Sep, two days later) superseded that with the now-BUILT dual caps `COMMITMENT_BUDGET_BODY_BYTES = 110,000,000` and `COMMITMENT_BUDGET_MAX_INPUTS = 700,000` (`consensus/consensus.h:40-41`), both already wired through the same `g_commitmentBudgetActive` flag (`consensus.h:52-55,64-66`) -- so the "cap" half of the bundle is functionally already riding the same activation flag as format, and only needs the flag replaced by a real bit, not new wiring. (`MAX_DIP0001_BLOCK_SIZE = 8,000,000`, `consensus/consensus.h:15`, is a separate, pre-existing, already-DIP0001-gated divergence specific to this research branch, `perf-constants.md:17` -- unrelated to 4.6's own activation and not what D-14's "8 MB" should be read as today.) **The THIRD bundled item -- the message-length raise -- has no scaffolding of any kind.** `MAX_PROTOCOL_MESSAGE_LENGTH` (`net.h:81`) is a flat `static const unsigned int = 3 * 1024 * 1024`; grepped across the tree, it is checked (`net.cpp:663-665`, F-197's own citation) but never conditioned on `g_commitmentBudgetActive`, `fDIP0001Active`, or anything else. Unlike the other two, this is new design work with nothing to copy, and F-197's own still-open `BLOCKTXN`-chunking gap (4.5.2) is bound to this SAME constant, so 4.6.1 inherits whatever 4.5.2 leaves unresolved there. **A real, source-flagged wiring trap, not hypothetical**: `validation.cpp:2415-2427`'s own comment says flipping `g_commitmentBudgetActive` from a bare global to a real deployment bit must NOT be a plain boolean swap -- it would "newly enforce these 3 rules against all of mainnet history on every future -reindex-chainstate," exactly the risk height-gating exists to avoid -- and carries its own `TODO(4.6): add && pindex->nHeight >= <activation height>` at this call site and "at F-83's own g_commitmentBudgetActive gate in AcceptBlock below" (`validation.cpp:5020`); both need the same fix, not just one. **One demonstrated, not theoretical, side effect of bundling everything behind `MaxBlockSize()`**: `net_processing.cpp:250-258`'s own comment records that the pre-2.3 relay-trickle cap formula coupled to `MaxBlockSize()` and so "would silently jump ~14x the day g_commitmentBudgetActive flips true...a side effect of an unrelated flag, not a throughput decision" -- already found and fixed once (2.3/F-5/F-138). Direct evidence that a global activation flag's blast radius needs its own audit sweep at 4.6 build time, the same shape as 4.1's `HaveBodies` call-site sweep, not an assumption that every downstream consumer has already been found. **Conclusion**: D-14's bundling decision holds and is mechanically sound for format+cap (precedented, scaffolded, tested, and already sharing one flag); the message-length raise is real, separate design work riding the same activation HEIGHT by decision but with no existing code; and the wiring itself has a known, source-flagged correctness trap (height-gating vs. a bare flag flip) that must be closed, not just "the bit exists."

**2. The dual-format transition window -- confirmed to be the P2P/consensus boundary, not 4.4's RPC boundary; the row's framing that this is undesigned is corrected, since the negotiation primitives already exist as opt-in scaffolding.** F-190/F-191/F-192/F-194 (4.4) confirmed their own dual-format work is a LOCAL RPC boundary only ("this is a local RPC boundary, not a P2P wire format, so there is no BIP9-style negotiation need," F-190) -- `getblocktemplate`/`submitblock` capability negotiation never leaves the calling process. §14.5's "dual-format window" and §13.H's "dual-format period and peer negotiation" (`transaction-decoupling.md:2068`) are a DIFFERENT problem: whether two nodes relaying blocks to each other agree on wire format. That mechanism is already built, gated behind an experimental opt-in flag: `NODE_COMMITMENTS` (bit 24, `protocol.h:331-344`, deliberately in the "experimental range...because the format is fork-local and unactivated"), a confirming handshake message `SENDCOMMITMENTS` (`protocol.h:212-219`, no payload; "not the service bit alone" licenses anything, per F-85's own unknown-getdata-wedge bug), `ShouldNegotiateCommitments`/`CanReceiveCommitments` (`protocol.h:412,427`), per-peer state (`fProvidesCommitments`, `net_processing.cpp:677`, set at `:3526` on receipt), the handshake offered at `net_processing.cpp:3374-3380`, consumed at both the fetch side (`-fetchbodyrange && CanReceiveCommitments`, `net_processing.cpp:5813`) and the send side (`net_processing.cpp:5907`). All of it sits behind `-commitmentblocks` (`init.cpp:634-638`, default 0, "Experimental; the format is not activated") -- manual, opt-in, not consensus-driven. **C8's own fix ("keep whole-block as the default relay form through the transition," `transaction-decoupling.md:2183`) is already the CURRENT default, but only because the flag defaults off, not because anything enforces it as a floor** -- and the per-peer gating already implements C8's fix shape precisely: never offer commitment form to a peer that has not confirmed support (`net_processing.cpp:1247-1248`'s own comment: "off (the DEFAULT) or this specific peer lacks NODE_COMMITMENTS (CanReceiveCommitments), nothing will EVER pick this block up"). **What 4.6 actually needs to build here is smaller than the row's own text implies**: swap the entry point from `-commitmentblocks`/`-fetchbodyrange` (manual CLI opt-in) to `UpdateManager::IsActive()` (real, height-gated, network-wide), while confirming the per-peer negotiation logic underneath needs no redesign -- it was already built to this exact shape. What remains genuinely unaudited by this pass: whether the decline case is rate-limited (thread 4, below).

**3. Old nodes' unpriced serving obligation -- distinct from §9.1a/§14.10's already-built miner obligation, and this one is genuinely unscoped, not silently covered by existing work.** §9.1a (`transaction-decoupling.md:1619-1652`) and §14.10 (`:2335-2349`) describe the MINER's own obligation to serve ITS OWN assembly-time-only transactions -- already enforced by 4.4.3/F-194's `-servebodyrange` gate on commitment-mode `getblocktemplate`. §14.5's "old nodes depend on new nodes materialising for them during the window, which is an unpriced serving obligation and a resource attack surface" (`:2200-2201`) names a DIFFERENT actor and a DIFFERENT obligation: a pre-4.6 (old-format-only) peer issuing an ordinary historical `getdata MSG_BLOCK`, answered by a node that may hold that block only as commitments plus a recent-bodies window. No code materialises a full legacy block on demand for such a peer: `ProcessGetBlockData` (`net_processing.cpp:2180`) is the only `MSG_BLOCK`-serving path, and 4.1.1's own audited direction (F-160) for a missing body is to gate on `HaveBodies` and decline (NOTFOUND), never reconstruct -- correct for a NEW windowed peer (which can retry via `GETBODYRANGE`/a Smartnode) but silently fatal to sync for an OLD peer, which has no such fallback and no concept of a body-range fetch at all. The design doc names this hazard in one sentence and proposes no fix (unlike C8, which got one). The nearest built mechanism this would lean on -- `NODE_NETWORK_LIMITED` reuse for windowed nodes (A1's own fix, F-160/4.1.3) -- was itself left as an OPEN scoping question by that audit ("no equivalent exists for body-specific windowing...flagged as an open scoping question rather than guessed at"), so 4.6 cannot build against it as settled. Per §0's own tier model (`:157-166`), the actual backstop for an old peer's historical request is a Smartnode ("Everything, forever") -- which already has general serving-cost protections (B4's off-`cs_main`, per-connection-budget fix, already built for `GETBODYRANGE`) but nothing that specifically prices or sunsets an indefinite tail of full-legacy-block service to never-upgrading peers. **Not responsibly scopable now**: needs an owner decision on whether that obligation is time-bounded (a sunset height/date after which old-format `getdata` may be declined) or indefinite, and if indefinite, whether it is ever priced -- which reopens the deferred §8 incentive layer, already deferred by owner decision elsewhere (F-197's own item-6 pull is the direct precedent for recognising, not building past, that boundary). Flagged, not sub-stepped.

**4. The downgrade surface -- the design doc's own use of the term is P2P format-negotiation, already partly mitigated by thread 2's existing per-peer gating; the brief's literal "software-version downgrade" reading is a SEPARATE, wholly unaddressed question.** §14.5's only use of "downgrade surface" (`:2201-2202`) is explicit: "format negotiation is a downgrade surface -- decline the commitment form and force a peer to serve full blocks." This is the same mechanism as thread 2/C8: any peer, honest-old or adversarial-new, can simply never send `SENDCOMMITMENTS`, and by the existing gating (`ShouldNegotiateCommitments`, `protocol.h:427`) the counterparty is structurally required to keep serving whole blocks -- not a new crash class, but an on-demand cost-forcing surface not specifically audited against a peer that ALWAYS declines (as opposed to the `GETBODYRANGE`-flood pattern B4 already covers). LOW-MEDIUM scope: confirm B4's existing per-connection budget (`-maxuploadtarget` fold-in) actually bites a peer that always declines rather than one that over-requests -- plausibly already covered mechanically, since whole-block relay already goes through existing `-maxuploadtarget` accounting today, but not confirmed by this audit or any prior one. **Separately, and NOT addressed anywhere in `transaction-decoupling.md` or `findings.md` by name**: an OPERATOR reverting to pre-4.6 SOFTWARE after network-wide activation. No section discusses this by name. By ordinary hard-fork reasoning (D-14's own "one fork" framing, §14.5's "old-format blocks stop") a reverted node simply cannot follow the chain past the activation height -- the generic risk every hard fork carries, not decoupling-specific -- but it currently produces no operator warning of any kind (grepped `init.cpp`/`validation.cpp` for any activation-height/downgrade check; none exists, unsurprising since the bit itself doesn't exist yet). Recommend folding a plain operator-facing warning into 4.6.1's own build (matching F-194/F-199's own "REQUIRE/WARN, don't just document" precedent) -- LOW effort, but a genuinely new scope item this audit is the first to name.

**5. The no-rollback question -- confirmed consensus-level, not operational, by the design doc's own two uses of the phrase, and now further confirmed structurally: the built `UpdateManager` state machine cannot represent a reversal at all.** Both of the design doc's only two "rollback" mentions are the same consensus-level claim: "no rollback once commitment-only blocks exist" (`:1911`) and "the scaling document's no-rollback reasoning" (`:2070`), listed as open question 4 of 6 in §12 (`:1909-1914`: "Sketched only...neither is a plan...needs re-examining against the two-regime model of §0, which may make a gentler path available, and against `UpdateManager` as the actual deployment mechanism") -- explicitly unresolved BY THE DESIGN DOC ITSELF, not merely undiscussed. This audit does not invent a resolution, matching F-190's own precedent of surfacing rather than deciding. **What this audit adds, found directly in source, not reasoned from the doc's prose**: `update/update.cpp:512-516` hard-codes `Active` and `Failed` as terminal -- "case EUpdateState::Failed: case EUpdateState::Active: { // Nothing happens, these are terminal states. break; }" -- so even if the owner's own re-examination concludes a "gentler path" is desirable, the CURRENT mechanism has no state to reach it from; that would be new state-machine surface, not a policy switch. A structural fact, not a design gap 4.6 can route around with call-site logic. Recommend: build nothing against a rollback path until the owner has answered §12's own open question 4 -- if the answer is "no rollback, full stop," D-14 already treats it that way and 4.6.1 can proceed unchanged; if the answer is "some gentler path," that is new `UpdateManager` design work this audit cannot responsibly propose today.

**6. The activation criterion and the 4.2 dependency -- CONFIRMED not built, genuinely load-bearing, and 4.6's own activation sub-step cannot be responsibly scoped until it ships.** Grepped the whole tree for anything matching 4.2's own one-line description (per-peer miss rate, per-range coverage, passive collection): `miss.?rate`, `coveragetelemetry`, `peer.?coverage`, `rangecoverage` all return zero matches in `src/`; the only "coverage" hits anywhere are unrelated (test coverage, `announcerring.h`'s IBD-time comment, `secp256k1`'s own `COVERAGE` build macro). `build-plan.md`'s 4.2 row (`:153`) carries NO closure marker of any kind -- unlike 4.1/4.4/4.5, which each read "X CLOSED...(F-NNN)," 4.2's status column reads only "straightforward," confirming the doc's own silence is an accurate reflection of the tree, not an oversight in this audit's reading: nothing has been built. The design doc is explicit this is exactly what 4.6's own criterion needs: §14.1 ("record miss rates and per-range coverage passively from day one...it is what §14.5's activation criterion requires," `:2145-2148`) and §14.5 itself ("The activation criterion cannot be measured, and the machinery cannot read it. The scaling document says [activation] is decided by coverage data. But deployment here votes by block signalling -- hashrate (`MinerRoundVoting`) or mined quorum commitments (`NodeRoundVoting`) -- and neither sees data coverage," `:2188-2191`), naming the exact attack this gap enables: signal activation via hashrate while coverage is actually poor, activate, lose history permanently, with no metric ever having existed to let anyone object (`:2193-2195`). §13's own dependency diagram (`:2104-2117`) lists J (coverage telemetry) as independent of everything and draws no explicit arrow into H (migration/activation) -- a real internal inconsistency against §14.1/§14.5's own prose, which states the dependency plainly; the diagram undersells it, the prose doesn't. **Conclusion, stated as plainly as the brief asked**: 4.6 cannot build a coverage-based activation criterion today, because there is no coverage data to read. Building an interim criterion against hashrate/quorum-commitment signalling instead would be building precisely the attack §14.5 names, not a stopgap. Recommend 4.2 be pulled forward and built BEFORE 4.6's own activation-criterion sub-step (would-be 4.6.3) is attempted -- not deferred inside 4.6's scope, and not silently worked around. 4.2 has no code dependency on 4.1/4.4/4.5's own work (§13's own diagram: "J ─────────► (coverage measurable)," independent of C-I), so nothing blocks starting it immediately.

**Sub-step breakdown, matching F-160/F-190/F-197's own precedent for a phase this size, but honest about what a six-thread phase this unresolved can actually support**: **4.6.1 -- the `UpdateManager` bit, wired for real** (thread 1: replace `g_commitmentBudgetActive`'s bare global with a real `EUpdate` bit read through `UpdateManager::IsActive()`; close the source's own flagged height-gating TODO at both `validation.cpp` call sites; design and wire the message-length raise, which has no existing scaffolding; swap thread 2's `-commitmentblocks`/`-fetchbodyrange` manual flags for the same bit; fold in thread 4's plain software-downgrade operator warning) -- HIGH confidence on format+cap (precedented, scaffolded, D-14/D-19 already decided and built the underlying caps), MEDIUM on the message-length raise (new design, inherits 4.5.2's own open `BLOCKTXN` state) and on the height-gating fix (a real, source-named correctness trap, not yet solved anywhere). **4.6.2 -- dual-format P2P negotiation, consensus-gated, plus the downgrade-surface rate-limit check** (threads 2 and 4's P2P half: confirm the existing per-peer `SENDCOMMITMENTS`/`CanReceiveCommitments` machinery needs no redesign, only a different trigger; confirm B4's existing per-connection budget covers a peer that always declines commitment form) -- MEDIUM-HIGH confidence, smaller than the row's own text implies since the negotiation mechanics already exist and are tested. **Explicitly NOT sub-stepped, flagged for the owner rather than forced into a breakdown**: **the activation criterion itself (would-be 4.6.3)** -- blocked on 4.2, which this audit recommends building first, not inside 4.6's own scope; **old nodes' unpriced serving obligation (thread 3)** -- needs an owner decision on sunset vs. indefinite vs. priced, and depends on 4.1.3's own still-open `NODE_NETWORK_LIMITED`-for-windowed-nodes question; **the no-rollback question (thread 5)** -- explicitly unresolved by the design doc's own text, and now confirmed to need new `UpdateManager` state-machine surface if the owner's answer is anything other than "no rollback, full stop." The row's own "4-6 weeks, partly unknown" estimate is corrected: 4.6.1/4.6.2 are plausibly within that window on their own; the phase as a whole cannot be estimated further until 4.2 ships and the owner answers the two flagged design questions -- not a scheduling gap this audit can close by writing a bigger number | `source`, direct read of `update/update.h`/`.cpp`, `validation.cpp`/`.h`, `net_processing.cpp`, `protocol.h`, `init.cpp`, `chainparams.cpp`, `consensus/consensus.h`, `net.h` against current line numbers; `transaction-decoupling.md` §0, §7A.7, §9.1a, §12, §13 (C-J), §14.1, §14.2-14.5, §14.10 read in full, not skimmed for keywords; D-11 through D-19 checked directly, D-14 found directly load-bearing and its own "8 MB" figure found stale against D-18/D-19; grepped the full tree for any 4.2-shaped coverage telemetry and found none; no code change, audit and scoping only, matching F-160/F-190/F-197's established discipline for this size of phase | **audited 2026-09-28 -- 2 of 6 threads sub-stepped (4.6.1, 4.6.2), 1 thread (activation criterion) explicitly blocked on 4.2 rather than sub-stepped, 2 threads (old-node serving obligation, no-rollback) flagged for the owner's own decision, 1 thread (downgrade surface) split between 4.6.2's build scope and a software-downgrade warning folded into 4.6.1 -- build not started; 4.2's own row flagged separately, see docs/build-plan.md's 4.6 and 4.2 rows** |
| F-206 | **4.6.2 built: the trigger swapped from the manual `-commitmentblocks`/`-fetchbodyrange` flags to a real, height-gated placeholder, ORed with (not replacing) the flags at every call site -- deliberately a placeholder, not a guessed `EUpdate` bit** | New `commitments_negotiation.h`/`.cpp`: `IsCommitmentFormatActive(pindexPrev)` is a `TODO(4.6.1)`-marked placeholder (`return false`) modelled on this codebase's own established pattern for exactly this "is a height-gated behaviour live right now" question -- `Updates().IsActive(EUpdate::DEPLOYMENT_V17, pindex)` (`validation.cpp:2250`) and `EUpdate::ROUND_VOTING` (`llmq/quorums_dkgsessionmgr.cpp:372`) are the two live precedents this was modelled on. Deliberately NOT a guessed new `EUpdate` enum value: `EUpdate` is one shared enum, 4.6.1 (a sibling task, not built in this worktree, its own branch deliberately not read per this sub-step's own isolation) owns picking the real bit's name and number, and inventing one here risks a collision neither side can see coming. `CombineNegotiationTrigger(fRealActivation, fManualOverride)` (a pure OR, `static inline` and header-visible, matching `protocol.h`'s own `CanReceiveCommitments`/`ShouldNegotiateCommitments` convention) is kept SEPARATE from `IsCommitmentFormatActive`'s own placeholder body specifically so the OR itself stays mutation-testable today: with the real-activation operand hardcoded false, no test observing the combined `ShouldNegotiateCommitmentsNow` end-to-end can distinguish "flag OR real-activation" from "flag alone" -- confirmed directly, the combined form's own would-be equivalent mutant (`return fManualOverride;`) passes every end-to-end assertion but fails `combine_negotiation_trigger_is_an_or`'s own `(true, false)` case, which is the whole reason for the split. Four call sites swapped, all ORing the existing `DEBUG_TEST` flag with the placeholder rather than replacing it, so every flag-driven test keeps exercising unchanged behaviour while there is no real bit to activate against: `net_processing.cpp`'s `fFetchBodyRangeCapable` (the fetch-decision gate, `:5813`), its body-range-in-flight maintenance reaper (`:5847` -- previously gated on a DIFFERENT trigger than the fetch gate it cleans up after, a real if minor pre-existing staleness bug this fix also closes: without it, a real-activation-driven in-flight request would never get reaped), and the GETBODYRANGE request-transmission gate (`:5913`, "the send side" F-205's own thread 2 names — the point the request message is pushed onto the wire, not a commitment-form block SEND path, which does not exist over ordinary relay today; see F-207). `init.cpp` gained a FOURTH, NEW check rather than a swap of the existing one: `-commitmentblocks`'s own early check (`:1843`) runs before `LoadChainTip`, so `::ChainActive().Tip()` is null there and would always read as inactive; a second check was added immediately before `connOptions.nLocalServices = nLocalServices` (after chain state has loaded), which correctly detects real activation for a node started fresh post-activation. **Flagged, not built**: this does not cover a node already running when activation height is crossed without a restart -- `nLocalServices` is fixed once at that point, and `CNode`'s own copy (`net.h`) is `const`, fixed per-connection at accept/dial time; making that live needs `CNode`/`CConnman`'s own service-bit plumbing made connection-time-dynamic, broader than this sub-step's "swap the trigger" scope. Matches ordinary Bitcoin-Core-style fork deployment (upgrade then restart), recorded as an explicit limitation rather than silently accepted. **Deliberately NOT touched**: `net_processing.cpp:3378`'s `ShouldNegotiateCommitments(pfrom->GetLocalServices(), ...)` SENDCOMMITMENTS-offering call — it already inherits the fix correctly via the now-activation-aware `nLocalServices`, and adding a second, independently-live-computed check there was considered and rejected: it would let this node's own SENDCOMMITMENTS offer diverge from what it actually advertised in its VERSION message, two sources of truth where one now suffices. TDD: RED confirmed both by construction (the test file was written against a not-yet-built module) and by direct reproduction after the fact — reverting `CombineNegotiationTrigger` to `&&`, to `return fManualOverride;`, and to `return fRealActivation;`, and `IsCommitmentFormatActive`'s placeholder to `return true;`, each independently rebuilt and reproduced real failing assertions (4, 1, 3 and 4 respectively), then reverted; the same four reversions are this fix's mutation-testing record — every mutant killed, none survived. 3 new test cases (`commitments_negotiation_tests.cpp`). Full suite: 647 cases (`!miner_tests`) clean, up from 644; `miner_tests` alone unchanged (the one known pre-existing failure) | `source` (direct read of `validation.cpp:2250`, `llmq/quorums_dkgsessionmgr.cpp:372`, `net.h`'s `CNode::nLocalServices` `const`-ness, `init.cpp`'s chain-load ordering relative to `-commitmentblocks`'s own check), TDD (genuine RED reproduced by construction and by reversion), mutation-tested (4 mutants across the trigger-combination logic and the placeholder itself, all die) | **built 2026-09-28** |
| F-207 | **4.6.2's B4 verification: a peer that always declines commitment negotiation costs nothing new today, for a more basic reason than F-205's own framing implied -- there is no commitment-form SEND path over ordinary relay to decline FROM. Audit only, no code change** | F-205's thread 4 asked to confirm B4's existing per-connection budget covers a peer that always declines commitment negotiation. Two things needed separating first, since this project's own history (F-121) already flagged bare review-local labels getting reused for unrelated findings across rounds: B4 itself (`transaction-decoupling.md` §14.3's own table) is "Seek-amplified fetch pinning `cs_main`" — about GETBODYRANGE SERVING cost (a peer asking THIS node for body ranges), whose own fix has four parts, only one built: off-`cs_main` (F-149, real), a per-connection budget in records/bytes (confirmed NOT built — F-150's own direct grep of `net_processing.cpp` found "`-maxuploadtarget` is consulted only for historical `ProcessGetBlockData` serving and MEMPOOL, never for BODYRANGE bytes", recorded as a hard deployment gate, mitigated only by F-151's `-servebodyrange` opt-in, default off, not a real budget), folding into `-maxuploadtarget` (not built), and charging per file opened (not confirmed). Thread 4's actual question is a DIFFERENT direction: a peer that never negotiates at all, forcing THIS node to serve THEM whole blocks — the ordinary `ProcessGetBlockData`/`GETDATA(MSG_BLOCK)` path, not GETBODYRANGE. Verified directly against source: `ProcessGetBlockData` (`net_processing.cpp:2180-2325`) sends a full `CBlock` via `NetMsgType::BLOCK` unconditionally for every `MSG_BLOCK` request, with no branch on `NODE_COMMITMENTS`/`CanReceiveCommitments` anywhere in it — confirmed further against `protocol.h`'s own `GetDataMsg` enum, which defines no commitment-form inv type at all (F-119 already removed the only related dead code, `SER_COMMITMENTS`/`BlockSerFlagsFor`, zero production callers). **There is no smaller wire form to downgrade FROM today** — every peer, negotiating or not, is served identically, so "declining" changes nothing about which code path serves that peer. The pre-existing protection that does apply equally to every peer requesting an old block, decline or not, is the historical-block-serving limit (`net_processing.cpp:2224-2235`: `connman->OutboundTargetReached(true)` disconnects a non-whitelisted peer requesting a block older than `HISTORICAL_BLOCK_AGE`, 7 days, once the budget is exceeded) — with two corrections to how "already covered" should be read: it is a single NODE-WIDE aggregate (`CConnman::nMaxOutboundTotalBytesSentInCycle`, `net.h:638`, one counter shared across every peer, not literally per-connection, confirmed directly), and it is OFF BY DEFAULT (`DEFAULT_MAX_UPLOAD_TARGET = 0` means unlimited, `net.h:98`; `OutboundTargetReached` returns `false` immediately when the limit is 0, `net.cpp:3532`) unless an operator explicitly sets `-maxuploadtarget`. Neither is decoupling-specific or new — both are unchanged, upstream-derived behaviour that has always applied identically to a declining and a negotiating peer alike, since negotiation state never enters `ProcessGetBlockData` at all. **Conclusion**: thread 4's claim holds, but not because a budget was specifically confirmed or built for the declining-peer case — because there is no cost asymmetry for that case to bound in the first place. B4's own real gap (GETBODYRANGE's still-missing per-connection budget) is real, already tracked, and untouched here — a different code path, already flagged (F-150/F-151), out of this sub-step's scope | `source` (direct read of `net_processing.cpp`'s `ProcessGetBlockData`/`:2224-2325`, `protocol.h`'s `GetDataMsg` enum, `net.h`/`net.cpp`'s `OutboundTargetReached`/`RecordBytesSent`/`nMaxOutboundTotalBytesSentInCycle`, `DEFAULT_MAX_UPLOAD_TARGET`) | **verified 2026-09-28 — no gap found on this axis, nothing built; B4's own real gap (GETBODYRANGE) remains F-150/F-151's open item, untouched** |
| F-208 | **4.2 built: passive per-peer body-range miss-rate and per-range coverage telemetry, closing the data-source gap the 4.6 audit found (F-205, sibling worktree, not yet merged into this branch's own findings.md -- see this row's own cross-reference below) and that SS14.5's activation criterion depends on** | **What was built**: `src/coveragetelemetry.h`/`.cpp` -- pure, TDD'd, mutation-checked logic, no net_processing.cpp scaffolding needed (matches `bodyrange.h`'s own established split). `CoverageRangeIndex(height, rangeSize)` buckets a block height into a fixed-size coverage range (`COVERAGE_RANGE_SIZE = 1000` blocks, a judgment call, not a re-derivation of anything measured -- see the scope correction below for why it does not attempt to align to `bodystore.h:MAX_BODYFILE_SIZE`'s own 128 MiB file boundaries). `BodyRangeTally`/`RecordBodyRangeTally`/`BodyRangeMissRate` is a plain hit/miss counter with a `-1.0` "never observed" sentinel, so a caller cannot misread 0 observations as a 0% miss rate. `RangeCoverageStats`/`RecordCoverageObservation` adds first-observed/last-hit/last-miss timestamps -- the raw material a LATER consumer (4.6, or an operator) needs to tell a shrinking gap (SS14.9: honest backfill) from a stable one (SS14.9: deliberate erasure) apart, polling this repeatedly over time and diffing; this phase deliberately does not compute that trend itself, matching SS14.5's own framing, quoted directly: "a query and a counter, not a challenge economy." **Scope correction against this row's own one-line brief** (which described the fetch protocol as built around "GETBODYRANGE requests for ranges of blocks"): confirmed false by direct read of `bodyrange.h:CGetBodyRange` -- its `nStartIndex`/`nCount` range one block's own body INDICES (`hashBlock` names a single block), never a span of multiple blocks. SS14.9's own honest-loss/deliberate-erasure table means a different, height-axis unit ("oldest-first", "mid-history", "file-aligned" are all properties of a span of chain history, not of one block's internal tx list). `coveragetelemetry.h` introduces its own, separate notion of a coverage range rather than reusing `CGetBodyRange`'s wire-level type, and says so in its own doc comment so the two are not conflated later. **Where the data lives, and why (a genuine design decision, not a default)**: hooked into `net_processing.cpp`'s EXISTING `BODYRANGE` response handler (`NetMsgType::BODYRANGE` arm), passively, at its two pre-existing observation points -- an empty response (a miss) and a validated non-empty first chunk (a hit) -- no new active probing or scanning added, matching this phase's own "passive... shipped with the protocol" scope. Two separate data structures, deliberately not one: (1) `CNodeState::bodyRangeTally` (per-peer, `net_processing.cpp`) records EVERY validated round trip regardless of chunk position -- this measures THIS PEER's own answering reliability, and every request/response pair is evidence of it. Ephemeral, torn down with the connection, matching `nMisbehavior`'s own per-connection scope and this file's own established convention for `nBodyRangePeerInFlight` and its siblings. (2) file-scope `mapBodyRangeCoverage` (cross-peer, keyed by `CoverageRangeIndex`, `net_processing.cpp`) records ONLY first-chunk (`nStartIndex == 0`) observations, from ANY peer -- gated this way so one abandoned multi-chunk fetch cannot skew a bucket's counts relative to a single-chunk one, and deliberately NOT keyed to peer identity, since SS14.9's own erasure signature ("identical across one operator's nodes") is a claim about the RANGE across the whole network's answering behaviour, not about any one connection. **In-memory only**, matching this file's own established precedent for transient body-range state (`mapBodyRangeInFlight`/`mapBodyRangePartial`/`g_body_retry_state` are all reset on restart too, confirmed by direct read) -- SS14.5's own "a query and a counter" framing does not require this to survive a restart, and losing it only means re-accumulating going forward, never losing a security property (no punitive or gating decision reads this data; see the consensus-adjacency note below). **Exposed via RPC**, matching this file's own `getpeerinfo`/`getnettotals` convention exactly (a plain data dump, no gating logic): `getpeerinfo` gains optional `bodyrange_hits`/`bodyrange_misses`/`bodyrange_missrate` per peer (omitted, not zeroed, until at least one observation exists, matching `pingtime`'s own "if available" convention); a new `getbodyrangecoverage` RPC dumps every observed coverage bucket (`rangesize`, then per-bucket `startheight`/`endheight`/`hits`/`misses`/`firstobserved`/`lasthit`/`lastmiss`). **Consensus-adjacency, confirmed by construction, not merely asserted**: every new code path is read-only with respect to validation -- the RPC handler and both `net_processing.cpp` hook points only ever WRITE into `mapBodyRangeCoverage`/`CNodeState::bodyRangeTally` or READ them back for display; nothing conditions a `Misbehaving` call, a disconnect, a relay decision, or any validation-path branch on this data, matching this phase's own explicit "never gate or influence validation" scope limit. **What this phase deliberately does NOT attempt, flagged rather than silently scoped out**: (1) classifying honest loss vs. deliberate erasure itself -- SS14.9's own table is a shape a LATER consumer reads across repeated polls, not a verdict this phase computes; (2) clustering "one operator's multiple nodes" (SS14.9's own erasure signature) -- would need peer IP/ASN correlation this telemetry does not attempt, a real follow-up scope decision, not a small addition; (3) cross-referencing a coverage bucket against `bodystore.h`'s own body-file boundaries to detect the "file-aligned" honest-loss shape precisely -- `COVERAGE_RANGE_SIZE`'s fixed block count cannot track a body file's own real (byte-occupancy-dependent) boundary exactly, so a later consumer wanting true file-alignment must cross-reference the store's own recorded file/height mapping separately; (4) persistence across restarts, and an eviction/rollup policy for `mapBodyRangeCoverage`'s own bucket count over a long mainnet-scale uptime -- both flagged in the map's own doc comment, not built. **TDD**: `src/test/coveragetelemetry_tests.cpp`, 19 cases, genuine RED confirmed against a deliberate placeholder implementation (27 assertion failures: bucket-index math, tally increments, the miss-rate sentinel, and every `RecordCoverageObservation` field) before the real implementation landed, then GREEN. **Mutation-checked**, 8 manual mutants against all four core functions (this codebase has no automated mutation tool; matches this project's own established manual-mutant convention): an off-by-one on the bucket boundary (`(nHeight+1)/nRangeSize`), ignoring `nRangeSize` entirely (hardcoded 1000), both `if (fHit)` branch-swaps (`RecordBodyRangeTally` and `RecordCoverageObservation`'s timestamp branch), the miss-branch incrementing hits instead of misses, the sentinel guard forced always-true, the miss-rate numerator swapped to hits, and the first-observed guard's own miss-first gap (dropping the `nMisses == 0` half of the guard, which every hit-first test would miss since a hit-first sequence never has `nMisses > 0` at the check point -- caught only by a dedicated miss-first test added specifically because of this) -- **all 8 killed**, file restored to the original and reconfirmed green after each. `net_processing.cpp`'s own two hook points and the RPC glue have no dedicated unit coverage, matching this tree's own established convention for message-processing/RPC glue (`bodyrange_tests.cpp`'s own precedent for the `GETBODYRANGE`/`BODYRANGE` `ProcessMessage` arms; every byte of actual decision logic lives in the pure functions instead, where it is fully mutation-tested). Full clean rebuild against the shared `depends/x86_64-pc-linux-gnu` prefix. **Full suite confirmed green**: run twice, once against this branch's pre-4.2 base commit (`ce9adb73e`) as a baseline and once against this change, both from the same shared depends prefix -- baseline: 644 cases, 1 failure (`miner_tests/CreateNewBlock_validity`, `test/miner_tests.cpp:413`, the same pre-existing `bad-blk-sigops` failure every prior 4.1/4.4/4.5 row has already documented); with this change: 663 cases (+19, exactly the new `coveragetelemetry_tests.cpp` cases), the identical single failure at the identical line, nothing else regressed | `source`, direct read of `bodyrange.h`/`net_processing.cpp`/`net_processing.h`/`rpc/net.cpp` before writing anything (confirming the CGetBodyRange range-unit correction, the existing observation points, and the getpeerinfo/getnettotals RPC convention rather than assuming any of them); TDD (genuine RED, 27 failures, before GREEN); 8 manual mutants, individually applied and reverted, all killed; full suite run twice (baseline vs. this change) from the same shared build prefix, diffed | **built 2026-09-28, full suite confirmed green (663/664, the one failure pre-existing and unchanged)** |
| F-209 | **Independent adversarial review of F-208 found a real security bug (coverage HITS recorded before content validation, not after) and confirmed three unresolved scope gaps that keep this phase from actually closing the honest-loss-vs-deliberate-erasure distinction F-208 was framed as unblocking** | **1. Security fix (CONFIRMED HIGH).** Both hit-recording call sites in `net_processing.cpp`'s `BODYRANGE` response handler (`CNodeState::bodyRangeTally`'s per-peer tally and `mapBodyRangeCoverage`'s cross-peer bucket) fired right after `ValidateBodyRangeResponse` (`bodyrange.h`) succeeded -- wire SHAPE only: right `nStartIndex`, count within bounds, non-null entries -- and BEFORE `ValidateBodyRangeChunkHashes` (the actual content check, comparing every returned body's hash against the range's own committed hashes) ran. A response that passed shape validation but failed content validation -- garbage transactions substituted for the real ones -- was still counted as a coverage HIT before the peer got banned for it (`Misbehaving`, score 100). Since this telemetry's whole purpose (SS14.9) is separating honest gaps from deliberate erasure, this let an eraser inject a fake "everything's fine here" signal into the very data meant to catch it, for the one-time cost of a single banned IP per fake hit -- the exact threat this phase exists to detect, defeated at its own recording site. **Fixed**: both hit-recording call sites moved to after `ValidateBodyRangeChunkHashes` succeeds; a hash-invalid, non-empty response is now recorded as neither a hit nor a miss (the peer lied about having the range, which is not the same signal as an honest "I don't have this" -- miss-recording stays keyed to genuinely empty responses only, unaffected, exactly as before). **TDD**: `src/test/bodyrange_hit_recording_tests.cpp` (new file) -- the bug lives in `net_processing.cpp`'s own untested message-processing glue, in the call ORDER itself, not in a pure predicate this project's existing convention (`bodyrange.h`'s `ShouldRequestBodyRange`/`IsBodyRangeChunkAligned`/etc.) already tests; a predicate-level test cannot catch a call-order regression, so this suite drives the real handler end to end via a genuine `PeerLogicValidation::ProcessMessages` pass, matching `blocktxn_tests.cpp`'s own `ConnectedPeer`/`QueueMessage` harness. A new test-only `SeedBodyRangeInFlightForTest` (declared `net_processing.h`, defined `net_processing.cpp`) seeds a single in-flight request directly -- reproducing `SendMessages`' own real fetch-selection machinery (`-fetchbodyrange`, `NODE_COMMITMENTS`, the announcer ring) a second time in a test was out of proportion to what this fix needed. Genuine RED confirmed first: reverted the fix (hit-recording back at the old, pre-fix call sites) and confirmed `hash_invalid_chunk_is_never_recorded_as_a_hit` genuinely failed (`nBodyRangeHits == 1`, not `0`; the coverage bucket's own hit count moved too) -- then GREEN after the fix. Two companion tests guard the paths the fix must NOT touch: a genuinely hash-valid chunk is still recorded as a hit (`hash_valid_chunk_is_still_recorded_as_a_hit`), and an honest empty response is still recorded as a miss (`empty_response_is_still_recorded_as_a_miss`). **Mutation-checked twice, isolating each call site independently**: (A) per-peer tally moved back alone, coverage left correct -- caught, exactly the `nBodyRangeHits` assertion failed and nothing else; (B) coverage bucket moved back alone, per-peer tally left correct -- caught, exactly the coverage-bucket assertion failed and nothing else. Both reverted, `net_processing.cpp` reconfirmed byte-identical to the fix, GREEN reconfirmed. **2. Scope gaps confirmed real, not fixed here (the owner's own explicit decision -- see build-plan.md's 4.2/4.6 rows).** Independent review found three concrete gaps in what F-208's data can actually support: (a) **per-request, not per-block, granularity** -- any dropped/stale/re-requested `GETBODYRANGE` inflates that request's own miss count without changing whether the block itself was ultimately served, scrambling the hit/miss ratio SS14.9's table depends on; (b) **partial-body erasure is invisible to the cross-peer view** -- `mapBodyRangeCoverage` records ONLY first-chunk (`nStartIndex == 0`) observations, so a peer serving just the first chunk of a large block and withholding every later chunk still registers as a clean cross-peer HIT for that bucket; the per-peer tally does see every chunk, but nothing aggregates per-peer "did this fetch ever actually complete" into the cross-peer view; (c) **tip-only accumulation, no persistence** -- `mapBodyRangeCoverage` only fills from THIS node's own outbound fetch requests, so an already-synced node issuing few new fetches barely accumulates history at all, and every bit of it is lost on restart (F-208's own doc comment already flagged the restart half; this review confirms both halves together are a real gap against F-208's own stated purpose, not merely a flagged limitation). **Net effect, reviewer's own suggested framing, adopted verbatim**: what this phase actually measures is **"this node's own fetch outcomes, bucket-coarse, request-weighted, tip-only once synced"** -- not a resolution of the honest-loss-vs-deliberate-erasure distinction, and not, as-is, sufficient data for 4.6's real activation criterion. F-208's own row is corrected below (build-plan.md's 4.2/4.6 rows too) to say so. This is explicitly future work for whenever 4.6's real activation criterion is actually being designed, since that is the consumer that will know exactly what shape of data it needs -- not redesigned speculatively here | `source`, direct read of `net_processing.cpp`'s `BODYRANGE` handler and `bodyrange.h`'s `Validate*` split before writing anything; TDD (genuine RED reproducing the exact pre-fix call order, then GREEN); two independent single-call-site mutation checks, each reverted and reconfirmed to fail only their own assertion; hash-valid and empty-response companion tests confirm the fix did not overcorrect; `bodyrange_tests`/`coveragetelemetry_tests`/`blocktxn_tests`/`denialofservice_tests`/`bodyrange_hit_recording_tests` (97 cases) reconfirmed green together; full suite run, see this row's own status | **SECURITY FIX confirmed 2026-09-28 (full suite green except the one pre-existing `miner_tests` failure). Scope gaps confirmed real, correction to F-208/build-plan.md's 4.2/4.6 rows landed same day; actual data-model redesign deferred to 4.6's own future build, per the owner's own decision** |
| F-210 | **4.6.1 built: the `UpdateManager` bit wired for real, closing TODO(4.6) -- new `EUpdate::COMMITMENT_MODE` (bit 3, `update/update.h`), mirroring `ROUND_VOTING`'s own one-bit-gates-several-behaviours precedent exactly, plus a height-gating fix broader than the TODO's own two named call sites once the nested calls underneath them were traced.** `g_commitmentBudgetActive` (`validation.h`/`.cpp`) is UNCHANGED as a variable -- every one of its ~15 existing read sites (`CheckBlock`, `ContextualCheckBlock`, `CheckCommitmentBlock`, `ContextualCheckCommitmentBlock`, `AcceptToMemoryPool`, `miner.cpp` block assembly, `rpc/mining.cpp`) still reads the same global, untouched. What changed is who writes it: new `CommitmentModeAtHeight` (`validation.h`/`.cpp`), an RAII guard installed at the top of `ConnectBlock` and near the top of `AcceptBlock` -- the two functions the TODO itself named -- pins the flag to `Updates().IsActive(EUpdate::COMMITMENT_MODE, pindex)` for THAT SPECIFIC block's own height, for the whole of the call, restoring the previous value on return. Because the guard wraps the whole function rather than patching each read individually, it also transitively fixes each function's OWN nested sub-calls -- `ConnectBlock`'s internal `CheckBlock` re-check (`validation.cpp:2319`, not named by the TODO) and `AcceptBlock`'s internal `CheckBlock`/`ContextualCheckBlock` calls near the end of `AcceptBlock` (also not named) -- call sites the TODO comment did not enumerate but that a full `-reindex`/`-reindex-chainstate` replay reaches through the identical path, confirmed by tracing every nested call rather than trusting the TODO's own two-site list as exhaustive (flagged per this sub-step's own instruction to surface, not silently patch around, anything the audit didn't name). For everything OUTSIDE those two functions -- mempool policy, block assembly, RPC reporting, and the not-reindexed shape of `CheckBlock`/`ContextualCheckBlock`/`CheckCommitmentBlock`/`ContextualCheckCommitmentBlock` that a genuinely NEW block goes through -- `g_commitmentBudgetActive` is instead refreshed once per real tip connection, in `dsnotificationinterface.cpp`'s `UpdatedBlockTip`, mirroring `fDIP0001ActiveAtTip`'s own established real-precedent pattern in the identical function. **Verified explicitly that this makes `-reindex-chainstate` safe against pre-activation history, the exact hazard the TODO named**: `ConnectBlock` (confirmed via a `validation.cpp` comment on `LoadBlockIndexDB` to be the function `-reindex-chainstate` actually replays, once per historical block, in height order, via `ConnectTip`) now reads a per-pindex value, not a global that could reflect a later block's or the final tip's state -- a replayed pre-activation block can never have post-activation rules newly enforced against it, and a replayed post-activation block still gets them, regardless of what the CURRENT tip's own state is. Proved with a real test, not just reasoning: new `src/test/commitmentmode_activation_tests.cpp`, two cases -- (1) `connectblock_relocated_rule_is_height_gated_not_globally_on`, the identical non-final-coinbase-violating block from `commitmentblock_tests.cpp`'s own always-on test, presented to a direct `ConnectBlock` call at two real-tip+1 heights straddling one real registered activation height, with one genuine ordinary block mined in between to advance the real tip itself between the two calls: accepted below, rejected at/above. **Two confounds found and fixed by actually running this test, not assumed away**: an arbitrary large activation height (100000, the original draft) desyncs the block's coinbase -- built by the real `BlockAssembler` against the real, nearby chain tip -- from regtest's own real, unrelated height-gated consensus rules (founder payment starts at height 500, `chainparams.cpp`), tripping `bad-cb-founder-payment-not-found` before the relocated check was ever reached; and a hand-fabricated second-level `CBlockIndex` (pprev chained off a first synthetic index, to get a one-height gap without mining) breaks other real invariants `ConnectBlock` enforces beyond height/pprev self-consistency -- `evoDb`'s own `VerifyBestBlock` and the coins view's `GetBestBlock` both compare against the REAL, currently-committed chain state, which no synthetic multi-hop pprev chain can satisfy. The fix that actually works, and what the committed test does: keep every direct `ConnectBlock` call at real-tip+1 (matching `commitmentblock_tests.cpp`'s own precedent exactly, so every invariant holds), and advance the REAL chain by one ordinary block between the "below" and "at" sub-scenarios instead of fabricating a second hop. (2) `commitment_mode_at_height_reflects_each_historical_blocks_own_height`, driving `CommitmentModeAtHeight` itself against two REAL, already-connected historical `CBlockIndex` pointers from one mined chain, with the activation height registered only AFTER both blocks already exist in the index -- the closest a boost-test binary gets to a genuine `-reindex-chainstate`-shaped scenario (no second process/re-read exists in this binary to run a literal `-reindex-chainstate`; that would be a `test/functional` python integration test, out of scope here and not additionally built -- flagged rather than silently skipped). **Registration deliberately deferred on every network, mirroring `QUORUMS_200_8`'s own real precedent of a bit that exists without being registered everywhere**: `EUpdate::COMMITMENT_MODE` is not added via `updateManager.Add()` in `chainparams.cpp` for mainnet, testnet, devnet, or regtest -- 4.2's own activation criterion is confirmed genuinely unbuilt (F-205's own thread 6), so no real `startHeight`/threshold exists yet to commit to; `Updates().IsActive()` is therefore `Unknown`/false everywhere today, identical to `g_commitmentBudgetActive`'s own permanent production default -- this sub-step changes the MECHANISM, not today's behaviour. Tests that need the bit genuinely active (`commitmentblock_tests.cpp`, `acceptancebit_tests.cpp` -- the ones calling `ConnectBlock`/`AcceptBlock` directly, where a bare `g_commitmentBudgetActive = true` no longer has any effect once `CommitmentModeAtHeight` pins it per pindex) now register a real `Update` via `Updates().Add()` using the `HeightActivated` fast path (`update.h`, precedented by `DEPLOYMENT_V17`/`ROUND_VOTING`'s own mainnet entries) instead; tests that only reach `CheckBlock`/`CheckCommitmentBlock`/`AcceptToMemoryPool` (`blockbudget_tests.cpp`, `sigopcount_tests.cpp`, `txvalidation_tests.cpp`, and `commitmentblock_tests.cpp`'s own `CheckCommitmentBlock`-only case) needed no change at all, since those call sites still read the ambient flag directly. **The message-length raise (K-7, `net.h:MAX_PROTOCOL_MESSAGE_LENGTH`) is a deliberate judgment call, not built**: read `net.cpp:663-665`, its own only real use -- a RECEIVE-side per-message byte cap in `CNode::ReceiveMsgBytes`, disconnecting an oversized sender. It determines what one node is willing to buffer on its own socket, not what makes a block or transaction valid, and two peers with DIFFERENT values simply degrade (one refuses a message the other would have sent) rather than diverging on chain state -- it has none of the synchronized-activation-height property `EUpdate` exists to coordinate. Judgment: this is a standalone protocol/policy parameter, not a consensus-gated deployment, and should NOT ride `EUpdate::COMMITMENT_MODE` or any other activation bit; D-14's original "one fork" bundling of the message-length raise alongside format+cap is superseded on this specific point. No new value is set here either -- F-205's own citation of F-197's still-open `BLOCKTXN`-chunking gap (4.5.2) means the real target number is undecided elsewhere, and inventing one now would be guessing at a parameter this sub-step was not scoped to decide. | F-211 | **Owner decision, 2026-09-28, closing all three of F-205's genuinely-unresolved threads (3, 4, 5) at once: decoupling activates on mainnet as a hard fork.** Old nodes are expected to stop working post-activation, not be served or priced for indefinitely | **Thread 3 (old nodes' unpriced serving obligation) -- resolved, not deferred.** §14.5's own hazard (a commitment-only block an old, pre-activation node cannot reconstruct) is accepted as the expected, intended consequence of a hard fork, not a gap to engineer around. No sunset/priced-serving mechanism is being built; old nodes are simply expected to be off the network after activation, the same as any hard fork's non-upgraded nodes. **Thread 4 (the downgrade surface) -- resolved on the same basis.** The design doc's own use of the term (P2P format-negotiation) was already mostly mitigated by 4.6.2's per-peer gating; the literal "operator downgrades software post-activation" reading -- never addressed anywhere in the design doc -- needs no new mechanism either, for the identical reason: a downgraded node is just an old, non-upgraded one, and a hard fork already answers what happens to those. **Thread 5 (the no-rollback question) -- confirmed, not merely restated.** This is a DIFFERENT question from 3/4 -- not about old-node compatibility but about whether the NEW chain can reverse its OWN activation post-hoc if a critical bug surfaces -- and the owner's answer carries the same logic across: no rollback mechanism, activation is one-way, a post-activation defect gets a forward-fixing patch (an ordinary hard-fork response), not a reversal. This matches, rather than requires new work against, what F-210 already found built: `update/update.cpp` hardcodes `Active`/`Failed` as terminal `UpdateManager` states with zero transition path out. The code's existing one-way behaviour is now the confirmed INTENDED design, not an unaddressed gap sitting behind it. **Net effect on 4.6's own scope**: none of the three threads need further design or code work before 4.6's activation-criterion sub-step can proceed -- all three are closed by owner decision, matching F-190's own precedent of surfacing unresolved threads for an explicit decision rather than silently choosing for the owner | owner, verbatim decision recorded in conversation, 2026-09-28 -- no code change, a scope/design decision only | **closed 2026-09-28 -- all three of F-205's flagged-for-owner threads now resolved** |
| F-212 | **4.2 reworked: the three data-model gaps F-209 found (per-request-not-per-block granularity, partial-body erasure invisible, tip-only/no-persistence) are closed -- the cross-peer coverage ledger is now keyed by individual block HEIGHT with a deduped, monotonic-upgrade status (`NOT_OBSERVED < MISS < PARTIAL < FULL`), a third status level (`PARTIAL`) separates "first chunk seen" from "full body reconstructed", and the ledger is persisted (`coveragetelemetry.dat`, this codebase's own established `CFlatDB<T>` convention) rather than in-memory-only. Supersedes F-208's original design; does NOT touch F-209's own security fix (hit-after-hash-validation ordering), which is preserved unchanged** | **Gap 1 (per-request granularity) -- closed.** `mapBodyRangeCoverage`'s type changed from `std::map<int, RangeCoverageStats>` (bucket-keyed, a raw hit/miss event COUNTER) to `coveragetelemetry.h`'s `CoverageHeightMap` (`std::map<int, CoverageHeightRecord>`, height-keyed). `RecordCoverageObservation`'s new signature takes one height and one `HeightCoverageStatus` and applies a monotonic-upgrade rule (`if (fObserved > rec.status) { rec.status = fObserved; ... }`) -- a height's status only ever moves UP the ladder, never down, and a repeated or weaker observation is a no-op on the status itself (though `nLastObservedTime` still updates, so "still being asked about" stays visible even when the status doesn't move). F-209's own worked example -- a block retried 18 times (F-185's stall threshold) before finally succeeding -- now leaves exactly ONE record at its best-ever status, not 17 misses plus 1 hit accumulated as independent events. `COVERAGE_RANGE_SIZE`-wide bucket rollups did not disappear: `SummarizeCoverageRange` computes one on demand by scanning the per-height ledger (replacing the old incrementally-maintained `RangeCoverageStats`), and `GetBodyRangeCoverageStats`'s own read side now derives the set of touched buckets from the height-keyed map rather than looking one up directly. **A rollup alone still cannot distinguish a 300-block contiguous erasure from 300 scattered corrupt blocks** (SS14.9's own example, pinned directly by a test: `rollup_counts_are_identical_for_contiguous_vs_scattered_misses`) -- that needs the per-height detail, which is why a NEW read function, `GetBodyRangeCoverageHeights` (bounded by a required height range, matching this file's own established convention against unbounded RPC work), and a NEW RPC, `getbodyrangecoverageheights`, expose it directly rather than this phase attempting to compute "shape" itself (SS14.5: "a query and a counter, not a challenge economy" -- still this phase's own scope limit, unchanged). **Gap 2 (partial-body erasure invisible) -- closed, reusing the existing completion signal per this task's own instruction, not re-deriving it.** A validated first chunk (`nStartIndex == 0`) now records `HeightCoverageStatus::PARTIAL`, not a full hit -- proof only that SOMEONE served the start of this height's body, never that the whole thing was reconstructed. The strong signal, `FULL`, is recorded at exactly ONE call site: immediately after `net_processing.cpp`'s existing body-range completion point succeeds -- where `acc.vBodies.size()` has already reached `BodyRangeWantedCount(pindex)` and `ChainstateActive().ProcessFetchedBodyRange(...)` returns `true`. This is the SAME completion fact the handler already computes to decide whether to call `ActivateBestChain` -- no new per-chunk-position tracking was added anywhere; `mapBodyRangePartial`'s own existing accumulation (`BodyRangeAccumulation::vBodies`, sized against `BodyRangeWantedCount`) already carried everything needed to know whether a fetch had genuinely completed, confirming this task's own hypothesis that this data already existed and only needed a new observer hooked onto it. A peer serving chunk 0 and silently withholding the rest now leaves that height at `PARTIAL` forever (the accumulation is eventually reaped as stale, `BODY_RANGE_PARTIAL_STALE_MICROS`, and never reaches the completion call), visibly distinct from a height that reached `FULL` -- the exact invisibility F-209 flagged. **Gap 3 (tip-only, no persistence) -- closed.** New `CCoverageTelemetryCache` (`coveragetelemetry.h`) wraps `CoverageHeightMap` with `Serialize`/`Unserialize`/`Clear`/`CheckAndRemove`/`ToString`, modelled directly on `spork.h`'s `CSporkManager` (a `SERIALIZATION_VERSION_STRING` guard, silently-empty on a format mismatch rather than throwing) -- deliberately reusing this codebase's own established `CFlatDB<T>` convention (`flat-database.h`; `sporks.dat`/`mncache.dat`/`governance.dat`/`netfulfilled.dat`/`powcache.dat` all use the identical whole-object, checksummed, magic-tagged dump/load pattern) per this task's own explicit instruction to find and reuse an existing pattern rather than inventing a new file format. `net_processing.h`'s `GetCoverageTelemetrySnapshot`/`LoadCoverageTelemetrySnapshot` are the copy-out/copy-in seam (the live ledger stays `cs_main`-guarded and process-global inside `net_processing.cpp`'s own anonymous namespace, exactly as `mapBodyRangeCoverage` always was -- these two functions are its only access point for code outside that file, matching `GetBodyRangeCoverageStats`'s own established shape). `init.cpp` wires: a new load step (Step 7a-bis, right after the sporks-cache load, before networking starts) folding a freshly-read `coveragetelemetry.dat` into the still-empty live ledger via `MergeCoverageTelemetrySnapshot`; a shutdown-time dump (`Shutdown()`, alongside every other small-cache dump, before `node.peer_logic.reset()` -- safe, since the ledger is process-global state independent of that object's own lifetime); and a NEW periodic dump (`DUMP_COVERAGE_TELEMETRY_INTERVAL`, reusing `DUMP_BANS_INTERVAL`'s own already-established 900s value rather than inventing a new number) matching `banlist.dat`'s own precedent -- without this, only a clean shutdown would ever persist accumulated history, and a crashed node would lose everything since its last clean stop, defeating gap 3's own point. `MergeCoverageTelemetrySnapshot` applies the SAME monotonic-upgrade rule `RecordCoverageObservation` uses for a live observation, so a persisted record and a live one can never be combined by two independently-drifting copies of the ordering logic -- the one difference: a merge preserves the INCOMING record's own historical `nLastChangeTime` (the status was already true at some point in the past), where a live observation always stamps the caller's current time. **What is still flagged, not solved, by explicit scope discipline (this task's own "don't build the classifier" instruction) or genuine remaining limitation**: (a) the classifier itself -- distinguishing honest loss from deliberate erasure by reading the shape of per-height data across repeated polls -- remains entirely a later consumer's job (4.6, or an operator), unchanged from F-206/F-208's own original scope; (b) clustering "one operator's multiple nodes" (SS14.9's own erasure signature) still needs peer IP/ASN correlation this telemetry does not attempt; (c) no eviction/rollup policy exists for the per-height ledger's own long-run size -- a live, long-uptime mainnet-scale node could in the worst case accumulate one ~25-byte record per block height ever observed (a fully-covered multi-million-block chain), flagged directly in `coveragetelemetry.h`'s own top-of-file doc comment rather than silently left for someone to discover; this is the SAME class of limitation F-208 already flagged for its own (then per-bucket) map, now restated at per-height granularity, not a new problem this rework introduced. **TDD**: `src/test/coveragetelemetry_tests.cpp` grew from 19 to 38 cases -- new coverage for `RecordCoverageObservation`'s dedup/monotonic-upgrade behaviour (including the exact 17-miss-then-1-hit scenario from F-209's own example), `SummarizeCoverageRange`'s rollup correctness (including the contiguous-vs-scattered rollup-blindness test above), `MergeCoverageTelemetrySnapshot`'s upgrade-only merge semantics, and `CoverageHeightRecord`/`CCoverageTelemetryCache` (de)serialization round trips including a garbage/future-version-string guard test. `src/test/bodyrange_hit_recording_tests.cpp` grew from 3 to 5 cases -- the three original F-207 regression tests ported from the old bucket-level `BodyRangeTally` read to the new per-height `HeightCoverageStatus` read (no change to what they actually guard), plus two new end-to-end tests driving the REAL `BODYRANGE` handler through a genuine multi-chunk fetch (`first_chunk_alone_leaves_a_multi_chunk_height_at_partial_not_full`, `both_chunks_arriving_advances_a_multi_chunk_height_to_full`) -- these needed a `CBlockIndex` with `BLOCK_HAVE_DATA` set but `BLOCK_HAVE_BODIES` clear (a locally-mined-and-connected test block already has its body, so `ProcessFetchedBodyRange`'s own `HaveBodies` guard would otherwise make it a no-op). **Two real methodology bugs found and fixed along the way, by actually running the full suite repeatedly rather than trusting the targeted-suite run alone**: the first construction connected the block normally (`CreateAndProcessBlock`) and then cleared `BLOCK_HAVE_BODIES` directly on the already-connected pindex -- pushing that SAME already-fully-validated block back through `ProcessFetchedBodyRange`/`ActivateBestChain` a second time reproducibly crashed a DIFFERENT, unrelated test's fixture teardown later in the same full-suite run (`CCheckQueue<T>::~CCheckQueue()`'s own `m_worker_threads.empty()` assertion) -- **twice, in two different victim tests** (`acceptancebit_tests`, then `submitblock_tests`, on two separate full-suite runs), consistent with re-entering `ConnectBlock`'s script-check thread pool against an already-connected tip being outside what this harness supports, not a logic bug in the telemetry code. An intermediate attempt narrowed the bit-clearing (dropping an extra `BLOCK_HAVE_BODY_RECORD` clear that wasn't part of `acceptancebit_tests.cpp`'s own established convention) and appeared to fix it on the FIRST full-suite re-run -- but a second full-suite re-run of that same "fix" crashed again, in `submitblock_tests` this time, proving the narrower bit-clearing was not the real fix, just a lower-probability trigger of the same latent issue. The actual fix: never connect the block normally in the first place. New `BuildWithheldBlock` (this test file) uses `g_perf_withhold_hashes`/`PerfWithholdGuard`, matching `acceptancebit_tests.cpp`'s own established convention for exactly this, so the block's first `ProcessNewBlock` call itself lands it in a genuine commitment-only state -- no earlier `ConnectBlock` pass to re-enter. Full suite run twice more after this fix, both green (only the known pre-existing `miner_tests` failure) -- see this test file's own top-of-file doc comment for the full accounting. **Mutation-checked, 7 manual mutants** (this codebase's own established convention, no automated tool): the monotonic-upgrade comparison weakened to `>=` (caught -- `nLastChangeTime` moved on a same-level repeat); the first-observed-time guard removed (caught -- `nFirstObservedTime` no longer stayed pinned); `MergeCoverageTelemetrySnapshot`'s upgrade comparison flipped to a downgrade (`<` instead of `>`, caught by 5 separate assertions across 3 test cases); the persisted-cache version-string guard bypassed entirely (caught -- deserializing a mismatched format threw, rather than the guard test's own expected silent-empty result); `SummarizeCoverageRange`'s `FULL` case miscounted into `nPartial` (caught, 3 assertions); and, in `net_processing.cpp`'s own production glue rather than the pure functions, the completion-hook call removed entirely (caught by both new end-to-end completion tests) and the first-chunk hit site changed to record `FULL` instead of `PARTIAL` (caught by both, one as a `BOOST_REQUIRE` hard-stop) -- **all 7 killed, all individually reverted and reconfirmed byte-identical to the fix, GREEN reconfirmed after each**. **Full suite**: 671 cases pre-rework (baseline, confirmed against this same branch before any F-212 code landed -- 1 known pre-existing `miner_tests` failure, unchanged from every prior row); | F-213 | **F-205's thread 6 (the activation criterion) scoped: resolved as two layers, not one. `UpdateManager`'s own miner/node bit-signalling structurally cannot read 4.2's coverage data -- not a wiring gap 4.2's rework closes, a correctness constraint no amount of wiring can close -- so coverage gates a HUMAN pre-registration decision (whether `chainparams.cpp` commits a real `startHeight` for `EUpdate::COMMITMENT_MODE`), never the vote arithmetic itself. Audit/scoping only, no production code touched** | **1. Confirmed, by reading the vote-counting code itself rather than re-quoting SS14.5's prose, exactly why "neither sees data coverage" (`transaction-decoupling.md:2188-2191`) is true.** `MinerRoundVoting::GetVote` reads only `CBlockIndex::nVersion` bits already loaded in memory (`update/update.cpp:110`, `curIndex->nVersion & update.BitMask()`); `NodeRoundVoting::GetVote` reads mined quorum-commitment TRANSACTION CONTENT and requires `HaveBodies` (`update/update.cpp:174`). Neither touches, nor could touch without new consensus data, `coveragetelemetry.h`'s `CoverageHeightMap` -- confirmed directly: `grep -rn "coveragetelemetry\|CoverageHeightMap\|GetBodyRangeCoverageHeights" src/update/` returns zero matches. **2. The deeper reason this cannot be fixed by more wiring: coverage data is local and non-deterministic across nodes, which disqualifies it from ever entering a consensus rule, independent of who could sample it.** `coveragetelemetry.h`'s own top-of-file doc and `net_processing.h`'s `GetBodyRangeCoverageHeights` doc are explicit that `mapBodyRangeCoverage` reflects only THIS node's own `GETBODYRANGE` round trips plus whatever it persisted and restored (F-212) -- two honest, synced nodes queried at the same moment can legitimately disagree about one height's `HeightCoverageStatus` depending on which peers each has talked to. A consensus rule must produce an identical result on every validating node; making an activation HEIGHT a function of state that is not guaranteed identical across nodes would make block validity depend on which peers a node happened to query -- exactly the nondeterminism consensus code must never have. This is why the design doc says "the machinery cannot read it" rather than "the machinery does not yet read it": it is a structural property of what the telemetry measures (this task's brief's own "a miner can't privately observe network-wide coverage" constraint, generalised to every node, not only miners). **3. Resolution: split "coverage informs activation" from "coverage gates the vote." It does the former, never the latter.** The consensus mechanism stays exactly the shape F-210 already built: `EUpdate::COMMITMENT_MODE` (bit 3, `update/update.h`), a real height reached through `UpdateManager`'s existing miner/node-threshold voting, unchanged by this finding. Coverage's actual role is a PRE-REGISTRATION precondition: something the owner (or an operational monitoring process, not proposed as code here) checks BEFORE `chainparams.cpp` ever calls `updateManager.Add()` for `COMMITMENT_MODE` with a real `startHeight` on mainnet. Registering the bit with a real `startHeight` is the governance action that starts the voting clock; coverage data is what makes taking that action responsible, not something the chain evaluates at block-connect time. This refines, rather than contradicts, §14.1/§14.5's "required by the activation criterion" language (`transaction-decoupling.md:2145-2148`, `:2188-2191`) and closes the real inconsistency F-205 thread 6 flagged in §13's dependency diagram (`:2104-2117`, J drawn independent of H despite the prose stating a dependency): the missing arrow should read "J informs H's registration precondition," not "H's runtime code depends on J" -- the diagram's fault is drawing no arrow, not drawing the wrong kind. **4. Concretely, what the owner should check** (this task's brief's own ask: specific query, threshold, range, sampled by whom). Range: the FULL height span the Smartnode tier is committed to retaining forever (`transaction-decoupling.md:162`, "Everything, forever"), not a recent window -- contrast the windowed mining/full tiers' 720-block body retention (§14.2 A1, `transaction-decoupling.md:2154`), which is a liveness concern, not the irrecoverable-history concern this precondition exists for. Query: `GetBodyRangeCoverageHeights` (`net_processing.h`), the per-height view, not `SummarizeCoverageRange`'s bucket rollup alone -- F-212's own test (`rollup_counts_are_identical_for_contiguous_vs_scattered_misses`) proves a rollup cannot distinguish a concerning gap shape from background noise, and this decision needs to see the actual gap set. Threshold: every queried height's `status` (`coveragetelemetry.h`'s `HeightCoverageStatus`) reads `FULL` -- not a percentage; one un-`FULL` height in the retained range is a specific, nameable, potentially-permanent hole the moment old-format full-block relay of that history stops post-activation. Sampled by whom: nobody, alone -- 4.2 was deliberately built without the classifier or IP/ASN clustering (`coveragetelemetry.h`'s own doc, gaps (a)/(b) F-212 left flagged) that would let one node's view stand for the network's. The precondition has to be checked by polling `getbodyrangecoverageheights` against several independently-operated, well-connected nodes spanning multiple Smartnode operators -- a procedural answer to "a miner can't privately observe network-wide coverage," not an architectural one, matching this task's brief exactly. **5. A concrete, buildable first-pass parameter set for the eventual registration**, once the precondition above passes: reuse `EUpdate::COMMITMENT_MODE` (bit 3), already built (F-210) -- no new bit. Mirror `ROUND_VOTING`'s exact shape (`chainparams.cpp:212-213`): `minerThreshold VoteThreshold(85, 85, 1)`, `nodeThreshold VoteThreshold(0, 0, 1)` (node-threshold structurally disabled, `GetThreshold` always returning 0). Node-threshold stays disabled rather than mirroring `QUORUMS_200_8`'s dual-threshold shape (`chainparams.cpp:387-397`) because `QUORUMS_200_8`'s node vote is a real, verifiable, on-chain fact -- quorum-commitment content -- appropriate because quorum parameters are literally what is being voted on; `COMMITMENT_MODE` has no equivalent verifiable on-chain fact for smartnodes to vote on (see 6, rejected). `forcedUpdate = false`, matching every currently-registered `Update` on every network without exception (confirmed by direct read of all `DEPLOYMENT_V17`/`ROUND_VOTING`/`QUORUMS_200_8` registrations in `chainparams.cpp`'s four network sections: mainnet `:208-214`, testnet `:372-397`, devnet `:540-545`, regtest `:708-713` -- every one passes `false`) -- this deliberately keeps an abort lever: F-211 already decided the fork does not NEED miner consent to be legitimate, but the non-forced shape means a rollout that goes wrong for reasons OTHER than coverage (ecosystem/pool readiness, a late-discovered bug) can still fail to lock in rather than being forced through regardless, at zero extra engineering cost since the machinery already works this way. `roundSize`/`startHeight`/`votingPeriod`/`votingMaxRounds`/`graceRounds` are deliberately NOT proposed here -- genuine owner/operational judgment calls this audit is not scoped to invent, matching F-205's own restraint on the message-length raise's numeric target; `startHeight` specifically must not be chosen until point 4's precondition passes. **6. Rejected option, recorded so it is not rediscovered and re-proposed without this reasoning**: extend `NodeRoundVoting`'s data source so smartnodes embed a self-attested "my local coverage view is FULL" bit into mined quorum-commitment transactions, literally gating the vote threshold on coverage. Rejected: (a) unverifiable self-attestation -- an operator sets the bit regardless of what its node actually observed, the identical trust failure §14.5's own attack describes, relocated from miners to smartnode operators; (b) even honest self-reports do not fix point 2's non-determinism -- one node's local view is not a network-wide fact, and averaging many local views into a threshold vote is a materially weaker guarantee than what "gating on coverage" implies, not a corrected version of it; (c) real new engineering cost (a new quorum-commitment wire field, new validation, new attack surface) purchasing a signal that, once built, still would not be trustworthy. **7. What 4.2's coverage data is actually FOR, stated plainly per this task's own instruction not to force a connection that isn't real**: (a) the one-time pre-registration precondition of point 4; (b) ongoing POST-activation operational monitoring for §14.9's targeted-erasure detection -- the metric's original stated purpose, shipped "with the protocol, not with the retention layer" specifically so it exists before any of this matters (`transaction-decoupling.md:2145-2148`) -- which is unrelated to activation timing and continues unchanged by this finding. Nothing here narrows 4.2's value; it corrects what "required by the activation criterion" means from "read at runtime" to "read before registration." **Conclusion**: 4.6's activation-criterion sub-step (would-be 4.6.3) can now be fully scoped and built. It needs: (i) the `chainparams.cpp` registration in point 5, with `startHeight` left as an explicit placeholder pending the owner's own coverage check; (ii) optionally, a small non-consensus operational script wrapping `getbodyrangecoverageheights` across several configured peer nodes to make point 4's manual check less tedious -- genuinely new, small, out of this audit's own scope (docs-only), flagged as real follow-up work, not built here | `source`, direct read of `update/update.cpp`'s `MinerRoundVoting::GetVote`/`NodeRoundVoting::GetVote`, `update/update.h`'s `UpdateManager`/`Update`/`VoteThreshold`, `chainparams.cpp`'s four network sections' full `Update(...)` argument lists (not just the `ROUND_VOTING` two this task's brief named), `coveragetelemetry.h`/`net_processing.h`'s own doc comments (`GetBodyRangeCoverageHeights`, `HeightCoverageStatus`, `CoverageHeightMap`); `transaction-decoupling.md` §0, §13, §14.1, §14.2 (A1), §14.5, §14.9 read in full; `docs/findings.md` F-205/F-209/F-210/F-211/F-212 read in full; grepped `src/update/` for any existing coverage-telemetry reference (none); no code change, audit/scoping only, matching F-205/F-197's own established discipline for undesignable territory | **scoped 2026-09-28 -- F-205's thread 6, the last of the six 4.6 threads, now has a concrete, buildable design rather than an open question.
