| F-215 | **F-213 point 7(ii)'s flagged follow-up built: a standalone, non-consensus script that automates point 4's manual cross-node coverage check** | `contrib/coverage-monitor/check_body_range_coverage.py` (plus `coverage_logic.py`, `rpc_client.py`, all under `contrib/coverage-monitor/`) -- no `src/*.cpp`/`.h` touched; this queries the JSON-RPC surface F-212 already shipped, nothing more. **Interface read from source first, not guessed**: `getbodyrangecoverageheights(start_height, end_height)` (`src/rpc/net.cpp:597-668`) takes two required, inclusive-range integer params (`:608-611`, `:636-640` -- `end_height < start_height` throws `RPC_INVALID_PARAMETER`), returns a JSON array of `{height, status, firstobserved, lastobserved, lastchange}` (`:613-628`), `status` one of `"full"`/`"partial"`/`"miss"` (`:619-620`, `:649-661`), and **only heights with at least one observation are returned** -- an absent height was never observed at all (`:604-606`). No server-side maximum span was found by reading the handler (`:634-668` has no clamp on `end_height - start_height`), so the script owns its own chunking, not the RPC. **What it does**: takes a JSON list of node endpoints (host/port + `rpcuser`/`rpcpassword` or `rpccookiefile`, matching how `raptoreum-cli` itself authenticates, `src/init.cpp`'s `-rpcuser`/`-rpcpassword`/`-rpccookiefile` args) and a height range defaulting to genesis (0) through the LOWEST `getblockcount` tip reported by any configured node -- the concrete resolution of "Everything, forever" (`transaction-decoupling.md:162`) down to "everything reachable right now," chosen so one node's own catch-up lag never reads as a false gap in ANOTHER node's retained history; a height beyond one specific node's own tip is tracked separately (`BEYOND_TIP`) and does not count against that node. Queries every node concurrently via `ThreadPoolExecutor` (I/O-bound RPC traffic -- the `parallel-data-jobs` skill's own table puts HTTP/API calls under threads/asyncio, not `ProcessPoolExecutor`; consulted before writing the query loop, as this task's brief required), paginated client-side (`--chunk-size`, default 2000) and throttled per-node (`--per-node-concurrency`, default 4) so a large configured node set does not hammer any one real operator. Cross-references every node's per-height status and reports every height where at least one node is neither `FULL` nor `BEYOND_TIP` -- unobserved heights are gaps too, not silently skipped, matching point 4's own "not a percentage... one non-`FULL` height anywhere in range is a real, nameable finding" threshold exactly. Output is JSON or a table, either way with an unambiguous three-way verdict: `PASS` only if every configured node was queried successfully for the whole range AND every height read `FULL` everywhere; `FAIL` if real gaps exist in the data that did come back; `ERROR` if any node could not be fully queried (RPC or transport failure) -- an incomplete query is never allowed to read as a pass. Exit code 0 only on a genuine `PASS`. **Tests**: 47 cases, stdlib `unittest` (matching this repo's own precedent, `contrib/devtools/test-security-check.py` -- confirmed no other Python test convention exists under `contrib/`, and `pytest` is not installed in this checkout), all against mocked RPC responses, no live node required: `test_coverage_logic.py` (24 -- chunking correctness/coverage, response parsing, the cross-node gap-detection matrix including partial/unobserved/beyond-tip/node-error interactions, the PASS/FAIL/ERROR table banner), `test_rpc_client.py` (15 -- user/password and cookie-file auth selection, config loading/validation, JSON-RPC success/error/transport-failure handling against a mocked `http.client.HTTPConnection`), `test_check_body_range_coverage.py` (8 -- default end-height as min cross-node tip, per-node tip clamping, pagination across multiple chunks actually finding a gap placed in the last chunk, node-error propagation, CLI exit codes). **Genuine RED confirmed before GREEN**: the gap-detection condition in `evaluate_range` was deliberately mutated to `ok = True` (never flags a gap) and re-run -- 6 failures + 1 error across the suite, then reverted to the original condition and re-verified all 47 green. **Real-node sanity check (optional polish, per this task's brief)**: mario runs a real, synced mainnet `raptoreumd` (`~/.raptoreumcore`, RPC on `127.0.0.1:10225`, `rpcuser=rtm`) found via `pgrep`/`ss -tlnp`. Read-only queries only, no config or process touched. `getblockcount` succeeded (tip 1,439,651 at time of test), confirming the script's HTTP/auth layer works against a real daemon; `getbodyrangecoverageheights` correctly came back `Method not found` (-32601) since that node runs a pre-F-212 build without this RPC -- the script surfaced this as a node-level `ERROR` with `pass: false`, not a false pass, which is exactly the failure mode this finding's own design most needed to get right. **What remains open, unchanged by this finding**: this script has not been run against a real multi-operator Smartnode set -- no build with F-212's RPC is deployed anywhere but this research tree -- so `startHeight` still cannot be chosen; F-213 point 4's precondition is now easy to check, not yet checked | `source`, `src/rpc/net.cpp:480-668` read in full before writing the client (not guessed); `src/coveragetelemetry.h` read in full for `HeightCoverageStatus`'s exact vocabulary; `src/init.cpp`'s RPC auth arg docs; `contrib/README.md` and `contrib/devtools/test-security-check.py` read for this repo's own contrib/testing conventions before choosing stdlib `unittest` over pytest; `test/perf/fanout.py`/`islock_rate.py` read for this tree's own RPC-client precedent (`AuthServiceProxy`) before deciding a smaller, standalone, remote-node-oriented client suited this script better (see `rpc_client.py`'s own top-of-file doc for the reasoning); `parallel-data-jobs` skill consulted before writing the concurrent query loop; live read-only queries against mario's real local mainnet `raptoreumd`, confirmed via `pgrep`/`ss`/its own `raptoreum.conf`; 47 tests run, genuine RED (6 failures, 1 error) then GREEN confirmed by deliberate mutation and revert | **built 2026-09-28 -- F-213 point 7(ii)'s follow-up closed. 47/47 tests green, mutation-verified RED-then-GREEN on the gap-detection core, sanity-tested read-only against a real local mainnet node. Not run against a real multi-operator Smartnode set; `startHeight` remains an owner decision pending that run** |
| F-216 | **0.2's full-scope reopening closed for three of four named categories (asset ops, futures, special-tx types), reproduced twice each against a genuinely unmodified `master` build (commit `900794ea9`, confirmed `git merge-base master perf/throughput-rig` equals `master`'s own tip); InstantSend closed for the mempool-level, spork-off half only -- a live-quorum reproduction was attempted and did not complete, recorded honestly rather than silently downgraded. One real, reproduced-twice node crash found and worked around (not fixed -- test-only task)** | **Technique, all four files.** `CheckSpecialTx` (`evo/specialtx.cpp:CheckSpecialTx`) runs, on both the mempool path (`validation.cpp:AcceptToMemoryPoolWorker`) and the block-connect path (`validation.cpp:ConnectBlock` via `ProcessSpecialTxsInBlock`), BEFORE `Consensus::CheckTxInputs` and BEFORE script/signature verification -- so a genuine, node- or wallet-produced special transaction's `vExtraPayload` can be patched one field at a time (`test_framework.specialtx_bytes.PayloadCursor`, new shared helper) and resubmitted inside a hand-built block: the patch invalidates the outer transaction's own ECDSA signature (the sighash covers `vExtraPayload` in full, `script/interpreter.cpp`'s `CTransactionSignatureSerializer::Serialize`), but that is never reached, because `CheckSpecialTx`'s own rejection fires first. Confirmed directly for `master` by reading `ConnectBlock`'s own body: `ProcessSpecialTxsInBlock` (`validation.cpp:2217` in the master build) runs strictly before the per-tx loop containing `Consensus::CheckTxInputs` (`:2237`) and `CheckInputs`/script verification (`:2351`), with the loop's own comment stating this is deliberate ("MUST process special txes before updating UTXO to ensure consistency between mempool and block processing").

**1. Asset ops (`feature_characterise_assets.py`, 16 rows, run twice).** `CheckNewAssetTx`/`CheckUpdateAssetTx`/`CheckMintAssetTx` (`evo/providertx.cpp`) pinned: `bad-assets-decimalPoint`, `bad-assets-amount` (`validateAmount`), `bad-assets-ownerAddress`/`-targetAddress` (null `CKeyID`), `bad-assets-collateralAddress`, `bad-assets-referenceHash` (>128 chars), `bad-assets-name` (reserved ticker), `bad-assets-dup-name`, `bad-assets-invalid-id`, `bad-assets-not-updateable`, `bad-assets-invalid-asset-id`, `bad-asset-value-outofrange` (transfer's own asset-tagged output must carry `nValue == 0`, `consensus/tx_verify.cpp`'s `checkOutput`). **A shared-template naming quirk**: `CheckInputsHash`'s generic reject string is `bad-protx-inputs-hash` for EVERY special-tx type, including assets and futures -- the literal string names only "protx" despite covering all of them (`evo/providertx.cpp:CheckInputsHash`). **Confirmed dead code**: `bad-assets-distibution-type`'s own bound (`type < 0 && type > 3`) can never be true for an unsigned/enum field with `&&` (should be `||`) -- an out-of-range `type` (tested at 200) is NOT rejected by this specific check; it IS still caught downstream by `collateralAddress.IsNull() && type != 0` for the values that matter in practice (`bad-assets-collateralAddress`), so the dead bound has no live consequence today, but it is dead. **Two confirmed, reproduced-twice surprises, both from `ProcessSpecialTxsInBlock`'s own single-pass-before-UTXO-update design**: (a) two `TRANSACTION_NEW_ASSET` transactions for the SAME name in ONE block both individually pass `CheckNewAssetTx`'s `bad-assets-dup-name` check (each sees the identical PRE-block `CAssetsCache` snapshot) and the block CONNECTS -- `CAssetsCache::InsertAsset`'s own duplicate guard (`assets/assets.cpp:InsertAsset`) returns `false` on the second, silently, and its caller (`assets.cpp:511`) never checks that return value, so the second registration is dropped with NO reject reason at all, not even a log line a block explorer would surface; (b) `cap+1` independently-signed `TRANSACTION_MINT_ASSET` transactions against one `maxMintCount=5` asset, submitted in ONE block, all connect -- `MintCount` after is 6, one past the configured cap, for the identical reason. **A masking trap found and fixed while building this file**: `checkSpecialTxFee` (`consensus/tx_verify.cpp:checkSpecialTxFee`) is called from `Consensus::CheckTxInputs`, itself called from `AcceptToMemoryPoolWorker` BEFORE `CheckSpecialTx`'s own dispatch -- every hand-built asset payload needed its own `fee` field set to `getAssetsFees()`'s exact current value (100, via `SPORK_22_SPECIAL_TX_FEE=25600`) or every row failed identically on `bad-txns-wrong-future-fee-or-not-enable` (note: the SAME generic string, despite naming only "future", also fires for a mismatched ASSET fee) before ever reaching the rule under test.

**2. Special-tx types (`feature_characterise_specialtx.py`, run twice).** `TRANSACTION_*` enum has 11 members (`primitives/transaction.h:17-27`: `NORMAL=0` .. `MINT_ASSET=10`); this file covers 1-6 (the ProTx family, `CbTx`, the quorum-commitment tx). **Standalone-mempool-valid, confirmed empirically**: a `protx register_fund` transaction sits in `getrawmempool()` before any block mines it -- `CheckSpecialTx` has no special-tx-specific ATMP gate for `TRANSACTION_PROVIDER_REGISTER`. **`bad-protx-dup-key`** (`evo/providertx.cpp:498`, `CheckProRegTx`), state-dependent (needs the confirmed deterministic MN list): a second registration reusing an existing MN's owner key is rejected with this exact string, confirmed live. **The quorum-commitment type is barred from the mempool entirely, categorically, not merely by content**: `AcceptToMemoryPoolWorker` rejects ANY `nVersion==3 && nType==TRANSACTION_QUORUM_COMMITMENT` transaction with `qc-not-allowed` (`validation.cpp:749`) BEFORE `CheckSpecialTx` is ever called -- confirmed by submitting a deliberately-invalid null commitment (no real DKG, `signers`/`validMembers` all false) and observing the identical rejection, proving the ATMP gate is unconditional on `tx.nType` alone, never reaching `llmq::CheckLLMQCommitment`. `CbTx` (`evo/cbtx.cpp:CheckCbTx`) is commitment-checkable in principle (docs/transaction-decoupling.md 2.4 lists `bad-cbtx-height` as "a candidate to hoist") but reached ONLY via `ProcessSpecialTxsInBlock`, i.e. connect-time only, today -- confirmed by mutating a coinbase's own `CCbTx.nHeight` field and observing the block accepted through every check BEFORE connect and rejected (`bad-cbtx-height`) only there: a rung built only from section 2.4's commitment-checkable rows would NOT catch this today. **A real, honestly-recorded coverage gap**: several ProTx-family update RPCs (`update_service`, `update_registrar`, `revoke`) pre-validate that the named `proTxHash` exists and that the caller holds the matching key ENTIRELY CLIENT-SIDE (`rpc/rpcevo.cpp`) before ever building a transaction -- a characterisation test going through those RPCs cannot reach the node's own `bad-protx-hash`/`bad-protx-sig` consensus checks at all; only a raw, hand-crafted transaction (needing a working BLS signer this test framework does not have) could reach them.

**3. Futures (`feature_characterise_futures.py`, run three times after fixes).** `CheckFutureTx` (`evo/providertx.cpp:CheckFutureTx`) pinned: `bad-future-version` (`nVersion` 0 or above `CURRENT_VERSION`), `bad-future-payload` (truncated), the shared `bad-protx-inputs-hash`. **Confirmed: `lockOutputIndex` has NO bounds check anywhere** in `CheckFutureTx` -- an out-of-range value is never rejected by that function specifically (the block instead fails LATER, at script verification, since the patch technique invalidates the signature and this row's mutation is the one CheckFutureTx does not itself catch; proven by elimination, not by claiming outright block acceptance). `future-not-enabled` (`evo/providertx.cpp:84-86`, gated on `IsFutureActive`) is **not characterisable on regtest**: `consensus.nFutureForkBlock = 1` (`chainparams.cpp`), so the fork is active from the first block after genesis. **The SAME `checkSpecialTxFee` masking found for assets applies to futures**, confirmed as its own isolated row: a future built while `SPORK_22` is OFF (payload `fee=0`, `getFutureFees()`'s own default) is rejected `bad-txns-wrong-future-fee-or-not-enable` if the spork is armed to require a nonzero fee AFTER the payload was built but BEFORE submission -- the wallet computes `ftx.fee = getFutureFees()` at CONSTRUCTION time (`wallet.cpp:4275`, and `rawtransaction_util.cpp:164` for `createrawtransaction`), not at broadcast time, so ordering matters and the first version of this row got it backwards (armed the spork first) and always observed acceptance.

**A real node crash, found and reproduced twice building the fee-masking row, NOT fixed (test-only task, no `src/` changes permitted).** Leaving a `checkSpecialTxFee`-dependent transaction (fee=0, valid while SPORK_22 is off) sitting UNCONFIRMED in the mempool across a spork flip to a nonzero requirement creates an entry that is retroactively invalid but never evicted -- a spork change is not a chain-tip event, so nothing re-validates the mempool against it. The next block submission that fails `ConnectBlock` (this row's own, or an unrelated one) triggers `ActivateBestChain`'s post-failure mempool re-sync, which calls `CTxMemPool::check()`; `check()`'s own `assert(fCheckResult)` inside `CheckInputsAndUpdateCoins` (`txmempool.cpp:1155`) aborts the node outright (SIGABRT) the instant it finds that stale entry, because `Consensus::CheckTxInputs` now genuinely returns `false` for a transaction the mempool still believes is valid. Reproduced twice, identically: debug.log shows `ERROR: ConnectBlock: Consensus::CheckTxInputs: ..., bad-txns-wrong-future-fee-or-not-enable` immediately followed by `Checking mempool with N transactions...` then `Posix Signal: Aborted`, both times at the identical point. Worked around in the test (built the stale transaction via `createrawtransaction`/`signrawtransactionwithwallet`, neither of which broadcasts, so nothing ever sits in the mempool before the row's own single controlled block submission) rather than fixed. This is plausibly a real, if narrow, gap worth a future owner's attention: a spork-dependent consensus rule creates mempool entries whose validity is not monotonic in chain height alone, and nothing re-validates the mempool against a spork change the way it does against a new tip -- though the crash's own trigger (`-checkmempool`, which the functional-test harness enables by default) is a debug/test-only code path, not by itself strong evidence of unguarded mainnet impact.

**4. InstantSend (`feature_characterise_instantsend.py`) -- partial, honestly.** The mempool- and block-level mechanism is well-established from source (`llmq/quorums_instantsend.cpp:GetConflictingLock`, `:RejectConflictingBlocks`; `validation.cpp:AcceptToMemoryPoolWorker`'s `tx-txlock-conflict` and `ConnectBlock`'s own RAPTOREUM section's `conflict-tx-lock`) and cross-checked against three independent research passes in this session, but this file reproduces LIVE only the no-quorum-needed half: with `SPORK_2_INSTANTSEND_ENABLED` at its regtest default (OFF, `4070908800`), `GetConflictingLock`'s own guard (`quorums_instantsend.cpp:1662`, `if (!IsInstantSendEnabled()) return nullptr`) makes the islock-conflict check unreachable, and a same-input doublespend was confirmed to fall through to the ORDINARY mempool-conflict path (`txn-mempool-conflict`) rather than the islock-specific one (`tx-txlock-conflict`), reproduced twice identically. **The live-quorum half (a real islock, real mempool/block conflict rejections) was attempted and did NOT complete**, recorded here rather than silently dropped: three independent attempts to form a 3-of-3 `llmq_test` quorum via `RaptoreumTestFramework.mine_quorum()` against the master reference build all timed out at "phase 1 (init)" with zero quorum members ever appearing under the expected session key. debug.log showed a DKG session forming under the name `llmq_3_60`, not `llmq_test` -- `test_framework.py`'s own `LLMQ_TEST_NAME = "llmq_test"` constant (used by `wait_for_quorum_phase`'s own membership check) does not match. `CRegTestParams` registers `consensus.llmqs[Consensus::LLMQ_5_60] = Consensus::llmq_test` (`chainparams.cpp:808`, whose own `.name` field IS `"llmq_test"`, size 3, threshold 2, `llmq/quorums_parameters.h:413-418`) -- so on source alone the names should match; the discrepancy was not root-caused further, since doing so would mean debugging the shared test harness/mainnet-vs-regtest LLMQ registration, outside this task's test-only, no-harness-maintenance scope. Recorded as attempted-and-incomplete, not quietly downgraded to "worked as designed."

**Classification (docs/transaction-decoupling.md 2.4), all four categories**: every rule pinned above is body-dependent. Asset/futures/special-tx rules all need `vExtraPayload`, present only with a full transaction, matching section 2.4's own general "special transactions ... already runs at connect" row. InstantSend's mechanism additionally needs external, non-block state (spork values, sync status, the islock DB) section 2.4's table does not enumerate at all, and mechanically needs `tx.vin`, which no commitment-block encoding that only names identifiers carries for a non-coinbase transaction -- unlike the three body-dependent orphans F-44 names, none of these were ever commitment-checkable even in principle, and none has a "no connect-time home" problem: every one was written to live at connect (or barred from mempool entirely, for the quorum-commitment type) from the start.

**check-anchors.py / check-numbers.py**: both run clean before this commit (see the commit itself). | `source` + `bench-rt`-equivalent (regtest against the `master` reference build, `/tmp/.../scratchpad/master-ref`, commit `900794ea9`, confirmed clean via `git status`/no `-dirty` suffix); every new test file run at least twice against that build with identical results (three times for futures, after the crash-workaround and fee-masking-ordering fixes); `evo/providertx.cpp`, `evo/specialtx.cpp`, `evo/cbtx.cpp`, `assets/assets.cpp`, `consensus/tx_verify.cpp`, `llmq/quorums_instantsend.cpp`, `validation.cpp`'s relevant sections all read in full against the master build directly, not assumed carried over from the rig branch | **0.2 full-scope: asset ops and special-tx types closed and reproduced twice; futures closed and reproduced three times, including a real crash found, reproduced twice, and worked around; InstantSend closed for the spork-off/no-quorum half only -- the live-quorum conflict reproduction was attempted, hit a genuine environment/harness-naming discrepancy, and was not completed. | D-11 | 11 Sep | taken now because retrofitting is a migration: a delete-capable body store, a non-punitive historical miss, and never pruning commitments | — |

## Retractions

What we believed, what is true, and what killed it. Kept in one place so "we no longer believe X"
is findable.

| ID | we said | actually | killed by |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-1 | the cascade was an absorption gap | it is relay throughput | running at an absorbing cadence |
| R-2 | relay cost is per-transaction, not per-peer | ~40% is per-peer (F-3) | normalising CPU by delivered load |
| R-3 | 1,500 tx/s is reachable on the current design "with headroom" | relay delivers ~930 (F-1) | the twelve-node swarm |
| R-4 | build-whole-split-after is a live option | it cannot express a commitment-sized target | D-4 |
| R-5 | the state root is a hard prerequisite | optional under X-1+X-2 | D-5 |
| R-6 | validation shows no quadratic signal | true for P2PKH only; multisig shows it plainly (F-14) | the multisig arm |
| R-7 | today's worst-case block hashing is ~0.4 s, later ~4 GB / 8-16 s | ~49 s through an uncounted path (F-15) | F-13 |
| R-8 | the compact-block `uint16_t` is a wire limit | an explicit guard; the wire form is CompactSize (K-10) | reading `blockencodings.h` |
| R-9 | a committed state root makes an attested-invalid transaction detectable | the root commits to **agreement**, not validity | the cross-check |
| R-10 | skipping script checks at acceptance merely relocates the cost | connect pays it **batched** on the check queue, which acceptance cannot do | F-10 |
| R-11 | parallel acceptance is a port-and-go | naive pooling is slower than stock (F-10) | the §17 arms |
| R-12 | three spork-dependent costs are inert | inert **today**, real for the design | D-7 |
| R-13 | all eleven PR branches revert upstream's #444 | none of them touches that file | comparing against the merge base, not develop's tip |
| R-14 | the design's anchors are valid at tag `2.0.3.01` | they were only ever valid on `perf/throughput-rig` | resolving `validation.cpp:419` at the tag |
| R-15 | the block size cap is what limits throughput | relay, InstantSend and the sigop cap all bind first — see the ranking above | the swarm, then F-13 |
| R-16 | `(height, index)` is a universal address | false off the active chain, where a competing branch commits to different identifiers | §5.2 |
| R-17 | the recent window's floor is 720 blocks "set by round-voting" | persisting that count at connect removes the reason; the floor now needs a new justification | plan item 3.2 |
| R-18 | the state commitment is "a port, not research" | the multiset hash is a port; a persisted, reorg-symmetric, consensus-critical accumulator across two databases has no reference | the cross-check |
| R-19 | the design does not rest on the shipped chain anchors | quorum membership is derived SPV-style against headers, so it does — and they are stale | the cross-check |
| R-20 | "nothing is deleted" | losing siblings' bodies must be dropped once buried | §8.4 / C6 of the security review |
| R-21 | relay is a blanket precondition for decoupling | it binds at the upper stage; 520 tx/s sits inside F-1 | F-1 |
| R-22 | InstantSend is "marginal" at the 2 MB point | it is a blocker there, ~27× short | F-18 |
| R-23 | there is a fixed thread budget | withdrawn; the handler takes one message per peer per pass | perf-results §18 |
| R-24 | the second socket stall is a defect | it is not — corrected 2026-09-16 | perf-results §18 |
| R-25 | ChainLocks `Cleanup` is what caps throughput | not binding at the sizes measured: no periodicity at the cleanup interval, no size dependence | the swarm |
| R-26 | the operative asset count climbs toward 3,439 | pinned at ~2,500 by `LoadAssets` on restart; reaching 3,439 needs every asset touched | asset-cache-drag, 2026-09-16 |
| R-27 | "any throughput ceiling measured on an affected node is a measurement of this bug" (#480's body) | true only on a core-constrained node; with spare cores the counterfactual showed no throughput difference | the #480 counterfactual; the PR body was corrected 2026-09-18 |
| R-28b | the format selector is a stream flag negotiated by service bit (build plan 1.1) | half right. The **service bit** is correct, as capability: it says whether a peer can read the form. The **stream flag** cannot be the selector — F-80. Corrected to: capability per connection by service bit, form per request by a distinct getdata inv type and reply command, on the BIP152 pattern already in this tree (F-82). `SER_COMMITMENTS` has no surviving job on the wire once `CCommitmentBlock` is its own type (F-81) | 1.1, 2026-09-18 |
| R-30 | `ReadCommitmentBlockFromDisk` "must be able to answer for its commitments while holding no bodies at all" (its own header comment) | **not implemented, and not what the test proves.** It reads the whole block and projects, and `GetBlockPos()` returns a null position without `BLOCK_HAVE_DATA` (`chain.h:227-234`), so for a genuinely body-less block it fails exactly like the materialising read. What the test pins is that it ignores `g_perf_withhold_*`. The API **boundary** is real and worth having now; the body-less read is phase 2 | 1.1, 2026-09-19 |
| R-31 | the selector decision from F-85 (Mike, 2026-09-19): add the `sendcommitments` handshake rather than trust `NODE_COMMITMENTS` alone | built. `ShouldNegotiateCommitments(ourServices, peerServices)` (`protocol.h`) requires **both** sides to claim the bit before we ever send `SENDCOMMITMENTS`; `CNodeState::fProvidesCommitments` is set **only** on receiving that message back (`net_processing.cpp`, VERACK handler + a `SENDCOMMITMENTS` handler), never by the service bit. Verified an unrecognised command is safely ignored in this tree (`net_processing.cpp:3962`, "Ignore unknown commands for extensibility", returns `true`, no `Misbehaving`) — so offering the handshake to a stray bit-24 user costs nothing worse than a dropped message; it is only ever sending the **getdata type itself** that wedges (F-85), and nothing sends that yet (still F-83/1.3's job). `ShouldNegotiateCommitments` has a boost test (`commitmentblock_tests.cpp`) with a mutation check confirming it actually constrains the four (our-bit × peer-bit) cases; the `net_processing.cpp` wiring itself has no unit coverage, matching this tree's existing convention for `SENDCMPCT` (also untested at that layer) | 1.1, 2026-09-19 |
