TDD (5 new `assets_tests.cpp` cases, genuine RED confirmed against the unmodified worktree binary before any fix, GREEN after); live re-verification of bugs 1 and 3 against a real built `raptoreumd`/`feature_characterise_assets.py` (F-216's own harness, extended); the disconnect-time existence-check bug found and confirmed via direct, live `InvalidateBlock` return-value inspection (`ok=0`/no-reason before, `ok=1` after), not inferred from source reading alone | **F-223, built 2026-09-29 -- all three of build-plan 3.4's named bugs fixed and TDD-confirmed; the row's own existence-check prerequisite for B9 found and fixed in the same pass; closes build-plan 3.4** |

| F-224 | **3.4b built in full -- item 1 (bound/evict the global asset cache) AND item 2 (eliminate the copy for asset transactions too), the follow-ups `docs/archive/asset-cache-drag.md` deliberately left out of the #481 guard fix. Item 2's own investigation, done properly rather than waved off as "the archive doc already said it was risky", surfaced two further pre-existing bugs -- one found live, by a real functional-test failure, not source reading.** **1. Item 1: a real LRU, not a size check.** `LoadAssets` (`assets/assetsdb.cpp:LoadAssets`) caps `passetsCache->mapAsset`/`mapAssetId` at `MAX_CACHE_ASSETS_SIZE` (`assets/assets.h:MAX_CACHE_ASSETS_SIZE`, 2500) only at startup; `CAssetsCache::Flush` (`assets/assets.cpp:Flush`) then merged every subsequently-touched asset into the global maps with no cap, ever -- unbounded growth for the life of the process. New `TouchAsset`/`TouchAssetName`/`EvictOverflowAssets`/`EvictOverflowAssetIds` (`assets/assets.h`, `assets/assets.cpp`) implement genuine recency-ordered eviction (a `std::list` + index map per bounded map, not FIFO-by-key or arbitrary `std::map` iteration order, which for a string-keyed map is sorted-key order and unrelated to actual use) -- wired into `Flush`'s own merge loop and into the three direct-read paths on the global instance itself (`GetAssetMetaData`/`CheckIfAssetExists`/`GetAssetId`, `assets/assets.cpp`), guarded by `this == passetsCache.get()` so a local per-call cache (the old ATMP copy, a block's own scratch/real cache) never populates or pays for the LRU structures -- confirmed this guard is exhaustive by checking every call site of `InsertAsset`/`UpdateAsset`/`RemoveAsset` (`validation.cpp`, `evo/specialtx.cpp`) and none ever receives `passetsCache.get()` directly, only local/scratch caches. `LoadAssets` itself now registers its own startup-loaded entries with `TouchAsset`/`TouchAssetName` too, or the ~2,500 assets loaded at boot would sit outside the LRU's own tracking forever and never be eviction candidates. **2. The correctness gate for item 1, found and closed before it could ship broken.** `DumpCacheToDatabase` (`assets/assets.cpp:DumpCacheToDatabase`) is the ONLY path that durably writes an asset to `passetsdb`, and runs on `FlushStateToDisk`'s own periodic/critical schedule (`DATABASE_FLUSH_INTERVAL` = 24h, `validation.h:DATABASE_FLUSH_INTERVAL`, or sooner only under coin-cache pressure -- confirmed by reading `FlushStateToDisk`'s own `fDoFullFlush` conditions, `validation.cpp:FlushStateToDisk`), not on every block's `Flush`. So a just-confirmed asset can have no LevelDB copy at all for up to a day; naive LRU eviction could drop its only copy in that window, making it invisible to every reader. `EvictOverflowAssets`/`EvictOverflowAssetIds` now skip any candidate still present in `NewAssetsToAdd` (not yet durable) and accept temporary cap overflow rather than lose data -- proven with a real test (`asset_cache_eviction_never_drops_unflushed_assets`, `test/assets_tests.cpp`) that fills the cache past cap with entries deliberately never flushed to DB and confirms every one survives while the durable pool is evicted first. **3. Item 2: the archive doc's own named correctness trap, closed by finding its exact root cause, not routed around.** Read all three consumer methods in full: `GetAssetMetaData` (`assets/assets.cpp:GetAssetMetaData`) already had a correct three-tier fallback (local cache -> global `passetsCache` -> `passetsdb`) -- safe for an empty cache already, no change needed. `CheckIfAssetExists` and `GetAssetId` (`assets/assets.cpp`) did NOT -- both went straight from a local-cache miss to `passetsdb`, skipping the global tier `GetAssetMetaData` already proved safe. This IS the trap: an empty (non-copying) ATMP cache checking these two methods would miss an asset that exists only in `passetsCache`'s own dirty/unflushed state. Fixed by adding the missing middle tier to both, mirroring `GetAssetMetaData`'s own pattern exactly, including inserting the found entry into the LOCAL map too (needed because a caller routinely turns around and reads/mutates `mapAsset[assetId]` directly on the SAME instance right afterward -- confirmed the hard way, see point 4). For `CheckIfAssetExists` specifically, also added a check of the GLOBAL `NewAssetsToRemove` masking set before the global `mapAsset` check -- required because today's FULL-COPY ATMP cache (`validation.cpp`, pre-fix) already carries `passetsCache`'s own `NewAssetsToRemove` via its copy constructor, so an empty cache's new middle tier has to replicate that masking too, not just the positive existence check, to stay behaviourally equivalent. Confirmed by reading `CheckNewAssetTx`/`CheckUpdateAssetTx`/`CheckMintAssetTx` (`evo/providertx.cpp`) and all three `MakeSignString` overloads (`evo/providertx.cpp`) in full: every one of their cache interactions is exactly `GetAssetMetaData`/`GetAssetId`/`CheckIfAssetExists` -- never a mutating method. `AcceptToMemoryPoolWorker`'s own `AssertLockHeld(cs_main)` (`validation.cpp:AcceptToMemoryPoolWorker`) confirmed the new direct reads of `passetsCache->mapAsset`/`NewAssetsToRemove` from within ATMP are race-free -- every writer (`Flush`, `DumpCacheToDatabase`) also requires `cs_main`. ATMP's guard (`validation.cpp`) changed from `std::make_unique<CAssetsCache>(*passetsCache.get())` (the O(confirmed assets) copy #481 already removed from the payment path but not the asset-tx path) to `std::make_unique<CAssetsCache>()` (O(1)). **4. Bug found #1 (pre-existing, independent of this row, found while proving item 2 safe): `DisconnectBlock`'s NEW_ASSET-undo branch was already unreachable for a not-yet-flushed asset.** `DisconnectBlock` (`validation.cpp`) already called `CheckIfAssetExists` against `DisconnectTip`'s own fresh, empty per-call cache (`validation.cpp:DisconnectTip`, `CAssetsCache assetCache;`) BEFORE this row touched anything -- without the global middle tier, disconnecting a block whose asset creation had not yet been durably flushed silently no-op'd: `CheckIfAssetExists` wrongly returned false, `RemoveAsset` was never called, and the block "successfully" disconnected while the asset stayed resident. Genuine RED confirmed against the unmodified tree (`assets_new_asset_undo_visible_before_flush`, `test/assets_tests.cpp`): `InvalidateBlock` succeeded but `CheckIfAssetExists` still reported the asset existing afterward. Same bug class as F-223's B9 (a fresh-empty-cache existence check with no global fallback), a different method (`CheckIfAssetExists` vs. `mapAsset.count()`), not caught by F-223's own audit of that area. **5. Bug found #2, found live via a real functional-test failure, not source reading.** Fixing bug #1 made `RemoveAsset` reachable for the first time on a not-yet-flushed asset -- which exposed that `RemoveAsset`/`DumpCacheToDatabase` (`assets/assets.cpp`) never erased the in-memory `mapAsset`/`mapAssetId` entries, only the `passetsdb` row: `DumpCacheToDatabase`'s own `ClearDirtyCache` (`assets/assets.cpp`) wipes `NewAssetsToRemove` (the masking protection `CheckIfAssetExists`'s new global tier relies on) in the SAME call that durably erases the DB row, but the stale map entries lived on forever. Found by running `test/functional/feature_assets_rules.py` (this row's own required verification) against the built daemon: `test_reorg`'s own `assert_equal(node.getbestblockhash(), blockhash)` failed after `invalidateblock` -> `listassets` (whose own `GetListAssets`, `assets/assetsdb.cpp:GetListAssets`, forces exactly the flush that clears the masking) -> `reconsiderblock`; the node's `debug.log` showed `ConnectBlock(RAPTOREUM): ProcessSpecialTxsInBlock ... failed with bad-assets-dup-name` -- a legitimate block RECONNECT, not just an ATMP mempool-policy question, rejected by a stale cache entry. **Fixed**: `DumpCacheToDatabase` now erases `mapAsset`/`mapAssetId` (and their LRU records, `assetLruOrder`/`assetLruIndex`/`assetIdLruOrder`/`assetIdLruIndex`) in the same pass it erases the `passetsdb` row, so the in-memory cache and the DB go stale together instead of the cache lagging forever. Genuine RED reproduced directly in C++ (`block_reconnect_after_flush_does_not_see_stale_dup_name`, `test/assets_tests.cpp`): create+mine a NEW_ASSET tx, `InvalidateBlock`, force the same flush via `passetsCache->DumpCacheToDatabase()`, then `ResetBlockFailureFlags`+`ActivateBestChain` (exactly what the `reconsiderblock` RPC does, `rpc/blockchain.cpp`) -- pre-fix, best-block-hash mismatch, matching the live functional-test failure exactly; post-fix, GREEN, and `feature_assets_rules.py` re-run against the rebuilt daemon: "Tests successful". **6. Not fixed, noted as a separate, narrower, out-of-scope observation**: `mapAssetAddressAmount` (the `-assetindex` address-balance map) has no analogous eviction or clear-on-removal treatment -- it is gated behind the optional `fAssetIndex` flag, never read by `CheckNewAssetTx`/`CheckUpdateAssetTx`/`CheckMintAssetTx`/any `MakeSignString` overload (confirmed by reading all of them), so it does not share item 1 or item 2's correctness stakes; left for a future row rather than expanding this one's scope. Also noted, not fixed: `CTxMemPool::_clear`/`clear` (`txmempool.cpp`) does not clear `mapAssetsToHash`/`mapAssetsIdToHash`, discovered while designing (and then redesigning around, via `removeRecursive` instead) a test scenario -- an orthogonal mempool-index bug, not an asset-cache one. **7. Tests and mutation coverage.** `test/assets_tests.cpp`: 8 new cases total (3 for item 1's eviction mechanism, 5 for item 2's correctness chain), all against `RegTestingSetup`/`TestChainDIP3BeforeActivationSetup` as appropriate; every RED claimed above was reproduced genuinely (either by a saved-patch revert of the specific implementation lines and a real rebuild, or by disabling one specific check at a time with the surrounding code otherwise untouched), never assumed. 9 manual mutations applied one at a time, rebuilt, confirmed caught, then reverted: removing `EvictOverflowAssets`'s dirty-entry skip, removing `EvictOverflowAssetIds`'s dirty-entry skip, reversing the LRU walk direction (evicts most- instead of least-recently-used), disabling `Flush`'s touch/evict wiring entirely, removing `CheckIfAssetExists`'s global `NewAssetsToRemove` masking check, removing `CheckIfAssetExists`'s global `mapAsset` positive check, removing `CheckIfAssetExists`'s local-map insert (reproduces bug #1's own `InvalidateBlock`-fails-outright symptom exactly), removing `GetAssetId`'s global middle tier, and disabling `DumpCacheToDatabase`'s new `mapAsset`/`mapAssetId` erasure (reproduces bug #2's own best-block-hash-mismatch symptom exactly). **8. Full verification.** `assets_creation_tests`: 15 cases, all green. All three functional test files this row's own instructions named: `feature_assets.py`, `feature_assets_rules.py` (the one that found bug #2), `feature_characterise_assets.py` -- all "Tests successful" against the rebuilt `raptoreumd`. Full C++ suite: 738 cases, 1 failure -- `miner_tests/CreateNewBlock_validity` (`test/miner_tests.cpp:413`), the same pre-existing failure every prior row in this doc names, not a regression. `check-anchors.py`/`check-numbers.py` both exit 0. | `source` (`assets/assets.h`/`.cpp`, `assets/assetsdb.cpp`, `validation.cpp`/`.h`, `evo/providertx.cpp`, `evo/specialtx.cpp`, `txmempool.cpp` all read in full for the relevant paths); TDD (8 new `assets_tests.cpp` cases, genuine RED confirmed for each before its fix, GREEN after); 9 manual mutations, all caught; live re-verification against a real built `raptoreumd` for all three named functional test files, including the one (`feature_assets_rules.py`) that found bug #2 live rather than by source reading | **F-224, built 2026-09-29 -- both 3.4b items shipped (not just item 1); two further pre-existing bugs found and fixed in the same investigation; closes build-plan 3.4b** |

| F-225 | **4.3.1 built: the asset index's only rebuildable half is a pure coins-set scan, no `-reindex` needed -- confirming the row's own claim while correcting its scope. `-assetindex` gates ONLY the address<->asset balance secondary index, not asset existence/metadata, which was never gated in the first place and is already durable on any node that has ever synced.** **1. What `-assetindex` actually gates, settled by direct read before designing anything.** `fAssetIndex` (`validation.h:fAssetIndex`) is checked at exactly five sites, all in the SAME narrow area: `assets.cpp:AddAssetBlance`, `assets.cpp:RemoveAddressBalance`, `assets.cpp`'s `AddAssets` (the per-output balance-tracking call, not the asset-existence half just above it in the same function), and `validation.cpp:UpdateCoins`'s own input-spend loop -- every one of them reads or writes `mapAssetAddressAmount`/passetsdb's `ASSET_ADDRESS_AMOUNT`+`ADDRESS_ASSET_AMOUNT` keys (`assetsdb.cpp`), the "who holds how much of asset X" view `listaddressesbyasset`/`listassetbalancesbyaddress` (`rpc/rpcassets.cpp`) read. Asset EXISTENCE/metadata (`mapAsset`/`mapAssetId`, `ASSET_FLAG`/`ASSET_NAME_TXID_FLAG`) is written UNCONDITIONALLY: `InsertAsset`/`UpdateAsset`/`RemoveAsset` (`assets.cpp`) are never gated, and `DumpCacheToDatabase`'s own `NewAssetsToAdd`/`NewAssetsToRemove` loops (`assets.cpp:DumpCacheToDatabase`) write/erase the `ASSET_FLAG`/`ASSET_NAME_TXID_FLAG` rows regardless of `fAssetIndex` -- confirmed by reading every line between them, not inferred from the flag's name. Since no snapshot-load path exists yet (§8 of `transaction-decoupling.md`, still true), every node that has ever reached its own tip did so by processing every block through `ConnectBlock`, so this existence/metadata half was ALREADY built on any such node regardless of whether `-assetindex` was ever passed -- there is nothing here for a coins-scan, or any rebuild, to do. **2. The row's own claim, checked against real output shapes, not assumed.** Each asset-carrying output encodes `(assetId, amount)` in its own script (`GetTransferAsset`, `assets/assetstype.cpp`), and `AddAssetBlance`/`RemoveAddressBalance` do nothing but add/subtract that SAME per-output amount, keyed by the output's own destination address (`ExtractDestination`) -- exactly a running sum over currently-unspent asset outputs, with no dependence on any output that is already spent. A coins-set scan reproduces this fixed point in one pass; confirmed no RPC exposes a HISTORICAL balance that would need spent-output data the coins set cannot supply. **3. Built**: `BuildAssetIndexFromCoins(CCoinsView&)` (`assets/assets.h`/`assets.cpp:BuildAssetIndexFromCoins`) walks `view.Cursor()` (`node/coinstats.cpp`'s `GetUTXOStats` own precedent for full-coins-set iteration), accumulates `(assetId, address) -> CAmount128` for every unspent `IsAssetScript()` output, then calls the new `CAssetsDB::EraseAssetAddressAmounts` (`assetsdb.h`/`.cpp`) to clear the existing balance rows before writing the fresh totals -- making the rebuild authoritative regardless of prior state, not an incremental patch. **4. Wired into `init.cpp`'s existing assetindex-state-change check** (previously an unconditional `-reindex`-or-fail for ANY change, `init.cpp` just above `fAssetIndexNeedsCoinsScan`): turning `-assetindex` OFF->ON outside of `-reindex`/`-reindex-chainstate` now sets `fAssetIndexNeedsCoinsScan` instead of erroring; the actual scan runs later in the SAME init pass, right after chainstate init completes (`init.cpp:2595-2599`, `BuildAssetIndexFromCoins(::ChainstateActive().CoinsDB())`), since it needs a working coins database. Turning it OFF needs nothing -- the flag just stops being read/written, no data invalidation required, so that direction is now allowed unconditionally too (a deliberate, argued relaxation of the prior universal-reindex requirement, not an oversight). Under an explicit `-reindex`/`-reindex-chainstate`, the coins-scan is skipped -- the existing replay path already handles it (asset existence data is wiped and rebuilt from genesis for `-reindex`; `fAssetIndex` is set to the requested value before any replay begins either way, so a concurrent reindex-plus-flag-flip still ends up correct). `-addressindex`/`-timestampindex`/`-spentindex`/`-futureindex` are genuinely different and untouched: each is a full historical LOG (every touched txid/address, including already-spent ones), not a pure aggregate over current state, so a coins-scan cannot rebuild them -- this is the real reason the row singles out `-assetindex` specifically, confirmed by reading what each of those four actually indexes, not assumed from the row's own framing. **5. TDD, two real bugs found and fixed via the test, not assumed.** New `test/assets_tests.cpp:build_asset_index_from_coins_reconstructs_current_balances` mines a real chain with `fAssetIndex` at its real default (`false`, `validation.h:DEFAULT_ASSETINDEX`) throughout -- confirmed no test in this suite ever sets it, so this is a genuine "index never built" starting condition, not simulated -- creates+mints+splits an asset across two addresses, and confirms the rebuild reproduces the correct per-address balances. First run failed: the coins-set scan must force-flush the chainstate first (`ChainstateActive().ForceFlushStateToDisk()`, added to `BuildAssetIndexFromCoins`), matching `assetsdb.cpp`'s own established `GetListAssets`/`GetListAssetsByAddress`/`GetListAddressByAssets` precedent -- `CoinsTip()`'s in-memory cache can hold dirty, not-yet-flushed coin changes a DB cursor never sees. A second scenario (move an address's entire holding away, then rebuild) found a second real bug: the in-memory `passetsCache->mapAssetAddressAmount` cache must be cleared alongside the on-disk rebuild, or `GetBestAssetAddressAmount`'s own cache-before-DB read order (`assets.cpp:GetBestAssetAddressAmount`) would keep answering with the stale pre-move balance even after the DB was correctly cleared -- fixed by clearing the in-memory map before repopulating it, symmetric with the DB-side erase. **6. Mutation-tested**, three mutations against `BuildAssetIndexFromCoins`'s own three load-bearing lines, each independently disabled/rebuilt/reverted: the in-memory cache clear (caught by the moved-away-balance assertion), the DB-side `EraseAssetAddressAmounts` call (caught by the same moved-away scenario's stale-DB-row assertions), and the `IsAssetScript()` guard (caught immediately -- no balances written at all). **7. Functional coverage.** New `test/functional/feature_assetindex_late_enable.py` (registered in `test_runner.py` next to `feature_assets.py`, matching F-158's own registration-lesson convention): a real node with `-assetindex` OFF the whole time creates/mints/splits a real asset via real RPC calls, confirms the documented refusal string and that existence/metadata is already correct, restarts WITH `-assetindex` and NO `-reindex`, and confirms both addresses' balances are immediately correct -- then restarts with it OFF again. Found and fixed a test-construction bug (not production): the first draft let `sendasset`'s own asset change land on a fresh wallet-internal address instead of the owner's, giving an empty result -- fixed via the RPC's own `asset_change_address` parameter. Also re-ran the two pre-existing asset functional tests (`feature_assets.py`, `feature_assets_rules.py`, both start nodes WITH `-assetindex` from a clean chain, exercising this row's own modified `init.cpp` code on the FIRST-ever startup path too) -- both still "Tests successful", confirming no regression to the ordinary from-genesis case. **8. Full suite.** `assets_creation_tests`: 18 cases (up from F-224's 15), all green on a clean rebuild. | `source` (`assets/assets.h`/`.cpp`, `assets/assetsdb.h`/`.cpp`, `init.cpp`'s assetindex-state-change block and chainstate-init sequence, `rpc/rpcassets.cpp`, `node/coinstats.cpp`'s `GetUTXOStats` read for the cursor precedent, `docs/transaction-decoupling.md` §8 re-read for the snapshot-load-path claim); TDD (new C++ test, genuine RED via non-existence then two further genuine REDs found live while extending it, both fixed); mutation-tested (3/3 killed, reverted clean); two new/re-run functional tests against a real built `raptoreumd` (`feature_assetindex_late_enable.py` new, `feature_assets.py`/`feature_assets_rules.py` re-confirmed); full `assets_creation_tests` suite green | **F-225, built 2026-09-29 -- closes build-plan 4.3.1; asset existence/metadata confirmed already unconditional, only the balance index needed the coins-scan** |
| F-226 | **4.3.2 built: wallet rescan becomes range-driven -- audit found most of it already existed from prior 4.1.x work, and the real gap was narrower than the row's own text suggests: a wrong status bit in one place, and a missing upfront check in the import-RPC family specifically.** **1. What was already built, confirmed by direct read before writing anything.** `interfaces::Chain::findBlock` (`interfaces/chain.cpp:findBlock`) already declines gracefully for a body-not-held block (F-174/F-179's own `HaveBodies` guard) -- a rescan reaching into unretained history was never a crash. `rescanblockchain` (`rpc/rpcwallet.cpp`) already has its own upfront range check, `pwallet->chain().findPruned(start_height, stop_height)` (`rpcwallet.cpp:3700`), throwing before `ScanForWalletTransactions` is ever called. So "range-driven" was already mostly true for that ONE entry point. **2. Two real gaps, both found by direct read, neither assumed.** (a) `findPruned` (`interfaces/chain.cpp:findPruned`) checked `(block->nStatus & BLOCK_HAVE_DATA) == 0` -- the same F-36-class bit conflation already fixed at every other site in this tree (e.g. `rpc/blockchain.cpp`'s `GetBlockChecked`, F-164), not `HaveBodies`. Currently equivalent in behaviour ONLY because `PruneOneBlockFile` (`validation.cpp:PruneOneBlockFile`) clears `BLOCK_HAVE_DATA` and `BLOCK_HAVE_BODIES` together today -- an accidental correctness, not a designed one, and not future-proof against any scenario where the two bits could diverge. (b) The `RescanFromTime`-based paths -- every wallet-import RPC (`importprivkey`/`importaddress`/`importpubkey`/`importmulti`, `rpcdump.cpp`) plus the wallet's own startup rescan (`wallet.cpp:6164`) -- had NO upfront range check at all, unlike `rescanblockchain`: a request reaching outside a windowed node's retention would walk every single height in the unretained range one block at a time (each one a harmless but wasted `findBlock` decline) before ever reaching usable data, discovered only via `ScanResult::FAILURE` at the very end rather than refused up front. **3. Fixed.** `findPruned` now calls `HaveBodies(block)` directly (`interfaces/chain.cpp:findPruned`). A new upfront check lives INSIDE `CWallet::ScanForWalletTransactions` itself (`wallet/wallet.cpp:ScanForWalletTransactions`, right after `block_height` is resolved and before the scan loop) rather than duplicated per-RPC, so every entry point -- `rescanblockchain` AND every import RPC AND the startup rescan -- benefits uniformly from a single check point. New `CWallet::ScanResult` status, `RANGE_NOT_HELD` (`wallet.h`'s `ScanResult::status` enum), distinguishes "structurally outside this node's own retention, detected before scanning a single block" from `FAILURE`'s "corruption/read error, discovered one block at a time" -- matching `BodyRecordVerification::NOT_HELD`'s own "nothing claimed held, nothing wrong" shape in spirit, not literally reusing the enum (different domain: at-rest integrity vs. a wallet scan's own range). `rescanblockchain`'s switch statement (`rpcwallet.cpp:3727`) reports it with an explicit, distinct message naming the cause, for the residual case where the tip moved between the RPC's own upfront check and the scan itself. `RescanFromTime` (`wallet.cpp:2383-2394`) routes `RANGE_NOT_HELD` through the SAME path `FAILURE` already used -- correct because `chain().findBlock`'s `time`/`time_max` outputs come from the `CBlockIndex` header (`GetBlockTime`/`GetBlockTimeMax`), never the body, so the lookup succeeds for a not-held block exactly as it does for any other; `RescanFromTime`'s own `int64_t`-timestamp return type has no room to carry the distinction any further up to `RescanWallet`'s own generic "some transactions may be missing" message (`rpcdump.cpp:RescanWallet`), left as-is -- a deliberate, narrower scope boundary, not an oversight: `importmulti`'s own per-key error message (`rpcdump.cpp`) already explicitly names "caused by pruning" as a possibility, so it was not judged to need the same explicit treatment `rescanblockchain` got. **4. TDD.** New `wallet/test/wallet_tests.cpp:rescan_range_not_held_on_a_pruned_windowed_node` sets `fPruneMode = true` for real (via a new `PruneModeGuard`, matching `acceptancebit_tests.cpp`'s own `PerfWithholdGuard` precedent for leaving shared global state correctly restored even if a `BOOST_REQUIRE` throws) around the SAME `PruneOneBlockFile` call the pre-existing `rescan` test (`wallet_tests.cpp`, unmodified) already uses -- confirming by direct read that `fPruneMode` is never touched anywhere else in this file, so the pre-existing test's own `FAILURE`-status assertions stay genuinely unchanged (`findPruned` is itself gated on `fPruneMode` and stays dormant without it). A second new test, `find_pruned_reports_the_first_not_held_height`, exercises `findPruned` directly against a real pruned file. **5. Mutation-tested.** The new upfront check in `ScanForWalletTransactions` disabled (`if (block_height && false)`), rebuilt, confirmed `rescan_range_not_held_on_a_pruned_windowed_node` fails on all four of its RANGE_NOT_HELD-specific assertions (status, last_scanned_block, last_scanned_height, balance), reverted clean. The `findPruned` bit-fix itself is NOT separately mutation-killable today -- `PruneOneBlockFile` clears both bits in lockstep in this codebase's current state, so no test can currently construct a scenario where the two checks diverge; recorded honestly as future-proofing (matching F-164's own identical precedent for the same bit class) rather than forcing a fabricated kill. **6. Full suite and functional.** `wallet_tests`: 11 cases (up from 9), all green including the pre-existing `rescan`/`importwallet_rescan` tests unchanged. `wallet_import_rescan.py` re-run against the built daemon: "Tests successful". `feature_pruning.py` could not be run -- confirmed, by reading `test_framework/test_framework.py:628-632`'s own existing comment, to be a PRE-EXISTING, already-documented incompatibility (`-dip3params` rejected because DIP3 is already permanently active in this tree) unrelated to this row's changes, not something this session introduced or is responsible for fixing. | `source` (`interfaces/chain.cpp`/`.h`, `wallet/wallet.cpp`/`.h`, `wallet/rpcdump.cpp`, `wallet/rpcwallet.cpp`, `validation.cpp`'s `PruneOneBlockFile` re-read); TDD (2 new `wallet_tests.cpp` cases, genuine RED confirmed against the disabled check); mutation-tested (the new check, killed and restored; the bit-fix honestly recorded as not currently killable); full `wallet_tests` suite green including the unmodified pre-existing `rescan` test; `wallet_import_rescan.py` re-run against the built daemon, green; `feature_pruning.py`'s own inability to run confirmed pre-existing and unrelated | **F-226, built 2026-09-29 -- closes build-plan 4.3.2; most of "range-driven" was already built by 4.1.x, the real gaps were a wrong bit and a missing check in the import-RPC family** |
| F-227 | **4.3.3 built: transaction-content RPCs return an explicit "bodies not held" -- audit found this convention already exists at nearly every site from the 4.1.x campaign; the real, narrower gap was entirely inside `getrawtransaction`, the one site that campaign never touched.** **1. What was already built.** `HaveBodies` guards with a consistent, already-established `"(bodies not held)"` message convention already exist at `rpc/blockchain.cpp`'s `GetBlockChecked` (serving `getblock`/`getblockheader`/3 more call sites, F-164) and its own `getmerkleblocks` inner loop (F-173), `rpc/rawtransaction.cpp`'s `gettxoutproof` (F-163's own site), `rest.cpp`'s `/rest/block` endpoint, and `rpc/smartnode.cpp` (F-175/F-178) -- this project's own de facto version of `BodyRecordVerification::NOT_HELD`'s shape (a distinct, explicit signal, not a generic failure), just not literally that enum, since this is a different domain (RPC content-return, not at-rest integrity). `TxIndex::FindTx` (`index/txindex.cpp:FindTx`) already has its OWN `HaveBodies` check (F-171) -- but discarded the result at its `return false` boundary, with no way for any caller to recover the distinction. `gettransaction` (wallet RPC, `rpcwallet.cpp:gettransaction`) reads only `pwallet->mapWallet` -- the wallet's own already-synced copy, confirmed by direct read to never re-dereference a block body on demand -- so it is genuinely out of this row's scope, not merely skipped. **2. Two real gaps, both in `getrawtransaction` specifically (`rpc/rawtransaction.cpp`), the one content-returning RPC the 4.1.x campaign's own sweep never touched.** (a) Its blockhash-scoped path checked `!(blockindex->nStatus & BLOCK_HAVE_DATA)` -- the same F-36-class wrong-bit conflation fixed everywhere else in this tree, including its own sibling `gettxoutproof` in the SAME FILE -- and on a miss fell through to `"No such transaction found in the provided block"`, a message that reads exactly like "this transaction doesn't exist", the precise outcome this row's brief says must not happen. (b) Its txid-only fallback path (`block_index == nullptr`, resolved via `g_txindex`) had NO distinction at all: `GetTransaction`'s (`validation.cpp:GetTransaction`) txindex branch just called `g_txindex->FindTx(hash, hashBlock, tx)` and returned whatever bool came back, discarding `FindTx`'s own internal `HaveBodies` knowledge entirely -- a txid-only lookup into a not-held block fell all the way to `"No such mempool or blockchain transaction"`, indistinguishable from a genuinely nonexistent transaction. **3. Fixed** by threading a new, opt-in `bool *bodies_not_held` out-param through `GetTransaction` (`validation.h`/`.cpp:GetTransaction`) and `TxIndex::FindTx` (`index/txindex.h`/`.cpp:FindTx`) -- defaulted to `nullptr` everywhere, so every existing call site (`rest.cpp`, `rpc/smartnode.cpp`, `rpc/rpcevo.cpp`, `rpc/rpcquorums.cpp`, `wallet/test/wallet_tests.cpp`'s own `SignTransaction` helper) keeps today's exact behaviour unchanged. `getrawtransaction` now passes `&bodies_not_held` and, when set, throws the SAME `"Block not available (bodies not held)"` message its own sibling `gettxoutproof` already uses (`rpc/rawtransaction.cpp`) -- for BOTH the blockhash-scoped and txid-only paths, closing gap (a) and (b) with one shared fix point rather than two separate patches. **4. TDD, one real test-design correction found empirically, not assumed.** New `acceptancebit_tests.cpp:gettransaction_distinguishes_not_held_from_not_found_via_block_index` uses `PerfWithholdGuard` (a block genuinely never given real bodies) and confirms `bodies_not_held` is set for that block's own transaction but NOT for a genuinely-absent random txid in a held sibling block. The second new test, `gettransaction_distinguishes_not_held_from_not_found_via_txindex`, first tried the SAME `PerfWithholdGuard`-from-the-start approach for the txindex path and failed at the wrong assertion (`bodies_not_held` stayed false) -- diagnosed correctly rather than patched around: `TxIndex::WriteBlock` (`index/txindex.cpp`) only ever indexes a block that genuinely had bodies at index time, so a withhold-from-the-start block was never indexed at all, and `FindTx` returns false at its OWN "no index entry" line, never reaching the `HaveBodies` check this test targets. Rewritten to match `TxIndex::FindTx`'s own doc comment precisely: mine a REAL block (bodies present, genuinely indexed), confirm the lookup succeeds first, THEN prune it for real via `PruneOneBlockFile` (matching `wallet_tests.cpp`'s own established pattern, isolated to its own block file via the same file-size-capping trick), and only then confirm `bodies_not_held`. **5. Mutation-tested.** Both new `bodies_not_held = true` assignments (in `GetTransaction` and in `TxIndex::FindTx`) independently disabled, rebuilt, confirmed each specific test fails on exactly the `bodies_not_held` assertion, reverted clean. **6. Full suite, covering all three of 4.3.1/4.3.2/4.3.3 together.** `acceptancebit_tests`: up 2 cases, both new cases green. A full clean rebuild (`make -C src clean` then `make -C src -j20 test/test_raptoreum`) and full run: 743 cases, 1 failure -- `miner_tests/CreateNewBlock_validity` (`test/miner_tests.cpp:413`, `"exception std::runtime_error expected but not raised"`), the same pre-existing failure every prior row in this doc names, not a regression; no other site's behaviour changed (default-`nullptr` param, confirmed by the full suite showing no other test's results moved). `check-anchors.py`/`check-numbers.py` both exit 0. | `source` (`validation.h`/`.cpp`, `index/txindex.h`/`.cpp`, `rpc/rawtransaction.cpp`, `rest.cpp`, `rpc/smartnode.cpp`, `rpc/rpcevo.cpp`, `rpc/rpcquorums.cpp`, `rpc/blockchain.cpp`'s `GetBlockChecked`/`getmerkleblocks` re-read, `rpc/rpcwallet.cpp`'s `gettransaction` read in full to confirm out-of-scope); TDD (2 new `acceptancebit_tests.cpp` cases, one rewritten after a genuine empirical diagnosis of why the first draft's premise was wrong); mutation-tested (2/2 killed, reverted clean); full clean-rebuild suite (743 cases, 1 pre-existing failure) | **F-227, built 2026-09-29 -- closes build-plan 4.3.3; the not-held-vs-not-found distinction was already the norm everywhere except getrawtransaction, now closed there too. Together with F-225/F-226, closes build-plan 4.3 in full.** |

| F-229 | **A real, live-devnet-observed warning ("Warning: unknown new rules activated: Expected: 0x20000001, Actual: 0x2000000b" at the devnet's own LockedIn->Active boundary) traced to a reference-point mismatch, fixed in both places it occurs -- and a second, more dangerous fix attempt abandoned after it corrupted 594 unrelated tests, worth recording as its own lesson.** **1. Root cause.** `miner.cpp`'s `BlockAssembler::CreateNewBlock` set `pblock->nVersion = Updates().ComputeBlockVersion(pindexPrev)` -- the PARENT. `UpdateManager::State`'s own roundNumber math (`update/update.cpp:357`) uses `blockIndex->nHeight` directly with no adjustment (unlike upstream Bitcoin Core's `ComputeBlockVersion(pindexPrev)` convention, which has an implicit +1 baked into its own `VersionBitsState`) -- so `ComputeBlockVersion(X)` answers "what does X's OWN height expect," and passing the parent evaluates one height too early. The two reference points agree almost always, except exactly at the block where a deployment's state crosses LockedIn->Active (`ComputeBlockVersion` stops including a bit once Active, per `update.cpp`'s own `State==Voting||LockedIn` check) -- there the miner, still looking at the LockedIn parent, sets a bit the validator (looking at the real new block) no longer expects. **2. A second, independent instance of the same bug, found while tracing the first.** `validation.cpp`'s `UpdateTip` has two version-mismatch checks: the single-block one Mike's log line came from (`ComputeBlockVersion(pindex)`, using pindexNew itself -- already correct, since pindexNew is real and indexed by the time this runs) and a "last 100 blocks" upgrade-warning loop that used `ComputeBlockVersion(pindex->pprev)` -- the same parent-reference bug, independently, on a REAL historical block that needed no adjustment at all. Fixed to `ComputeBlockVersion(pindex)`, matching the single-block check right above it. Zero risk: every pointer involved is real and already indexed. **3. The abandoned fix, and why.** The first attempt made `miner.cpp` construct a lookahead `CBlockIndex` (`nHeight`/`pprev` set, never inserted into any real index) and called the existing `ComputeBlockVersion(&indexNextBlock)` on it -- reading-wise safe (`MinerRoundVoting::GetVote` only reads ancestors' `nVersion` via `pprev`, confirmed by direct read, never the passed-in index's own fields) but `State()`/`GetVote()` both cache their results keyed by raw `CBlockIndex*` (`states`/`cache` maps, `update.h:384,393` and `update.cpp:96,118,146`) in `UpdateManager`, which lives for the whole process via `globalChainParams->Updates()` (`chainparams.cpp:1085`). A stack-local object's address is only valid for one call; once that stack frame was reused (essentially guaranteed under any real mining load), an unrelated later object at the same address could silently alias the stale cached entry. This is not hypothetical: applying it and running the full suite produced 594 cascading failures starting from a genuine, non-corruption assertion (`assets_tests.cpp:252`, a real block rejected) that then took down `ECC_Start()` in every later test case -- reproduced identically on a clean rebuild, and confirmed NOT caused by the new unit test file by rebuilding with it unregistered from the Makefile (594 failures persisted). **4. The actual fix.** `UpdateManager::ComputeNextBlockVersion(pindexPrev, nHeight)` (new method, `update.h`/`update.cpp`) queries `State()` only on the real, persistent `pindexPrev` (cache-safe, identical to every other caller) and corrects for the LockedIn->Active transition using `StateInfo::FinalHeight` -- already the exact height `State()` itself uses internally to make that same transition, so no new vote-counting logic, no synthetic `CBlockIndex`, and no cache writes keyed on anything but a real pointer. Scoped narrowly to the transition actually observed live; the analogous Defined->Voting transition (a proposal beginning its voting period at a round boundary) is structurally similar but requires replaying a fresh round's vote count, not just an already-known height -- left unaddressed, since it is unobserved, informational-only (see below), and rarer than LockedIn->Active by construction (once per deployment's registration vs. once per deployment's entire lifetime either way, but Voting itself needs no fall-through vote check the way LockedIn's grace period does... left as a known, named gap, not silently absent). **5. Zero consensus impact, either bug.** Both mismatches are read only inside `UpdateTip` (`validation.cpp:3232`), called after a block is already connected -- purely a `LogPrintf` warning for node operators, never a validity gate. Confirmed by reading the call site directly, not assumed. **6. Verification.** Full suite before any of this work: 1 failure (the same pre-existing `miner_tests/CreateNewBlock_validity` every recent row already names). After the real fix: full suite, clean rebuild, same 1 failure -- reproduced on the untouched base commit too (temporarily reverted all five touched files via `git checkout <base> --`, rebuilt clean, same single failure), confirming it predates and is unrelated to this work. New test (`test/updatemanager_versionbits_tests.cpp`) builds a standalone `UpdateManager` (own registration, never the real singleton) and a `std::deque`-backed fabricated chain (stable addresses for the test's own lifetime only), walks it forward to a genuine LockedIn->Active boundary via the real `State()` machinery (not assumed from the grace-round formula), and checks `ComputeNextBlockVersion(parent, activeHeight)` against the raw, already-real `ComputeBlockVersion(activeChild)` -- the same disagreement Mike's live log line showed, now asserted to be resolved. | Live devnet log line (Mike, 2026-09-29): `warning='Warning: unknown new rules activated: Expected: 0x20000001, Actual: 0x2000000b'` at the devnet's own real LockedIn->Active boundary -- not predicted from source, found operating the thing | direct read of `miner.cpp`/`validation.cpp`/`update.cpp` call sites and `UpdateManager`'s cache members; the abandoned attempt's 594-failure cascade reproduced on a clean rebuild and isolated by unregistering the new test file (failures persisted, ruling it out); the real fix's full suite run compared against the untouched base commit's own full suite run (identical single pre-existing failure, not a new one) | **built and verified 2026-09-30, ready to merge into `perf/throughput-rig`** |
| F-230 | **Mike's two rumoured hardening questions ("does the node hold an empty handshake too long, and does a bad block from an IP actually ban it") investigated against live sources -- issue 1 confirmed real but unchanged upstream and closed with no code change per the owner's own call; issue 2 confirmed real and fixed: `ProcessFetchedBodyRange`'s two failure paths never notified `BlockChecked`, so the one invalidity class the commitment-only accept cannot see was never scored against its source peer. Stabilising the regression test then surfaced a second, independent bug -- in the test's own exception safety, not the fix -- that was corrupting a later, unrelated test in full-suite runs only.** **1. Issue 1, investigated and closed.** `DEFAULT_PEER_CONNECT_TIMEOUT=60` (`net.h:212`), enforced by `CConnman::InactivityCheck` (`net.cpp:1345`, configurable via `-peertimeout`): confirmed real (a version-less connection genuinely holds a slot for up to 60s) and confirmed UNCHANGED, fetched live, in both RTM's real upstream (`Raptor3um/raptoreum` master) and current Bitcoin Core master -- not a stale or RTM-specific default. Assessed low-severity: the slot is already bounded by `-maxconnections` regardless of the specific timeout value, so this is a minor amplifier of an already-bounded resource, not an unbounded hold. No PR recommended -- 15 years of the identical default going unchanged upstream is itself evidence the maintenance cost of tightening it is judged not worth the benefit there, and this fork inherits that tradeoff rather than overriding it unilaterally. Closed per the owner's own explicit "ok ignore it" -- no code change made or intended. **2. Issue 2, the real gap.** `ProcessFetchedBodyRange` (`validation.cpp:5276`) has two failure paths -- `MaterialiseBlock`'s own hash-mismatch guard (`validation.cpp:5302`) and the body-dependent `CheckBlock`/`ContextualCheckBlock` pair (`validation.cpp:5342`, the real sigop count/serialized size/aggregate input count `CheckCommitmentBlock`'s own count-only bound at commitment-accept time is documented as "necessary but not sufficient" for) -- and neither called `GetMainSignals().BlockChecked()`. Every OTHER path that can determine a block invalid in this tree -- `AcceptBlock`'s own re-offered-whole-block branch, `ConnectTip`'s own `ConnectBlock` call -- already does, so `PeerLogicValidation::BlockChecked` (`net_processing.cpp:2199`) never had a chance to `Misbehaving()` the peer `mapBlockSource` still names for that hash, exactly and only for the one invalidity class a commitment-only accept cannot see. **Fixed** by adding the identical `GetMainSignals().BlockChecked(materialised, state)` call to both failure paths, matching `AcceptBlock`/`ConnectTip`'s own convention of signalling at the point the verdict is actually decided. Zero consensus impact: this only affects `Misbehaving()` scoring of the peer that supplied the bad body, never any block-validity determination itself -- `state`'s own `IsInvalid()`/`nDoS` values were already being computed correctly by both paths before this fix; only the notification was missing. **3. TDD.** New `bad_body_from_getbodyrange_notifies_blockchecked` (`test/acceptancebit_tests.cpp:274`) builds a real commitment-only-accepted block via the established `PerfWithholdGuard` pattern, then calls `ProcessFetchedBodyRange` directly with a separately-constructed commitments/bodies pair carrying one genuinely invalid extra transaction (a non-coinbase tx with an empty `vout`, `CheckTransaction`'s own `bad-txns-vout-empty`) -- legitimate since the function takes both as plain parameters and never cross-checks them against `pindex`'s own real commitments, exactly the shape a real `GETBODYRANGE` response handler hands it. Genuine RED confirmed without the fix (`spy.calls.size() == 0`, not the required `1`), GREEN with it. **4. A second, independent bug found live while stabilising this test, not assumed away.** A full-suite run with the fix and new test both in place showed 2 failures, not the expected 1: the known pre-existing `miner_tests/CreateNewBlock_validity` (every recent row in this doc names it), plus a NEW segfault in `submitblock_tests/commitment_format_resolves_a_quorum_commitment_tx_the_mempool_never_held`'s own fixture destructor, cascading into a `CCheckQueue<CScriptCheck>` assertion failure (`m_worker_threads.empty()`) in a later test entirely. Run in isolation (`--run_test=submitblock_tests/...`), that same test passed cleanly -- ruling out a deterministic bug in its own logic and pointing at cross-test state corruption instead. Root cause, found by reading the new test's own teardown, not by guessing: `BlockCheckedSpy` was registered via a bare `RegisterValidationInterface`/`UnregisterValidationInterface` pair with the `Unregister` call placed unconditionally last -- the exact shape `PerfWithholdGuard`'s own precedent comment in this same file (`test/acceptancebit_tests.cpp:45-47`, "a thrown `BOOST_REQUIRE` between inserting and erasing would leave the flag set for every later test in the process") already exists to warn against, applied to global validation-interface registration instead of `g_perf_withhold_hashes`. Had any `BOOST_REQUIRE` between registration and the unconditional `Unregister` call thrown, the spy -- a stack-local object about to be destroyed on test-function return -- would stay registered as a live `CValidationInterface` listener for the rest of the process; the next real `BlockChecked()` call anywhere else (including inside `submitblock_tests`, which processes blocks of its own) would then invoke a dangling pointer. The SAME unguarded pattern is pre-existing at two OTHER call sites in this file (`RelayAtomicitySpy`'s own two tests, `relay_signals_never_fire_for_a_commitment_only_block` and `relay_signals_fire_for_an_ordinary_block`) -- left as a known, named gap for a future pass (their own assertions have evidently never failed in a full-suite run, so the risk has never fired there), not silently treated as equivalent-but-untouched. **Fixed** by adding `ValidationInterfaceGuard<Spy>` (`test/acceptancebit_tests.cpp:226`), a template RAII guard following `PerfWithholdGuard`'s own exact shape, and applying it to `BlockCheckedSpy`'s registration in the new test -- `UnregisterValidationInterface` now fires from the guard's destructor regardless of any exception thrown between construction and the end of scope. **5. Verification.** Before this stabilisation fix: full suite, 2 failures (the known pre-existing one plus the new segfault). After: clean rebuild, full suite, 746 cases, exactly 1 failure -- the same pre-existing `miner_tests/CreateNewBlock_validity`, confirmed by direct log inspection that `commitment_format_resolves_a_quorum_commitment_tx_the_mempool_never_held` now enters and leaves cleanly inside the full run itself, not merely in isolation. `check-anchors.py`/`check-numbers.py` both exit 0. | Owner-reported rumours, investigated against live sources (RTM's real upstream and Bitcoin Core master, fetched live, for issue 1; direct read of `validation.cpp`/`net_processing.cpp`'s `BlockChecked` call sites for issue 2); TDD (new `acceptancebit_tests.cpp` case, genuine RED confirmed before the fix, GREEN after); the second bug found by a genuine full-suite failure that did not reproduce in isolation, root-caused by reading the test's own teardown path rather than reverting code blind | **F-230, built 2026-09-30 -- issue 1 closed with no code change per owner instruction; issue 2's `BlockChecked` gap fixed and regression-tested; the test-hygiene bug the stabilisation pass surfaced fixed via RAII, confirmed by a clean full-suite rerun; ready to commit** |
| F-231 | **Confirmed live, not just from source reading: real asset and future transactions materialise correctly through a genuine commitment-only accept and a real GETBODYRANGE/BODYRANGE round trip between two independent node processes -- the first test crossing decoupling machinery with an actual asset/future payload; every prior test on either side of that boundary used none of the other's mechanism.** Mike, 2026-09-30: "does our chain fully support assets and futures ... I'm curious if they will work in decoupled blocks." `transaction-decoupling.md` §2.4's own table already classified special transactions (assets, futures, and CbTx alike -- one dispatch, `ProcessSpecialTxsInBlock`) as body-dependent, "already runs at connect" -- meaning `ConnectBlock` calling it never distinguishes how a block's bodies arrived. That was a sound conclusion from reading `ConnectTip`/`ConnectBlock` directly, but nobody had ever built the test proving it: `feature_assets.py`/`feature_futures.py`/their own characterisation siblings run with no decoupling flags at all, and `feature_bodyrange_e2e.py`/its siblings never construct a real asset or future payload. New `test/functional/feature_decoupled_assets_futures.py` (registered in `test_runner.py`) closes the gap, built on `feature_bodyrange_e2e.py`'s own proven two-node withhold/serve shape (`-fetchbodyrange`+`-perfwithholdheight`+`-perfwithholdcount=1` vs. `-commitmentblocks`+`-servebodyrange`) rather than re-deriving it. **A real, reproducible race found and fixed while building this, not assumed away**: the first draft kept both nodes connected throughout, matching the reference test's own topology -- and lost a genuine race every time. `-perfwithholdheight` forced a correct commitment-only accept (`AcceptBlock: accepting ... as commitments; bodies not held`, confirmed in the fetcher's own debug.log), but because the fetcher was already fully synced and live-connected, the SAME just-mined block also arrived via an ordinary BIP152 compact-block tip announcement (`cmpctblock`/`getblocktxn`/`blocktxn`) -- a second, independent `AcceptBlock` call for the same height, landing after the first had already consumed `-perfwithholdcount`'s one-shot budget, so it completed as an ordinary full accept and silently skipped GETBODYRANGE entirely. `transaction-decoupling.md` §3's own "tip regime" section explains why: compact-block relay is specifically for announcing a new block to an already-synced, live-connected peer, a condition this test's topology satisfied by construction. **Fixed** by disconnecting the fetcher from the server before creating and mining the withheld content, and only reconnecting afterward -- a peer that is genuinely behind by one block catches up via headers-then-block-download, which `-fetchbodyrange` routes through GETBODYRANGE with no competing path, confirmed via `assert_debug_log(["received: bodyrange"])`/`assert_debug_log(["received: getbodyrange"])` on both sides. A second thing tried and dropped, not a bug: asserting the fetcher's RPCs say "bodies not held" mid-fetch, matching `acceptancebit_tests.cpp`'s own established C++-level pattern for the same claim -- on localhost a one-block GETBODYRANGE round trip resolves faster than this test process can reliably observe the gap between "header known" and "body known," so the assertion just raced against its own precondition. Not re-tested here because it needs no new evidence: F-227's own fix (`GetTransaction`/`TxIndex::FindTx`'s `bodies_not_held` out-param) is driven purely by the block's `HaveBodies` status and never inspects the transaction's own content, so there is no type-specific path for an asset or future payload to diverge on. **What the test actually proves**: a real `createasset` transaction and a real `sendtoaddress`-with-`future` transaction, mined together into a commitment-only block, recovered via a genuine wire-level GETBODYRANGE/BODYRANGE exchange, land on the fetcher with `listassets`/`getassetdetailsbyname` output byte-identical to the server's own, and the future transaction's `futureTx.maturity`/`lockTime` payload intact -- indistinguishable from a node that received the block the traditional way. Chain-height and withheld-height constraints are both real, not arbitrary: `CHAIN_HEIGHT=830` is `feature_assets.py`'s own bar for Round Voting active plus matured, spendable coins (regtest's subsidy ramp pays only 4 RTM/block until 720, and the asset fee is 100); the withheld height sits at `831 % 30 == 21`, outside `llmq_test`'s own DKG mining window (`feature_bodyrange_e2e.py`'s own established constants). **Separately, while investigating the smartnode angle of the same question**: `docs/transaction-decoupling.md` §3A is a dedicated, already-owner-decided section on exactly how Smartnode quorums enter this design -- ChainLocks are Option B, "a record, not a validity gate" (decided 2026-09-12), and §3A.7's own manufactured-quorum-split attack (decoupling makes an existing, race-bound weakness attacker-schedulable) was assessed "required, and before the fetch protocol," then built and reviewed (F-136/F-137, 2026-09-21). F-137's own review explicitly deferred "a real 3-of-3 functional-test scenario proving the literal retry path, which has never executed outside the pure-function unit tests" -- still true today, and the natural next live-verification target after this row. | Owner's own factual question (2026-09-30), verified against live sources rather than reasoned from the design doc alone; a genuine live race found, diagnosed via the fetcher's own debug.log (`AcceptBlock: accepting ... as commitments; bodies not held` immediately followed by a second, silent full accept), and fixed by restructuring the test's own topology, not by patching around the symptom | **F-231, built 2026-09-30 -- assets and futures confirmed working end to end in a genuinely decoupled block; the smartnode/ChainLock retry mechanism's own live-test gap (F-137) remains open and is queued next** |
| F-232 | **F-137's own deferred gap closed: the DIP8 signing-attempts retry mechanism (F-136/F-137), live-fired by a genuine 3-of-3 quorum split with real network partitioning, converging correctly -- and confirmed mutation-tested, not just argued, after a first draft was caught being tautological before it shipped.** **1. The gap.** F-137's own review (2026-09-21) built and reviewed the DIP8 retry fix with 17 pure-function unit tests plus one real-daemon functional run of the happy path (`feature_llmq_chainlocks.py`) -- but explicitly deferred "a real 3-of-3 functional-test scenario proving the literal retry path, which has never executed outside the pure-function unit tests." Still true until this row. **2. The first draft was tautological, caught before shipping** (Mike, 2026-09-30: "make sure the test is not tautological and really tests the architecture"). It split three real smartnodes (not `feature_llmq_chainlocks.py`'s own controller) onto two competing single-block tips, then let the winning side pull ahead by mining two MORE blocks before reconnecting -- so the height that finally got chainlocked was H+2, a height nobody had ever attempted before. `TrySignChainTip` signs `::ChainActive().Tip()` only (`quorums_chainlocks.cpp:262-265`); DIP8's own one-shot-per-height guard (the bug F-136 fixed) only ever blocks a REPEATED attempt at the SAME height, so two members making their own first-ever attempt on a brand-new height needs no retry logic at all -- that draft would have passed identically with F-136/F-137 fully reverted. **3. The fix: never extend either branch.** mn1 mines ONE block (H) alone while isolated, attempts to sign it alone, fails (1 of 3). Mocktime advances past `CLSIG_ATTEMPT_LOOKBACK`'s own 2-slot tolerance (F-137) so the failed round's own share is provably expired, then ONLY mn1 reconnects and is asked to sign H again in a fresh attempt slot; mn2 (never isolated, held nothing at H before) makes its own first-ever attempt in the same slot. Two votes for H -- one a genuine retry (mn1), one genuinely fresh (mn2) -- reach threshold=2 at the ORIGINAL contested height, not a taller descendant. mn3 mines its own competing H' while isolated too (matching `transaction-decoupling.md` 3A.7's "the remainder are still on the honest sibling"), but by construction (equal work, no voluntary reorg onto a merely-equal alternative) never contributes a vote either way -- deliberately irrelevant to the discriminating proof, not silently assumed harmless. **4. Mutation-tested, not just argued.** `DecideChainLockSignAction` (`quorums_chainlocks.h`) temporarily reverted to the exact bug it replaced -- `nTipHeight == nLastSignedHeight` returning `kNone` unconditionally, dropping the `nAttemptNum` re-check entirely -- rebuilt, and run: genuine RED, hanging at the post-reconnect wait with no CLSIG ever formed, killed by an external 90s timeout. Reverted the mutation, rebuilt, reran: clean GREEN, identical to the pre-mutation run. **5. A genuinely separate, real gap found and dropped, not hidden.** Reconnecting mn3 afterward to observe its losing H' marked "conflicting" (matching `feature_llmq_chainlocks.py`'s own established assertion shape) does not work as a simple follow-on: mn3 mined H' itself, giving it equal work to H, and Bitcoin Core's own tie-break never voluntarily reorgs onto a merely-equal alternative -- getting mn3 onto H needs either extra work (which would reopen the exact tautology this row exists to avoid) or `EnforceBestChainLock` overriding the tie-break via the real ChainLock, a separate mechanism and its own timing this row does not need to also exercise. Dropped rather than forced. **6. Also confirmed, narrower scope than first assumed.** A genuine network partition (isolate, mine, reconnect) does NOT route recovery through GETBODYRANGE at all -- `net_processing.cpp:1329` gates that path on the block already carrying `BLOCK_HAVE_DATA` (a prior commitment-only accept), which a peer that simply missed an ordinary announcement never enters; confirmed live via the reconnecting controller's own debug.log showing an ordinary `headers`/`cmpctblock`/`getblocktxn`/`blocktxn` recovery instead. Decoupling's own GETBODYRANGE recovery stays proven separately (F-231); this row's own scope is the retry mechanism specifically, and `-fetchbodyrange`/`-servebodyrange`/`-commitmentblocks` are left enabled on every node here only to confirm they coexist correctly with chainlocks/DKG, not because this scenario exercises them. New `test/functional/feature_llmq_chainlocks_decoupled.py`, registered in `test_runner.py`. | Mike's own request to continue "in order, quorum test first" from a prior turn's punch list, then his own live challenge ("make sure the test is not tautological") applied directly against the first draft before it was reported done | genuine RED-then-GREEN mutation test against the real DIP8 retry fix, not merely reasoned about; the tautology in the first draft found by tracing `TrySignChainTip`'s own signature (`::ChainActive().Tip()` only) against what height the test's own extra mining would actually leave contested | **F-232, built 2026-09-30 -- F-137's own deferred live-retry gap closed; mutation-confirmed non-tautological; ready to commit** |
| F-233 | **A real gap closed: this node never verified its own body-store data before serving it to a peer -- a silently corrupted local record (bit rot, a bad sector) would be served unchanged, and the honest peer's own hash check would correctly Misbehaving()-ban this node for it, with no way to notice short of an operator manually running verifybodystore. Closed with a periodic self-verification sweep, C3's own fix note ("self-verification of the store at rest") -- the check already existed (VerifyBodyRecordAtRest, F-158-era), nothing ran it automatically.** **1. The gap, precisely.** `BuildBodyRangeResponse` (`bodyrange.cpp`) reads whatever bytes `ReadBodyRange` returns and ships them -- no hash check against the block's own committed identifiers, ever, on the serving side. `VerifyBodyRecordAtRest`/the `verifybodystore` RPC (`rpc/net.cpp`) already do the real check, but both are operator-triggered diagnostics; nothing ran either automatically. **2. Deliberately NOT "repair."** `VerifyBodyRecordAtRest`'s own doc comment (`validation.h`) already argues, at length, against ever un-asserting `BLOCK_HAVE_BODIES` on a connected block live -- no precedent anywhere in this tree, and every `HaveBodies()` reader (`ConnectTip`, both `net_processing.cpp` serving sites, `rest.cpp`) assumes that bit, once true, stays true. This fix does not touch it. It touches a narrower, already-live-mutable flag with exactly one reader: `RecordBodyPositionByHash`'s own `fServeable` (`bodystore.h`) -- its own doc comment already describes it flipping false->true live when a withheld block's body arrives later; this is the same flag, the other direction, and its only consumer (`LookupServeableBodyPositionByHash`) is called from nowhere but the GETBODYRANGE serving path (`ValidateGetBodyRange`). Flipping it changes what this one node offers to peers and nothing else. **3. Built.** `ScrubBodyRecordAtRestOnce(pindex, consensusParams)` (`validation.h`/`.cpp`) runs `VerifyBodyRecordAtRest` and, on any result other than `OK`/`NOT_HELD`, logs a `LogPrintf` warning naming the height/hash/reason and quarantines the record via `RecordBodyPositionByHash(hash, pindex->GetBodyPos(), /*fServeable=*/false)`. `ScrubBodyStoreAtRest()` is the scheduled wrapper: a rotating height cursor walks 20 blocks per call, off `cs_main` for the actual I/O (matching this file's own established discipline), so a 720-block retention window (A1's own framing) is fully swept roughly every 6 hours at a 10-minute tick -- deliberately slow, matching "background health check", not a hot-path gate (a per-request re-hash would undo F-150's own work keeping `BuildBodyRangeResponse` cheap). Wired into `init.cpp`'s scheduler, gated on `-servebodyrange` specifically -- the risk only exists for a node serving bodies to anyone at all. **4. TDD, genuine RED-then-GREEN.** New `test/acceptancebit_tests.cpp:a_corrupt_body_record_is_quarantined_and_stops_being_served` mines two real blocks, corrupts one via the same swap-two-bodies technique this file's own `body_record_at_rest_detects_a_hash_mismatch_from_swapped_bodies` already established (readable, self-consistent, wrong -- the shape a real bit-flip takes), confirms the corrupted record is still serveable BEFORE scrubbing (reproducing the gap itself), then confirms it is not AFTER, while the untouched sibling block stays serveable throughout (a targeted quarantine, not a blanket one) and scrubbing an already-OK record does nothing. Mutation-tested: the quarantine call temporarily disabled, rebuilt, run -- genuine RED at exactly `!isServeable(corruptBlock.GetHash())`; reverted, rebuilt, reran -- clean GREEN. **5. Full suite.** Clean rebuild, full run, same one pre-existing `miner_tests` failure every recent row names, no regression. `check-anchors.py`/`check-numbers.py` both exit 0. | Mike, 2026-09-30: "have we tested situations... where a node tries to share bad body data?" -- investigated, found real, fixed same session per the owner's own standing "find weaknesses, patch immediately" authorization | genuine RED-then-GREEN mutation test against the real quarantine call; full suite rerun clean before commit | **F-233, built 2026-09-30 -- ready to commit** |
| F-234 | **A real, confirmed gap closed: on a pruned node, the body store (`bdy*.dat`) was never pruned at all -- unbounded growth for exactly the data `-prune` exists to bound, regardless of whether pruning mode is even active. Mike, 2026-09-30: "if node is pruned, bodies should be pruned identically to block data."** **1. Confirmed, not assumed.** `PruneOneBlockFile`'s own prior comment already admitted it: "no body-file pruning exists yet (F-141/F-142's own confirmed grep)". Grepping fresh confirmed the same thing live: `BodyFileSeq()` is never passed to `fs::remove` anywhere in the tree; `UnlinkPrunedFiles` only ever deletes `blk*.dat`/`rev*.dat`. This is broader than C3's own "stale sibling bodies become permanent" framing (`transaction-decoupling.md` 14.4) -- it is not just losing-fork bodies, it is EVERY historical block's body, on ANY node running `-prune`, including the active-chain ones that classic pruning already correctly reclaims. **2. Why not a format migration.** The natural analogue -- give `CBodyFileInfo` its own `nHeightFirst`/`nHeightLast`, mirroring `CBlockFileInfo` exactly -- would need an on-disk/DB format change to an already-persisted structure (`DB_BODY_FILES`), a real migration-compatibility question for existing datadirs. Avoided entirely: `ChainstateManager::FindOrphanedBodyFiles` (`validation.h`/`.cpp`) answers the same question empirically, with a single pass over the already-in-memory block index per prune event (infrequent; cheap even at real chain lengths) rather than a persisted per-file height range -- correct regardless of how body-file boundaries fall relative to blk-file boundaries (`bodystore.h`'s own independent-numbering decision, 2.1.1), because it asks "does anything still standing name this file" directly instead of inferring it from a height window. **3. Built.** `PruneOneBlockFile` gained an optional `setBodyFilesTouchedOut` output param: whenever it prunes a block that had a body record, it now clears `BLOCK_HAVE_BODY_RECORD`/`nBodyFile`/`nBodyPos` and erases the hash-index entry entirely (a new `EraseBodyPositionByHash`, `bodystore.h`/`.cpp` -- distinct from `RecordBodyPositionByHash(hash, pos, false)`'s own "still meaningful, just not currently serveable" case for a withheld block whose body may yet arrive) in the SAME event as the classic block data, not merely marking it unserveable as before. `FindOrphanedBodyFiles` then answers, once every file in that prune event is processed, which touched body files nothing in the index still references; `ResetBodyFileInfo` (new, `bodystore.h`/`.cpp`, needed a new `CBodyFileInfo::SetNull()`) clears each one's own size bookkeeping and marks it dirty for the next flush (the SAME atomic `WriteBatchSync` batch F-132 already folds body-file info into); `UnlinkPrunedBodyFiles` (new, `validation.h`/`.cpp`) deletes the actual `bdy*.dat` bytes, called from `FlushStateToDisk` immediately alongside the existing `UnlinkPrunedFiles`, after that same batch is durable -- matching `UnlinkPrunedFiles`'s own crash-safety ordering (DB state marked gone before the bytes are actually removed, never the reverse). Wired into both `FindFilesToPrune` (automatic, target-based) and `FindFilesToPruneManual` (the `pruneblockchain` RPC). **4. TDD, genuine RED-then-GREEN.** Four new `acceptancebit_tests.cpp` cases: `pruning_a_block_clears_its_body_record_identically_to_its_block_data` (the direct claim -- mine a real body, prune its blk-file, confirm the body-record bit/fields/index-entry are ALL gone in the same event); `find_orphaned_body_files_never_reports_a_still_referenced_file` (the safety property -- body-file 0, genuinely still referenced by `TestChain100Setup`'s own pre-mined chain, must never come back orphaned); `find_orphaned_body_files_reports_a_genuinely_unreferenced_file` (the other half, a synthetic file number nothing has ever heard of); `unlink_pruned_body_files_deletes_the_actual_bytes` (mechanical -- a record written directly to a synthetic, definitely-unused file number, distinct from file 0, which every other test case in this binary shares and depends on staying intact). Mutation-tested: the still-referenced guard in `FindOrphanedBodyFiles` disabled (`if (false && ...)`), rebuilt, run -- genuine RED, `setOrphaned.count(nFile) == 0U` failing `[1 != 0]`, exactly the safety property this function exists to guarantee. Reverted, rebuilt, reran -- clean GREEN. **5. Full suite.** Clean rebuild, full run, same one pre-existing `miner_tests` failure every recent row names, no regression. `check-anchors.py`/`check-numbers.py` both exit 0. | Mike, 2026-09-30, correcting the scope of the C6 catalog entry I had just reported as "already tracked, not worth a new test" -- the pruned-node case is a sharper, more concrete, and more clearly unintended gap than the losing-sibling framing I had evaluated | genuine RED-then-GREEN mutation test against the real safety guard; full suite rerun clean before commit | **F-234, built 2026-09-30 -- ready to commit** |
| F-235 | **The wire-level malicious-body defense already proven at the unit and scripted-single-exchange level (`ValidateBodyRangeChunkHashes`/`Misbehaving`, exercised earlier this session by a raw-socket peer script) now proven for real between two genuine daemons: a bad actor's own on-disk body record, deliberately altered, served to an honest peer over a real GETBODYRANGE/BODYRANGE round trip -- detected on the very first mismatched chunk. Mike, 2026-09-30: "if a node bad actor starts to push altered bodies to other nodes ... have we tried something like this?" No production code changed; this closes a test-coverage gap in already-shipped detection logic.** **1. What's proven.** New `test/functional/feature_malicious_body_server.py`: a "malicious" node mines a real block with two real, equal-length transactions, then has its own `bdy00000.dat` altered in place (the two transactions' serialized bytes swapped, byte-for-byte -- the same technique `acceptancebit_tests.cpp`'s own `body_record_at_rest_detects_a_hash_mismatch_from_swapped_bodies` and F-233's quarantine test already use for this exact shape of corruption). An "honest" node, disconnected before the block was mined (so reconnecting forces a genuine GETBODYRANGE fetch rather than ordinary compact-block relay -- F-231's own established lesson), reconnects and requests it. Confirmed live: `net_processing.cpp:4254`'s `ValidateBodyRangeChunkHashes` check catches the mismatch on the FIRST chunk, and the existing `Misbehaving(peer, 100, "sent a bodyrange chunk that does not match")` call (`net_processing.cpp:4262-4264`) fires immediately (`0 -> 100`, "BAN THRESHOLD EXCEEDED" in the very same log line) -- confirmed via `rpc/net.cpp`'s own real `banscore` field on `getpeerinfo`, not a log-timing guess. The honest node's chain height never advances past the corrupted block, confirmed both immediately and again after an 8-second real-time hold (ruling out some later retry quietly accepting the bad data). **2. Two real design questions surfaced and resolved while building this, both worth recording.** First: the malicious node cannot simply be stopped and restarted after the alteration to "serve it fresh" -- `validation.cpp`'s `VerifyDB`, run against any non-empty coinsview on every ordinary startup (`init.cpp:2632`), calls `VerifyBodyRecordAtRest` on the chain tip UNCONDITIONALLY (`validation.cpp:6217`'s own comment: "not gated behind nCheckLevel"), and there is no `-checkblocks`/`-checklevel` combination that skips the tip block itself -- confirmed live, a restart after the identical swap produced "Corrupted block database detected" on the malicious node's OWN startup, before it ever reached the network code that would serve the block. This is a genuine defense-in-depth property for anyone running the real, unmodified binary: the only window where a stock node can unknowingly serve corrupted-at-rest data is between it happening while already running and F-233's own 10-minute `ScrubBodyStoreAtRest` sweep catching it -- so this test corrupts the file live, with no restart, both to avoid testing the wrong thing (a bad actor doesn't run the stock binary and get caught by its own boot check; they run their own build, or never restart) and to land inside that exact window. Second: the test's own two-node connection, made via `connect_nodes()`'s `addnode(..., "onetry")` (`util.py`), sets `pnode->m_manual_connection = true`, and `net_processing.cpp`'s `fShouldBan` consumer (`net_processing.cpp:5325-5341`) checks that BEFORE the `addr.IsLocal()` branch, logging "Warning: not punishing manually-connected peer" and never setting `fDisconnect` or calling the banman at all -- confirmed by the actual log line, not the `IsLocal()`-on-loopback exemption a first draft of this test wrongly assumed. A real, normally-discovered (non-manual) peer connection is not exempted and would be torn down immediately; that specific difference is a connection-management detail the test does not need to also exercise, since the security property that matters -- the corrupted data is never accepted -- doesn't depend on it. **3. Debugged live, not shipped on the first pass.** First run: malicious node failed to even start after the swap (VerifyDB catch above) -- fixed by removing the restart. Second run: a same-size-transaction assumption failed for real (`226 == 225`, ECDSA DER-length/low-S variance) -- fixed by pooling a small batch of transactions and picking any two that happen to match in length rather than assuming the first two always would. Third run: the original `wait_until(lambda: not honest.getpeerinfo())` assertion timed out at 30s even though detection had already fired instantly -- traced to the manual-connection exemption above, replaced with the real `banscore` RPC check. Confirmed clean across 5 consecutive runs after all three fixes. **4. Registered** in `test_runner.py`, matching F-158's own registration-is-part-of-the-fix lesson (a test that exists but isn't listed is exactly how that regression shipped past a green run). | Mike, 2026-09-30, a direct question about whether a deliberately malicious (not merely corrupted) node had been tested against a real peer, asked mid-turn while a different item was being verified | five consecutive clean runs of the new functional test against the real, unmodified binary; no production code touched, so no mutation test applies -- this closes a coverage gap in already-shipped detection (`ValidateBodyRangeChunkHashes`/`Misbehaving`), not new behavior | **F-235, built 2026-09-30 -- ready to commit** |

|---|---|---|
| F-4, F-7, F-30, F-32 | four numbers with no single value | a measurement at a stated regime, or one choice recorded here |
| F-29 | `DisconnectTip` across a body gap is unreachable without dual validation — and is a cost dual validation had not been charged | answers from trí (D-6) |
| F-18 at quorum 50 | the InstantSend gap is priced from a quorum a tenth the live size | **NOT 0.4** — it formed an 8-member quorum on ten smartnodes, and `-llmqtestparams` overrides only size and threshold, so the swarm can never answer this. Needs a **50-smartnode loopback regtest on mario**, which is how F-18 was measured in the first place |

| F-93 | whether the OP_TRUE-style trivial-spend shape that exposes the ATMP O(n²) cost can actually reach a policy-enforcing node's mempool over real relay — `Solver()` almost certainly classifies a bare `OP_TRUE` scriptPubKey as `TX_NONSTANDARD`, which `AreInputsStandard` would reject without `acceptnonstdtxn=1` (set on the bench node for exactly this reason) | independent check of `Solver()`'s classification, and whether any standard-and-relayable script shape gets close to the same per-input byte-minimum |
| F-94 | the exact line responsible for the ATMP-only O(n²) cost | a profiler run (perf/gprof) against the isolated node while replaying the N-sweep |
| F-94 | whether the body-byte cap needs to budget for this at all, now that ConnectBlock itself does not show the blowup — the ~70-140 ms/tx figure is real but far smaller than F-93's mempool number | 1.2, once F-94's noisy connect-time sweep is tightened |
